  - **DECLARATION DUTY (the anti-drift half):** every new replicated class states its tier in a header comment AND in its task handoff; **"tier not declared" is a QA FAIL**, as is a Tier-B class carrying a hand-typed distance. **P2 WAVE DUTY: the unit fleet is Tier B by default and must be BANDWIDTH-MEASURED at full fleet** — a blanket `bAlwaysRelevant` across dozens of units is explicitly WRONG; if the measurement hurts, the lever is update-frequency throttling / `NetUpdateFrequency` and dormancy, NOT shrinking the arena band (units must be visible marching).
  - **D9 CORRECTION (recorded so the signed doc is not misread):** the scatter's "client obstacles are a SUPERSET of the server's ⇒ never rubber-bands" argument is VOID unless the seed actually arrives — under default relevancy the client got a strict SUBSET (zero), the exact inversion the design promised to avoid. **The argument's precondition is Tier A membership**; the doc's §3.5 reasoning is otherwise intact.
  - **COROLLARY — `HasAuthority()` and non-replicating actors (⚠️ AMENDED 2026-07-29: the manager's original APPLICATION of this was REFUTED by QA against installed UE 5.8 source, `qa/TASK-356.md` loop-1 — the correction travels with the law; the principle survives, the diagnosis does not).**
    - **THE NARROW PRINCIPLE (true, keep it):** an actor **spawned LOCALLY ON A CLIENT** with `bReplicates = false` keeps `ROLE_Authority`, so `HasAuthority()` returns TRUE there and any authority guard on it silently inverts. Never guard gameplay on `HasAuthority()` in a locally-client-spawned non-replicating class.
    - **THE APPLICATION THAT WAS WRONG (recorded so it is not re-derived):** I read the flagged fourth item (a client `ServerRequestPlayAgain` executing locally while its own PC read `bReplicates=False`) as proof of the inversion. It is not. **NET-SPAWNED actors — PlayerController, pawn, PlayerState — take their roles FROM THE CHANNEL**, so a CDO-level `bReplicates=false` on a client's own PC is NORMAL: `APlayerController`'s constructor never sets it (only `ANoPawnPlayerController`, PlayerController.cpp:6813); the SERVER enables it per-instance at login (World.cpp:4936-38) and the flag is not itself replicated. Moreover `AActor::GetFunctionCallspace` (Actor.cpp:5467-5666) **never reads `bReplicates`** — a client's only non-Remote outcome is Absorbed-with-`LogNet`-warning, which does NOT execute the body, so it cannot produce the observed "body ran, no warning, host silent". **Nothing in P1 was mis-guarded.**
    - **THE ACTUAL CAUSE — A TEST-LANE ARTIFACT, and this is the durable clause: NEVER VALIDATE RPC ROUTING FROM PYTHON REMOTE-EXEC.** `FEditorScriptExecutionGuard` (declared in `PyUtil::InvokeFunctionCall`, PyUtil.cpp:644) sets `GAllowActorScriptExecutionInEditor` (ScriptCore.cpp:455), which is the **FIRST branch** of `GetFunctionCallspace` (Actor.cpp:5469-74) ⇒ callspace **Local**. A python invoke therefore CANNOT distinguish a working relay from a broken one — it forces local execution either way. **RPC routing is verified ONLY through the real input path (the actual button / key / UI the player uses), never a scripted invoke.** Any verification claim about a `Server*` RPC sourced from remote-exec is void on its face.
- **PIPELINE-DOCS COMMITS ARE ALWAYS PERMITTED (ratified 2026-07-29):** `.claude/pipeline/**` docs (signed architecture, audits, QA reports, handoffs) may be committed **docs-only, at any time, by any agent that holds Git**, clearly labeled with **no integration claim**. The hard gate governs CODE and ART integration — it was never a reason to leave 30 KB of signed architecture untracked, and this repo has already lost never-committed docs once (`1a04971`). A docs-only commit is NEVER a substitute for a gated integration commit, and never carries code/asset paths.
- **PHASE-GATE LAW (the M7.6 ladder shape):** P0 audit+architecture → **P1 FIRST PLAYABLE INCREMENT (hard gate: two clients, one `L_Arena` match, identical authoritative core state — castle HP both sides, own gold, match start/win/lose)** → P2 gameplay replication + RPC surfaces (units/hero/combat/placement/spells/group orders) → P3 lobby polish + client-deck handoff + practice-mode coexistence + edge cases → P4 hardening + capstone 2-client playtest + the GDD slice clip. Only the current phase is task-decomposed; later phases stay one-liners until their gate.
- **M8 PARALLEL LAW (while any other lane holds uncommitted shared-module C++):** M8 tasks are READ-ONLY / NEW-FILES-ONLY until the conflicting lane's code commit lands (UBT compiles the whole module — parked C++ breaks every compile; two agents in one file clobber). Single owner per file within M8; `Config/DefaultEngine.ini` is single-owner like any file.
  - **⚠️ AMENDED 2026-08-02 — THIS LAW GUARDS EDIT CONFLICTS ONLY, AND THAT GAP COST A COMPILE GATE (TASK-401).** "New files only" makes a task safe to *write* beside another lane; it does **NOT** make it safe to run beside a *build*. See **"⛔ THE QUIET-MODULE LAW — build-gate serialization (2026-08-02)"** below: **file-disjointness is not build-disjointness**, no programmer task in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/` may be in flight while a compile gate runs, and two compile gates are mutually exclusive.

## ⛔ THE QUIET-MODULE LAW — build-gate serialization (2026-08-02)
Added 2026-08-02 after a **live pipeline defect**: TASK-401 (the FOLLOW lane's compile gate) came back `Result: Failed` on **three diagnostics from `SiegeAssistantCommand.cpp` and `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp`** — files a live agent in a *different batch* was actively writing while the build ran. **The FOLLOW lane itself compiled completely clean** (7 lane TUs + 4 unity blobs, zero diagnostics; a grep of every §7 pinned symbol ∩ `error|warning|unresolved` returned zero rows — the registry held). **A clean lane was failed by a lane it has nothing to do with.** This is an ORCHESTRATION error, not an agent error, and it is recorded as law because it is invisible in any single task's spec — the same class as the board write-race.

> ### **FILE-DISJOINTNESS IS NOT BUILD-DISJOINTNESS.**
> `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/` is **ONE UBT module**. Any in-flight `.cpp` in that module — **even a brand-new file owned by an unrelated batch** — is compiled by, and can fail, every other lane's compile gate.
> **The M8 PARALLEL LAW guards EDIT CONFLICTS, not COMPILE-GATE CONTAMINATION.** "New files only" makes a task safe to *write* beside another lane; it does **not** make it safe to run beside a *build*.
> **Therefore: no programmer task in that module may be dispatched `▶ NOW` while any compile gate is running, and two compile gates are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE.** A batch's compile gate requires a **quiet module**, not merely non-overlapping files.

- **THE ORCHESTRATOR'S DUTY (this is where the fix lives):** before dispatching a compile/integration task, **quiesce the module** — no programmer task in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/` may be in flight. Before dispatching a programmer task in that module, **confirm no compile gate is running.** Cross-batch is exactly the dangerous case, because each batch's own board looks safe in isolation.
- **A "quiet module" means: every C++ task in that module is finished (handoff written), not merely non-conflicting.** A half-written new file is the worst case — it is syntactically incomplete *and* nothing in any board flags it.
- **THE PLUGIN BOUNDARY IS REAL AND IS WHY IT EXISTS.** `Plugins/SiegeLlama/` is a **separate UBT module**, so plugin-only work is genuinely parallel-safe against a game-module compile gate. This is a concrete, now-demonstrated payoff of the plugin split (CONVENTIONS "In-match LLM command assistant" §6 ruling B) — prefer plugin-side work when a game-module gate is imminent.
- **DIAGNOSTIC ATTRIBUTION IS PART OF THE LAW.** A compile failure is attributed to **the file the diagnostic names**, never to the lane that happened to run the build. **Foreign diagnostics are routed to the owning batch's QA gate as early information, and are NEVER recorded as the running lane's findings** or counted against its QA loop budget. A build-master that reports "my gate failed" without naming which lane owns each diagnostic has not finished the report.
- **A CONTAMINATED GATE IS RE-RUN, NOT RE-LITIGATED.** Once the module is quiet, re-run it. Do not "fix" another batch's in-flight file to make your own gate pass — that is a single-owner violation on top of a serialization violation.

### Editor-close discipline at a build gate (build-master, added 2026-08-02)
- **A graceful editor close can hang on a Slate "Save Content" modal.** ⚠️ **"Save Selected" sits DIRECTLY BESIDE "Don't Save"** — a blind click is a coin-flip that can write an unwanted asset, and `L_Arena` is under a **never-save** law. **RULING: terminating the process is the CORRECT call** when the only dirty assets are known-discardable; a blind modal click is not. Verify afterwards and report the evidence (precedent: `L_Arena` confirmed **byte-identical, 535,522 B, 7/29 03:53:38**, with only `L_Arena` and `BP_Unit_Wizard` dirty and both discardable).
- This does **not** override the standing rule that **closing the editor is Jonathan's choice when he is present** — it governs only an unattended build gate.

## ⛔ GIT HAZARD LAWS (2026-08-02) — three traps, all found live, all silent

### (a) `git diff` CANNOT SEE A LINE-ENDING-ONLY CHANGE. A BYTE-LEVEL RESTORE IS VERIFIED BYTE-LEVEL.
A `.uproject` plugin toggle done as a Python read/modify/write round-trip was restored and came back **805 B instead of 859 B** — **CRLF → LF on all 54 line endings** — and **`git diff` showed it PERFECTLY CLEAN because `core.autocrlf` normalises the difference away before the comparison.** Only the checksum caught it.
- **LAW: any "I put the file back" claim is proven by CHECKSUM OR BYTE SIZE, never by `git diff`.** Record the sha/size before the edit and re-compare after. This generalises past `.uproject`: **`autocrlf` makes `git diff` structurally blind to whitespace-class corruption**, which is exactly the corruption a scripted round-trip introduces.
- **PREFER A TEXT-PRESERVING EDIT OVER A PARSE/RE-SERIALISE ROUND-TRIP** for JSON/INI/config files. A round-trip rewrites every byte it did not need to touch; a targeted string edit does not.

### (b) ⚠️ THE `.gitattributes` LFS TRAP — THE RULE MUST LAND *BEFORE* THE FIRST COMMIT THAT ADDS THE BINARIES
**No `*.dll` / `*.lib` LFS rule exists in this repo today, and `Plugins/` already holds 72 MB across 20 binaries with 0 tracked files — headlined by `ggml-vulkan.dll` at 49.9 MB.** **Jonathan ruled LFS.**
- **LAW: the `.gitattributes` LFS rule is committed BEFORE — or in the same commit as — the first commit that adds `Plugins/SiegeLlama/`.** ⚠️ **Git history is append-only: blobs committed raw stay raw FOREVER**, and un-doing it means a history rewrite on a repo Jonathan pushes. **This is a BLOCKING PRE-FLIGHT on the commit task, never a follow-up note.**
- Verify with `git lfs status` / `git check-attr filter -- <path>` **before** committing, and paste the output — a `.gitattributes` that exists but does not match the path is the same defect as no rule at all.

### (d) ⛔ A **DIRECTORY** PATHSPEC IS A BOUNDED `git add -A`. STAGE **EXPLICIT FILE PATHS** WHENEVER ANYTHING IS IN FLIGHT (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **A DIRECTORY PATHSPEC IS SAFE AGAINST *OTHER* DIRECTORIES AND DEFENCELESS AGAINST **ITS OWN** DIRECTORY'S CONCURRENT WRITES.**

- ⚠️ **MEASURED: the manager authorised `qa/` + `handoffs/` as DIRECTORIES; build-master staged SIX EXPLICIT FILE PATHS instead**, on the stated ground that ***a directory pathspec is precisely what would have swept in `qa/TASK-480.md` had it appeared between the status read and the `add`*** — and TASK-480 was **writing into `qa/` at that moment.** ✅ **The instruction was the weaker form and the implementer caught it.**
- ⛔ **THE RULE: when any task is live, stage by FILE PATH. A directory pathspec is acceptable only when the directory is provably quiet** — and *provably* means checked, not assumed.
- ✅ **AND CHECK THE RACE RATHER THAN ASSUMING IT DID NOT FIRE:** build-master **verified `qa/TASK-480.md` did not yet exist** instead of reasoning that it probably had not appeared. ⚖️ **"It probably hasn't landed yet" is a prediction about another agent's timing, and this pipeline has already ruled that a claim about the working tree DECAYS FASTER THAN ANYTHING ELSE A SPEC CAN ASSERT.**
- ⛔ **SECRET-SCAN ANY DOCUMENT YOU DID NOT AUTHOR BEFORE STAGING IT.** ⚠️ **A commit is append-only and a leaked token cannot be un-committed** — the same permanence argument the `.gitignore` weights rule already makes. **State the scan's result, including the benign hits** (e.g. `HF_TOKEN` appearing only as a variable name inside text saying it must never be printed).
- ⚖️ **AND THE COMMITTER'S BOUNDARY, STATED CLEANLY AND WORTH COPYING VERBATIM:** *"Whether the report's findings are sound is QA's verdict, already rendered; my gate is that the evidence EXISTS IN GIT, IS THE RIGHT FILE, and CARRIES NO SECRETS."* ⛔ **A committer does not re-adjudicate the artifacts it commits, and it does not imply it read them for correctness.** ✅ **Naming the limit is part of discharging the duty.**

### (e) ⛔ A `-- <pathspec>` IS **CWD-RELATIVE**; A `HEAD:<path>` IS **ROOT-RELATIVE**. MIXING THEM PRODUCES AN EMPTY DIFF AND A VACUOUS `0` (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **THE TWO GIT PATH FORMS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE, AND THE PENALTY FOR SWAPPING THEM IS A ***PASS***.**

- ⚠️ **MEASURED, AND THE CALL IS COMING FROM INSIDE THE HOUSE: `qa/TASK-479.md` §9 WARN-1 instructs prefixing every diff path with `GitClaudeUnrealTest/`** — correct for `HEAD:<path>` (hazard (c)) and **WRONG for `git diff -- <pathspec>`.** ⛔ **Executed literally from the project directory — which IS the agent cwd — it matches nothing, returns an EMPTY diff, and the additive proof reports `0 deletions`.** ⇒ ⚖️ **THE EXACT VACUOUS PASS THE DISCIPLINE EXISTS TO PREVENT, PRODUCED BY FOLLOWING THE DISCIPLINE.**
- ⛔ **THE FIX, EITHER FORM:** run the diff **from the repo toplevel**, or use a **root-anchored magic pathspec** — `git diff -- ':/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Plugins/…'` — which is cwd-independent by construction. ✅ **Prefer the anchored form: it is correct from any directory, which is the property that survives a script being run from somewhere new.**
- ⛔ **AND THE POSITIVE CONTROL IS WHAT CAUGHT IT: CHOOSE A CONTROL THAT ***MUST*** RETURN NON-ZERO.** ⚠️ **A control that merely *might* be non-zero cannot distinguish "no deletions" from "no matches"** — and those two produce identical output. ✅ **TASK-481 caught this only because its control was a file it KNEW had deletions (the spike, 62).**
- ⚖️ **THIS IS HAZARD (c)'s FAMILY WITH A DIFFERENT OPERAND — same root cause (the toplevel is one level above the project directory), THIRD distinct consequence.** ⛔ **File it under the MECHANISM, per (c)'s own extension: *paths mean different things to different git subcommands, and this repo's layout makes the difference silent.***

### (c1) ⚠️ THE HEADLESS-COMMANDLET FALLBACK IS NARROWER THAN IT IS USUALLY OFFERED — IT CAN ONLY REACH **REFLECTED** SURFACE
The `-run=pythonscript` commandlet is repeatedly offered as a stand-in when PIE is unavailable. **It reaches UPROPERTY/UFUNCTION-reflected surface ONLY.** Worked example: **`USiegeAssistantSnapshot` has NO reflected functions at all** — `CanonicalKind` is a **private static in the `.cpp`** and `GetUnitKinds()` a **plain inline getter** — so a commandlet **cannot call either**, and offering it as a PIE substitute for those would waste a dispatch.
- **What the route CAN do: read `UPROPERTY` tables off a CDO or an asset.** That is how the `DA_AssistantVocabulary` / `DT_Cards` CardID round-trip was closed without an editor.
- **LAW: before offering the commandlet as a PIE substitute, name the REFLECTED symbol it will call.** If the answer is a private static, a free function, or a non-`UFUNCTION` inline, the route does not exist and PIE is genuinely owed.

### (c) `git show HEAD:<path>` TAKES A REPO-RELATIVE PATH, AND FAILS SILENTLY-LOOKING WHEN IT DOES NOT
**The repo root is ONE LEVEL ABOVE the project directory here.** `git show HEAD:Source/...` therefore exits **non-zero with `fatal:` and prints nothing** — **which reads exactly like "the symbol is absent from HEAD."** A build-master nearly filed the right conclusion for entirely the wrong reason.
- **LAW: any spec, QA note or handoff quoting a `git show HEAD:` path uses the REPO-RELATIVE path** (`GitClaudeUnrealTest/Source/...`), and **any "absent from HEAD" claim must distinguish an empty result from a `fatal:`** — check the exit code, or grep the tree with `git ls-tree` first.
- ⛔ **EXTENDED 2026-08-03 (TASK-478) — THE SAME TRAP HAS A SECOND, WORSE CONSEQUENCE THIS CLAUSE DID NOT NAME, AND IT ATTACKS THE VERIFICATION HARNESS ITSELF:**
  > ### ⛔ **AN EMPTY `git show HEAD:<path>` READS AS *"THIS FILE IS NEW"* — SO A DIFF-BASED **ADDITIVE PROOF** SILENTLY BECOMES A PROOF ABOUT NOTHING, AND IT PRODUCES A ***PASS***.**
  - ⚠️ **"Absent symbol" (this clause's original framing) is a claim someone must then ACT on. "The file is new" is a PREMISE that makes an entire proof vacuous while every step of it still passes.** ⇒ **The second consequence is worse because nothing downstream looks wrong.**
  - ⛔ **MANDATORY POSITIVE CONTROL, and it is one command: before trusting any `git show HEAD:` baseline, CONFIRM THE BLOB IS NON-EMPTY** (`git cat-file -s HEAD:<repo-relative-path>`, or compare `git show` byte length against the working file). ✅ **TASK-478 caught exactly this with a blob-size check.** ⚖️ **A baseline of zero bytes is not a baseline; it is the absence of one wearing a baseline's shape.**
  - ⭐ **AND THE FIX IS BETTER THAN A CHECK — NAME THE SAFE COMMAND, BECAUSE THE HAZARD IS *COMMAND-DEPENDENT* (added 2026-08-03, measured by build-master as a POSITIVE CONTROL rather than assumed):**
    ```
    git cat-file -s HEAD:GitClaudeUnrealTest/.claude/pipeline/TASKBOARD.md   → 2377819      ✅ loud, usable
    git cat-file -s HEAD:.claude/pipeline/TASKBOARD.md                       → fatal, exit 128  ✅ LOUD FAILURE
    git show     HEAD:<project-relative-path>                                → EMPTY, exit 0    ⛔ SILENT, reads as "new file"
    ```
    ⇒ ⛔ **USE `git cat-file -s` AND PASTE THE BYTE SIZE. A WRONG PATH THEN FAILS *LOUDLY* WITH EXIT 128 INSTEAD OF RETURNING AN EMPTY STRING THAT LOOKS LIKE A FINDING.**
    ⭐ **AND IT DOES MORE THAN FAIL LOUDLY — IT NAMES *WHICH* FAILURE, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT ACTUALLY CLOSES THE AMBIGUITY (added 2026-08-03, found by RUNNING the clause rather than reading it):**
    ```
    wrong path prefix   → fatal: path '…' does not exist in 'HEAD'
    genuinely new file  → fatal: path '…' exists on disk, but not in 'HEAD'
    ```
    ⚠️ **BOTH EXIT 128 — SO THE EXIT CODE ALONE DOES NOT SEPARATE THEM. ⛔ READ THE MESSAGE, NEVER JUST THE STATUS.** ⇒ ⚖️ **This is precisely the question `git show HEAD:`'s empty return could not answer — *"is this file NEW, or did I MISTYPE THE PREFIX?"* — and it is the question the whole additive-proof class turns on.** ✅ **A loud failure that does not say which failure is only half a fix; quote the `fatal:` line verbatim.**
    ⚖️ **THIS IS THE §17 FAMILY ANSWERED *STRUCTURALLY* RATHER THAN BY DISCIPLINE — the same move as TASK-478's no-default-argument threading (a missed call site becomes a compile error).** ⇒ **Prefer the tool whose failure mode is loud over the tool whose failure mode needs a rule to survive.** ⚠️ **A hazard closed by "remember to check" reopens the first time somebody is tired; a hazard closed by choosing a different command does not.**
  - ⛔ **BINDING ON EVERY STAGE-A ADDITIVE PROOF AND ON TASK-480's RE-RUN**, all of which rest on this command. **`git diff -U0 | grep -E "^-[^-]"` returning nothing is only evidence if the baseline was real.**
  - ⚖️ **AND THE LESSON THAT OUTLIVES THE COMMAND — IT IS WHY THIS EXTENSION IS WRITTEN RATHER THAN THE CLAUSE MERELY CITED: THIS LAW ALREADY EXISTED, AND WAS STILL VERY NEARLY MISSED.** ⛔ **(c) was framed by its CONSEQUENCE (*"an absent-symbol claim"*) instead of by its MECHANISM (*"the toplevel is one level up, so project-relative paths resolve to nothing"*)** — so a reader hitting **the same mechanism** with **a different consequence** did not recognise the clause as theirs. ⇒ ⛔ **A HAZARD LAW MUST BE FILED UNDER ITS MECHANISM. A law indexed by one of its symptoms fails to cover the next one**, and this is the second time today a clause was correct, present, and did not reach the person it was written for (cf. §5's `chat=` row). **See also §14 — this is that law aimed at the verifier rather than at the search.**

## 🔍 THE RELAYED-DIAGNOSIS LAW (2026-08-02) — verify against the artifact, never against the message

> ### **A RELAYED DIAGNOSIS IS A LEAD, NOT A FINDING. QA CONFIRMS THE ROOT CAUSE FROM THE CODE — NEVER FROM THE REPORT THAT QUOTES IT.**

Added 2026-08-02 because this stopped being luck and became a pattern **three times in one batch**, each time the same way: *someone checked the artifact instead of trusting the message.*

| # | The relayed claim | What the artifact said |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The place symbol is `my_castle` | **`my_castle` appears NOWHERE on disk.** The sealed corpus asserts **`own_castle`** in both files, including the scoring holdout. A transcription slip, caught independently by two agents. |
| 2 | `C2228`/`C2737` are a complete-type **include-law** violation | **`Engine/DataTable.h` was already included at line 6.** Adding it changes nothing and **both errors would have survived to the next gate.** The real cause was **C++'s most-vexing parse** one line above. |
| 3 | `C4172` is "returning the address of a temporary" (the obvious read) | True, but **the root cause was not the obvious one**: `FJsonObject::Values` is a `TMap` whose iterator does not yield exactly `TPair<FString, TSharedPtr<FJsonValue>>&`, so naming that type **CONVERTED rather than BOUND**, materialising a temporary every iteration. The fix deleted the pointer-returning helper outright rather than patching the line. |

- **THE DUTY IS QA's, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** When a diagnosis arrives via a board spec, a handoff, a Slack post or an orchestrator relay, **QA re-derives it from the source before accepting it.** A report that repeats a relayed root cause without independent confirmation has not reviewed anything — it has forwarded.
- **⚠️ A WRONG ROOT CAUSE IS WORSE THAN NO ROOT CAUSE.** It ships a **wrong fix** (an include that changes nothing) *and* a **wrong lesson** (a coding law mis-taught to every future task), and it burns the QA loop that would have caught it. Diagnosis #2 above was already written into a task spec as the wrong criterion before it was caught.
- **THIS APPLIES TO A CORRECT-LOOKING FIX TOO.** A fix can be sound while its stated reason is wrong — and then the reason is what propagates. **QA scrutinises the ROOT CAUSE of a fix, not only its outcome.** (TASK-416's author explicitly asked QA to re-check TASK-417's `C4172` fix rather than take the relay; that request is the law working as intended and is the behavior to copy.)
- **THE MANAGER IS NOT EXEMPT.** Two of the three entries above were the manager's or the orchestrator's own errors, corrected in place. **Correcting the record is cheap; leaving a wrong lesson in a law file is not.**
- ⚖️ **AND THE SHARPEST FORM OF THAT — RATIFIED 2026-08-03, PROPOSED BY BUILD-MASTER AT COMMIT `5e85389` AND ADOPTED AS LAW:**
  > ### ⛔ **A RULING IS A RELAY TOO. VERIFY A LAW CHANGE AGAINST THE ARTIFACT BEFORE COMMITTING IT — INCLUDING, AND ESPECIALLY, THE MANAGER'S.**
  - **WHAT HAPPENED:** build-master was handed six law changes to commit and **re-verified every one against the source before staging**, on its own stated grounds that *a ruling is not exempt from the relayed-diagnosis law.* ✅ **Nobody asked it to. It is the correct instinct and it is now the rule.**
  - ⚖️ **WHY THIS IS THE HARDEST CASE AND THEREFORE THE MOST IMPORTANT ONE: A LAW CHANGE IS THE *LEAST* LIKELY ARTIFACT TO BE CHECKED AND THE *MOST* EXPENSIVE ONE TO GET WRONG.** A wrong fix fails at the next gate; **a wrong law is quoted as authority by every task after it** and fails silently, everywhere, for as long as the file lives. ⚠️ **Authority is exactly the property that suppresses checking** — which is why it must be written down that authority does not confer exemption.
  - ⛔ **THE COMMITTER'S DUTY IS *PRESENCE AND ACCURACY*, NOT *ENDORSEMENT*.** Confirm each change is in the file and that any code claim it makes matches the code. ✅ **It is NOT the committer's job to audit a body of law for internal consistency, and build-master correctly REFUSED to imply it had** — see the audit clause below. **Stating the boundary of a check is part of performing it.**
  - **MEASURED, IN THIS PIPELINE, TWICE IN ONE DAY:** the manager's own §3 D2 row was corrected from the artifact after a relay said *"key-set difference"* and the source said otherwise; and this clause's own commit carried **a stale self-describing count** that its author had measured correctly and carried too long. ⇒ ⚠️ **A NUMBER THAT WAS ACCURATE WHEN TAKEN AND STALE WHEN USED IS THE HARDEST DEFECT TO CATCH, BECAUSE NOBODY WAS CARELESS.** ⛔ **A relay carries THE COMMAND, never THE READING** — TASK-434's remedy, and it generalises past counts to every figure that can move between measurement and use.

## Ancient Grounds + Sorcerer + 180° terrain symmetry (2026-08-01)
Added 2026-08-01 (batch **ANCIENT-GROUNDS**, TASK-358..378). Design authority = Jonathan's approved plan `C:\Users\wesel\.claude\plans\there-is-one-new-glittery-bentley.md` — **the plan file wins over any board summary**; it carries the verified file:line citations, the code sketches and the derivations, and this section does not re-derive them. Jonathan's directive: a new *ancient grounds* area of interest the size of the mid capture zone, one per side placed symmetrically; a new 60-cost **Sorcerer** unit that is commandable but cannot attack and empowers friendly units standing in an ancient ground; a boost bar above the health bar; and — follow-up — **all terrain becomes 180°-turn mirrored instead of X-mirrored**.

### 1. THE 180°-ROTATIONAL SYMMETRY LAW (supersedes the asymmetric-scatter law at "Battlefield & procedural terrain (M6.5)" and the X-mirror wording in the mines law)
- **The transform, exactly — the ONLY legal terrain symmetry on this project:** for every placed instance/actor, the twin is
  `loc' = (−X, −Y, Z)` · `yaw' = Fmod(yaw + 180, 360)` · `scale' = scale (UNCHANGED)`.
  This is a proper rigid motion on the yaw-only/uniform-scale transforms the scatter uses: **no negative scale, no HISM winding flip, no approximation.** The retired X-mirror was a fake reflection and is dead.
- **Generation method (Jonathan's, and it is the better algorithm): generate ONE HALF, then duplicate rotated 180°.** Half the RNG draws, half the ground traces, the same ~15,000 `AddInstance` calls, the same perf budget. Per-layer `TargetCount` semantics already count primary + twin, so a target of 340 becomes ~170 pairs.
- **⚠️ GENERATE THE BLUE HALF (X ≤ 0). THIS IS NOT ARBITRARY AND MUST NOT BE FLIPPED:** `PlayerStart` exists ONLY at **(−23800, 0, 100)** — there is no Red-side PlayerStart (`SiegeGameMode.cpp:684-687` confirms the fallback IS the design). Generating Blue means the PlayerStart keep-clear disc (r=800) is honored for real and its rotated image over-clears a harmless empty patch at (+23800, 0). Generating **Red** would let a legally-placed prop rotate **onto the hero spawn** — broken. The mine pass already draws `X < 0`, so this is consistent, not new.
- **ZERO RNG DRAWS IN THE ROTATION STEP (hard QA criterion).** The twin is computed, never sampled. This is what preserves intra-build determinism and host==client. Same binary + same seed ⇒ byte-identical layout and byte-identical log lines.
- **Existing seeds WILL produce new layouts. That is EXPECTED and is NOT a regression** — documented precedent: the TASK-140 draw-order change did exactly this ("Radius-aware scatter placement" law, M6.6). The contract that must hold is *intra-build* reproducibility + host==client, never cross-build layout stability.
- **Inline, never a bulk post-pass.** The twin is emitted inside the per-instance loop, because three shipped invariants die under a post-pass: `VisualToProxy` index parallelism (tree visual + collision proxy must be added in lockstep or the cull paths orphan visible trees), `SpacingGrid` registration of the twin, and `HillSurfaceComponents` registration for pass-1 blockers.
- **Twin ground Z is RE-TRACED (`GroundZAt(−X, −Y)`), never copied** — it preserves the null-safe code shape, and under an exact rotation it MUST return the primary's Z, which converts an assumption into a free QA assertion.
- **The hill-parity rollback machinery becomes DEAD CODE and is DELETED.** `TryResolveMinePair`'s clone/re-trace/rollback path exists because the fake mirror could land a twin off the cloned hill's footprint; under a true rotation the twin lands on the geometrically identical point of the rotated hill — same Z, same slope, always. Deleting it is part of the law, not an optional cleanup.
- **Config:** the `bool bMirrorSymmetric` toggle on `USiegeScatterConfig` is REPLACED by an explicit single global symmetry-mode field. The `GenerateScatter seed=… mirror=…` log token stays **grep-able** (QA reads it) — it records the new mode value.
- **Deliberately still asymmetric, and that is correct:** `ValidateTraversability`'s destructive culls (`CullCorridorBlockers`, `RemoveBlockingInstancesInDisc`) stay side-agnostic (mirroring a cull would delete more geometry for zero traversability gain) — but they **LOG when they fire**, because they locally break symmetry; the PlayerStart keep-clear disc (one real + one harmless rotated over-clear); and near-origin self-pairing (a grass blade at (−10,10) rotates to (10,−10) — cosmetically invisible, and blocking layers are already excluded near Y=0 by the corridor rule).
- **🚩 KNOWN RESIDUAL, OUT OF SCOPE, NEEDS A FRESH JONATHAN RULING:** both castles read **yaw 0** (three independent in-engine readbacks) and `ACastle` puts its gate blocker on the local −Y side, so **both castle gates face world −Y** — the one gameplay-visible asymmetry left under this law. Fixing it means `Castle_Red` yaw 180, which is an `L_Arena` SAVE, and the one-time exception is **SPENT** (`bf5e562`, "the never-save law resumes in full"). Flagged, not done. *(Already fine: the 8 POI landmarks and the two gold-node props at (0, ±5000) are already exact 180° rotational pairs; ground/boundaries/navmesh bounds/capture zone are centered and therefore rotation-symmetric.)*

### 2. `AAncientGround` — the area of interest
- **Class/files:** `AAncientGround`, plain `AActor`, `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/AncientGround.h` / `.cpp` (gameplay-actor plain-name law, like `ACastle`/`ACaptureZone`).
- **MUST NOT derive from `ACaptureZone` (load-bearing).** `TActorIterator<ACaptureZone>` is a *unit-spawn-eligibility gate* (`SiegePlayerController.cpp:3559`, `SiegeBotController.cpp:1183/1380` — the bot takes the FIRST instance) plus the play-again reset (`SiegeGameMode.cpp:835`). Subclassing would make ancient grounds spawnable-in and let one win the bot's first-instance race. **Duplicate** the ~80 lines of decal/box boilerplate from `CaptureZone.cpp:59-216` and **record the 2-mirror as debt in BOTH headers.**
- **Shape (deliberately mirrors `ACaptureZone`):** `SceneRoot` + `UDecalComponent GroundDecal` (relative pitch −90, no collision primitive); `FVector2D ZoneHalfExtent = (840, 840)` — a **PAIRED TUNABLE with `ACaptureZone::ZoneHalfExtent`**, both headers cross-note; `bool IsPointInZone(const FVector&) const` (2D XY box, Z ignored — byte-copy of `CaptureZone.cpp:112`); `float BoostTickInterval = 1.0f` (`// GDD §x.x` mechanic rule, NOT a CSV column); `EndPlay` clears the timer. **No `Reset…()` and no `SiegeGameMode` edit** — the ground latches no state and PlayAgain step 7 already calls `ClearScatter()` + `GenerateScatter()`, which re-places the pair for free.
- **⚖️ NET RELEVANCY TIER: C — NOT REPLICATED (`bReplicates` stays false).** Declared in the header comment **and** in the task handoff; an undeclared tier is an automatic QA FAIL under the M8 NET RELEVANCY LAW. The ground rides the already-Tier-A replicated `ChosenSeed`/`GenerationIndex`, so it is spawned identically on every machine from the seed.
- **⚠️ AUTHORITY IS PUSHED, NEVER READ — the single most dangerous spot in this feature.** The ground is spawned **locally on the client** from the replicated seed, so it keeps `ROLE_Authority` there (the M8 law's NARROW PRINCIPLE). A `HasAuthority()` guard would silently run a rogue client-side boost sim. **Law:** `void InitAncientGround(bool bAuthoritative);` — called by the scatter, threading ITS `bAuthoritativeGenerate`; the boost tick gates on the STORED `bAuthoritativeBoost` flag **ONLY**. This mirrors `RunScatterPasses(Seed, bAuthoritativeGenerate)` in the same file. **A `HasAuthority()` call anywhere in `AncientGround.cpp` is a QA FAIL.**
- **Boost tick = 1 Hz timer, NEVER per-tick (TASK-004 law).** One `TActorIterator<ASummonedUnit>` sweep per tick, two team buckets: count friendly empowerers in the zone, then grant every eligible friendly occupant that many stacks. **Sorcerers never self-boost.** **FRIENDLY-ONLY (Jonathan ruling): a Blue sorcerer boosts only Blue units** — a contested ground empowers both sides through their own sorcerers. **Each sorcerer adds one stack per tick** (2 sorcerers = 2 stacks/s).
- **Placement = a new scatter pass, not a level actor.** **`void PlaceAncientGrounds(int32 Seed, bool bAuthoritativeGenerate)`** (private) on `ASiegeBattlefieldScatter`, inserted in `RunScatterPasses` right after `PlaceMines(Seed)` and **before** the `if (!bAuthoritativeGenerate) { … return; }` early-out, so the client's `OnRep_GenerationIndex` path runs the identical pass off the replicated seed. Follows §1 (draw on the Blue half, emit the rotated twin), so it needs no exception.
  - **⚠️ AMENDED 2026-08-01 (TASK-365 ruling R2 — this clause used to read one-arg `PlaceAncientGrounds(int32 Seed)` and contradicted itself.)** The same clause demands `InitAncientGround` be called with the flag `RunScatterPasses` carries, and the authority law (the next bullet up) is the load-bearing one — the two sentences could not both be satisfied literally. **The shipped two-arg form is the law.** QA ACCEPTED it on the merits: the method is **private**, appears **nowhere** in the §7 pinned registry (only `InitAncientGround(bool)` does), and has no other caller ⇒ **zero cross-task compile surface.** Both alternatives are worse and are BANNED here: a member stash is order-coupled and one refactor away from a stale `true` on a client; a self-`HasAuthority()` read would return the right answer *here* (this actor really is replicated) but splits one authority decision into two sources that can drift — and models the exact pattern `AAncientGround` must never copy.
- **⚖️ THE PASS IS `SymmetryMode`-INDEPENDENT BY DESIGN — do NOT wire it to the toggle (recorded 2026-08-01, TASK-365 ruling R4).** The ancient-ground pass emits the rotational pair **unconditionally**, whatever `EScatterSymmetryMode` says. *"One per side, placed symmetrically"* is a **FAIRNESS LAW** out of Jonathan's directive, not a terrain-aesthetics setting: branching here would let a config value hand one team an objective the other cannot reach. `Asymmetric` is a retired off-state that already needs a fresh Jonathan ruling to select at all, and an asymmetric SINGLE-ground mode is unspecified — inventing one is a scope violation, not a feature. A future agent that "completes" the toggle by making this pass honor it is introducing a gameplay-fairness regression. Reusing the scatter delivers with zero new code: random-per-match with **no `L_Arena` save**, host/client agreement via the replicated seed, `OnRep_GenerationIndex` re-running the pass on the client, a fresh layout on Play Again, `RemoveBlockingInstancesInDisc` to clear trees, and `FindHillSurfaceAt` for the slope gate.
- **Seed discipline (hard QA criterion):** dedicated stream `FRandomStream GroundStream(Seed ^ 0x41474E44)` ("AGND"; mines use `^0x4D494E45`), **exactly two draws per attempt in fixed X-then-Y order**, everything downstream draw-free — the discipline `PlaceMines` already enforces. Layer streams stay untouched.
- **`USiegeScatterConfig` fields, category `Scatter|AncientGrounds`** (defaults are the law; all FLAGGED tunable): `AncientGroundHalfExtent` **(840, 840)** ≡ `ACaptureZone` · `AncientGroundMinAbsX` **4000** · `AncientGroundMaxAbsX` **21000** · `AncientGroundMaxAbsY` **10800** · `AncientGroundMineClear` **1800** (tested at BOTH P and P′) · `AncientGroundClearRadius` **1200** (`RemoveBlockingInstancesInDisc` at P and P′) · **flat ground only — reject hills outright** (a 1680² gathering box needs flat ground; do NOT parity-clone a hill for it).
- **📐 THE KEEP-CLEAR ARITHMETIC — CORRECTED 2026-08-01 (TASK-365 WARN/R3). `CastleKeepClearRadius` is `1500`, NOT `4500`.** The `r=4500` figure appears in the approved plan's §2 band table, in `BattlefieldScatter.cpp`'s no-disc-test rationale comment, and in `handoffs/TASK-361-programmer.md` §8 — **all three are wrong and none of them is the binding constraint.** Shipped values, read from source: **`CastleKeepClearRadius = 1500.f` (`ScatterConfig.h:354`)** and `PlayerStartKeepClearRadius = 800.f` (`:358`).
  - **The `|X| ≤ 21000` ceiling is still correct and the omitted keep-clear disc test is still correct** — but because the test is a **provable no-op**, NOT because the ceiling "is the castle disc in closed form". At the real radius the castle disc only bites at **`|X| ≥ 23500`**, clearing the ceiling by **2500 uu** at the center and **1660 uu** at the 21840 footprint edge; the PlayerStart disc at `(−23800, 0)` r=800 is 2800 uu clear. The ground is also no-collision/no-nav, so keep-clear — a *traversability* construct — does not apply to it at all.
  - **⚠️ THE REAL BINDING CONSTRAINT ON RAISING `AncientGroundMaxAbsX` IS `SpawnBoxHalfExtent = (2460, 2460)` (`Castle.h:260`)** — the spawn box edge sits at **`|X| = 22540`**, i.e. **700 uu past the 21840 footprint edge**. Anyone tuning the band upward measures against **22540**, never against the castle disc. Recorded because the wrong figure misleads in the dangerous direction: it suggests the band edge already sits *inside* a keep-clear disc (it does not, by 2500 uu) and that 4500 is the ceiling's cause (it is not).
- **The reserved corridor (`|Y| ≤ 1000`) is NOT excluded** — same ruling as the mines: the actor is no-collision/no-nav so it cannot break the traversability guarantee, and a lane objective is good contested design.
- **48 attempts, then a deterministic fallback at `P = (−12000, +6000)` logged at `Error`** (the mines' "never ships short" discipline). Grep-able line: `[BattlefieldScatter] AncientGroundsPass seed=%d P=(...) M=(...) fb=%s culls=%d`. Tracked in `TArray<TObjectPtr<AAncientGround>> SpawnedAncientGrounds`, destroyed in `ClearScatter` exactly like `SpawnedMines`.
- **Material:** `M_AncientGround` (Content/Materials/, `/Game/Materials/M_AncientGround`) — clones `M_CaptureZone`'s STRUCTURE (`MD_DeferredDecal`, `BLEND_Translucent`, Emissive + Opacity only, BaseColor unconnected, **stock nodes — the Custom-HLSL BAN applies**) but NOT its shaping: concentric **rune rings** inside the square footprint + a slow `Sine(Time)` pulse, so it never reads as a recolored capture zone where they meet near the centerline. Vector param **`GroundColor`**, default jade **(0.10, 0.85, 0.55)** — maximally far from Blue (0.05,0.30,1.00), Red (1.00,0.10,0.05) and neutral grey. `SortOrder 10` (draws above `M_CaptureZone`). ⚠️ **`DecalSize` after the −90 pitch: `.X` is the projection half-DEPTH (1024); `.Y`/`.Z` are the ground half-extents and MUST equal `ZoneHalfExtent` (840/840) — the decal matches the mechanic box, never shrinks.** ⚠️ Set **`FadeScreenSize = 0.001`** explicitly (the 0.01 default culls decals at this arena's zoom-out). Soft-load null-safe: missing material ⇒ log once, no visual, **the mechanic still runs**.

### 3. `ASorcererUnit` — commandable but never attacks
- **Class/files:** `ASorcererUnit : public ASummonedUnit`, `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/SorcererUnit.h` / `.cpp` (~70 lines). Constructor sets `AggroRadius = 0` and `DefendRadius = 0`. `StateCheckInterval` stays at the base **0.25 s** — unlike the Miner, this unit MUST keep its state timer (it is commandable).
- **`Profile = Standard` in cards.csv is LOAD-BEARING.** `IsGroupCommandEligible()` (`SummonedUnit.cpp:1576`) gates on `Profile == ECardProfile::Standard`, so `Support` (the Cleric's never-attack route) would make the Sorcerer **UNCOMMANDABLE**. The Miner's structural seal is also unavailable (a commandable unit needs its state timer, and `UpdateStateGrouped` acquires through controller-owned radii the unit cannot zero).
- **THE SEAL = two new virtuals on `ASummonedUnit`, declared in its PUBLIC block** (the `ShouldHoldDeathAnim()` idiom, but **NOT its access level** — that one is `protected:` and `AAncientGround` calls these from outside, so `public:` is mandatory or the batch does not link):
  - `virtual bool CanEverAttack() const` — base `return true;` · `ASorcererUnit` overrides `false`.
  - `virtual bool IsAncientGroundEmpowerer() const` — base `return false;` · `ASorcererUnit` overrides `true`. Class identity, **not** a CSV flag (mechanic-rules-aren't-card-stats law).
- **THREE guard points, ALL required, ALL QA-verified (not just one):** (1) `EnterAttack()` — the structural chokepoint all four attack entries funnel through: `if (!CanEverAttack()) { EnterIdle(); return; }` (standing down, not a silent return, keeps the state machine honest); (2) `UpdateStateGrouped()` — force `CurrentTarget = nullptr` instead of the two `AcquireEnemyNearPoint` calls, falling through to tier-3 station-keeping (without this a grouped sorcerer walks to an enemy and stands there); (3) `PerformAttack()` — add `|| !CanEverAttack()` to the existing `bAIFrozen`/`bSpellFrozen` gate (defense in depth). ⚠️ **WHY three:** `AttackCadence = FMath::Max(Row->Cadence, 0.05f)` means a **Cadence-0 row that ever reached Attack would fire 20×/s.** *Not needed, and the handoff says so, so QA does not hunt:* the legacy Standard body and DEFEND stance are already dead via the ctor's `AggroRadius=0`/`DefendRadius=0`, and Siege/Detonate paths are unreachable (`Standard`, `bSuicide false`).
- **Blueprint:** `BP_Unit_Sorcerer` at `/Game/Blueprints/Units/BP_Unit_Sorcerer`, reparented to **`ASorcererUnit`** (not `ASummonedUnit`). The spawn path's `IsChildOf(ASummonedUnit)` check passes by inheritance — **NO spawn-path change at all.**
- **Per-unit asset set (the standard per-card visual law, verbatim):** `SM_Sorcerer` (`/Game/Meshes/SM_Sorcerer`, placement-ghost string contract; feet-center, ≤15k tris, `UVMap`, Nanite OFF, two-slot `[TeamRegion, SorcererPBR]`) · `T_Sorcerer_{D,N,ORM}` (`/Game/Textures/`, ORM LINEAR) · `MI_Sorcerer_PBR` (`/Game/Materials/Instances/`, from `M_AssetPBR`) · `SK_Sorcerer` (`/Game/Characters/`) bound to the **EXISTING shared `SK_Footman_Skeleton` — never create a skeleton** · `A_Sorcerer_{Idle,Walk,Attack,Death}` (`/Game/Characters/Anims/`; **produce the Attack clip** — the Cleric precedent, it is free and it is the ready-made hook for a consecration gesture) · **no `ABP_Sorcerer`** (falls back to `ABP_Footman`) · `T_CardArt_Sorcerer` (`/Game/UI/CardArt/`, 512², jade key) + source PNG `Content/RawAssets/CardArt/Sorcerer.png` · concept `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/Sorcerer.png` → accepted `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/Sorcerer.png` · raw FBX `Content/RawAssets/Characters/Sorcerer.fbx` · SK LOD chain per the M7.6 law (LOD1 50% @ 0.4 / LOD2 20% @ 0.15 + `OnlyTickPoseWhenRendered` + URO).
- **⚠️ `BP_Unit_Sorcerer` MUST hand-author the static `VisualMesh` yaw −90 and Z = −(the READ capsule half-height).** "Half-height == 90" is **BANNED** (shipped values span −74.5 to −145) — READ the value in-editor and negate it. `BP_Unit_Wizard` is the unit that missed this and needed a follow-up task. The **skeletal** yaw is C++-owned (`SkeletalVisualYawOffset = -90.f`, ABSOLUTE assignment; `+=` is an automatic QA FAIL) and must NOT be hand-authored.
- **Art identity — the Sorcerer must NOT read as a Wizard reskin.** The shipped `Wizard` prompt literally contains *"battle-mage fire **sorcerer** … hooded bearded wizard … fireball"*, so a generic sorcerer prompt returns a Wizard reskin with ~90% probability. **Identity = the LEY-WARDEN / GEOMANCER** — a ritualist who consecrates ground, not a spellcaster who throws things: carved **stone ritual mask** + antler crown (no face, no hood, no beard); **both hands on a rune-carved monolith-staff planted in the ground** (no weapon presented — "never attacks" is legible from the pose); stone-and-moss vestments with a **flat slab mantle** across the shoulders; cool **jade/teal** ley-light keying to the ground decal; reads at 15 m as "a standing stone with legs". `TeamRegion` target = the slab mantle (reuse the Cleric/Wizard `shoulder_caps` selector — **no `helm_dome`**, a mask is not a helmet). ⚠️ **Two gates BEFORE Meshy credits are spent:** (a) the monolith top must sit at **~head height, not above it** — `rig_character.py` fits SiegeBiped anchors as fractions of measured mesh height, so an overshooting staff slides the whole rig down the body; (b) set the preview beside `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/Wizard.png` — **if a stranger would call them the same unit, reroll Stage 0.**

### 4. The permanent stacking damage boost (mechanic rules — UPROPERTY defaults, NEVER CSV columns)
- **Storage is `int32 PermanentDamageStacks`, NEVER a float.** The UI's whole job is distinguishing **exactly** 100/200/300% from just-past-them; a float accumulated over 80 additions of 0.05 makes "exactly 100%" epsilon-dependent. Integer stacks make the band boundaries exact by construction. Mirrors `AHeroCharacter::SharpenedBladeStacks`.
- **On `ASummonedUnit`:** `int32 PermanentDamageStacks = 0` (VisibleInstanceOnly, Transient) · `float PermanentDamageBonusPerStack = 0.05f` (`// GDD §x.x`, EditAnywhere) · `int32 MaxPermanentDamageStacks = 80` (`// GDD §x.x` — 80 × 5% = the **+400% cap**) · `float GetPermanentDamageMultiplier() const` (**BlueprintPure**, the QA readback hook) `= 1.f + PermanentDamageBonusPerStack * PermanentDamageStacks`.
- **Compose point — ONE line in `ComputeOutputDamage`, immediately after the War Banner aura:** `Output *= GetPermanentDamageMultiplier();` — multiplicative, exactly 1.0 at zero stacks. One insertion covers **both melee and ranged** and every keyword unit. **NEVER mutate `AttackDamage` in place** (house buff law).
- **ALSO compose onto the Sapper blast** — `ApplyDetonation` currently passes raw `AttackDamage`, bypassing the chokepoint: `const float BlastDamage = AttackDamage * GetPermanentDamageMultiplier();`. **This is a deliberate change to a shipped unit** (dying IS how a Sapper attacks; an unboosted blast would be a visible lie). Charge/Slayer/Aura are deliberately NOT retro-applied there — that is a separate pre-existing gap, recorded, not fixed here.
- **Mutation + eligibility API (pinned):** `void AddPermanentDamageStacks(int32 Stacks)` (clamps to Max; **broadcasts ONLY on an actual change**) · `void ClearPermanentDamageStacks()` (reset path: **UNCONDITIONAL broadcast**) · `bool CanReceiveDamageBoost() const` = `!bDead && CanEverAttack() && AttackDamage > 0 && Profile != Support`. That predicate excludes the Sorcerer, Miner and Cleric — units whose damage routes through neither compose point — which also keeps their boost row hidden. Authority is by construction (the sole caller is the gated ancient-ground tick): **comment it, do not bolt on a guard that buys nothing in P1.**
- **Reset:** `ClearPermanentDamageStacks()` in `HandleDeath`, next to the existing HP broadcast — required because the rigged-death path defers `Destroy()` up to 2 s, during which a boosted corpse would show a full boost bar. **Play Again needs ZERO work** (its step 2 destroys every unit). **Match-end freeze deliberately does NOT reset** — a permanent boost survives to the end screen; stated here so QA does not file it as a miss.
- **M8 P2 DUTY (record in the header, never silently omit):** `PermanentDamageStacks` becomes `UPROPERTY(ReplicatedUsing = OnRep_PermanentDamageStacks)` when the unit fleet replicates, with the OnRep re-broadcasting so the client's seed-then-bind path is identical.

### 5. The boost bar — `WBP_CombatantHealthBar` is EXTENDED, never duplicated
- **One widget, one component.** A second `UWidgetComponent` at a higher Z is **broken by construction**: the component is `EWidgetSpace::Screen`, so `FWorldWidgetScreenLayer` projects the world location but lays out at a constant pixel `DrawSize` — as the camera zooms, world ΔZ maps to a *changing* screen gap (the bar floats away at close zoom, overlaps at far zoom). No fixed `BarHeightZ` works, per-frame correction violates the never-per-tick law, and it would double the exact screen-space registration surface TASK-130 spent five attempts on. **The TASK-131 corruption fear does NOT apply here:** that was a Blueprint **class reparent** that orphaned BIE overrides; re-nesting `Bar` under a `VerticalBox` is a slot/outer change — the widget keeps its identity, name, variable binding and brush properties, and the parent class is never touched.
- **Widget-tree names are LAW (the artist and the C++ both read this block):**
  ```
  [ROOT] VerticalBox "BarStack"
    ├─ [0] Border      "BoostOutline"   slot Size=Fill 1.0, Padding 1.5 uniform, Pad Bottom 1
    │        └─ ProgressBar "BoostBar"
    └─ [1] ProgressBar "Bar"            slot Size=Fill 2.0   ← EXISTING, properties UNTOUCHED
  ```
  `DrawSize` in `CombatantHealthBarComponent.cpp` goes **(90, 12) → (90, 22)** — boost bar ~5 px inside a 1.5 px frame (~8 px total) and health bar ~12 px, i.e. today's health-bar height exactly.
- **The outline replaces the earlier colored-dot design (Jonathan's ruling).** A `UBorder` wrapping the progress bar paints its brush in the uniform padding ring = a clean frame. `UBorder::SetBrushColor(FLinearColor)` is a stock BlueprintCallable node, unlike a ProgressBar's `OutlineSettings` (no runtime BP setter; it would need a full `FProgressBarStyle` struct param — banned in a BIE). **Why it does the dot's job:** the ambiguity is "full bar of the lower color" vs "nearly-empty bar of the higher color", and the outline is visible at ANY fill level — 100% reads as a solid light-blue block, 100.1% as a grey bar in a **dark blue frame**.
- **Both new widgets use `WhiteSquareTexture`, `DrawAs = Image`, tint white.** A *material* fill brush swallowing a runtime tint was TASK-131's actual root cause — **do not reopen that door.**
- **`BoostBar` carries its OWN medium-grey track `(0.22, 0.22, 0.24, 0.85)`**; `Bar` keeps its near-black `(0.03,0.03,0.03)@0.7` track. This exists to solve the band-4 legibility problem (pure black on the near-black health track is ~1.3:1 — the strongest unit would get the worst indicator).
- **Hide via `SetRenderOpacity(0.0)`, NEVER `SetVisibility`** — a float pin (no enum, per the widget-param law), and it keeps the health bar pinned at a constant head offset for every unit, boosted or not. Set it on **both** `BoostOutline` and `BoostBar` (render opacity propagates to children — belt-and-braces).
- **C++ ↔ widget contract = ONE atomic BlueprintImplementableEvent** on `UCombatantHealthBarWidget`:
  `void SetDamageBoost(float FillFraction, float R, float G, float B, float RowOpacity);`
  ONE event, not two, because unlike the team tint **the color changes with the value** — splitting fill and color creates a frame where band-3 purple paints at a band-4 fill. **All banding math is C++; the widget is a dumb pipe with ZERO conditionals.** EventGraph = one linear exec chain, one `MakeLinearColor(R,G,B,1.0)` fanned to both consumers: `SetPercent(BoostBar, FillFraction)` → `SetFillColorAndOpacity(BoostBar, $C)` → `SetBrushColor(BoostOutline, $C)` → `SetRenderOpacity(BoostOutline, RowOpacity)` → `SetRenderOpacity(BoostBar, RowOpacity)`. **The alpha literal MUST be 1.0 — read it back.**
- **⚠️ `SetDamageBoost` landing as a `K2Node_CustomEvent` never fires from C++, silently — this exact defect hid the health-bar bug five times.** Author via `add_event`, assert the node's object **class** via `get_node_infos`, and **never trust `bIsImplemented`**.
- **Banding (in `UCombatantHealthBarComponent`, C++ only):** `Band = Clamp(CeilToInt(BoostPercent / 100), 1, 4)` · `Frac = Max((BoostPercent − (Band−1)*100) / 100, MinBoostFillFraction /*0.04*/)`. `CeilToInt` is upper-inclusive by design: **100% = full light blue · 100.1% = nearly-empty dark blue · 400% = full black**, with no special case.
- **Band colors — `EditDefaultsOnly FLinearColor` on the component (tint is DATA, never hardcoded in the WBP):**

  | Band | Boost | Linear RGB | ≈ hex | Contrast vs the boost track |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | 0–100% | `(0.55, 0.80, 1.00)` | `#C4E7FF` | 2.99:1 |
  | 2 | 100–200% | `(0.010, 0.020, 0.350)` | `#1927A0` | 2.96:1 |
  | 3 | 200–300% | `(0.200, 0.010, 0.420)` | `#7C19AD` | 2.09:1 |
  | 4 | 300–400% | `(0.010, 0.010, 0.014)` | `#191920` | **4.50:1 — the best on the bar** |

  The ramp darkens monotonically (light → navy → purple → black) so "deeper = stronger" reads without a tooltip. Band 3 is deliberately a DEEP purple — a bright violet computes to ~1.06:1 and vanishes. **Residual weak case, documented escalation:** the band-4 near-black *outline* sits against the world, not the track, so over a tree shadow it can disappear (the fill still carries the magnitude at 4.5:1). If the PIE gate flags it, the levers are: thicken the Border padding to 2.5 px, or set `BoostOutline`'s brush to `DrawAs=RoundedBox` with a 1 px light halo. **Do NOT ship a blind change — that ordering is the whole lesson of TASK-131.**
- **Data plumbing — `IHealthBarProvider` gains DEFAULTED virtuals (so `ABuilding` and `AHeroCharacter` need ZERO changes):**
  `DECLARE_DYNAMIC_MULTICAST_DELEGATE_OneParam(FOnCombatantDamageBoostChanged, float, BoostPercent);` · `virtual float GetDamageBoostPercent() const { return 0.f; }` · `virtual FOnCombatantDamageBoostChanged* GetDamageBoostChangedDelegate() { return nullptr; }` (**a POINTER, so "not boostable" is expressible**). In `BeginPlay`: **seed UNCONDITIONALLY** (this drives a non-boostable actor's row to opacity 0 rather than leaving the design-time state — the `qa/TASK-005` seed-then-bind law), **then** bind only if the delegate is non-null. `ASummonedUnit` must broadcast on **EVERY** mutation — the same discipline as `OnHPChanged`'s four sites; miss one and the bar is stale forever.

### 6. Dev/test lever (extends "Debug exec cheats")
- `USiegeCheatManager::SetTestDamageBoost(float Percent, bool bAllFriendly)` — `UFUNCTION(exec)`, routed through **`AddPermanentDamageStacks`/`ClearPermanentDamageStacks`, NEVER a raw field write**, so the delegate still broadcasts and the bar is exercised through the shipping path. Without it, the human PIE gate would be "walk a unit onto a ground and hope you hit exactly 100.0%". Non-shipping by construction, like every other exec cheat.

### 7. Cross-task signature registry (PINNED — UBT compiles the whole module, so every parallel task compiles against THIS list, character-for-character)
```cpp
// ASummonedUnit — PUBLIC block (AAncientGround calls these from outside)
virtual bool  CanEverAttack() const;                    // base true;  ASorcererUnit → false
virtual bool  IsAncientGroundEmpowerer() const;         // base false; ASorcererUnit → true
bool          CanReceiveDamageBoost() const;
void          AddPermanentDamageStacks(int32 Stacks);
void          ClearPermanentDamageStacks();
float         GetPermanentDamageMultiplier() const;     // BlueprintPure
float         GetDamageBoostPercent() const override;   // IHealthBarProvider
FOnCombatantDamageBoostChanged* GetDamageBoostChangedDelegate() override;

// AAncientGround
void InitAncientGround(bool bAuthoritative);

// HealthBarProvider.h
DECLARE_DYNAMIC_MULTICAST_DELEGATE_OneParam(FOnCombatantDamageBoostChanged, float, BoostPercent);

// UCombatantHealthBarWidget — BlueprintImplementableEvent
void SetDamageBoost(float FillFraction, float R, float G, float B, float RowOpacity);

// USiegeCheatManager — UFUNCTION(exec)
void SetTestDamageBoost(float Percent, bool bAllFriendly);
```

### 8. cards.csv — the `Sorcerer` row (§3.0 single-source law)
- Row name / DisplayName **`Sorcerer`** / "Sorcerer". `CardType Unit` · `Cost 60` · `MaxCopies 2` · `HP 70` · **`Damage 0` / `Range 0` / `Cadence 0`** · `Speed 350` · **`Profile Standard` (LOAD-BEARING — see §3)** · `DeckCount 2` · every keyword column default/None · `CardArt /Game/UI/CardArt/T_CardArt_Sorcerer.T_CardArt_Sorcerer`.
- **`Footman` DeckCount `11 → 9`** in the same edit — the `sum(DeckCount) == 50` invariant is held by construction (11+2 → 9+2+2). The Sorcerer is immediately playable.
- **60 gold is 15 above the current max (DeepMine 45) and 24 above the next unit (Ogre 36).** HP 70 / Speed 350 / MaxCopies 2 are defaults — **ALL FLAGGED, tune at playtest.**
- **⚠️ TRUTH LAW (the CONVENTIONS:364 Wizard precedent) — and the fix is CODE, not the `Notes` cell.** `UDeckBuilderWidget::GetCardDescription` composes from row fields and does **not** read `Notes` (verified: `DeckBuilderWidget.cpp:424-471`, composers at `:734-926`). For a Damage-0/Range-0/Cadence-0 Standard unit the generator prints only "Unit · Cost 60 gold · Max 2 per deck / Health: 70 / Move speed: 350" — it emits **no melee lie** (the melee branch requires `Row.Damage > 0` inside a `Row.Range > 0` block, both false) but it also says **NOTHING about what the card does**, which fails the truth bar in the other direction. **Law: the Sorcerer gets a CardID-keyed glossary rule line** in `AppendRuleLines` — the exact shipped idiom of `MinerRole` / `DeepMineRole` / `MasonsRole` (`GlossaryCardID_Sorcerer` mirroring the play path's keying). The line states: cannot attack; while it stands in an ancient ground it permanently strengthens every friendly unit standing there; the boost stacks, is kept when they leave, is lost on death, and is capped. **Magnitudes that exist as UPROPERTY mechanic rules are interpolated from those properties or stated qualitatively — never a hardcoded number that can drift.**
- The `Notes` cell stays truthful metadata (it is not player-facing here) and must not claim a melee attack.
- **Bot decks:** `SiegeBotController::BotDecks` is checked in this batch and the decision recorded — the default is **the bot does NOT play Sorcerers this pass** (the ancient-ground behavior is unmodeled in bot AI; a bot sorcerer would wander and do nothing). Recorded as a flagged follow-up, not a task.

### 9. FLEET-INCONSISTENCY LEDGER — found while shipping the Sorcerer, ALL deliberately NOT fixed (added 2026-08-02, manager; sources `handoffs/TASK-371-artist.md` §7, `TASK-372-artist.md` §15, `TASK-375-artist.md` §3b, `TASK-375-facing-fix.md`, `TASK-376-buildmaster.md`)
Each was measured, each is out of the ANCIENT-GROUNDS batch's scope, and **none is a defect introduced by this batch.** They are recorded so the next reader does not re-discover them, and so nobody "fixes" one unilaterally and desyncs the fleet.
- **`SM_Wizard` is a LOD OUTLIER:** `lODGroup` **`None`**, `lod_count` **1**, where `SM_Cleric` / `SM_Ogre` / `SM_Longbowman` / `SM_MilitiaMob` (and the new `SM_Sorcerer`) are `LargeProp` with a full chain. A real, cheap perf/consistency gap on a shipped asset — **needs a Jonathan/manager decision, not an assignee's initiative** (it is the STATIC lane, unrelated to the SK-LOD law above). **✅ RULED 2026-08-02 — JONATHAN ASKED FOR IT DIRECTLY; the decision this clause was waiting for is GIVEN. `SM_Wizard` takes `lod_group = 'LargeProp'` (the fleet's shipped STATIC-mesh setting — NOT the castle's explicit 50%/25% reduction chain, NOT the SK 50%/20% chain) → TASK-404.** Three properties of this fix are law: (a) it is a **STATICMESH** change and shares nothing with `Tools/regen_sk_lods.py` (TASK-392, SkeletalMesh) — the two must never be conflated in a task, a commit or a verdict; (b) `SM_<CardID>` is the **placement-ghost/preview** mesh, not the in-play skeletal actor, so the risk surface is the ghost and the card preview, not combat; (c) the durable fix belongs in the reimport lane (`Tools/reimport_meshes.py::_reimport_one()` already applies `LargeProp`) — the one-off apply is legitimate only because `SM_Wizard` predates that line, and the handoff must state whether a future same-path reimport would re-apply it for free.
- **Anim clips import at 24 fps, not the authored 30 (~20% slow: Idle 2.0 s → 2.5 s).** Cause is in `rig_character.py::main` — **the anim FBXs are exported BEFORE `render_previews` sets `scene.render.fps`**, so the manifest `fps: 30` never reaches them. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED: it is fleet-consistent with the unit it must match** (`A_Wizard_*` is also 24 fps — same rig lane; Footman/Cleric are 30 fps only because they came through the Meshy RETARGET lane). **A unilateral fix desyncs the Sorcerer from the Wizard** — if it is ever taken, it is ONE deliberate decision covering **both** units plus their re-export and re-import.
- **`A_Wizard_*` shipped with `bForceRootLock = false`** while Footman / Cleric / Sorcerer are `true`. The Wizard is the odd one out (likely a missed step in TASK-302's import) — candidate for the same combined follow-up as the fps item.
- **The Sorcerer's capsule is `88 / 34`, the raw `ACharacter` default** — full entry + the ≈`91 / 40` mesh-matched figure in "Skeletal rig & animation workstream (M7)" → the STATIC `VisualMesh` clause.
- **The texture-skip trap is now ARMED for the Sorcerer** (`MI_Sorcerer_PBR` exists) — full entry in "Fleet Meshy remaster" → "THE TEXTURE-SKIP TRAP".
- **ℹ️ NOT a defect, recorded so it is never re-opened as one: `TextureTools.get_size` reports the RESIDENT MIP, not the asset.** On a cold editor it returns **32×32** for `T_CardArt_Sorcerer` **and equally for the shipped Wizard / Footman / Archer**. **The authoritative field is the asset-registry `Dimensions` tag** (reads `512x512`). TASK-373's 512×512 reading was correct only because it queried while the texture was still fully resident post-import.

## FOLLOW command + the DEFAULT-STANCE law + the MINER command rework (2026-08-02)
Added 2026-08-02 (Jonathan directive, verbatim: *"I want to add another button to control units. This one is 'Follow' — it basically is a command that makes the unit follow around the player. There is only one mouse scroll circle used for this, and it is just the circle used to indicate what units follow; the units do not attack while in this command. Also, I want this to be the default behavior of all units that spawn, except for units that cannot follow commands such as the Ogre."* · follow-up: *"put the follow button on the 'c' key"* · and the miner rework, verbatim: *"we are going to change miner behavior a little bit, they now do receive all the commands, but they function differently for the miners…"*). Batch **FOLLOW-COMMAND**, TASK-395..408. **EXTENDS — does not supersede — "Group orders — 3-zone HOLD + AMBUSH"**: HOLD and AMBUSH keep their 3-stage pick, their zones, their leash rules and their byte-identical set. Follow is a THIRD group type with a ONE-stage pick.

### 1. THE COMMAND — enum, key, and the single-circle pick
- **Enum:** `ESiegeGroupCommandType : uint8 { Hold, Ambush, Follow }` in `UnitCommand.h` — **`Follow` is APPENDED**, so `Hold == 0` / `Ambush == 1` are byte-preserved. `ESiegeUnitCommand { Attack, Hold, Defend }` (the STANCE enum, whose byte layout `WBP_HUD`'s switch pins depend on) is **NOT touched** — Follow is a group order, never a stance.
- **`FSiegeUnitGroup` is UNCHANGED.** A Follow group carries `Type = Follow`, `PositionRadius == AttackRadius == 0`, both centers `ZeroVector`, both marker decals null. Every zone test in `UpdateStateGrouped` is therefore unreachable for a Follow group *because Follow never enters that function* (see §4) — not because a zero radius happens to fail.
- **Input asset:** ONE new **`IA_CmdFollow`** (`/Game/Input/Actions/IA_CmdFollow`, Digital/bool) mapped to **`C`** in the existing `/Game/Input/IMC_Hero`. Controller soft-ref `CmdFollowActionAsset` (`TSoftObjectPtr<UInputAction>`) → resolved `CmdFollowAction` → `ETriggerEvent::Started` → `OnCmdFollowPressed()`. **Null-safe resolve is law** (missing asset ⇒ C inert, logged once, never a crash — the shipped `IA_Cmd*` pattern). ⚠ **`IMC_Hero` is a BINARY `.uasset` the manager cannot text-verify.** Letter keys in use today: **Q** Rally · **T** Attack · **E** Defend · **R** Hold · **F** Ambush (plus Space jump, Shift sprint, Left-Alt UI cursor, 1–6 cards, LMB attack, RMB/Esc cancel). **C reads as FREE** — the C++ side has no `EKeys::C` and no `Crouch` binding anywhere — but the art-director MUST open `IMC_Hero` in-editor and CONFIRM C is unmapped before adding, and **FLAG (never silently stomp)** any conflict.
- **ONE CIRCLE, ONE STAGE (this is the whole difference from Hold/Ambush).** C enters the EXISTING pick machinery at **`EGroupPickStage::Select` only** and CONFIRMS there: mouse-wheel resizes the select circle (`GroupRadiusWheelStep` 100, min 200, max 5000, default `GroupSelectRadiusDefault` **1200** — all reused unchanged), LMB confirms, RMB/Esc cancels. **There is NO position zone and NO attack zone for Follow**, and the pick must never advance to `Position`/`AttackZone`. The wheel stays POLLED in `PlayerTick` (no new InputAction) and INERT outside a pick.
- **The select circle is a TRANSIENT pick visual and is DESTROYED at confirm.** Hold/Ambush transfer their circles to the group as persistent ground markers because those groups own a fixed piece of ground; a Follow group owns none, so a persistent marker would sit lying at a stale spot. `PositionMarkerDecal`/`AttackMarkerDecal` stay null for Follow.
- **`CancelGroupPick()` remains the ONE teardown call** and must cover the Follow stage at all 8 existing teardown sites (the melee-release-before-early-out law). Follow's pick is mutually exclusive with spell targeting and placement mode, exactly like the other two.

### 2. ⚠️ THE DEFAULT-STANCE LAW — FOLLOW IS THE SPAWN DEFAULT, AND NOTHING PLAYER-SIDE AUTO-ENGAGES ANY MORE
**This is a deliberate, Jonathan-confirmed change to the core game loop, not a side effect. Record it as such; do not let a later task "restore" auto-march as a bugfix.** Today a played Blue unit marches at the enemy castle unprompted. From this batch on:
- **Every FOLLOW-ELIGIBLE Blue unit auto-enrolls in the follow group at `BeginPlay`, on EVERY spawn path** — player placement (`SpawnUnitSwarm`), `ABarracks` spawner output, `SummonTestUnit` — because the enroll lives on the UNIT's `BeginPlay`, not on one controller call site. It walks to the hero and stays passive until the player gives it Attack / Defend / Hold / Ambush.
- **THERE IS EXACTLY ONE FOLLOW GROUP PER CONTROLLER.** `int32 DefaultFollowGroupId` (transient, `INDEX_NONE`), lazily created by `EnsureDefaultFollowGroup()`. Pressing **C** does NOT create a second follow group — it ADDS the circled eligible units to that same one (stealing them out of any Hold/Ambush group, per the existing steal law). This is what makes "the spawn default" and "the C command" the same object, and it is why Jonathan's "there is only one … circle used for this" is satisfiable.
- **Enrollment is UNCONDITIONAL and does not inherit the last order.** A unit spawned after the player pressed T still spawns FOLLOWING. **Accepted ergonomic consequence, FLAGGED:** reinforcements must be re-commanded each wave. If that plays as fiddly, the lever is a "new spawns inherit the last issued order" toggle — NOT a silent change to this law.
- **The release law is unchanged and now load-bearing:** **T** (Attack stance) and **E** (Defend stance) clear ALL groups, follow group included — T is therefore the "everyone attack" button. The follow group re-creates lazily on the next spawn or C press. Play Again clears it with everything else.
- **WHAT IS *NOT* COVERED, stated precisely so the pacing claim is honest:** (a) **Siege units — Ogre and Sapper — are NOT follow-eligible and are UNCHANGED: they still auto-march at the enemy castle.** "Nothing auto-engages" is true of the *commandable* player fleet, not of the whole board. (b) **Bot / Red units are entirely unchanged** — the opponent still plays the game by itself. (c) The LEGACY Standard body and `UpdateStateStandardCommanded` are **KEPT byte-identical**: they still serve Red, still serve Siege, and still serve any Blue unit whose group is released. Deleting them is not part of this batch.

### 3. ELIGIBILITY — two predicates, not one, because Jonathan widened only ONE of them
Today `IsGroupCommandEligible()` gates on `Profile == Standard && Team == Blue && !bDead && !bAIFrozen`. That single predicate now SPLITS, because Jonathan widened *Follow* to the Cleric and the Miner but said nothing about giving the Cleric zone orders:

| Unit | Profile | Follow (C) | Zone orders (R / F) | Stances (T / E) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Footman · Archer · Knight · MilitiaMob · Pikeman · Cavalry · Longbowman · Wizard · **Sorcerer** | Standard | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Cleric** | Support | ✅ **NEW** | ❌ (**manager ruling, FLAGGED** — see below) | ❌ unchanged |
| **Miner** | None (`AMinerUnit`) | ✅ **NEW** | ✅ **NEW** (miner semantics, §5) | ✅ **NEW** (miner semantics, §5) |
| **Ogre · Sapper** | Siege | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ — **explicitly excluded by Jonathan; auto-march UNCHANGED** |
| any Red / bot unit | any | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |

- **Mechanism (class identity, the shipped `CanEverAttack()` idiom — NOT a new CSV column, NOT a Profile change):** two new PUBLIC virtuals on `ASummonedUnit`. `Profile` is private, so these are the only sanctioned outside-callable eligibility surface, exactly as `IsGroupCommandEligible()` already is.
- **`IsGroupCommandEligible()` KEEPS ITS NAME AND SIGNATURE** (the controller's stage-1 select sweep calls it) and becomes `CanTakeZoneOrders() && Team == Blue && !bDead && !bAIFrozen`. Its meaning narrows to "zone orders" — say so in its doc comment, because the name no longer covers Follow.
- **🚩 CLERIC RULING (manager, DEFAULT SHIPS, FLAGGED for Jonathan): the Cleric is FOLLOW-ONLY this pass.** Jonathan named the Cleric only for Follow; giving it Hold/Ambush would mean reshaping `UpdateStateSupport`'s heal body, which he did not ask for. **A FOLLOWING CLERIC STILL HEALS** — healing is not attacking, and an escorting medic is the obvious intent of a support unit told to follow. The mechanism is the programmer's call; the OUTCOME is law. If Jonathan wants Cleric zone orders, the natural shape is the miner's (go to the position circle, never do the attack tier, keep healing) — recorded, not built.
- **The Sorcerer is Standard, so it spawns FOLLOWING.** Consequence, flagged not fixed: a sorcerer must now be explicitly Held into an Ancient Ground; it will no longer wander into one by marching.

### 4. THE FOLLOW BODY — dispatch, the hero anchor, and the never-attack guarantee
- **DISPATCH POINT (load-bearing):** `ASummonedUnit::UpdateState`'s order today is *freeze early-out → `TrackChargeMovement` → **Profile dispatch (Siege → `UpdateStateSiege`, Support → `UpdateStateSupport`, both `return`)** → group dispatch → stance gate → legacy body*. A Support Cleric therefore **returns before the group dispatch is ever reached**. **The Follow dispatch is HOISTED ABOVE the Profile dispatch** — if `CommandGroupId` resolves to a live group of type `Follow`, run `UpdateStateFollow(Group)` and return, whatever the profile. Hold/Ambush dispatch stays exactly where it is. This hoist is the ONE structural change to `UpdateState`, and every other branch stays byte-identical.
- **`UpdateStateFollow` NEVER ACQUIRES AND NEVER ATTACKS.** It force-nulls `CurrentTarget` and never calls `AcquireTarget` / `AcquireEnemyNearPoint` / `EnterAttack`. This is a per-BODY seal (a following Footman is still a normal attacker the moment it leaves the group), which is why it must not be conflated with `CanEverAttack()` — that one is per-CLASS and permanent.
- **THE HERO ANCHOR IS RESOLVED LIVE, EVERY STATE TICK, AND NEVER CACHED.** `ASiegePlayerController::GetFollowAnchor()` returns the owning controller's live pawn (the hero) or nullptr. The unit reaches it through **`ASiegePlayerController::FindControllerForTeam(World, Team)`** — the M8 owning-team resolve. **`GetFirstPlayerController()` stays BANNED in gameplay code** (M8 TEAM LAW). Resolving live is also what makes hero respawn work for free: the game mode's respawn path may hand back a **different pawn actor**, and a cached pointer would follow a corpse forever.
- **🚩 HERO-DEATH RULING (manager, Jonathan did not specify — DEFAULT SHIPS, FLAGGED):** while the anchor is null or the hero is dead, **following units HOLD POSITION** — `EnterIdle()`, no target, no attack, no march — and **resume following the instant a live hero pawn resolves again**, including a brand-new post-respawn pawn. Rejected alternatives, recorded so they are not re-derived: marching to the corpse (a conga line to a hidden body deep in enemy territory), and falling back to Defend (that would make them fight, breaking Jonathan's ruling that following units never attack). **Accepted consequence:** the hero respawns at `PlayerStart (−23800, 0, 100)`, so on resume a squad caught mid-field walks the full distance home. That is a pacing item for the playtest, not a defect.
- **⚠️ ANTI-REPATH IS A REQUIREMENT, NOT A POLISH ITEM.** The anchor MOVES. Re-issuing `EnterAdvanceToLocation` at a fresh station every 0.25 s state tick is precisely the re-path-every-tick mill that produced the TASK-280 / TASK-282 "units freeze just past midfield" and "halt just short of the castle" bugs. Law: **re-issue the move only when the recomputed station has drifted more than `FollowRepathTolerance` from the last issued goal**, and keep the existing 150 uu arrival tolerance for the idle test. A follow implementation that re-paths unconditionally is a QA FAIL.
- **Formation:** the per-unit station is `HeroLocation + GroupStationOffset`, with `GroupStationOffset` computed ONCE at enroll from a deterministic golden-angle sunflower inside `FollowFormationRadius` (the shipped squad-spread recipe, re-anchored on a moving point instead of a fixed circle). Per-unit scalars only — **no arrays on units** (the shipped law).
- **Replication posture (M8 DECLARATION DUTY): this batch adds NO replicated property and NO new replicated class**, so **no new relevancy tier is declared** — and that sentence must appear in the handoffs, because "tier not declared" is a QA FAIL and "there is nothing to declare" is only acceptable when it is stated. Follow-group state lives on `ASiegePlayerController` (owner-relevant, a player's private command surface) exactly like `UnitGroups` does today. In M8 P1 units are server-only, so the whole feature is play-verifiable **host/single-player only** until P2.

### 5. ⚠️ THE MINER COMMAND SEMANTICS TABLE — Jonathan's words are the spec
The miner now receives **all five** commands and runs a different body for each. `AMinerUnit`'s income/tenure bookkeeping is **NOT rewritten** by any of them: `UpdateMining`'s retarget gate, `TryRegisterArrivedMiner`, `AddMinerIncome`/`RemoveMinerIncome`, the per-tenure latches, eviction and the death path all stay the single owner of "am I registered and earning". **The command layer only chooses WHERE the miner is steered and WHETHER a mine is a legal destination.**

| Command | Miner behavior (Jonathan's ruling) | Notes / law |
|---|---|---|
| **Attack (T)** | Find the **nearest mine** and start mining. | This is today's shipped behavior: `SeekBestMine()` → `AGoldNode::FindBestMineFor(World, Team, From)` → walk → register → income. **Byte-identical to the current miner loop** — that equivalence is the cheapest regression proof in the batch, so state it explicitly. |
| **Defend (E)** | Come back to the **own castle and hide inside it**. | **REUSE TASK-350, DO NOT INVENT A MECHANIC.** The 3× castle is hollow and walk-in, and own-team units already enter (per-team `UNavArea_*CastleInterior` + `UNavFilter_Team*` + the `GateBlockerVolume` that ignores the own channel). "Hide inside" = advance to **`ACastle::GetInteriorAnchorLocation()`** on `FindOwnCastle()`'s result and idle there. **No mining, no attacking.** Own castle destroyed ⇒ idle in place. A nav failure that strands the miner at the gate is ACCEPTABLE (log once) — it must never crash and never fall back to attacking. |
| **Hold (R)** | Exactly as Hold works for other units, **except it never does the attacking portion** — it goes to the position circle only, and **mines a mine if one lies inside the position circle**. | Tier-1 (attack zone) and tier-2 (position-zone enemy) acquisition are **SKIPPED ENTIRELY** for a miner. The ladder becomes: mine inside the position disc → else the per-unit station inside that disc. |
| **Ambush (F)** | **Identical to Hold for miners** (Jonathan said so in as many words) — position circle, no attacking, mine a mine in the circle. | For `AMinerUnit` the Hold and Ambush bodies COLLAPSE. The leash-exemption is meaningless without a target. Implement once; do not fork. |
| **Follow (C)** — and the spawn default, see the flag below | **The same as every other commandable unit**: walk to the hero's live station, never attack. | Jonathan: *"The miner will use the 'follow' command the same way as all other commandable units follow it."* |

- **Mine-in-the-circle finder (name reserved here before the task issues):** `static AGoldNode* AGoldNode::FindBestMineInDisc(UWorld* World, ETeamId Team, const FVector& Center, float Radius, const FVector& From)` — `FindBestMineFor`'s exact tier-1/tier-2 selection and its no-churn / strict-`<` tiebreak, with one extra gate: the candidate's LOCATION must lie within `Radius` of `Center`. **`FindBestMineFor` itself is NOT modified** (the bot shares it). Null result = no mine in the circle ⇒ the miner just stations.
- **Castle interior anchor:** `FVector ACastle::GetInteriorAnchorLocation() const` (BlueprintPure) = actor transform applied to `FVector InteriorAnchorRelativeLocation` (EditDefaultsOnly, default **`FVector::ZeroVector`** — the castle's origin is ground-centre by law, so zero IS the interior floor centre). **MEASURE, DO NOT ASSUME:** the handoff must report the resolved world point, that it nav-projects, and that it lies inside the shell. Flagged tunable.
- **🚩 MINER SPAWN-DEFAULT RULING (manager, DEFAULT SHIPS, FLAGGED — this is the biggest silent consequence in the batch).** Read literally, "Follow is the spawn default for every commandable unit" + "the miner is now commandable" means **every miner you play walks to the hero and earns ZERO gold until you personally order it to mine.** That is a 24-gold economy card that does nothing until micro'd, and Jonathan's stated intent was about combat (*"nothing auto-engages… the player personally orders every fight"*), not about the economy. **DEFAULT THAT SHIPS: a miner spawns in its MINING behavior (i.e. the `Attack` semantics — today's byte-identical loop); Follow applies to a miner only when it is explicitly circled with C.** The literal reading is a one-line flip if Jonathan wants it. **This must be the first item on his playtest gate.**
