### 6. ⚠️ THE MINER SEAL — what may be re-opened, what may not, and the two candidate approaches
`AMinerUnit` is sealed **structurally** (class doc + `qa/TASK-021-report.md` WARN-1): `StateCheckInterval = 0` (so `SetTimer` with rate ≤ 0 CLEARS instead of scheduling — the base state machine's only driver never runs) · `AggroRadius = 0` · `ClearAllTimersForObject(this)` immediately after `Super::BeginPlay()`. **The seal exists because the Miner row carries `Cadence 0` and `LoadStatsAndStart` clamps to `MinAttackCadence` 0.05 s — a miner that ever reached the Attack state would swing 20×/s.** Commands need a decision loop, so this seal must be re-opened **without re-opening the ability to attack**.
- **NAMED PRECEDENT: `ASorcererUnit` (TASK-360).** It is commandable and cannot attack, and it achieves that by **keeping its state timer** and sealing at the chokepoints via `CanEverAttack() → false`, enforced at **THREE guard points in `ASummonedUnit.cpp`** — `EnterAttack()` (stands the unit down to Idle), `UpdateStateGrouped()` (acquires nothing), `PerformAttack()` (refuses). Those guards are ALREADY SHIPPED and already cover any class that returns false.
- **`AMinerUnit::CanEverAttack()` MUST return `false`, whichever approach is chosen.** It is free, it is already pinned, and it makes the three shipped guard points cover the miner permanently. This is not optional and is not the approach decision.
- **THE APPROACH IS THE PROGRAMMER'S, WITH A WRITTEN JUSTIFICATION — but here is the trap that decides it, measured from the shipped code, so nobody rediscovers it the hard way.** `UpdateState`'s profile dispatch (`SummonedUnit.cpp:1197-1211`) routes **Siege** and **Support** away, and lets **Standard *and* `None`** fall through to the legacy Standard body. **The Miner is `Profile == None`.** So the moment `StateCheckInterval` becomes non-zero, the miner starts running the **legacy Standard body** — which, with no target, sets `Goal = FindNearestEnemyCastle()` and issues `EnterAdvance`. **An unsealed miner marches on the enemy castle**, and `AggroRadius = 0` does not prevent it (that only blocks acquisition). Any approach that lifts the timer seal must handle this explicitly.
  - **(A) UNSEAL** — restore `StateCheckInterval`, drop the `ClearAllTimersForObject` sweep, add `CanEverAttack() → false`, and route `Profile == None` away from the legacy body. Maximum reuse of `UpdateStateFollow`/`UpdateStateGrouped`; but it re-opens the exact trap the seal exists for and risks **double-driving the walk** (the base's `EnterAdvance` fighting `EnsureWalkingToNode` for the same movement component).
  - **(B) KEEP THE SEAL, READ COMMANDS IN THE MINER'S OWN POLL** — `UpdateMining` already runs every `ArrivalCheckInterval` (0.25 s, the same cadence as the state timer). Resolve the owning-team controller + group there and steer the destination. Zero risk of a 20×/s attack, zero risk of a castle march, zero double-drive; costs a small duplication of the group resolve and the station-offset math.
  - **Manager's leaning is (B) or a hybrid** on blast-radius grounds, but **(A) with the fall-through handled is acceptable if the programmer's evidence supports it.** What is NOT acceptable is choosing silently: the handoff states the approach, the double-drive analysis, and the `Profile == None` fall-through disposition.
- **REGRESSION LAW for the miner (QA criteria, all of them):** an **un-commanded** miner is behaviorally identical to today (walk → register → +1 gold/s → wait-mode → eviction → all-depleted idle) · the §3.3 miner cap, the per-tenure income latches and the death bookkeeping are untouched · `AGoldNode`'s exclusive-occupancy claim is untouched · `FreezeAI` still kills every miner timer at match end · a miner **NEVER** enters Attack under ANY of the five commands, and QA cites the guard points by file:line.

### 7. PINNED CROSS-TASK SIGNATURE REGISTRY (the contract that makes the parallel C++ tasks safe)
⚠️ **UBT compiles the whole module.** Every task in this batch compiles against this list **character-for-character**; a task that "improves" a pinned signature breaks the link and is an **automatic QA FAIL**. Access levels are part of the pin.

### 8. Tunables (ALL `EditDefaultsOnly`, ALL flagged for Jonathan's feel pass) + reused constants
- **NEW:** `ASiegePlayerController::FollowFormationRadius` **900** uu (the ring the squad spreads in around the hero) · `ASiegePlayerController::FollowRepathTolerance` **250** uu (the anti-repath band of §4) · `ACastle::InteriorAnchorRelativeLocation` `ZeroVector`.

### 9. Card-face ACCEPTANCE GATE — the tool name, reserved here before its task issues
The card-face acceptance checker (`accept.py`) exists only in a session scratchpad, exactly like the render script that was lost. Its durable home is **`Tools/ArtPipeline/cardart_accept.py`**, sibling to `cardart_render.py`, under the same CLI + exit-code discipline; `Tools/**/*.py` is **CODE ⇒ QA-gated**. **Three criteria are added to it as law, because the shipped gate structurally could not see the Sorcerer defect** (`handoffs/SORCERER-DARKNESS-diagnosis.md` §4d/§5b): (1) the key is sampled from **all FOUR corners**, not the two top ones — the defect lived entirely in the two the gate skipped; (2) an **ABSOLUTE backdrop luminance floor** (≥ **0.08** linear, ~the fleet minimum) joins the relative ΔE2000 test — a gate made only of relative criteria drifts as a body; (3) a **gradient-direction assertion, bottom/top ≥ 1.0** — the fleet runs 1.36–6.76 and the Sorcerer 0.46, so this one criterion alone would have failed the shipped card and passed all twelve others. **Why this is not optional: the ΔE ≥ 10 uniqueness rule structurally pushes each new key DARKER** (shipped keys run `val` 0.225→0.655, mean 0.394, and the unoccupied colour volume is increasingly in the dark corner), so the blind spot gets worse with every card added. **The hardened gate must land BEFORE the 29-card roster re-render (TASK-387)** or the whole roster inherits the bias.

## In-match LLM command assistant (v1, text-only) — 2026-08-02
Added 2026-08-02 (Jonathan approved the feature; batch **LLM-ASSISTANT**, TASK-409+). Design authority = the approved plan `C:\Users\wesel\.claude\plans\there-is-one-new-glittery-bentley.md` (**the plan file wins over any board summary**; it carries the research findings, the derivations and the six spike bars, and this section does not re-derive them). The player types a natural-language order — *"send 10 footmen with a sorcerer to the nearest ancient ground"* — and a **local LLM shipped inside the game** translates it into the game's existing unit commands, surveying state first and asking a clarifying question when the request cannot be met.

**Jonathan's four rulings (DECIDED 2026-08-02 — no agent re-opens them):** (1) **CPU fallback is required** — it must run on the modal 8 GB Steam card *and* CPU-only; no vendor lock-in (this is why the backend is **Vulkan + CPU, never CUDA**). (2) **V1 is a text-only vertical slice** — in-match console, the order verbs + unit selection, the clarification loop; **no voice, no macro UI, no settings screen.** (3) **Command only** — the AI never plays cards and never spends gold; the player spawns, the AI orders. (4) **Prompt-engineer first**, fine-tune only if measurement demands it.

### 1. ⚠️ THE CENTRAL LAW — VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT KILLS THIS FEATURE IF BROKEN

> **One utterance + one snapshot → one constrained JSON; the game owns the dialogue; a multi-turn model loop is a QA FAIL.**

This is written first because **the most likely way this design dies is someone adding "just one more turn."** BFCL multi-turn scores collapse with model size (Qwen3-4B **35 %**, 1.7B 17 %, 0.6B **1.4 %**) while single-shot structured output holds at **~82 %** for 4B. We are building a **translator, not an agent.** The model never sees a prior turn, never holds conversation state, never chains a tool call. Each clarification turn is a **fresh single-turn call**; the FSM carries the context forward as one game-authored line in Zone C (`pending: guard footman×10 -> ancient_ground_near ; problem: only 8 available`). Any code that feeds a model's own previous output back to the model is an automatic QA FAIL, whatever it is called.

> **Grammar guarantees existence, executor guarantees legality, FSM owns the conversation.**

Three owners, three responsibilities, no overlap. The **GBNF grammar is generated per request from live game state**, so unit kinds and place names are literal enum alternatives and a small model *physically cannot* name a unit that does not exist — grounding moves out of the weights and into the grammar. The **executor** re-checks everything the grammar cannot know (counts, eligibility, authority). The **FSM** decides what to say and when. Constrained decoding guarantees **syntax, never semantics** — it cannot prevent a valid-shaped wrong command — which is why **confirm-before-execute with ghost circles on the ground is a requirement, not polish.**

- **⚠️ Constrain identity hard, leave quantity soft — DELIBERATELY.** `count` runs **1–30**, *not* 1–live-max. If the grammar capped at the live 8, a player asking for 10 would get a silently-emitted 8 — the exact valid-shaped-wrong-command failure this design exists to prevent, and the clarification would become **undetectable**. A later task that "tightens" `count` to the live roster is introducing the defect, not fixing one.

### 2. ⚠️ THE ASSISTANT IS STRICTLY ADDITIVE — AND THIS MATTERS MORE THAN IT SOUNDS
**Every keyboard command still works, byte-identically. The console is never a requirement for any action. No future task may route a key through the assistant.**

This is not a style preference. The FOLLOW batch made **Follow the spawn default**, so the player-side commandable fleet no longer auto-engages and **the player personally orders every fight.** An assistant that is merely unhelpful is fine; an assistant that becomes the *path* to a command and is unreliable makes the game **worse than before it existed.** So:
- `IA_CmdFollow` / `IA_CmdAttack` / `IA_CmdHold` / `IA_CmdDefend` / `IA_CmdAmbush` / `IA_Rally` / cards 1–6 keep their exact shipped behavior and their exact shipped code paths. The assistant calls the **same public APIs the keys call** — never a parallel implementation, never a "better" one.
- A model-load failure, a GGML fault, a timeout, a NO-GO verdict, or a missing GGUF **must never block match start and never degrade any key.** `bAssistantFaulted` is a session latch: it disables the console and nothing else.
- **Jonathan's playtest gate includes, as a hard criterion: "verify every keyboard command still works identically with the console closed."**

### 3. THE LLM EMITS SYMBOLS ONLY
**Never coordinates, never prose, never player-facing text.** The model's entire output surface is one constrained JSON object of `uint8` / `int32` / `FName`-shaped symbols. **Every sentence the player reads is a game-authored template** filled from a reason code. Consequences that are law, not preference:
- Resolved `FVector`s for named places stay **game-side**. The model sees `ancient_ground_near`; it never sees a number that means a position.
- There is no "let the model phrase it nicely" path. A template table is the only source of player-facing strings.
- Because the command struct is `uint8`/`int32`/`FName` only, **M8 P2 takes `FSiegeAssistantCommand` as-is** over the wire (~20 bytes), with inference staying client-local.

### 4. ⛔ THE UNIT-REGISTRY REJECTION — RECORDED SO NOBODY "OPTIMIZES" IT IN
`USiegeAssistantSnapshot::Capture` does **six LOGICAL passes — up to EIGHT actual `TActorIterator` traversals — once per typed sentence** (~0.2 ms). ⚠️ **COUNT CORRECTED 2026-08-03 FROM THE CODE (`qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-7): *"six passes"* was literally false, and `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h`'s own comment had said so all along.** Three direct loops in `Capture`, plus five traversals inside the shipped finders, **two of which are called twice** (`FindNearestCastleForTeam` ×2, `FindNearestAncientGround` ×2, `FindBestMineFor` ×1) — up to eight, seven when the hero is possessed. **The rejection is UNAFFECTED and is NOT re-opened:** its argument is *"~0.2 ms we never pay per frame"*, and 33 % more of an unmeasurable cost is still unmeasurable. ⚠️ **But the number quoted as the justification was understated by a third, and a rejection is only as good as the figure it cites — the header was right and the document was not.** ⚠️ ***"never per tick"* is NOT REACHED, which is not the same as verified: this class has NO CALLER (§12g), so it has never run at any frequency at all.** **DO NOT ADD A UNIT REGISTRY.** A registry is **new lifetime state to get wrong** (spawn/death/destroy/level-travel/Play-Again bookkeeping, and every one of those paths is a shipped bug class in this repo) **to save 0.2 ms that we never pay per frame.** The player types a handful of sentences per match; the cost is unmeasurable next to a single inference call. A future task proposing a registry, an actor cache, a "dirty flag", or a subscription list for this feature is **rejected on sight** — cite this clause.

### 5. NAMING + FOLDER LAW (the cross-task contract)

| Thing | Exact name | Location |
|---|---|---|
| Plugin | `SiegeLlama` | `Plugins/SiegeLlama/` (`SiegeLlama.uplugin`) |
| Plugin runtime module | `SiegeLlama` | `Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/SiegeLlama/` (`Public/`, `Private/`, `SiegeLlama.Build.cs`) |
| Vendored llama.cpp | `LlamaCpp` | `Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/LlamaCpp/` — see §6 ruling B |
| Inference subsystem | `USiegeLlamaSubsystem` (`UGameInstanceSubsystem`) | plugin — `SiegeLlamaSubsystem.h/.cpp` |
| FSM / executor component | `USiegeAssistantComponent` (`UActorComponent`) | game — `SiegeAssistantComponent.h/.cpp` |
| State snapshot | `USiegeAssistantSnapshot` (`UObject`) | game — `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h/.cpp` |
| Grammar builder | `USiegeAssistantGrammar` | game — `SiegeAssistantGrammar.h/.cpp` |
| Command struct + intent enum + log category | `FSiegeAssistantCommand`, `ESiegeAssistantIntent` | game — `SiegeAssistantCommand.h/.cpp` (the `UnitCommand.h` / `TeamId.h` pure-data-header precedent) |
| Vocabulary data-asset class | `USiegeAssistantVocabulary` (`UDataAsset`) | game — `SiegeAssistantVocabulary.h/.cpp` |
| Vocabulary asset instance | `DA_AssistantVocabulary` | `/Game/Data/DA_AssistantVocabulary` |
| Console widget | `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` (`UUserWidget`) | game — `SiegeAssistantConsoleWidget.h/.cpp` |
| Reserved widget asset name (see §6 ruling A) | `WBP_AssistantConsole` | `/Game/UI/WBP_AssistantConsole` — **RESERVED, not authored in v1** |
| Input action (Wave 1) | `IA_AssistantConsole` | `/Game/Input/Actions/IA_AssistantConsole`, mapped in `/Game/Input/IMC_Hero` |
| Game-lane log category | **`LogSiegeAssistant`** | declared/defined in `SiegeAssistantCommand.h/.cpp` |
| Plugin log category | **`LogSiegeLlama`** | declared/defined in `SiegeLlamaSubsystem.h/.cpp` |
| Model weights (dev) | `<ProjectRoot>/Models/` | **gitignored wholesale** — see §7 |
| Model fetch tool | `Tools/fetch_llm_model.py` | `Tools/**/*.py` is **CODE ⇒ QA-gated** |

- **TWO log categories, deliberately.** The plugin knows nothing about Siegebound and must stay reusable/replaceable; giving it `LogSiegeAssistant` would couple it to the game lane it is architecturally forbidden to know about. `LogSiegeLlama` = tokens, backends, timings, faults. `LogSiegeAssistant` = snapshots, grammars, commands, FSM transitions, refusals. Both follow the `LogSiege<Domain>` law.
- **DEV/SPIKE CONSOLE COMMANDS REGISTER VIA `FAutoConsoleCommand` / `FAutoConsoleCommandWithWorld` IN A NEW FILE — NEVER as a `UFUNCTION(exec)` on a shipped class.** `USiegeCheatManager` and `ASiegePlayerController` stay untouched by this batch.
  - ⚖️ **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-03 (TASK-500 B2) — THE WORD *"NEVER"* IS SCOPED TO ***SPIKE*** SURFACE, AND THE SCOPE IS WRITTEN HERE RATHER THAN 1160 LINES AWAY.** ⛔ **I had reinterpreted this clause in "THE FINE-TUNE RUNG" §8 and LEFT THIS SENTENCE READING ABSOLUTE — which by my own doctrine is *an exception wearing an amendment's clothes*.** ⚠️ **The rule I applied to CUDA and to §16 says a letter that outlived its reason is REPAIRED, never reinterpreted from a distance, ***because the next reader obeys the letter*** — and TASK-479 shipped code against this letter before it was fixed.**
  - ✅ **WHAT THE CLAUSE ACTUALLY PROTECTS, stated as the property instead of the mechanism: THE *SPIKE* MUST NOT BECOME A SHIPPED EXEC SURFACE**, which is what keeps the inference lane genuinely **new-files-only** under the M8 PARALLEL LAW and lets it work from any module including the plugin.
  - ✅ **THEREFORE PERMITTED, NARROWLY: a SHIPPED-LANE dev-only diagnostic on `USiegeCheatManager`** — because a `Siege.Llama.*` console command **structurally cannot reach the shipped path**, so the shipped lane would otherwise be *unobservable by construction*. **Conditions: dev-only-guarded (`#if !UE_BUILD_SHIPPING`) · read-only · delegating, never re-implementing · rationale block in the header.** **Live instance: `DebugCaptureAndComposePrompt` (TASK-479).**
  - ⛔ **STILL BANNED, UNCHANGED: any `Siege.Llama.*` / spike command as a `UFUNCTION(exec)`, and any exec that MUTATES assistant state.** ⚠️ **`ASiegePlayerController` stays untouched by the inference lane.** This is what keeps the whole inference lane genuinely **new-files-only** under the M8 PARALLEL LAW, and it works from any module including the plugin. Command namespace: **`Siege.Llama.*`** (plugin) and **`Siege.Assistant.*`** (game lane).

### 6. THE TWO MANAGER RULINGS (asked for by name in the dispatch; both DECIDED)

**RULING A — the console widget's tree is CODE-AUTHORED in v1. This is a NAMED, SCOPED, CONDITIONAL EXCEPTION to the `U<Name>Widget` ↔ `WBP_<Name>` law under "Widgets with C++ bases", and it does not generalize.**
`USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` builds its tree in `RebuildWidget()` via `WidgetTree->ConstructWidget<>` and ships with **no `.uasset`**. Reasoning, in the order that decided it:
1. **The law's purpose is satisfied vacuously.** That law exists so the artist's asset name and the programmer's code reference cannot drift apart. When there is no asset, there is nothing to drift. The law is not being violated; it has no subject.
2. **This repo's single most expensive UI failure mode is exactly the thing a `WBP_` here would invite.** The template-donor rule says every new widget starts as a **duplicate of a donor** — and a duplicated+reparented WidgetBlueprint has **silently broken RUNTIME repaint** on this project (design-time fine, ~9 wasted fixes), while a `K2Node_CustomEvent` masquerading as a BIE hid the health-bar defect **five times**. A code-authored tree cannot have either defect.
3. **No human Designer step on a v1 vertical slice's critical path**, and the tree cannot drift from the C++ that drives it.

**The exception's five conditions — it is a ruling, not a waiver, and all five are QA criteria:**
- **(a) SCOPE.** It applies to `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` **only**. Every other new widget on this project still obeys the `WBP_` law. Citing this clause for another widget is a misuse.
- **(b) THE ESCAPE HATCH IS BUILT IN, AT ZERO COST.** Every child widget is still declared as a `UPROPERTY(meta=(BindWidgetOptional))` member with the exact names below, and `RebuildWidget()` **constructs a child only if that member is still null**. A `WBP_AssistantConsole` authored later therefore wins automatically with **zero C++ change**.
  - ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-08-03 — THIS CLAUSE ALSO SAID *"after `Super::RebuildWidget()`"* AND THAT ORDERING WAS WRONG.** ~~after `Super::RebuildWidget()`~~ **Build the tree and set `WidgetTree->RootWidget` FIRST, then `return Super::RebuildWidget();`** — `UserWidget.cpp:1214` reads `RootWidget` and returns an `SSpacer` when it is null, so anything built after Super is discarded and **the widget renders empty while passing every property readback.** Full derivation and provenance: the identical correction under "Settings screen + the assistant CONFIRM STEP…" §3(b). ✅ **`USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` already ships the CORRECT order** (`ConstructConsoleTree(); return Super::RebuildWidget();`) — **the defect was in this text, never in the code.**
- **(c) THE ASSET NAME IS RESERVED NOW.** `/Game/UI/WBP_AssistantConsole` may not be used by anything else, so the fallback path never has a naming fight. Taking the fallback costs **one art task and zero C++ change** — it can be taken at any time, by Jonathan or by a later manager ruling, without a code task.
- **(d) THE CONTRACT IS UNCHANGED.** The widget still clones `USessionMenuWidget`'s shipped contract exactly: `BindWidgetOptional` members, `BlueprintCallable` wrappers, and **FString/int32/bool/uint8-only BlueprintImplementableEvents** (FSM state is pushed as a `uint8`, never an enum — the widget-param law).
- **(e) VERIFICATION IS A HUMAN PIXEL CHECK.** Because there is no `.uasset` to read back, correctness closes on **rendered pixels or Jonathan's eyes**, never on a tree/property readback (the standing UMG verification law — MCP readback has repeatedly passed on visually-broken UMG here).
- **Pinned child-widget member names (the C++ and any future WBP both read this line):** `InputBox` (`UEditableTextBox`) · `TranscriptText` (`UTextBlock`) · `StatusText` (`UTextBlock`) · `ConfirmButton` (`UButton`) · ~~`CancelButton` (`UButton`)~~ · `RootPanel` (`UVerticalBox`).
  - ⚖️ **AMENDED 2026-08-04 (Jonathan's ruling 3, batch ASSISTANT-EXCLUDE) — `CancelButton` IS STRUCK FROM THE PIN, AND THE PIN IS EDITED RATHER THAN THE MEMBER QUIETLY DELETED.** His directive, verbatim: *"instead of it being a cancel button and an accept button, lets make it to where there is no cancel button(they just simply close the chat box), and instead of an accept button they press \"z\""*. ⇒ **The `CancelButton` member, its `HandleCancelClicked` thunk and its `CancelLabelText` all go.** ⛔ **A future `WBP_AssistantConsole` MUST NOT re-introduce a widget named `CancelButton` expecting it to bind** — the C++ no longer declares it, so a WBP child of that name would bind to nothing and look wired. **The strikethrough stays instead of a deletion precisely so the next author meets the retirement rather than an absence.**
  - ✅ **`ConfirmButton` STAYS PINNED AND STAYS BOUND.** ⚠️ **His ruling replaces the *only way to accept*, it does not forbid the button existing** — but the shipped v1 tree stops constructing one, so ⛔ **`ConfirmButton` is now a `BindWidgetOptional` that a future WBP may supply and the code-authored tree does NOT create.** ⚖️ That keeps ruling A(b)'s escape hatch intact at zero cost, which is the whole reason the hatch is written as "construct only if still null".

**RULING A-2 — THE CONSOLE'S CLOSE ROUTES ARE ENUMERATED HERE, AND `Enter`-ON-AN-EMPTY-BOX IS ONE OF THEM (Jonathan's directive, 2026-08-03, verbatim: *"we need a way to be able to close the AI chat window in the game. Lets make it to where if you press "enter" without anything typed in the box then it will close"*).**
⚠️ **WHY THIS CLAUSE EXISTS RATHER THAN A CODE COMMENT: the close routes were never written down, and the gap surfaced TWICE — first as flagged item (t) (Escape does not close), then as a live playtest complaint.** A behaviour nobody enumerated is a behaviour every later task re-decides.

- **THE ENUMERATED CLOSE ROUTES (`AS-§6 A-2`), and this list is the contract:**
  1. **The open key (`IA_AssistantConsole`, `Enter`) pressed while the console is open** — `ASiegePlayerController::OnAssistantConsolePressed` is **already a toggle** and its close half is **deliberately un-gated** (a close that can be refused can strand the cursor). ⚠️ **Whether Enhanced Input ever RECEIVES that press while the input box holds Slate focus is UNMEASURED** — see the ordering clause below.
  2. **`CancelPressed()` with no confirm prompt up** — shipped, ratified.
     - ⚖️ **AMENDED 2026-08-04 — ROUTE 2 SURVIVES THE BUTTON'S REMOVAL, AND ITS FATE IS RULED EXPLICITLY RATHER THAN LEFT TO THE DIFF.** `CancelPressed()` is `BlueprintCallable` **public API** and is an enumerated close route; **deleting a shipped public entry point to remove a button is a breaking change bought for nothing.** ⇒ ⛔ **`CancelPressed()` KEEPS ITS EXACT SHIPPED BEHAVIOUR AND ITS EXACT SIGNATURE.** What changes is only that **nothing in the v1 tree calls it any more** — it becomes the *non-key discard route*, for a future `WBP_AssistantConsole`, a gamepad path, or an accessibility path. ⚠️ **It joins `ToggleConsole()` in the deliberately-uncalled set, and like `ToggleConsole()` it must SAY SO in its header comment** — an uncalled `BlueprintCallable` that does not admit it is how the next reader concludes it is dead and deletes it.
  3. **`SetConsoleEnabled(false)`** — the fault latch closes it.
  4. **⭐ NEW: `Enter` committed on an EMPTY (whitespace-trimmed) input box.** ⛔ **The trigger is `HandleTextCommitted` with `CommitMethod == ETextCommit::OnEnter`, NEVER `SubmitPressed`** — `SubmitPressed` is `BlueprintCallable` and reachable by non-key routes, and its existing empty-string refusal (no model call on a blank prompt) is a **separate, ratified protection that stays exactly as it is.**
- ⛔ **A CLOSE IS NOT A CANCEL, AND THIS DOES NOT CHANGE THAT.** `CloseConsole()` deliberately broadcasts **no** cancellation when a confirm prompt is up — *"the player closed the window"* and *"the player discarded the order"* are different statements and only the FSM may turn one into the other. **The empty-`Enter` route reuses `CloseConsole()` verbatim and adds no new broadcast.**
  - ⚖️⚖️ **AMENDED 2026-08-04 BY JONATHAN, AND THE AMENDMENT IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS — READ WHICH HALF MOVED.** His ruling 3, verbatim: *"there is no cancel button (they just simply close the chat box)"*. ⇒ **CLOSING THE CONSOLE WHILE A CONFIRM PROMPT IS UP IS NOW THE PLAYER'S CANCEL GESTURE. It is his product decision and it is not re-litigated.**
  - ✅ **THE HALF THAT DOES NOT MOVE — AND IT IS THE HALF THIS CLAUSE WAS ACTUALLY PROTECTING: THE *WIDGET* STILL BROADCASTS NOTHING NEW. THE *FSM* IS STILL THE ONLY THING THAT TURNS A CLOSE INTO A DISCARD.** ⭐ **Behaviourally this ruling is nearly free, because the FSM ALREADY DOES IT: `USiegeAssistantComponent::NotifyConsoleClosed()` treats a close in `AwaitConfirm` as a discard today** — it clears the preview, clears the pending intent and returns to `Idle`. **So the ruling ratifies shipped behaviour rather than requesting new behaviour**, and the widget's `CloseConsole()` stays byte-unchanged. ⛔ **An implementation that adds a cancellation broadcast to the WIDGET to satisfy this ruling has misread it and is a QA FAIL.**
  - ⭐ **THE ONE GENUINE DELTA, AND IT IS A *PLAYER-VISIBLE* ONE: THE DISCARD IS CURRENTLY SILENT.** The `"Cancelled"` transcript line and the `OnConsoleCancelled` broadcast were reachable **only** through the button. With the button gone, an order can vanish with **nothing on screen saying why** — ⚖️ **and "the order silently disappeared" is exactly the report shape this feature cannot afford** (it reads as *"the assistant ate my order"*, which is a trust failure, not a UI nit). ⇒ ⛔ **RULED: A CLOSE THAT DISCARDS A PENDING ORDER MUST PUSH THE GAME-AUTHORED `Cancelled` TRANSCRIPT LINE, FROM THE FSM, VIA THE EXISTING REASON-CODE TEMPLATE TABLE** (§3: every sentence the player reads is a template, never authored at a call site). **The line is pushed on the AwaitConfirm→Idle close path ONLY** — a close with no pending order says nothing, because there is nothing to report.
  - ⚠️ **AND THE TWO PATHS THAT LOSE THEIR CONSOLE ROUTE ARE NAMED, NOT LEFT TO ROT:** `OnConsoleCancelled`'s `Thinking`-abort and `Deferred`-drop handling becomes **unreachable from the console** once the button is gone. ⛔ **Do NOT delete that handling** — the delegate is public API and the FSM paths are correct; **they are simply without a caller in v1**, exactly like `ToggleConsole()`. **Say so at the binding site so the next reader does not "clean up" a live contract.**
- ⛔ **THE ORDERING HAZARD IS NAMED BECAUSE IT IS UNMEASURED, NOT BECAUSE IT IS EXPECTED.** `Enter` now has **three jobs**: open · submit · close-when-empty. If Slate consumes the key (the standing reasoning, and what Jonathan's *"I cannot close it"* report corroborates), the controller toggle never sees it and the three never collide. **If it does NOT consume it, an empty-box press could close and then immediately re-open.** ⇒ **Any implementation of route 4 MUST carry a re-open suppression window and MUST state in its handoff which ordering it relied on.** ✅ **The rollback that makes the suppression safe already ships: `OnAssistantConsolePressed` re-reads `IsConsoleOpen()` after `OpenConsole()` and releases the posture when the widget refused** — so a refused re-open cannot leave the cursor stranded.
- 🧑✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-04 — JONATHAN RULED, AND THE RULING IS WRITTEN HERE AS THIS CLAUSE ITSELF DEMANDED.** ~~OPEN, AND JONATHAN'S ALONE: does `Escape` close the console too?~~
  > ### ⛔ **`Escape` IS LEFT EXACTLY AS IT IS. IT NEVER CLOSES THE CONSOLE, IT IS NEVER ABSORBED BY ANY ASSISTANT WIDGET OR COMPONENT, AND THAT IS NOW PERMANENT — NOT PENDING.**
  - **He chose option (a) of the three this clause offered** (leave it · Escape-closes-while-open · his own answer), presented with the cost of each. ⇒ **The shipped cancel routes — placement, spell targeting, group-pick — keep firing byte-identically while the console is open**, and `AS-§2`'s *"the assistant never degrades a shipped key"* holds without an exception being carved for the one key most likely to need one.
  - ⛔ **THE STANDING PROHIBITION IS NOW PERMANENT AND ITS BASIS HAS CHANGED.** Until today *"no agent may absorb `Escape`"* was a **hold pending a decision**; from today it is **the decision**. ⇒ ⛔ **Any future task that absorbs `Escape` — `NativeOnKeyDown`, `NativeOnPreviewKeyDown`, an Enhanced Input action, a Slate `FReply::Handled()` on `EKeys::Escape`, or a viewport-level intercept — is overturning a Jonathan ruling and is an automatic QA FAIL.** ⚠️ **This binds the `Z`-accept work specifically: a preview-key handler that returns `Handled` for `Escape`, even "harmlessly", breaks this clause.** The handler consumes the accept key and **nothing else**.
  - ⚖️ **AND THE ITEM THIS RETIRES IS NAMED SO IT STOPS RE-SURFACING: flagged item (t) (*"Escape does not close the console"*) is CLOSED AS RULED-INTENDED, NOT AS FIXED.** It surfaced three times — as flagged item (t), as TASK-508 question (b), and in the playtest list — because nobody had ruled. **It is ruled. A fourth appearance is a reading failure, not a new finding.**
  - ✅ **The close routes he DOES have are unchanged and are the three that already ship:** `Enter` on an empty box (route 4) · the open key pressed while open (route 1) · `SetConsoleEnabled(false)` (route 3). ⚠️ **Route 2 (`CancelPressed()`) loses its button but keeps its API** — see the amendment on route 2 above.

**RULING B — `Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/LlamaCpp/` SATISFIES the third-party-location law. It is not a deviation; it is the correct application of it.**
The law is **`<ModuleRoot>/Source/ThirdParty/<LibName>/`**, where `<ModuleRoot>` is the **project root *or* a plugin root** — it was always a relative rule ("vendored third-party code lives in a `Source/ThirdParty/` subtree, never intermixed with first-party source"), never an absolute path. Three things settle it:
1. **It is Epic's own convention** — engine and marketplace plugins vendor into `<Plugin>/Source/ThirdParty/<Lib>/` and reference it from the plugin's `Build.cs`.
2. **Putting llama.cpp in the GAME module's `Source/ThirdParty/` would defeat the entire reason the plugin exists.** The game module is one UBT unit under per-file single-owner locks; its `PublicIncludePaths` and link settings are global to that unit, so third-party include paths and `/MD`-vs-`/MT` link settings would leak into every file every other lane owns. The plugin boundary is what lets the go/no-go spike run with **zero edits to any locked file.**
3. **Reversibility.** A NO-GO verdict deletes one directory. Vendoring into the game module would leave residue in a file five other tasks touch.
- **Vendored layout, pinned:** `LlamaCpp/include/` (`llama.h`, `ggml.h`, and their transitive headers only) · `LlamaCpp/lib/Win64/*.lib` · `LlamaCpp/bin/Win64/*.dll` · `LlamaCpp/LICENSE` (llama.cpp's MIT text, verbatim) · `LlamaCpp/VERSION.md` (upstream tag/commit + build flags + toolset, so the binaries are reproducible).
- **⚠️ LINK THE C API ONLY** (`llama.h` + `ggml.h`). **Do NOT link llama.cpp's `common/` helpers** — they are C++ with STL in their signatures and are precisely where `/MD` vs `/MT` and toolset-mismatch link failures come from. Cost of not using them: ~60 lines of sampler setup we want to own anyway.
- **`Build.cs` additions to the GAME module: `Json`, `JsonUtilities` — and nothing else. NOT `HTTP`, NOT `Sockets`.** In-process llama.cpp needs neither. A sidecar `llama-server.exe` was **considered and REJECTED**: a Windows Defender firewall prompt on first launch of a shipped game is unacceptable. A future task adding `HTTP`/`Sockets` for this feature is re-opening a closed decision.
- **Backend: Vulkan + CPU. NEVER CUDA.** One binary covers NVIDIA / AMD / Intel — this is Jonathan's no-lock-in ruling made concrete.
  - ⚖️ **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (Jonathan's ruling, at the fine-tune rung) — THE LAW IS SCOPED TO THE ***SHIPPED RUNTIME BACKEND***. CUDA IS PERMITTED ***HOST-SIDE, AT AUTHORING TIME ONLY***.** ⛔ **This is an AMENDMENT, not an exception and not a waiver** — the same repair `§12a(a)` and `§8` received, and for the same reason: **the sentence pinned a MECHANISM ("never CUDA") where it meant a PROPERTY ("no player is locked to one vendor, and no vendor's runtime is redistributed").** A LoRA is trained on the author's machine and **ships as a merged GGUF that contains no CUDA anything**; forbidding CUDA in a gitignored `.venv` protects nobody and would have cost the feature. ⚖️ **A letter that outlived its reason is REPAIRED, never granted an exception, because the next reader obeys the letter.**
  - **THE FOUR BOUNDARIES — all CHECKABLE, and they ARE the amendment. A CUDA use that fails any one of them is still banned by the original law:**
    1. ⛔ **NO CUDA DLL, LIB, HEADER OR TOOLKIT COMPONENT ENTERS `Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/`** — the vendored tree stays exactly as §6 ruling B pins it (`llama.h`/`ggml.h` C API, Vulkan + CPU binaries).
    2. ⛔ **THE SHIPPED ARTIFACT IS A BACKEND-AGNOSTIC `.gguf`** that loads and runs on the NVIDIA card, the Arc iGPU **and** CPU alike. **A model that only runs on one vendor is the lock-in this law exists to prevent, whatever produced it.**
    3. ⛔ **CUDA LIVES ONLY IN A GITIGNORED `.venv`** under `Tools/LlmFinetune/` (the torch-cu128 wheels bundle their own runtime; **no CUDA Toolkit is installed**). It is never referenced by any `Build.cs`, `.uplugin`, `.uproject`, packaging step or runtime path.
    4. ⛔ **`Docs/ThirdPartyNotices.md` GAINS NO CUDA ENTRY — BECAUSE NOTHING CUDA IS REDISTRIBUTED.** ⚠️ **That is the test, not the paperwork: if an honest notices file would need a CUDA row, boundary 1 or 3 has already been breached.**
  - ⚠️ **STATE THE RESIDUAL PLAINLY: a *trained-with-CUDA* model is not a *CUDA-dependent* model, and the only thing that proves it is boundary 2 — LOADING THE SHIPPED GGUF ON A NON-NVIDIA PATH.** That check is a gate deliverable (fine-tune rung §F), not an assumption.

### 7. LICENSING DUTY + REPO HYGIENE (both are shipping blockers, and both are cheap to get wrong)

- **`Docs/ThirdPartyNotices.md` is created by this batch and must carry BOTH:** (i) llama.cpp's **MIT** licence text verbatim, and (ii) **the model's own licence** verbatim, with the exact repo/file/quant it applies to. Missing notices is a shipping defect, not a docs nit.
- **⚠️ THE EXACT GGUF QUANT'S MODEL CARD MUST BE VERIFIED AT CONTENT LOCK — NOT THE FAMILY.** A permissive base does not guarantee a permissive quant re-upload: quantizers re-host under their own terms, and a "Qwen3.5-4B-GGUF" repo is a *different* card from Qwen's. The handoff records the **repo id, the exact filename, the quant, and the licence line quoted verbatim.** "It's Apache because the family is" is not verification.
- **⛔ BANNED MODELS — NAMED, BECAUSE THESE ARE EXACTLY WHAT A NAIVE SEARCH RECOMMENDS.** Every purpose-built small **function-calling** model is non-commercial and **must never be shipped**: **xLAM-2**, **Hammer 2.1**, **Arch-Function** (CC-BY-NC or research-only). Shipping one is a licensing breach.
- **⛔ THE TEACHER-MODEL LAW — JONATHAN'S RULING, 2026-08-03, AT THE FINE-TUNE RUNG. IT IS THE BANNED-MODELS RULE ONE LEVEL UP, AND IT IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME FAILURE SHAPE.**
  > ⛔ **ANY MODEL USED TO *PROPOSE* TRAINING DATA MUST BE AN OPEN PERMISSIVE MODEL (Apache 2.0 / MIT). ⛔ NO COMMERCIAL-API OUTPUT MAY APPEAR ANYWHERE IN THE TRAINING DATA — NOT AS A GOLD, NOT AS A DRAFT, NOT AS A PARAPHRASE, NOT AS A "JUST TO GET STARTED" SEED.**
  - **WHY IT IS A SHIPPING BLOCKER AND NOT A PREFERENCE:** several commercial providers' terms forbid using their outputs to train a competing model, **and this model ships inside a product Jonathan sells.** The banned-models clause above protects the *weights we redistribute*; this clause protects the *data those weights were fitted to* — **and the second one is worse, because it is invisible in the artifact.**
  - ⛔ **CONTAMINATED WEIGHTS ARE NOT REPAIRABLE BY APACHE COMPLIANCE ON THE BASE.** A clean Apache base fine-tuned on tainted rows produces a tainted derivative; the licence on `Qwen/Qwen3-4B` says nothing about it. **The only remedy is re-running the data stage, which is the expensive one — so this is verified BEFORE authoring, never after.**
  - **VERIFICATION, in the §7 idiom — the exact same standard as the quant re-upload rule:** the handoff records **the teacher's repo id, revision sha, and its `license:` line quoted verbatim.** *"It's open because I downloaded it from HF"* is not verification. ⚠️ **And an API endpoint serving an open-weights model is NOT automatically clear — the ENDPOINT's terms are a separate document from the WEIGHTS' licence.** Prefer local weights; if an endpoint is used, quote its terms too.
  - **AND IT REACHES FURTHER THAN THE TEACHER:** the same bar binds any tool that *rewrites, augments, paraphrases or back-translates* a row. **The question is never "did a model write the final answer" — it is "did any commercial-API output touch this file."**
- **✅ PERMISSIVE BASES CLEARED FOR EVALUATION:** **`Qwen/Qwen3-4B-GGUF` → `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf` (Apache 2.0, ungated, publisher = Qwen, own verbatim LICENSE in the GGUF repo) — THE RULED SPIKE MODEL as of 2026-08-02, sha256 verified** · Qwen3.5-4B (Apache 2.0 — **licence-clean but ARCHITECTURALLY UNSUITABLE, see below**) · Phi-4-mini (MIT) · SmolLM3-3B · Granite 4.x.
- **⚠️ `Qwen3.5-4B` IS LICENCE-CLEAN AND STILL WRONG FOR THIS DESIGN — DO NOT RE-PROPOSE IT WITHOUT RE-OPENING §8.** Its `config.json` reads `Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration` (multimodal) with **32 layers of which only 8 are `full_attention` — the other 24 are `linear_attention`** (Gated DeltaNet), vocab 248,320. **Bar #3 IS `llama_memory_seq_rm` prefix reuse, and that mechanism is architecture-dependent**: recurrent layers carry rolling state, not a per-token KV cache, so three quarters of the model cannot reuse a static prefix the way the three-zone layout assumes — and llama.cpp's hybrid layer-type support is still an open upstream discussion. Measuring bar #3 on it would print `DROP=0.0%` beside *"THE PROMPT LAYOUT IS WRONG"*, **blaming §8 for a property of the model class.** `Qwen3-4B` is dense, 36 layers, **no `layer_types` key at all**, uniform full attention — the architecture §8 was actually written against. ⚠️ Also: **Qwen publishes no `-Instruct` variant of Qwen3.5-4B and no official GGUF for it**; the `-Instruct` suffix that reached the board and `DefaultModelFileName` was drafting drift naming **a file that cannot exist**.
- **⚠️ CORRECTION 2026-08-02 — GEMMA IS NOT APACHE 2.0 AND IS NOT CLEARED.** This list previously recorded "Gemma 4 E4B (Apache 2.0, one clause to confirm)". Verified against the artifact: `google/*` GGUF repos carry `license: gemma` **and** `gated: manual`. The Gemma Terms are not Apache, and a manual gate is itself disqualifying for an unattended fetch. **Treat Gemma as UN-CLEARED.** This is the §7 failure mode in its own list — *"it's Apache because I read that somewhere"* is not verification, and the entry sat here for days looking cleared. Also clean and already relevant: **llama.cpp (MIT)**, Whisper + whisper.cpp (MIT), Silero VAD (MIT) — the last two are **not** v1 scope (no voice) and are listed only so a later voice pass does not re-research it.
- **⚠️ `.gitignore` WILL SILENTLY SWALLOW THE VENDORED BINARIES.** The file globally ignores `*.dll`, `*.lib`, `*.a`, `*.so`, `*.exe`, `*.pdb`. Negations are **mandatory**, must be placed **AFTER** those rules (last matching pattern wins), and the parent directories must not themselves be ignored (they are not — only `Plugins/*/Binaries/` and `Plugins/*/Intermediate/` are, and the vendored tree is under `Source/`). Exact block:
  ```gitignore
  # --- SiegeLlama vendored third-party (LLM-ASSISTANT batch) ----------------
  # The global *.dll / *.lib rules above would silently swallow these.
  !Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/LlamaCpp/lib/**/*.lib
  !Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/LlamaCpp/bin/**/*.dll
  # Model weights NEVER enter git (~2.5 GB). Tools/fetch_llm_model.py fetches
  # them into /Models, which is ignored wholesale.
  *.gguf
  /Models/
  ```
- **ACCEPTANCE, NOT ASSERTION:** `git check-ignore -v` on one vendored `.dll`, one vendored `.lib` and one `.gguf` is run and **pasted into the handoff**. The two vendored paths must report the negation line (or no match); the `.gguf` must report the ignore line. "I added the lines" is not the criterion.
- ⛔ **HOW TO READ `git check-ignore -v` — ADDED 2026-08-03 (TASK-499, found live and MISLABELLED ONCE BY ITS OWN AUTHOR BEFORE RE-DERIVATION CAUGHT IT). ⚠️ THE ACCEPTANCE PROCEDURE THIS CLAUSE MANDATES EMITS EVIDENCE A CAREFUL READER WILL MISREAD, WHICH IS §17's SHAPE SITTING INSIDE THE CLAUSE WRITTEN TO PREVENT §17's SHAPE.**
  1. ⛔ **EXIT 0 DOES NOT MEAN "IGNORED". `git check-ignore -v` EXITS 0 WHEN A ***NEGATION*** MATCHED TOO.** ⇒ ⛔ **THE `!` PREFIX IN THE OUTPUT LINE IS THE DISCRIMINATOR, NEVER THE EXIT CODE.** ⚠️ **This is the `Build.bat`-returns-0-on-failure class exactly** — a tool whose success code answers a different question than the one being asked.
  2. ⛔ **A ***TRACKED*** PATH PRINTS NOTHING AT ALL WITHOUT `--no-index`** — ⚠️ **which reads exactly like *"no rule protects this file"*, i.e. the OPPOSITE of the truth.** ⇒ **`--no-index` is MANDATORY in every acceptance paste**, and the handoff states that it was used.
  3. ✅ **THE ONLY SAFE ACCEPTANCE SENTENCE IS THE FULL OUTPUT LINE, QUOTED** — `<file>:<line>:<pattern>\t<path>` — **with the reader told whether the pattern carries `!`.** A verdict word (*"ignored"* / *"not ignored"*) without the line beneath it is an assertion again, which is the one thing this clause forbids.
- ⛔ **AND THE ROOT CAUSE OF THE DEAD-NEGATION DEFECT IS A GIT PATTERN RULE THIS REPO HAD WRITTEN DOWN NOWHERE. ONE CLAUSE CLOSES THE WHOLE CLASS:**
  > ### ⛔ **`dir/*` EXCLUDES THE *CONTENTS* AND A LATER `!dir/keep` WORKS. `/dir/` EXCLUDES THE *DIRECTORY*, GIT NEVER DESCENDS INTO IT, AND ***EVERY*** `!` UNDER IT IS DEAD — SILENTLY.**
  - **THE MEASURED INCIDENT:** the dead `!Models/...` negations sat **13 lines under** a **working** `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/*` + `!.../.gitkeep` pair. ⚖️ **Someone copied a pattern that DEMONSTRABLY WORKED into a directory using the other form — and the two forms look like stylistic variants and are not.** ⚠️ **This is the most dangerous kind of copy: the donor was correct, the copy was correct-looking, and only the FORM differed.**
  - ⇒ **Before writing any `!` negation, check which form the parent rule uses.** If it is `/dir/`, the negation **cannot** work and the honest fix is to **strike it or move the file** — ⛔ **never to leave a dead `!` in place "just in case", because a rule that looks like it works and does not is the defect, not a hedge against it.**
- ✅ **RATIFIED 2026-08-03 — STRIKING THE NEGATIONS BEAT REPAIRING THEM, AND THE ARGUMENT IS BETTER THAN "SIMPLER".** Making `Models/` traversable would have created **a working re-include surface inside the one directory holding the 2.5 GB GGUF**, whose named failure mode is *a future agent adds `!Models/Siegebound-Assistant-*.gguf` because the pattern is right there and looks sanctioned.* ⚖️ **A capability nobody needs, sitting next to the thing it could destroy, is a liability even while unused** — and git history is append-only, so that mistake is permanent. ✅ **Removing the top block's `Models/*` to kill the FALSE AFFORDANCE was the same judgement applied twice.** ⇒ **Model documentation lives in `Docs/`, never `Models/`, and that sentence is now in `.gitignore` itself where the next author will meet it.**
- ✅ **RATIFIED: `Docs/ModelManifest.md` IS ***NOT*** CREATED UNTIL STAGE F.** Build-master declined to create it on the grounds that **a manifest with no artifact to describe would be fabricated provenance** — ⚖️ **exactly right, and it is the §7 licensing instinct applied to our own paperwork: a notices file whose fields are not yet true is worse than an absent one, because it will be read as verified.** The path stays **pinned and reserved**; it is authored when there is something to record.
- **The GGUF is never in `Content/`** either — cooking/packaging a 2.5 GB blob as a uasset-adjacent file is a Wave-2 `RuntimeDependencies` problem, deliberately deferred. **Memory-mapped loading is load-bearing for the 8 GB claim** (the weights are page-cache-backed, not resident) — ⚠️ **the field is `load_mode = LLAMA_LOAD_MODE_MMAP`; this clause named `use_mmap = true` until 2026-08-02 and that member no longer exists. Rename only, requirement unchanged — see §8.**

### 8. PROMPT LAYOUT, THREADING, AND MULTIPLAYER POSTURE (short form — the plan carries the derivations)

- **THREE PROMPT ZONES, AND THE LAYOUT IS LOAD-BEARING.** `ZONE A` **1139 tok — MEASURED — as-built, DELIBERATELY UNBOUNDED** — STATIC (system prompt, schema, place vocabulary, synonym table, 3 few-shots) · `ZONE B 32 tok (MEASURED)` SLOW (castle HP bands, mid owner, gold band) · `ZONE C 320 tok (MEASURED)` FAST (roster, pending-intent line, the utterance); **B + C together carry the ≤ 400-token cap, and as-built they are 352 tok = 88 % OF IT — not the 41 % this clause used to claim.** The `164` was low by **2.1×** and low in the direction that flatters the design; `~600` for Zone A was low by **1.9×**. **All token figures in §8 and §10 are MEASURED as of 2026-08-03 on `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M` — the ruled spike model (§7) — and are valid FOR THAT TOKENIZER ONLY** (see 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE below for why that qualifier is the whole reason this correction had to wait for a model choice). **Fixed key order, every key always emitted** (an empty value prints `none`), everything **quantized into bands**, **no timestamps and no coordinates.** This is what lets **`llama_memory_seq_rm`** (`llama.h:735` — ⚠️ **this clause read `llama_kv_cache_seq_rm` until 2026-08-03, and that symbol does not exist anywhere in the vendored header; see §7's mmap clause, which had already ruled this class**) keep the prefix and turn a **1517**-token prefill into **347** from turn two onward — **MEASURED, `DROP = 77.1 %`, identical on all three tiers and all six bench runs** (the illustration read "~500 → ~150" until 2026-08-03: the mechanism was right, both numbers were low by ~3×, and the *ratio* they implied survived because they were low together — see 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE). Reordering zones or making Zone A state-dependent silently destroys the KV reuse and is a QA FAIL.
  - **Zone A is byte-identical for the life of the process** and changes **only** when `DA_AssistantVocabulary` changes (a content-lock-time change, never a per-request one). Calling the Zone-A builder twice in one process returning different bytes is a defect.
  - **⚖️ THE ≤ 400-TOKEN HARD CAP GOVERNS ZONE B + ZONE C ONLY. ZONE A IS DELIBERATELY UNBOUNDED *IN VARIANCE* — AND *BOUNDED IN SIZE* BY THE CONTEXT (amended 2026-08-03; the word "unbounded" stood alone here and was being read as size — the size bound is the ⚖️ ZONE-A SIZE BOUND clause below and it is TASK-423's to assert). THE DIRECTION IS STRUCTURAL AND STANDS; THE NUMBERS WERE DERIVED AND ARE NOW MEASURED (relabelled 2026-08-02, measured 2026-08-03).** The earlier "~350 tok" guidance for Zone A was a guess and it was **wrong in the dangerous direction**: because Zone A is the *static prefix*, **bar #3's KV-reuse ratio IMPROVES as it grows.** That conclusion follows from the layout alone — it needs no token count and nothing below weakens it. **Zone A shipped long on purpose** — and at **1139 tok** it shipped nearly twice as long as this clause said, which by its own argument makes bar #3 *better*, and the measurement agrees (77.1 %). ⚠️ **The headroom half of that sentence, however, was reassurance produced by counting the wrong unit, and it is corrected here: B + C is 955 of 1440 CHARS (66 % used, 485 spare) and 352 of 400 TOKENS (88 % used, 48 spare).** The same seam, measured in **the unit that actually binds**, has **less than a third** of the slack the char reading advertises. A char count is a *proxy*, and a proxy that reports 34 % free where 12 % is free has not been conservative — it has been consoling. ⚠️ **What does NOT follow are the figures that were attached to it.** `165/765 = a 78 % drop (PASSES)`, `165/515 ≈ 68 % (MARGINAL FAIL)` had Zone A been trimmed to 350, and `Zone B+C = 164 tokens = 41 % of the ≤ 400 budget` were written into this clause as **"measured at TASK-416"**. **They were never measured.** No model was resident on this project until 2026-08-03, so **no tokenizer had ever run on these strings** — every one of those figures was computed from a char count times an assumed chars-per-token ratio. **All of them were relabelled DERIVED — PENDING TASK-413's printed counts, and on 2026-08-03 TASK-413 printed them. EVERY ONE WAS LOW** (🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE, below, now carries the measured table). ⚠️ **A future task that "optimizes" Zone A down to hit a 350 number would still be trading a better KV-reuse ratio for a worse one** — that hazard is structural and is recorded so nobody does it. ⇒ **The size of the gap is no longer unverified, and it is far larger than the derived figures implied: the shipped layout measures 77.1 %, while a Zone A trimmed to 350 tok would leave `(728 − 347) / 728 ≈ 52 %` — not the 68 % "MARGINAL FAIL" this clause feared but well BELOW it** (arithmetic on measured operands — `turn2_prefill = 347` is fixed by Zone B's start, so trimming Zone A shrinks only the denominator — **not itself a measurement, and labelled as such**). What Zone A must *not* do is become state-dependent (that breaks the prefix entirely, which is a different and fatal thing).
  - **Enforcement until a tokenizer is in the loop: Zone B+C ≤ `MaxSnapshotChars` — RULED DOWN TO 1085 chars on 2026-08-03 (= 400 tok × the MEASURED 2.71 chars/token of the region the cap actually governs). The 3.6 that stood here was an ASSUMPTION, and it was OPTIMISTIC on every single measured reading.** The ratio was the assumption every token figure in this section rested on. ⚠️ **The word `conservative` sat on that ratio until 2026-08-02 and has been STRUCK — the label was inverted, and inverted in the UNSAFE direction (ruled 2026-08-02).** Tokens = chars ÷ ratio, so a char cap standing in for a token budget is **conservative only if it assumes the SMALLEST plausible ratio** — a smaller ratio yields a *larger* token count for the same characters. This section separately argues that Zone B/C's symbol-dense text tokenizes **worse than prose** (the `164`-outlier bullet below), i.e. that the true ratio may sit **BELOW** 3.6. **Those two statements cannot both hold.** If the true ratio is below 3.6, **1440 chars admits MORE than 400 tokens and the cap silently OVER-admits** — the opposite of what "conservative" promises a reader deciding whether the budget can be trusted. 3.6 is the middle of the plausible range, not the safe end of it. ⇒ ✅ **AND IT WAS BELOW. MEASURED 2026-08-03: 2.71 across B+C, 2.13 in Zone B, 3.56 whole-prompt. `1440 ÷ 2.71 = 531 tokens` admitted against a 400-token budget — THE CAP OVER-ADMITTED BY ~33 %.** The inverted-label ruling above was reasoned out before any number existed and the number vindicated it exactly; **that is the argument for taking a labelling defect as seriously as an arithmetic one.** As-built **B + C = 955 chars (68 + 887) = 352 tok — BOTH MEASURED**, char headroom 485, **token headroom 48**. ⚖️ **`MaxSnapshotChars` → 1085 (ruled 2026-08-03, §10 edited in the same pass, from measurement and not from a second guess) — CONDITIONAL, and the condition is binding: the ruling holds only while the truncation the tighter cap now performs is OBSERVABLY LOGGED.** A cap that silently degrades the prompt is **worse than a loose one** — WARN-5 already established that this builder's collapse path is silent (the `<`-boundary warning that never fires), so tightening the cap without a log would trade a *latent* over-admission for an *active* silent truncation. **A tighter number and a silent failure is not an improvement; it is the same defect wearing a better constant.** ⇒ ✅ **STATUS 2026-08-03 — THE CONDITION IS LANDED, NOT OUTSTANDING (recorded 2026-08-03; corrects a reading, not a ruling).** The **post-TASK-413 fix pass shipped the observable log IN THE SAME PASS AS THE CAP** — the condition and the constant it rides on landed together — and it closed **WARN-5's `<`-boundary bug** on the way: the old test was *"did the printed count fall short of the cap"*, **false at exactly the cap**, so a >8-kind board logged **nothing, ever**; it now fires on ***"did any kind fail to print in full"***, **names which cause bit**, and **escalates on every deepening** instead of firing once. ⚠️ **It is UNDER QA and is NOT called verified here.** ⇒ **On that basis the interim state IS better than the 1440 it replaced — pending QA, and because the truncation became OBSERVABLE, not because the cap became tighter.** ⚠️ **AND *"OBSERVABLE"* IS NOT *"OBSERVED"* — corrected 2026-08-03 (`qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-8): the log has never emitted a line, because `USiegeAssistantSnapshot` HAS NO CALLER.** **1085 and 1440 are equally inert today**; this whole comparison is an argument about code that will first execute at TASK-423. See §12g. ⚖️ **AND 1085 IS AN *AUTHORITY* VALUE — THE ROLE IS PART OF THE NUMBER (role-labelled 2026-08-03, value unchanged).** It is correct **because** it assumes the **SMALLEST** plausible ratio, which is what "safe" means for a bound that is the only thing standing between the snapshot and the budget. ⛔ **That is exactly why it MUST NOT be carried into TASK-423's PRE-FILTER role, where "safe" INVERTS and means assuming the LARGEST plausible ratio.** `MaxSnapshotChars` is **retired at that commit, not re-pointed** — two-constant ruling and the pre-filter floor in resolution (2) below.
  - **🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE — ✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-03: THE TOKEN COUNTS ARE NOW MEASURED, AND EVERY DERIVED FIGURE WAS LOW (added 2026-08-02, when only the chars were measured; the reasoning is KEPT rather than deleted, because it is what made the correction a single clean pass instead of a third guess).** A figure labelled *measured* that was in fact computed from an assumed ratio is a **silent false success** — the same defect class as `Build.bat` returning exit 0 on a failed build and MCP readback passing on visually-broken UMG (§11's list). This one was sitting **inside the document that governs the lane**, where it gets quoted as evidence, which is why it is stripped of its authority here rather than merely footnoted.
    - **MEASURED — quote these freely: the CHAR counts.** Zone B = **68 chars** and Zone C = **887 chars** for the default `order:` line ⇒ **B + C = 955 chars** — against `MaxSnapshotChars` **1440** as it then stood, headroom 485; against the ruled **1085**, headroom **130**; and **48 tokens either way, which is the figure that actually binds.** Counted by hand from the source strings and independently re-derived at the TASK-412 gate (`qa/TASK-412.md` §5 confirms both as **exact**), and **re-derived a third time at TASK-433 from `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp`'s own format strings — 68 and 887 exactly**, which is what retired the *"the fixture may be transcribing a string the builder does not produce"* suspicion. Chars are countable without a model; **these are real and are unaffected by everything below.**
      ⚠️ **BUT READ THE NOUN: 68 / 887 / 955 ARE THE 13-KIND SPIKE FIXTURE'S ZONES, NOT THE SHIPPED BUILDER'S (qualified 2026-08-03, `qa/TASK-433.md` §2 scope finding).** The fixture deliberately does not honour `MaxRosterKinds = 8` (a declared deviation, and the right call), so on the **same t0 board** the shipped builder emits Zone C **670 ⇒ B + C = 738.** ✅ **The direction of error is SAFE** — the shipped string is *shorter*, so every headroom figure in this section is pessimistic — **and the composition is near-identical, so the 2.71 ratio itself holds.** ⚠️ **It is recorded anyway because it is instance 6's tell firing one section later: *"as-built"* consumed as a different noun than the one measured.** ⇒ **Quote 955 as the FIXTURE's and 738 as the BUILDER's; the 2.71 that sizes the authority constant was measured on the former.**
    - **✅ MEASURED 2026-08-03 — `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`, fixture `t0`, model `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M`. These replace every `DERIVED — PENDING TASK-413` figure in §8 and §10, in the one pass the single-source rule below required.** Both operands of every ratio are printed measurements.

      | zone | chars | tokens | **chars/token** |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | Zone A (static prefix) | 4314 | **1139** | 3.79 |
      | **Zone B** | 68 | **32** | **2.13 ← WORST** |

      | **B + C — the region `MaxSnapshotChars` governs** | 955 | **352** | **2.71** |
      | assembled prompt | 5349 | **1504** | 3.56 |

      ⛔ **STALENESS BANNER — ADDED 2026-08-03 (TASK-500 B3). THIS TABLE IS *PRE-LADDER*. THE ZONE-A ROW AND EVERY ROW DERIVED FROM IT ARE NO LONGER TRUE OF THE SHIPPED PROMPT.**
      - ⚠️ **The ladder wave GREW Zone A: `1139 → 1356 → 1362 tok`, `4314 → 5116 chars`** (§12g's measured table, and FINE-TUNE §11 states `1362 (5116 chars), NOT the pre-ladder 1139`). ⇒ ⛔ **`zoneA 4314/1139`, `assembled 5349/1504` and the `turn1_prompt = 1517` below are ALL SUPERSEDED. Both figures are quotable and both read authoritative; they cannot both describe today's prompt.**
      - ✅ **STILL VALID AND NOT RETRACTED: the Zone B, Zone C and B+C rows** — §12c froze Zone B/C bytes for that wave and the `t0` tripwire (`zoneB_chars = 68`, `zoneC_chars = 887`) still holds. **The 2.71 joint ratio that sizes the authority constant is UNAFFECTED.**
      - ⛔ **NO REPLACEMENT NUMBER IS WRITTEN HERE, AND THE REFUSAL IS THE POINT.** Adding Zone A's growth to the old totals would be **arithmetic on a measured operand presented as a measurement** — ⚠️ **the exact defect this section has now paid for three times** (the `164`, the `265`, the *"corrected estimate"* that was still 1.3× wrong). ⇒ ⚖️ **THE NUMBERS ARE RE-READ FROM `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s PRINTED OUTPUT AT THE NEXT RUN, NOT DERIVED HERE.** **Whoever takes that reading edits this table in one pass, quoting the run.**
      - ⛔ **BINDING ON TASK-498:** it may **NOT** pin `1504` / `1517` as constants. **It reads `turn1_prompt` and the assembled-string count from the SAME run that produces its `ids=1` dump**, and compares the Python tokenizer against *that*. ⚠️ **A token-parity task built on the wrong pair reports parity against the wrong string — and would report it as a PASS.**

      Fixture `t1` (`zoneB_chars=71`, `zoneC_chars=886`) printed **identical token counts and identical zone boundaries** — the layout is stable across snapshots, not tuned to one fixture. Also measured, and recorded because §10 sizes work from it: **the assembled prompt STRING is 1504 tokens** — ⚠️ **but what the CONTEXT holds is `turn1_prompt` = 1517, because BOS/EOS and the chat-template wrapper add ~13 tokens that are NOT inside the 1504 (corrected 2026-08-03, the ninth seam).** ⇒ **Occupancy is 1613 of a REQUESTED `ContextTokens` 2048 (78.8 %) once `MaxOutputTokens` 96 is reserved** — see 📏 THE LIVE HEADROOM FIGURE below, which owns the corrected slack arithmetic. **Quote 1504 for the string and 1517 for the context; they are different measurements of different things and this section conflated them once already.**
    - **⚠️ WHY THIS CORRECTION HAD TO WAIT FOR A MODEL, AND WHY THAT WAS RIGHT: A TOKEN COUNT IS A PROPERTY OF A SPECIFIC TOKENIZER, NOT OF A STRING.** Qwen3.5-4B's vocab is 248,320; Phi-4-mini's, SmolLM3's and Granite's are all different — **there is no model-independent chars/token ratio to measure.** So this clause was never blocked on *a* model; it was blocked on **the model that ships**, and correcting it from a substituted model would have produced a second correction later — precisely what *"never guessed twice"* forbids. TASK-413 part 1 stopped for exactly this reason and **that judgement is upheld here.** ⇒ **These figures are re-measured, not carried, if the shipped model ever changes.**
    - **⚠️ AND THE OUTLIER IS IDENTIFIABLE — IT IS THE `164`.** `qa/TASK-412.md` BLOCKER-1 estimates the shipped Zone B+C at **~265 tok**, reached separately from §8's `164` — and **955 ÷ 3.6 = 265.** So a second author working the same problem landed on the 3.6 ratio, and **`164` is the figure that does not fit.** ⚠️ **State the strength of this honestly: the QA report does not show its arithmetic, and 265 is exactly what 3.6 produces, so it may be the SAME assumption re-applied rather than a genuinely independent one — consistency, not proof.** It is enough to identify `164` as the outlier; it is **not** enough to promote 3.6 to a measurement. The content argues the same way: Zone B/C are **symbol-dense** (underscores, newlines, JSON punctuation, band labels) and such text tokenizes **worse** than prose, so ~3.6 chars/token is unremarkable here while 5.8 would be surprising. **This is a lead, not a licence to substitute 265** — see the single-source rule below. ⇒ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-03, AND THE REFUSAL PAID FOR ITSELF. The true figure is 352.** `164` was indeed the outlier — **but 265 was ALSO WRONG, and wrong in the same optimistic direction**, because the 3.6 both authors reached for is itself optimistic (measured 2.71). **Substituting the "corrected" estimate would have swapped a 2.1× error for a 1.3× one and stamped it FIXED.** The content argument was right for the right reason, and then some: symbol-dense text tokenizes worse than prose, and it tokenizes worse than 3.6 as well.
    - **⛔ → ✅ CONSEQUENCE, AND IT LANDED ON A LIVE DECISION: BAR #3's REFERENCE FIGURE WAS UNVERIFIED — AND ON 2026-08-03 IT WAS CORROBORATED.** Bar #3's reference is `165/765 = 78 %`, and **this very section pins 68 % as a MARGINAL FAIL** — so the entire claim rests on a **10-point margin computed from the suspect figure.** If `164` is wrong, that margin is **not evidence of anything**. ⚠️ **TASK-410's fix loop is being measured against this reference right now** (`qa/TASK-412.md` BLOCKER-1 cites it as the shipped-layout comparison). **Until TASK-413 printed real counts, bar #3's reference was not evidence and no PASS or FAIL could be declared against it. It has now printed them, and the resolution goes THE DOCUMENT'S WAY — recorded here as plainly as the doubt was.** Measured **`DROP = 77.1 %`** (`turn1_prefill=1517 → turn2_prefill=347`) against the reference's `165/765 = 78.4 %`: **corroborated to within 0.9 pp**, identical on all three tiers and all six runs, with the reuse point landing **9 tokens into Zone B** exactly where QA derived it. ⚠️ **State the mechanism, because it is instructive rather than lucky: the reference's OPERANDS were both wrong by ~2× (`~600` vs 1139; `164` vs 352) and the RATIO survived because the error was near-common to numerator and denominator.** A drop percentage is a ratio of the same misestimate, so it was robust to precisely the mistake that was made — **the figure was right, and it was right for a reason its author had not established.** ⇒ **The conclusion §8 drew from it stands, and now stands on a measurement instead of on that arithmetic. A suspicion that resolves in the document's favour is retired as explicitly as one that does not — a cleared doubt left hanging is its own kind of stale label, and it teaches the next reader to discount the warnings that are still live.** ⚖️ **AND THE DISTINCTION IS PRESERVED RATHER THAN QUIETLY BANKED, because banking it is how the next `164` gets written: A NUMBER THAT IS ACCIDENTALLY RIGHT IS NOT EVIDENCE.** `78 %` was corroborated within 0.9 pp **and it was right for a reason its author had not established** — both operands wrong by ~2×, the error near-common to numerator and denominator, so the *ratio* survived a mistake that would have destroyed either operand alone. **The agreement vindicates the conclusion; it does NOT vindicate the method that produced it**, and a later author who reads only the agreement will reach for the same char-times-assumed-ratio arithmetic and will not be lucky twice.
    - **⚖️ ONE correction, from measurement, ONCE. `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s printed token counts (TASK-413) are the SINGLE SOURCE that replaces every figure in this clause** — quoted verbatim, editing §8 and §10 together in one pass. ⛔ **Do NOT substitute a corrected estimate in the meantime, including 265.** Token counts are unknowable until the model is resident (`qa/TASK-412.md` §5 and note 4 both say so), and **a second guess dressed as a fix is exactly how the first one got here.** *"Never guessed twice"* is already the rule one bullet up — this clause is what it meant. ⇒ ✅ **SPENT 2026-08-03.** The one pass happened: §8's zone figures, the prefill illustration, the enforcement bullet and §10's `MaxSnapshotChars` were all edited together from that single printed source. **The allowance is used up — any further change to these figures requires a NEW measurement, not a re-reading of this one.**
    - **What a corrected ratio DOES and DOES NOT retract — and the measurement did BOTH, in opposite directions.** The 5.8 premise said the cap binds ~40 % **EARLY** (silent truncation); 3.6 said it binds exactly right. **Measured 2.71 says neither: it binds LATE**, admitting 531 tok against a 400-token budget. So the *silent-early-truncation* concern is **withdrawn** together with the 5.8 premise — the cap was never truncating early — while **its mirror image, silent OVER-admission, is CONFIRMED**, and that is what the 1085 ruling addresses. ⚠️ **Withdrawing a concern is not the same as clearing a seam: this was the same seam failing in the other direction, and one retraction very nearly took the whole question off the table with it.** ⚠️ **This changes NOTHING about the `MaxRosterKinds = 8` seam recorded in the snapshot bullet below:** the roster tail collapses and the truncation warning fails to fire **at any ratio whatsoever**, because that defect is a `<`-comparison boundary, not an arithmetic one. **A corrected premise must never be allowed to quietly take a valid finding down with it** — retract the argument that depended on it, and only that argument.
    - **⚖️ RESOLUTION — THE RECURRING DEFECT IS THE PROXY, NOT THE CONSTANT, SO THE FIX IS STRUCTURAL (ruled 2026-08-02).** Every round of this seam has had the same shape: **a CHARACTER cap is a proxy for a TOKEN budget, and the proxy's fidelity is an unmeasured guess.** Retuning 1440 to a better ratio leaves that defect class exactly where it is, one measurement later. ⚠️ **THE 1085 RULING MUST BE READ AGAINST THIS SENTENCE, NOT AROUND IT: 1085 IS AN INTERIM TIGHTENING, NOT THE FIX.** What separates it from the defect this clause names is narrow, and stating it is the price of making the ruling at all — it comes from **the shipping tokenizer's own printed output** rather than a better guess; it is **ruled once and the allowance is spent**; and it is **explicitly superseded by (2) below.** A cap derived from a measurement is still a proxy. **If the next round of this seam is another constant, the fix has not landed.** The rule instead, in three parts:
      1. **TASK-413 must PRINT the actual chars/token — and must report the WORST (SMALLEST) observed value across the zones, NOT the average.** An average ratio applied to a cap is the same inversion in a new place: the roster line and the synonym table do not tokenize alike, and **a cap must survive the worst of them**. The printed worst case is the figure that gets quoted; an averaged number is not an answer to this clause. ⇒ ✅ **DONE. The printed worst is 2.13 (Zone B) against a whole-prompt average of 3.56 — the demand was right, and the average would have over-admitted by 31 % on its own.** ⚖️ **THE CAP IS RULED ON 2.71, NOT 2.13, AND THE DEVIATION IS DECLARED RATHER THAN QUIET: `MaxSnapshotChars` is a JOINT cap on B+C, so the ratio that binds it is the JOINT ratio — 2.71 — and Zone B is 7 % of the region it governs (68 of 955 chars). Sizing a joint cap from a component that cannot dominate it is a different error, not a safer one.** ⚠️ **The stricter bound is recorded so nobody re-derives it: `400 × 2.13 ⇒ ~850 chars`, and it becomes the correct number the moment Zone C's composition approaches Zone B's symbol density.** Zone C is the roster and the pending-intent line — a board dense in underscored kind symbols and `other_kinds:` collapse text moves it exactly that way. **The 2.71 is a property of today's content, not a constant: whoever changes Zone C's shape re-measures its ratio.**
      2. **TASK-423's shipped subsystem enforces the TOKEN budget DIRECTLY, by TOKENIZING.** It has `llama_tokenize` resident — which the snapshot builder does not — so it counts tokens rather than estimating them, and **`MaxSnapshotChars` is demoted to a cheap PRE-FILTER that runs BEFORE the tokenizer, never the authority.** That takes the ratio off the critical path entirely and ends the tuning loop permanently. **A subsystem that ships enforcing only the char cap has not implemented this clause.** ⇒ ⚖️ **THE MEASUREMENT PROMOTES THIS FROM "the tidy fix" TO "the reason the cap question never has to be answered again": TASK-423 SUPERSEDES THE 1085 RULING ENTIRELY.** Once the tokenizer enforces 400 tokens directly, the char cap stops being a budget and becomes a **pre-filter** whose only duties are to be cheap and generous-but-finite.

      ⛔ **AND *"ENTIRELY"* IS ITSELF THE DEFECT — THE EIGHTH SEAM OF THIS BATCH (ruled 2026-08-03; found by reading this clause against the ruling it supersedes, MINUTES after the seventh was named in the clause immediately below).**
      > ⚖️ **THE 1085 RULING WAS *CONDITIONAL*. THE CONDITION WAS WRITTEN AS A RIDER ON THE **CAP** RATHER THAN ON **THE ACT OF TRUNCATING** — SO SUPERSEDING THE CONSTANT DISCARDS THE CONDITION ALONG WITH IT.**

      **Stated concretely, because the abstraction hides what it costs:** §8 **nowhere states what the shipped subsystem DOES when B+C exceeds 400 tokens**, nor **that the consequence must be visible.** Every word of the resolution above is about *which bound is authoritative* and *what value it takes*; **not one word is about what happens to the content that does not fit.** ⚠️ **So TASK-423 could ship a correct, authoritative, tokenizer-enforced count with a SILENT consequence — precisely the defect the condition exists to prevent, inherited by its successor with nobody restating it.** The successor is **more** trustworthy on the axis the ruling argued about (it measures instead of estimating) and **no more** trustworthy on the axis the condition guarded — and the second fact is invisible **because** the first one is loud. **A supersession that is a genuine improvement is the most dangerous possible carrier for a dropped precondition.**

      ⇒ ✅ **THE FIX IS TO RE-ANCHOR THE CONDITION ONTO THE ACT, WRITTEN SO THAT RETIRING ANY CONSTANT CANNOT ORPHAN IT:**
      > ⚖️ **THE OBSERVABLE-TRUNCATION CONDITION BINDS *ANY ENFORCEMENT OF THE SNAPSHOT BUDGET, BY ANY MECHANISM, UNDER ANY NAME*** — chars or tokens, pre-filter or authority, `MaxSnapshotChars` or `MaxSnapshotTokens` or `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` or whatever later replaces them. **IF THE SNAPSHOT REACHES THE MODEL SHORTER THAN IT WAS BUILT, SOMETHING SAYS SO.**
      > ⚖️ **QA HOOK (deliberately the same shape as the enforcement hook above): A SUBSYSTEM THAT TRUNCATES THE SNAPSHOT WITHOUT AN OBSERVABLE LOG HAS NOT IMPLEMENTED THIS CLAUSE — REGARDLESS OF WHICH BOUND IT ENFORCED.**

      - **It therefore binds BOTH of TASK-423's cut sites, not one.** The **authority** cut (`MaxSnapshotTokens`) and the **pre-filter** cut (`SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars`). ⚠️ **"The pre-filter should never fire in normal play" is a reason to LOG it, not a reason to skip it** — this section already rules that a pre-filter firing in a real match is a bug report about Zone C's shape, and **an event that is meaningful precisely because it is unexpected is the last one that may be silent.**
      - **It survives the retirement of every name inside it.** That is the entire point of anchoring on the act: **a future commit that retires a constant can no longer take the condition with it.**
      - ⚠️ **AND THE CONDITION IS NOT DISCHARGED BY THE POST-TASK-413 LOG.** That log lives in the **snapshot builder's** collapse path. **TASK-423 adds NEW cut sites in a NEW component**, and this clause binds those too — **inheriting the older log is not implementing it.**

      ⚠️ **BUT NOT BY RE-POINTING THE SAME CONSTANT AT A NEW JOB — WHICH IS WHAT THIS CLAUSE SAID UNTIL 2026-08-03, AND IT IS THE SEVENTH SEAM OF THIS BATCH (ruled 2026-08-03; found by reading this clause against a role it had not yet occupied, not by reading the code).**
      > ⚖️ **THE LAW, WRITTEN GENERALLY BECAUSE IT IS WHAT MAKES THIS NON-OBVIOUS CASE OBVIOUS NEXT TIME:**
      > **A PRE-FILTER THAT CAN REJECT WHAT THE AUTHORITY WOULD ACCEPT IS NOT A PRE-FILTER. IT IS A SECOND, HIDDEN AUTHORITY.**

      **Why the same number cannot hold both jobs — THE SAFE DIRECTION INVERTS WITH THE ROLE, AND ONLY THE ROLE CHANGES.** As an **AUTHORITY** (today: no tokenizer in the snapshot builder, so the char cap is the only bound) safety means assuming the **SMALLEST** plausible ratio — a smaller ratio means the same characters buy *more* tokens — so **1085 is right**, and 850 is the stricter reading of that same duty. As a **PRE-FILTER** (once TASK-423 tokenizes) the duty **inverts**: a pre-filter must **never cut what the real budget would admit**, so it must assume the **LARGEST** plausible ratio. **The same number is correct in one role and wrong in the other, and this clause handed it across that boundary unchanged.** A pre-filter left at the authority's value does not pre-filter — it silently re-imposes the old, tighter, *character-based* verdict **in front of** the tokenizer that existed to end it, and by WARN-5's own finding that truncation is invisible.

      **⇒ 1. TWO NAMED CONSTANTS, NEVER ONE RETUNED VALUE.**
      - `MaxSnapshotChars` **= 1085 is the AUTHORITY value** (§10) — correct exactly until TASK-423 lands.
      - **TASK-423 introduces a SEPARATE constant, `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars`, and RETIRES `MaxSnapshotChars` IN THE SAME COMMIT.** Not re-pointed, not renamed in place, not "kept for compatibility": **after that commit a grep for `MaxSnapshotChars` returns nothing.** ⚠️ **A single name whose safe direction silently flips on somebody else's commit is precisely how this defect got here** — two names put the role at every call site, which is where the next reader is standing.

      **⇒ 2. SIZING THE PRE-FILTER IS ARITHMETIC ON ALREADY-PRINTED OPERANDS. IT DOES NOT SPEND A FRESH MEASUREMENT AND IT DOES NOT RE-OPEN THE SINGLE-SOURCE ALLOWANCE** (both operands are already in the table above: `5349 ÷ 1504 = 3.56`).
      - **FLOOR, HARD: `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` ≥ 1424 chars** (`400 × 3.56`, the **whole-prompt** ratio — the *highest* ratio ever measured on this project's assembled text, and above every B+C reading: 2.71 joint, 2.77 Zone C, 2.13 Zone B). **A value below the floor is a defect, not a tuning choice.**
      - ⚠️ **ROUND GENEROUSLY UPWARD FROM THE FLOOR, BECAUSE THE COSTS ARE WILDLY ASYMMETRIC AND THE SIZING MUST REFLECT THAT RATHER THAN SPLIT THE DIFFERENCE.** Erring **high** costs **one wasted tokenize on pathological input**. Erring **low** **silently truncates real content**, down the path WARN-5 establishes is invisible. ⚖️ **RULED VALUE: `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` = 3000 chars** — `400 × 7.5`, i.e. **~2× the highest ratio ever measured here** (3.79, Zone A), **2.1× the floor**, and **3.1× as-built B+C** (955 chars). **Raising it later needs no argument. Lowering it toward the floor is not an optimisation** — an argument for a tighter bound is an argument about the **authority**, and it belongs to `MaxSnapshotTokens`.
      - **Its only duties, unchanged: CHEAP · FINITE · NEVER BINDING IN NORMAL PLAY.** If the pre-filter ever fires in a real match, that is a bug report about Zone C's shape — **not** a signal to retune the pre-filter.

      **⇒ 3. ⚠️ THE FORECLOSURE SENTENCE IS AMENDED, BECAUSE THAT CLAUSE WAS THE ACTUAL HARM.** It read: *"A task litigating 1085 vs 850 after TASK-423 has landed is litigating a pre-filter."* It was written to stop cargo-cult retuning and **it is right about 1085 vs 850** — both are authority-side values and neither belongs in a pre-filter argument. **But as written it dismissed the entire CATEGORY of post-TASK-423 re-derivation, including the mandatory one.** Replaced by two halves that both bind:
      - ⛔ **FORBIDDEN — re-litigating the AUTHORITY value once it is retired.** After TASK-423, 1085 and 850 are history; a task arguing 1085-vs-850 **about the pre-filter** has confused the two roles this ruling separates.
      - ✅ **REQUIRED — the ROLE-CHANGE re-derivation.** TASK-423 **must** size `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` from the floor above and **declare the role in the same breath as the value.** **Carrying 1085 forward under any name is a QA FAIL.**

      — and TASK-423's spec carries the measured context occupancy (**1517 tok in context — 1504 assembled string + ~13 chat-template wrapper — ⇒ 1613 of 2048 with the output budget reserved; corrected 2026-08-03, the ninth seam**) because the budget is checked against the **actual** context, never the requested one (§10).

  - **⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND — "UNBOUNDED" MEANT VARIANCE AND WAS BEING READ AS SIZE. THE CLIFF NOW HAS A RAIL (ruled 2026-08-03; the sixth seam of this batch, found by re-reading the clause that AUTHORISES Zone A's growth rather than the code that suffers it).**
