    **Unbounded in variance, bounded in size.** Zone A must not vary with world state — that is what earns the KV prefix, it is what the clause above is right about, and **nothing here weakens it.** But *size* is a different property with a different owner: Zone A, Zone B+C and the output budget all draw on **one finite context**, so an unbounded prefix inside a fixed `n_ctx` is a **cliff, not a freedom.** Three documents pointed at that cliff and not one of them put a rail on it: **§9a sanctions widening the place vocabulary**, **§8 tells the next author that growing Zone A *improves* bar #3** (true, and it is an incentive to walk exactly this path), the bound **does not exist** — and the only ceiling anyone could check against is `ContextTokens`, **a number §10 itself forbids trusting** (it is a *request*; the actual is `llama_n_ctx(ctx)`). An authorised growth path, a documented incentive, no bound, and a ceiling that may not be believed.
    - **⚖️ 1. `ContextTokens` DOES NOT CHANGE BEFORE THE RE-RUN. THE CLIFF GETS A GUARDRAIL FIRST; THE CLIFF CAN BE MOVED LATER, ON MEASUREMENT, ON JONATHAN'S CALL.** Raising 2048 is the obvious move and it is **the wrong one to make now**: **bars #1, #3 and #4 have ALREADY PASSED at 2048**, and `n_ctx` drives **KV-cache size and prefill cost** — so changing it would **silently invalidate three passing measurements** to fix a problem that is **not yet biting** (78 % occupied, not 100 %). **The ordering is the ruling: bound first, resize second, and only from a number.** A task that raises `ContextTokens` in the same pass as the re-run has destroyed the comparison the re-run exists to make.
    - **⚖️ 2. THE BOUND IS A STARTUP BUDGET ASSERTION, IT IS TASK-423's, AND IT IS MANDATORY — NOT ADVISORY.** ⛔ **TIMING CORRECTED 2026-08-03 — *"on subsystem init"* WAS IMPOSSIBLE, AND THE WRONG WORDING IS STRUCK RATHER THAN DELETED.** ~~On subsystem init, **after the context exists**~~ ⇒ ✅ **AT `SetStaticPrefix` — i.e. THE MOMENT THE ASSEMBLED ZONE-A STRING FIRST CROSSES INTO THE PLUGIN — and after the context exists.** **Zone A is built in the GAME lane (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp`, `BuildZoneA`), on the far side of the plugin boundary**, and §8's own lane split forbids the plugin from including any game-lane header. ⇒ ⛔ **At subsystem init the plugin does not HAVE a Zone-A string to tokenize; the assertion could only have run against a baked constant, which `:757` forbids BY NAME.** **The two clauses could not both be honoured, and the one that had to give was the TIMING.**
      - ✅ **EVERY SUBSTANTIVE REQUIREMENT SURVIVES UNCHANGED — this is a one-word move, not a weakening:** tokenized from the **built** string, **never** a recorded figure, every operand read **live**, computed against **`llama_n_ctx(ctx)`** and never the requested 2048, failing **loudly** and **naming which operand overflowed.** ⚠️ **The only thing that moved is WHEN, and it moved to the earliest moment the check is POSSIBLE — which is also the earliest moment it is MEANINGFUL, since a Zone A that has not arrived cannot overflow anything.**
      - 📌 **AND THE PROVENANCE MATTERS BECAUSE IT IS THE FOURTH OF ITS KIND THIS WAVE (§15): QA DERIVED THIS FROM THE ARTIFACTS RATHER THAN ACCEPTING THE IMPLEMENTER'S CLAIM** — it located Zone A's builder, confirmed the plugin boundary holds (**all six `Siegebound`/`SiegeAssistant` occurrences in the subsystem files are inside COMMENTS** — no game-lane type, no game-lane include), and only then ruled the spec text wrong. ⚖️ **Same diagnosis as the `Super::RebuildWidget()` defect: a correct requirement paired with an ORDERING I asserted without executing anything.** §15's manager duty exists precisely for this clause's failure mode.
      Computed against the **actual** `llama_n_ctx(ctx)` and **never the requested 2048** (§10's own WARN-2 — the requested value is precisely the one that lies):
      ```
      ZoneA_tokens + MaxSnapshotTokens + MaxOutputTokens + safety_margin  ≤  n_ctx_actual
      ```
      ⛔ **`ZoneA_tokens` IS TOKENIZED AT LOAD FROM THE ASSEMBLED ZONE-A STRING — NEVER THE LITERAL `1139`, NEVER ANY RECORDED FIGURE (ruled 2026-08-03; the EIGHTH SEAM WEARING DIFFERENT CLOTHES, and found in the same reading).** Zone A is byte-identical for the life of the process, so it is measurable **exactly once, at init, at zero recurring cost** — **there is no efficiency argument for the constant.** The argument against it is fatal: **§9a explicitly sanctions widening the place vocabulary, and widening it GROWS ZONE A — that is the one growth path this guardrail exists to catch.** A `ZoneA_tokens` implemented as `1139` is an operand that **cannot grow**, so the assertion **passes forever**, and passes most confidently at the exact moment a vocabulary pass pushes Zone A past the ceiling.
      > ⚖️ **A GUARDRAIL THAT CANNOT OBSERVE THE THING IT GUARDS IS WORSE THAN NONE, BECAUSE IT REPORTS SAFE.**

      **This is the eighth's shape in a second costume: A CHECK THAT SURVIVES IN *FORM* WHILE LOSING THE *PROPERTY* THAT MADE IT A CHECK.** The form is fully intact — the assertion is present, the operands are named, it fails loudly, it reads exactly as this clause demands — and the single thing that made it a *check* (an operand that tracks reality) is gone, **with nothing about the code looking wrong to a reviewer.** ⇒ **EVERY OPERAND OF THIS ASSERTION IS READ FROM THE LIVE SYSTEM AT INIT:** `ZoneA_tokens` from `llama_tokenize` on the **built** Zone-A string, `n_ctx_actual` from **`llama_n_ctx(ctx)`** (§10 — never the requested 2048). `MaxSnapshotTokens` / `MaxOutputTokens` / `safety_margin` are **budgets** and are correctly constants. ⚖️ **THE LINE THAT DECIDES WHICH IS WHICH: A BUDGET MAY BE A CONSTANT. A MEASUREMENT MAY NOT.** Any operand of a guardrail that describes *the world as it currently is* is measured at the moment the guardrail runs, or the guardrail is decorative.

      **It must fail LOUDLY at load, naming the measured operands and WHICH ONE OVERFLOWED** — never at the first sentence of a match, and never as a silent truncation. **Both failure shapes it prevents are this batch's signature presentation, and both point the investigator at the wrong component:** a `llama_decode` rejection reads as ***"inference failed"*** (the model looks broken while the `.ini` value that caused it looks perfectly correct), and a silent truncation reads as ***"the model is bad"*** (diffuse accuracy loss with no cause attached — §9c's shape exactly). **A subsystem that ships without this assertion has not implemented this clause**, on the same footing as the direct token-budget enforcement in the resolution bullet above. Two naming pins so the assertion and the enforcement read the same symbols: **`MaxSnapshotTokens`** is the name of the ≤ 400-token B+C budget once TASK-423 makes it a real constant (value already law, §10), and **`safety_margin` is NAMED AND NON-ZERO** — its value is TASK-423's to choose and declare, but zero is not acceptable, because the measured **1504 is the assembled prompt string** and BOS/EOS and any chat-template wrapper are **not inside that number.**
      > ⚖️ **AND THE FLOOR IS MEASURED — ~13 TOKENS — SO `safety_margin` STOPS BEING A JUDGEMENT CALL (recorded 2026-08-03 as the payoff half of the NINTH SEAM; ARITHMETIC ON ALREADY-PRINTED OPERANDS — it spends no fresh measurement and does not re-open the single-source allowance).** The printed log carries the wrapper directly: **`turn1_prompt = 1517` against `assembled_total = 1504` ⇒ ~13 wrapper tokens**, of which **~8 sit AHEAD of Zone A** (`zoneB_start ≈ 1147` against Zone A's `1139`) and **~5 trail.** ⚠️ ***"Zero is not acceptable"* is an instruction with NO NUMBER IN IT, and an instruction with no number is how a placeholder becomes a shipped guess** — while the number was sitting **four lines away in this same section**, already printed by the same run this clause quotes for everything else.
      > ⚠️ **TWO CONDITIONS MAKE THE FLOOR HONEST, AND BOTH TRAVEL WITH THE NUMBER WHEREVER IT IS QUOTED:**
      > 1. **IT IS TEMPLATE- AND MODEL-SPECIFIC.** 13 is the wrapper of **this chat template encoded by `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M`'s tokenizer** — a wrapper is a property of the template **and** of the tokenizer that encodes it, which is 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE's own argument applied to the wrapper instead of the zones. ⇒ **If EITHER the template or the model changes, this is RE-MEASURED, never carried.**
      > 2. ⛔ **IT IS A FLOOR, NOT A VALUE — `safety_margin` IS SET ABOVE IT WITH MARGIN, NEVER AT IT.** A margin sized **exactly** to the measured wrapper **has no margin in it**: it covers the one thing that has been measured and nothing else — a template revision, a differing BOS policy, a tokenizer edge on the first snapshot token. **Setting `safety_margin = 13` satisfies the arithmetic and defeats the purpose.** ⚠️ **This is the SEVENTH seam's error in a new costume — treating the tightest value that clears the known case as the safest one** — and it is barred here for exactly the reason it was barred there: the tight value is correct only against the case you happened to measure, and a margin's whole job is the case you did not.
    - **📏 THE LIVE HEADROOM FIGURE — RECORDED SO THE NEXT AUTHOR SEES THE ROOM THEY ARE SPENDING. ⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-03 (THE NINTH SEAM): EVERY SLACK FIGURE BELOW WAS OVERSTATED BY ~13 TOKENS, BECAUSE IT COMPUTED CONTEXT OCCUPANCY FROM `1504` — THE ASSEMBLED *STRING* — WHILE THE CLAUSE ONE STEP ABOVE ALREADY SAID THE WRAPPER IS NOT INSIDE THAT NUMBER.**
      **As-built occupancy: `turn1_prompt` = 1517 tok in the context (MEASURED) — the assembled string is 1504 and the chat-template wrapper adds ~13 — ⇒ 1613 of 2048 = 78.8 % once `MaxOutputTokens` 96 is reserved.** **Worst case the caps admit today: Zone A 1139 (measured) + B+C at its 400-tok cap + 96 + ~13 wrapper = 1648 ⇒ ~400 tokens of slack** against a zone nothing currently bounds. ⚠️ **At the old 1440-char cap the same arithmetic was 1139 + 531 + 96 + 13 = 1779 ⇒ ~269** — i.e. **lowering `MaxSnapshotChars` to 1085 BOUGHT ~131 tokens of context headroom as a side effect** (that delta is unaffected by the correction — the wrapper is common to both sides), a real benefit that was **not among that ruling's reasons** and is recorded here so it is not spent unknowingly. ⚠️ **400/269/531 and the 78.8 % are ARITHMETIC ON MEASURED OPERANDS, not measurements** — labelled, per this section's own standard. **The superseded figures were 413 / 282 / 78 %; they are struck rather than deleted, because the size of the error is the least interesting thing about it.**
      > ⚖️ **WHAT MADE THIS ONE WORTH RECORDING IS THE ADJACENCY, NOT THE MAGNITUDE.** ~13 tokens on a 2048 context changes no decision. But the two clauses are **one step apart**: the `safety_margin` pin **names the boundary explicitly** (*"BOS/EOS and any chat-template wrapper are not inside that number"*) and this bullet **computes straight across it**, labelling **both operands MEASURED** — which they are, of a quantity that is not the one the sentence needs. ⚠️ **A boundary that the neighbouring clause has already named is the LAST place a carry should survive, and it survived anyway** — that is the finding, and it is why the class heading below now counts six.
      > ⚖️ **AND THE FIRST CANDIDATE OF THIS ROUND DIED, WHICH IS RECORDED DELIBERATELY BESIDE THE ONE THAT LIVED.** A **phantom seam derived from arithmetic on quoted figures was proposed and killed by reading the log.** The suspicion was that the reuse point (*"9 tokens into Zone B"*) could not reconcile with `DROP = 77.1 %` given Zone A = 1139; the derivation produced two candidate seam positions and neither matched the printed 9. ⇒ **Checked against the printed artifact instead of ruled from the document, and it reconciled exactly** (`zoneB_start ≈ 1147`, `reused = 1156`, `1503 − 1156 = 347`). ⚠️ **§9c's law earned its keep against its own author — which is the strongest form of evidence a law of that shape can get** — and it inoculates the next reader against the specific temptation this section creates: **§8 is dense enough that plausible contradictions can be DERIVED from it faster than they can be CHECKED.** A derived contradiction is a hypothesis, and the printed log is what settles it.
  - **🔁 NAMED DEFECT CLASS — THE UNCHECKED CARRY. §8 HAS NOW PRODUCED IT SIX TIMES IN ONE SECTION, SO THE CLASS IS NAMED RATHER THAN THE INSTANCES FIXED (named 2026-08-03 as THE UNIT SUBSTITUTION across two instances; GENERALISED 2026-08-03 when the third and fourth turned out not to be about units at all — ⚠️ NAMING THE CLASS IS WORTH MORE THAN THE SIX FIXES).**
    ⚠️ **AND THE FIFTH ARRIVED MINUTES AFTER THE CLASS WAS NAMED — IN THE VERY CLAUSE THAT RECORDED THE FOURTH; THE SIXTH ARRIVED MINUTES AFTER THAT, OUT OF VERIFYING AN UNRELATED SUSPICION. THAT TIMING IS ITSELF THE FINDING: THE CLASS IS *GENERATIVE*, NOT EXHAUSTED.** A named class is a lens, not a tally — **naming it did not close the list, it made the next instance findable in minutes.** ⇒ **Nobody may read the count in this heading as a completion state.** The correct posture toward §8 is that **this class is still producing**, and the next instance is expected rather than surprising. ⚖️ **THAT PROPERTY IS NOW A FINDING ABOUT THE SECTION RATHER THAN ABOUT ANY CLAUSE IN IT — see 🛑 THE STOPPING RULE below, which is what this section does with that fact instead of continuing to mine it.**
    > ⚖️ **THE INVARIANT — ALL SIX ARE THIS ONE SENTENCE: A QUANTITY WAS CARRIED ACROSS A BOUNDARY — OF UNIT, OF ROLE, OF AUTHORSHIP, OR OF SCOPE — AND ITS MEANING CHANGED WHILE ITS NAME AND ITS VALUE DID NOT.**
    > **Sub-law (UNIT boundary — instances 1 & 2):** A budget is checked in the unit that BINDS it. A proxy unit may inform a decision; it may never conclude one — and the conversion between the two units is an assumption until it is measured.
    > **Sub-law (AUTHORSHIP boundary — instance 3):** A number that is accidentally right is not evidence. An artifact compared to another artifact of the same authorship cannot detect an error they share.
    > **Sub-law (ROLE boundary — instance 4):** A pre-filter that can reject what the authority would accept is not a pre-filter. It is a second, hidden authority.
    > **Sub-law (ROLE boundary, THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION — instance 5):** **A CONDITION ATTACHED TO A VALUE TRAVELS NO FURTHER THAN THAT VALUE'S NAME.** Attach a condition to **the act it governs**, never to the constant that happens to perform it — otherwise the next supersession discards it silently **while looking like an upgrade.**
    > **Sub-law (SCOPE boundary — instance 6):** **`MEASURED` IS A CLAIM ABOUT PROVENANCE, NOT ABOUT COMPLETENESS.** A number can be honestly measured and still be **the wrong number for the sentence**, because it measures a **narrower thing** than the sentence needs. ⇒ **State what a measured quantity EXCLUDES beside the value, and treat an adjacent clause that names an exclusion as binding on every clause that consumes the number.**
    - **Instance 1 — the chars/token inversion** (already recorded above): a **character** cap standing in for a **token** budget, wearing the label `conservative`, which was inverted *in the unsafe direction*. Measured 2.71 against an assumed 3.6 ⇒ the cap **over-admitted by ~33 %**.
    - **Instance 2 — "comfortable headroom", found 2026-08-03.** It was **true in chars** (955/1440 = **66 %** used) and **false in tokens** (352/400 = **88 %** used). The section reasoned about a *token* budget using a *character* measurement and reported **34 % free where 12 % was free**. Same seam, same document, same class, opposite artefact — **reassurance manufactured by counting the wrong unit.**
    - **Instance 3 — the figure that was accidentally right (AUTHORSHIP boundary), recorded 2026-08-03.** Bar #3's `165/765 = 78 %` was **corroborated to within 0.9 pp** by measurement — with **both operands wrong by ~2×** (`~600` vs 1139; `164` vs 352). The *ratio* survived because the error was near-common to numerator and denominator, and the estimate reached a second author (`qa/TASK-412.md`'s 265) who re-applied the **same** assumed 3.6 ratio and produced agreement that looked independent and was not. **The value crossed from author to author unchanged, and the confidence attached to it grew at every crossing while nothing was ever verified.** ⚠️ **The agreement vindicates the conclusion; it does NOT vindicate the method** — a later author who reads only the agreement reaches for the same char-times-assumed-ratio arithmetic and will not be lucky twice.
    - **Instance 4 — the bound whose safe direction depends on a role nobody restated when the role changed (ROLE boundary), ruled 2026-08-03.** `MaxSnapshotChars` **1085** is correct as an **authority** (assume the smallest plausible ratio) and wrong as a **pre-filter** (must assume the largest), and the resolution clause above handed it across that boundary **with the same name and the same value**, describing the new job in one sentence while changing nothing about the number that would do it. **Nobody re-derived it because nothing about it appeared to change** — which is the class's signature, not an oversight. Full ruling + the two-constant fix: resolution (2) above.
    - **Instance 5 — the CONDITION that failed to cross the boundary at all (ROLE boundary, opposite direction), ruled 2026-08-03.** The 1085 ruling was **conditional** on the truncation being observably logged; resolution (2) says TASK-423 *"supersedes the 1085 ruling **entirely**"*, and because the condition was written as a rider on **the cap** rather than on **the act of truncating**, **retiring the constant discards the condition with it.** §8 nowhere says what the shipped subsystem does when B+C exceeds 400 tokens, nor that the consequence must be visible — so **the successor could ship an authoritative token count with a silent cut: exactly the defect the condition exists to prevent, inherited with nobody restating it.** Full ruling + the re-anchoring: resolution (2) above.
      ⚖️ **THE PAIR IS THE POINT, AND IT IS WHY BOTH ARE FILED UNDER ONE BOUNDARY: INSTANCE 4 — A *VALUE* CROSSED THE ROLE BOUNDARY UNCHANGED. INSTANCE 5 — A *CONDITION* FAILED TO CROSS IT AT ALL. SAME BOUNDARY, OPPOSITE DIRECTION.** Together they say the boundary must be audited **in both directions**: *what came across that should have been re-derived*, and *what did not come across that should have.* **Checking only the first is what produced the second, one clause later.**
      - **Same shape, second costume — the `ZoneA_tokens` operand** (recorded in ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND above): a startup assertion implemented with the **literal 1139** keeps every visible property of a check — present, loud, operands named — while losing the only one that mattered, an operand that can move. **A check that survives in FORM while losing the PROPERTY that made it a check**, and, like instance 5, it looks correct precisely because nothing about it appears to have changed.
    - **Instance 6 — the measured number that measured a NARROWER THING than the sentence needed (SCOPE boundary), ruled 2026-08-03 — the NINTH SEAM of this batch.** 📏 THE LIVE HEADROOM FIGURE computed context occupancy from **1504, the assembled prompt STRING**, and labelled **both operands MEASURED** — which they honestly were. **The context does not hold the string; it holds the string plus BOS/EOS plus the chat-template wrapper**, ~13 tokens the `safety_margin` pin **one clause above already named as excluded.** ⇒ **The 413 / 282 slack figures were each overstated by ~13 tok; corrected to ~400 / ~269, and occupancy to 1613 of 2048 (78.8 %).** ⚠️ **The magnitude is trivial and the position is not: the exclusion was written down, in this section, one step away, and the carry crossed it anyway** — so this instance is **not** an argument for better labelling (the label was correct) but for the sub-law above: **`MEASURED` speaks to where a number came from, never to whether it is the whole of what the sentence is about.** ⚖️ **AND THE PAYOFF OUTRANKS THE CORRECTION** — the same printed operands turn `safety_margin`'s *"zero is not acceptable"* into a **measured ~13-token floor** (⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND, the `safety_margin` pin). **An instruction with no number is how a placeholder becomes a shipped guess; finding the seam is what produced the number that closes it.**
    - **The tells, so this is recognisable next time — one per boundary:**
      - **UNIT: the sentence's CLAIM and the sentence's EVIDENCE carry different units.** Whenever a figure crosses units — chars↔tokens, ms↔frames, bytes↔actors, cm↔units — **state both units explicitly and name the conversion as an assumption**, or do not draw the conclusion. This class is why §8's numbers are now written `955 chars = 352 tok` with **both** units on **both** sides.
      - **AUTHORSHIP: two sources agree and neither shows its arithmetic.** Ask what they *share* before treating agreement as confirmation.
      - **SCOPE: a number is labelled `MEASURED` and the sentence using it names a DIFFERENT thing than the measurement did** — *"the assembled string"* consumed as *"what the context holds"*, *"as-built"* consumed as *"worst case"*, *"this fixture"* consumed as *"any board"*. ⚠️ **The tell is textual, not numerical: read the noun the measurement attaches to, then read the noun the sentence needs, and check they are the same noun.** ⚖️ **And when a neighbouring clause states an exclusion, that exclusion binds every clause that consumes the number — proximity is not a substitute for it having been applied.**
      - **📌 STANDING GAP — §8 IS NOW WELL-AUDITED IN ONE DIMENSION AND THE CODE IMPLEMENTING IT IS NOT AUDITED AT ALL (recorded 2026-08-03, and it is the most important line in this ledger). ⇒ ✅ HALF-DISCHARGED THE SAME DAY: THE *READING* AUDIT LANDED (TASK-433, below); THE *EXECUTION* AUDIT IS STILL OWED AND IS TASK-423's. The original reasoning is KEPT rather than deleted, because the gap being findable at all is what produced the audit.** ⚠️ **ALL NINE SEAMS IN THIS BATCH WERE FOUND BY READING THIS DOCUMENT.** Every finding has been about what §8 **says**; **none has been about what `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp` DOES.** The code path has never been read against these clauses — so **agreement between the document and the code has never been checked in the direction that matters**, and this section's growing precision is **not evidence about the shipped builder.** ⚖️ **THIS IS §9c's SAME-AUTHORSHIP LAW APPLIED ONE LEVEL UP: THE DOCUMENT HAS BEEN REVIEWED AGAINST ITSELF.** §9c already ruled that comparing an artifact to another artifact of the same authorship cannot detect an error they **share**; **re-reading a clause against its neighbouring clause is that same comparison with the two artifacts collapsed into one** — the most tightly shared authorship there is. ⇒ **A DOC-VS-CODE AUDIT IS OWED.** ⛔ **It is deliberately NOT specced here** — it is Wave 1 work, the spike is mid-run, and it routes after Jonathan's verdict. **Recorded so the audit's absence stays visible instead of being mistaken for its completion, which is precisely what nine well-written findings would otherwise imply.**
        ⇒ ✅ **THE AUDIT LANDED 2026-08-03 — TASK-433, `qa/TASK-433.md` — AND IT WAS READ-LEVEL, WHICH IS THE RESULT AS MUCH AS THE FINDINGS ARE.** Twelve §8 clauses enumerated against the shipped builder: **AGREES 7 · DISAGREES 3 · NOT IMPLEMENTED 1 · UNTESTABLE BY READING 1**, plus **2 BLOCKERs in code that §8 does not describe** (both in `SanitizeForPrompt`, both **silent** truncations — the utterance and the `pending:` line), 8 WARN, and **6 candidate defects derived and 5 killed against the artifact.** ✅ **The highest-value result was a POSITIVE one and it is the one to quote:** §9c's same-authorship law was pointed at §8's most-quoted operands — *the 68/887 figures come from a spike fixture that TRANSCRIBES the builder, so they may be measurements of a string the builder does not produce* — and the reviewer **hand-counted both zones from `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp`'s own format strings and got exactly 68 and exactly 887.** ⇒ **The transcription is byte-faithful and the operands under 1085 are sound**, reported as loudly as a defect would have been.
        ⛔ **AND THE SECOND AUDIT IS OWED, BECAUSE THIS ONE COULD NOT RUN ANYTHING.** §9c is explicit that **reading is the floor, not the ceiling**, and the audit's own §5 says where the road ended: Zone-A byte-identity across two calls, the truncation log's actual output, every token figure, and **the whole `Capture` → assemble → infer path** are all argued-from-construction and **never executed — because the class has no caller** (§12g). **TASK-423 owes the first observation**, and the parser is named: **a PIE run with `LogSiegeAssistant Verbose` on a >8-kind board.** ⚠️ **"It has now been read" must never be re-told as "it has now been checked."**
      - **⚠️ CITE THE SYMBOL. A LINE NUMBER IS A COURTESY THAT ROTS — AND IT ROTS SELECTIVELY, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KNOWING (ruled 2026-08-03 from `qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-4).** Measured across §8/§9c's citations: **7 of 7 GAME-LANE `file:line` anchors DO NOT LAND on the code they name; 3 of 3 VENDORED-HEADER anchors (`llama.h`) are EXACT**, as is `Castle.h:190`. **The anchors that rotted are precisely the files this batch edits; the ones that held are frozen.** ⇒ **Read every citation in this section with that prior: a `SiegeAssistant*.cpp` anchor is untrusted by default, an `llama.h` anchor is trustworthy.** ⚠️ **A stale anchor beats a missing one only in the sense that it is worse: in a heavily-commented file it lands on prose that reads plausibly** — `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h:186` lands *inside a comment about the very cap it was cited for*, and a hurried reader would accept it and move on.
        ⚖️ **AND THE PROOF THAT THE FIX IS THE LAW AND NOT THE OFFSETS: TASK-433's OWN CORRECTED ANCHORS WERE STALE THE SAME DAY.** It read `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp` at **963 lines with `BuildZoneA` at `:538-681`**; hours later the loop-2 gate read the same function at **`:595-1041`** on the landed ladder delta. **Re-anchoring this section to those figures would have bought a few hours of accuracy and re-taught the exact habit that produced the defect.** ⇒ ⛔ **NO BULK RE-ANCHORING PASS. Every citation from here names the SYMBOL — `MaxRosterKinds`, `AppendRosterBlock`, `SanitizeForPrompt`, `PlaceVocabulary[]` — and a line number may ride along as a same-day convenience, NEVER as the identifier.**
      - **🛑 THE STOPPING RULE — TEXT-AUDIT PASSES OVER §8 ARE ENDED, AND FURTHER TEXT FINDINGS ARE *BATCHED* INTO THE OWED DOC-VS-CODE AUDIT RATHER THAN DISPATCHED INDIVIDUALLY (ruled 2026-08-03, after the ninth).** Nine instances, **each found faster than the last**, several within **minutes** of naming a class. ⚖️ **THE TWO REASONS, BOTH WORTH WRITING DOWN:**
        1. ⚠️ **THE MARGINAL TEXT FINDING HAS GONE SMALL WHILE THE UNAUDITED SURFACE HAS NOT.** The ninth is worth **~13 tokens**; the **code path is entirely unmeasured.** Every one of the nine is about what §8 **SAYS** — **not one is about what `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp` DOES.** Continuing to spend passes on the cheaper surface because it is the one already in hand is a **reallocation error**, and naming it is the point of this rule.
        2. ⚖️ **CONTINUING TO READ THE DOCUMENT AGAINST ITSELF IS PRECISELY THE SAME-AUTHORSHIP FAILURE §9c NAMES** — now with **nine data points showing it yields diminishing returns.** §9c already rules that comparing an artifact to another artifact of the same authorship cannot detect an error they share; **this section has now demonstrated both halves of that law on itself — the method kept finding real defects, and the defects it kept finding were the ones the method is capable of seeing.**
        - ⚠️ **FRAME THE STOP HONESTLY — THIS IS A REALLOCATION, NOT A COMPLETION. §8 IS NOT CLEAN. IT IS AUDITED IN ONE DIMENSION, AND THE NEXT FINDING IS EXPECTED.** ⛔ **Nobody reading this ledger later may mistake *"we stopped looking"* for *"there is nothing left."*** The class heading above says the class is **generative**; this rule does **not** retire that statement, it decides **what to do about it.**
        - **THE MECHANIC: a §8 text finding noticed from here on is WRITTEN INTO THIS LEDGER AS A BATCHED ITEM FOR THE DOC-VS-CODE AUDIT — not escalated, not ruled on individually, not made the subject of its own pass.** It is carried into that audit's scope so it is checked **against the code**, which is the dimension that has never been read. ⚖️ **A finding that is recorded and deferred is still a finding; a pass spent re-reading prose that could have been spent reading `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp` is not.**
        - **📥 BATCHED FOR THE DOC-VS-CODE AUDIT — the list, kept here so the rule has somewhere to put things (EMPTY at ruling time, 2026-08-03, and an empty list is the expected state on day one, NOT a clean bill).** Append one line per deferred finding: **the clause, what looks wrong in the text, and the code symbol that would settle it.** ⚠️ **A finding appended here is NOT ruled and NOT dismissed — it is queued**, and the audit that opens this list owes a verdict on every line in it.
          - 📌 **STATUS 2026-08-03: TASK-433 opened this list and found it EMPTY — verdicts owed 0, verdicts delivered 0.** ⚠️ **The audit refused to report that as a clean bill, correctly: it means the stopping rule had not yet had to catch anything, NOT that §8's text is exhausted.** The entry below is the first, and it was found while writing this pass rather than by hunting — which is what the "generative" heading predicted.
          - **§9a's context-budget re-check bullet + §10's `ContextTokens` clause — THE NINTH SEAM'S CORRECTION NEVER LEFT §8 (queued 2026-08-03, manager).** §9a still reads *"today's slack is **~413 tokens**"* and §10 still reads *"the feature occupies **1600** of a REQUESTED 2048 — **78 %**"*, while 📏 THE LIVE HEADROOM FIGURE **struck 413 / 282 / 78 %** on the same day and replaced them with **~400 / ~269 / 78.8 % (1613 of 2048)**. ⚠️ **Same ~13-token wrapper carry, surviving in the two clauses that CONSUME the figure after being fixed in the one that OWNS it** — so if it holds it is the SCOPE sub-law crossing a **SECTION** boundary rather than a unit boundary, i.e. **instance 7 of the named class, not two typos.** ⛔ **NOT RULED HERE — queued, per the stopping rule, precisely because arguing it from the text is the move that rule exists to stop.** **What settles it:** the printed `SPIKE_TOKENS … assembled_total_tok=` line beside `turn1_prompt` from `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt` (the tripwire in `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` prints both) — one re-run says whether 1504-vs-1517 is still the live pair, and the correction is then **one pass over all three sites, not three passes.**
      - **ROLE: the value did not change, so nobody re-checked it — ⚠️ ASK INSTEAD WHAT CHANGED AROUND IT.** A constant handed to a new enforcer, a new caller, a new layer or a new owner has been **re-derived or it has not**, and "it's the same number" is the answer that hides which. ⚖️ **THE GENERAL DUTY: WHEN A QUANTITY CROSSES A BOUNDARY, RE-STATE ITS MEANING ON THE FAR SIDE — even when, especially when, the number itself is unchanged.**
      - **ROLE, THE MIRROR TELL (instance 5): a thing is RETIRED or SUPERSEDED — ⚠️ ASK WHAT WAS *ATTACHED* TO IT.** A supersession reliably inventories what the old thing **did**; it almost never inventories what the old thing **carried**. **Conditions, caveats, preconditions and "this holds only while…" riders are what die quietly at a supersession**, because the replacement is better on the axis that was argued and nobody re-checks the axis nobody argued. ⚖️ **THE DUTY: A SUPERSESSION IS NOT COMPLETE UNTIL EVERY RIDER ON THE SUPERSEDED THING HAS BEEN RE-HOMED OR EXPLICITLY DISCHARGED.** "Entirely" is the word to distrust — it is a claim about scope written by someone thinking about capability.
- **The snapshot is NOT a world dump — it is the set of things the player can legally name.** Roster **aggregated by CardID and group, never per-unit**; the **fixed named-place vocabulary PINNED CHARACTER-FOR-CHARACTER IN §9a** (`enemy_castle` · `own_castle` · `mid` · `ancient_ground_near` · `ancient_ground_far` · `nearest_mine` · `hero`) whose resolved `FVector`s stay game-side; quantized match facts.
  - **`ancient_ground_near` / `_far` are derived, not stored:** under the 180°-rotational symmetry law there are **exactly two** ancient grounds and they are rotational twins, so `near` = `FindNearestAncientGround(World, OwnCastleLocation)` and `far` = `FindNearestAncientGround(World, EnemyCastleLocation)`. Exact by construction, no extra state.
  - **The castle locations come from the SHIPPED helper, never a fresh search: `ACastle::FindNearestCastleForTeam(World, Team, From)`** (pinned in the FOLLOW section's §7 registry, `Castle.h:190`). It already skips DESTROYED castles, so `own_castle` / `enemy_castle` / the ancient-ground derivation all inherit that for free. **Reimplementing a castle `TActorIterator` in the assistant lane is a QA FAIL** — one search, one owner.
  - **⚠️ "AGGREGATED" UNDERSTATES IT: `MaxRosterKinds = 8` COLLAPSES THE ROSTER TAIL WHILE THE GRAMMAR STAYS UNCAPPED — SO BAR #5's ACCURACY NUMBER IS CONDITIONAL (recorded 2026-08-02 from `qa/TASK-412.md` WARN-5; found by TASK-410, the seam is TASK-416's to close).** `BuildZoneC` prints at most **8** kinds (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h:186`) and collapses the remainder into one **`other_kinds: <n> kinds, <n> units`** line — **counts only, no symbols** (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp:775-789`) — while `USiegeAssistantGrammar::Build` generates its `kind` alternatives from the **uncapped** `GetUnitKinds()` (`SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp:250-262`). On a **>8-kind board the sampler may legally emit a symbol Zone C never printed**, and a kind the player can legally command may have no symbol in front of the model at all. **The collapse is silent to the developer:** the one-shot truncation warning (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp:873`) tests `KindsToPrint < FMath::Min(Num, MaxRosterKinds)`, which is **false at exactly the cap** — dropping 5 of 13 kinds logs **nothing**. ⇒ ✅ **THE SILENCE IS FIXED — landed in the post-TASK-413 pass, UNDER QA, NOT YET VERIFIED, AND NOT YET EXECUTED (2026-08-03).** ⚠️ **The class has no caller, so the predicate has never run** — though it was traced by reading and **WOULD** fire on the sealed fixture's own 13-kind t0 board (8 printed, 5 collapsed, cause bit `"the MaxRosterKinds cap"`, Warning on the first sentence): `qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-8, and §12g. The predicate is no longer a comparison against the cap but ***"did any kind fail to print in full"***; it **names which cause bit** (kind cap vs. the snapshot budget) and it **escalates on every deepening** rather than firing once. ⚠️ **Read the REST of this bullet as still live:** the log makes the collapse *visible to the developer* — it does nothing about the **Zone-C ↔ grammar divergence** the collapse creates, so **bar #5's "valid only while ≤ 8 kinds are alive" precondition stands entirely unchanged, and still travels attached to the number.** **A defect that has become observable has not become absent** — that distinction is exactly what this batch keeps having to relearn in the other direction. It is not silent to the *model* (the `other_kinds:` line says something was hidden), so it presents as an unnecessary `Clarify` or a substituted unit, never as an error.
    **⇒ THE MEASURED BAR #5 IS VALID ONLY WHILE ≤ 8 KINDS ARE ALIVE.** The spike deliberately prints all **13** fixture kinds and does not honour the cap (a declared deviation, `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp:318`, and the right call — honouring it would have converted six *model* results into six *harness* artefacts). **Shipped accuracy on a >8-kind board will therefore be WORSE than measured.** ⚠️ **This condition travels ATTACHED TO THE NUMBER** — wherever bar #5 is quoted (headline, checkpoint, handoff, Slack, `Docs/`) the condition is quoted with it. A measured result whose precondition gets dropped in the second re-telling is how a measurement becomes a false promise.
- **THREADING/LIFETIME:** `USiegeLlamaSubsystem : UGameInstanceSubsystem` (survives level travel, so Play Again never reloads 2.5 GB — the `USiegeSessionSubsystem` precedent) · **one dedicated `FRunnable` at `TPri_BelowNormal`** (`llama_context` is not thread-safe; a long-lived runnable makes single-thread ownership structural rather than a comment) · **queue depth 1, enforced by the FSM** (input disabled while thinking — this deletes the out-of-order/stale-context bug class entirely) · **cancellation via `llama_context_params::abort_callback`** — necessary, and the only thing that can interrupt a long prefill at all (polling after generation is far too late), but **NOT sufficient on GPU: see the footnote below** · three timeouts: **soft 4 s** (UX only, keeps waiting), **hard 10 s** (abort — a promise on CPU, a *request* on GPU), **token budget 96** · **the completion delegate fires on the GAME THREAD**, always.
  - **⚠️ `abort_callback` IS DOCUMENTED CPU-ONLY, SO THE 10 s DEADLINE IS NOT A GPU GUARANTEE — CORRECTED 2026-08-02 FROM THE VENDORED HEADER (`llama.h:382-385`, confirmed at the TASK-412 gate, `qa/TASK-412.md` §9).** The header says it in as many words: *"Abort callback / if it returns true, execution of `llama_decode()` will be aborted / **currently works only with CPU execution**"*. On the **CPU-only tier** it does bound a run mid-decode, which is what this section always assumed. On the **full-offload and partial tiers it cannot interrupt work already submitted to the device** — the hard deadline can only fire **between** graph submissions, so a submission in flight runs to completion whatever the callback returns. ⇒ **On GPU the token budget (`MaxOutputTokens` 96) is the primary bound on run length and the deadline is the coarse backstop — the reverse of how §10 reads.** `abort_callback` stays mandatory (still free, still the only prefill-length lever on CPU), but *"the only correct mechanism"* was **wrong as written: necessary, never sufficient.** ⚠️ **A later task that lowers or drops `MaxOutputTokens` because "the 10 s abort covers us" is deleting the GPU tiers' only real bound.** Whoever measures the GPU tiers reports the **worst observed delay between abort request and worker exit**, not the deadline value — an unmeasured deadline is an assumption, and this one is header-documented as false on two of the three tiers.
  - **⚠️ `use_mmap` NO LONGER EXISTS IN THE VENDORED BUILD — THE FIELD MOVED, THE REQUIREMENT DID NOT (b10235; `llama.h:306-322` + `:205-211`, confirmed at the TASK-412 gate, `qa/TASK-412.md` §9).** `struct llama_model_params` has **no `use_mmap` member**; mmap-backed loading is selected by **`ModelParams.load_mode = LLAMA_LOAD_MODE_MMAP`** (`enum llama_load_mode`, value 1), which is what the spike already sets. **Read this as a rename, NOT a relaxation:** mmap loading remains **load-bearing for the modal-8 GB-card claim** (§7) — the weights stay page-cache-backed rather than resident — so a build that leaves `load_mode` at the default has silently dropped the assumption bar #4 is measured against, and it will do so **without a compile error**, which is why it is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered. ⚠️ **TASK-423 is specced against this section:** `use_mmap` is the name a search will fail to find, and **"the field is gone" must never be read as "the requirement is gone."**
  - ⚠️ **AND THE CLASS RECURRED INSIDE THE RULING'S OWN SECTION, WHICH IS WHY IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED QUIETLY (2026-08-03, `qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-3).** §8 named **both** spellings of the KV-reuse symbol **four lines apart** — `llama_memory_seq_rm` (correct, `llama.h:735`) and `llama_kv_cache_seq_rm`, **a symbol a grep of the whole vendored header cannot find** (the eight `seq_rm` hits are all `llama_memory_seq_*`). §8's copy is fixed. ⛔ **THREE SHIPPED GAME-LANE COMMENTS STILL NAME THE DEAD SYMBOL** — `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h`, `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp` (inside `BuildZoneA`'s body) and `SiegeAssistantVocabulary.h` — with **no runtime consequence, because the game lane never links llama**, which is precisely why no compile will ever catch them and why they survived. **The plugin lane is right in both places** (`SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` calls `llama_memory_seq_rm` and sets `load_mode = LLAMA_LOAD_MODE_MMAP`). ⇒ **A comment-only correction is owed on the next touch of those three files**, and it is worth doing on schedule rather than opportunistically: **TASK-423 is the first task that will grep for this mechanism, i.e. the first reader guaranteed to be misled by it.**
- **A GGML CRASH MUST NOT KILL THE GAME.** SEH around the eval, a **`bAssistantFaulted` session latch**, and a model-load failure that **never blocks match start**.
- **MULTIPLAYER — V1 IS HOST/STANDALONE ONLY, FORCED, NOT CHOSEN.** Group state is not replicated, and `SetUnitCommand` / `BeginGroupPick` / `PlayHandSlot` / `DiscardHandSlot` all carry the D5 observer lockout. **The assistant must never be more capable than the keys:** it refuses on `!HasAuthority()` with the same approved wording. The console still opens and says so — silence would read as a bug.
  - **⛔ DO NOT WRITE A `ServerExecuteAssistantCommand` RPC IN V1.** It would be a fifth lockout bypass baking in a shape M8 P2 will restructure.
  - **M8 DECLARATION DUTY, and every header and handoff in this batch must state it verbatim: "adds no replicated property, no new replicated class, no new relevancy tier."** "Tier not declared" is a QA FAIL, and "there is nothing to declare" only counts when it is stated.
  - **🚩 `Capture(World, ETeamId::Red)` RETURNS CORRECT TOTALS BUT ALL-ZERO ORDERABLE/FOLLOWABLE COLUMNS — FLAGGED, NOT FIXED, AND M8 P2 MUST NOT INHERIT THIS AS A SURPRISE (found at TASK-416, 2026-08-02).** Both shipped eligibility predicates — `IsFollowCommandEligible()` and `IsGroupCommandEligible()` — **hardcode `Team == ETeamId::Blue`**. So the snapshot's `ETeamId` parameter **implies a generality the columns do not have.** This is **correct for v1** (host/standalone, Blue is the only commanding player) and it was deliberately **NOT "fixed"**, because fixing it in the assistant lane would mean **reimplementing predicates that are banned from reimplementation** — the Cleric-follows-but-cannot-hold split has exactly one owner. **The parameter is kept** (removing it would bake the Blue assumption into the signature, which is worse). ⚠️ **When M8 P2 makes Red a real commanding player, the fix belongs in the SHIPPED predicates, not here** — and every caller then works unchanged.
- **EXECUTOR SEAM (Wave 1, named here so no implementer invents it):** `Attack`/`Defend` are **latched army-wide stances with no selection** — "send 10 footmen at the castle" is **not** the Attack stance. So selection-bearing verbs (`send`/`guard`/`ambush`) stay separate from army-wide ones (`charge`/`fallback`): `send`/`guard` → `CreateUnitGroup(Hold, …)` · `ambush` → `CreateUnitGroup(Ambush, …)` · `follow` → `EnrollInDefaultFollowGroup(Unit)` (already public, already idempotent) · `charge`/`fallback` → `SetUnitCommand(Attack/Defend)` · `rally` → `AHeroCharacter::Rally()`. Selector resolution filters on the **shipping** eligibility predicates `IsFollowCommandEligible()` / `IsGroupCommandEligible()` — **never reimplemented** (the Cleric-follows-but-cannot-hold split is already law) — sorts **nearest to the TARGET, not to the hero**, and **on shortfall returns it, never truncates silently.**

### 9. PINNED CROSS-TASK SIGNATURE REGISTRY (the contract that makes the parallel tasks link)
⚠️ **UBT compiles the whole module.** Every task in this batch compiles against this list **character-for-character**; a task that "improves" a pinned signature breaks the link and is an **automatic QA FAIL**. Access levels are part of the pin. **Several of these tasks CANNOT COMPILE ALONE and are not expected to** — they compile together at the batch's integration compile (the TASK-395/396 precedent). That is designed, not a defect; do not open a QA loop over it.

```cpp
// ── Plugins/SiegeLlama — module SiegeLlama ────────────────────────────────
// SiegeLlamaSubsystem.h
DECLARE_LOG_CATEGORY_EXTERN(LogSiegeLlama, Log, All);
DECLARE_DELEGATE_ThreeParams(FSiegeLlamaCompletionSignature, bool /*bSuccess*/, const FString& /*Output*/, const FString& /*Error*/);

UCLASS()
class SIEGELLAMA_API USiegeLlamaSubsystem : public UGameInstanceSubsystem
{
public:
    bool IsReady() const;                       // model loaded, worker alive, not faulted
    bool IsBusy() const;                        // a request is in flight (queue depth 1)
    // Returns false immediately when !IsReady() or IsBusy(). OnComplete ALWAYS fires on the GAME THREAD.
    bool RequestCompletion(const FString& Prompt, const FString& Grammar, const FSiegeLlamaCompletionSignature& OnComplete);
    void CancelActiveRequest();                 // routes through llama abort_callback
};

// ── Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound — game lane ─────────────────────
// SiegeAssistantCommand.h  (pure-data header; the UnitCommand.h / TeamId.h precedent)
DECLARE_LOG_CATEGORY_EXTERN(LogSiegeAssistant, Log, All);

UENUM()
enum class ESiegeAssistantIntent : uint8 { None, Send, Guard, Ambush, Follow, Charge, Fallback, Rally };

// ⚠️ THE SELECTION IS MULTI-KIND. CORRECTED 2026-08-02 (manager) — the first pin
// carried a SINGULAR `FName Kind`, which could not express the feature's own
// flagship sentence, "send 10 footmen WITH A SORCERER to the nearest ancient
// ground". The approved plan is internally inconsistent here: its GBNF sketch
// shows `kind` as a RULE (singular by nature) but its go/no-go bar #5 scores
// exact-match on "{intent, KINDS, COUNTS, where}" — plural. Plural is the intent;
// the sketch simply never drew the top-level object. Found while specifying the
// evaluation corpus's expected outputs, which is exactly what that task is for.
static constexpr int32 SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds = 3;

USTRUCT()
struct FSiegeAssistantCommand      // uint8 / int32 / FName ONLY — M8 P2 takes this as-is over the wire
{
    ESiegeAssistantIntent Intent = ESiegeAssistantIntent::None;
    // INDEX-ALIGNED PARALLEL ARRAYS, never an array of structs — that is what keeps
    // the payload to FName/int32 only and preserves the "P2 takes it as-is" property
    // (~30 bytes at the cap). Kinds.Num() == Counts.Num() is an invariant the parser
    // enforces; a mismatch is a parse failure, never a silent truncation.
    TArray<FName> Kinds;                 // canonical unit symbols, e.g. "footman"; HARD CAP SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds
    TArray<int32> Counts;                // 0 == "all"; grammar range is 1..30 (NOT the live max)
    FName Where           = NAME_None;   // canonical place symbol
    FName TriggerKind     = NAME_None;   // deferred-intent trigger; NAME_None == "now"
    int32 TriggerAtLeast  = 0;
};

// SiegeAssistantCommand.h — free function, NOT a member
bool ParseSiegeAssistantCommand(const FString& Json, FSiegeAssistantCommand& OutCommand, FString& OutError);

// SiegeAssistantVocabulary.h
USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
struct FSiegeAssistantSynonym { FName Canonical = NAME_None; TArray<FString> Aliases; };

UCLASS(BlueprintType)
class USiegeAssistantVocabulary : public UDataAsset
{
public:
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> UnitSynonyms;
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> PlaceSynonyms;
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, Category = "Siegebound|Assistant") TArray<FSiegeAssistantSynonym> IntentSynonyms;
    FString BuildSynonymTable() const;   // the Zone-A synonym block, deterministic ordering
};

// SiegeAssistantGrammar.h — PURE + DETERMINISTIC: no UWorld, no engine state, no UObject inputs.
// This is deliberate — it is what makes automation tests cheap and what decouples A4 from A3.
// Emits a SELECTION LIST of 1..SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds {kind, count} pairs
// (see the FSiegeAssistantCommand correction above). The cap is IN THE GRAMMAR, not
// only in the parser: an unbounded repetition rule is precisely what a small model
// rambles into, and a bounded alternation costs nothing.
UCLASS()
class USiegeAssistantGrammar : public UObject
{
public:
    static FString Build(const TArray<FName>& UnitKinds, const TArray<FName>& PlaceNames);
};

// SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h
USTRUCT()
struct FSiegeAssistantRosterEntry { FName Kind = NAME_None; int32 Count = 0; int32 GroupId = INDEX_NONE; };

UCLASS()
class USiegeAssistantSnapshot : public UObject
{
public:
    void Capture(UWorld* World, ETeamId Team);                       // six TActorIterator passes, per SENTENCE, never per tick
    FString BuildZoneA(const USiegeAssistantVocabulary* Vocabulary) const;   // STATIC — byte-identical for the life of the process
    FString BuildZoneB() const;                                      // SLOW  — quantized match facts
    FString BuildZoneC(const FString& Utterance, const FString& PendingLine) const;  // FAST
    const TArray<FSiegeAssistantRosterEntry>& GetRoster() const;
    const TArray<FName>& GetPlaceNames() const;
    const TArray<FName>& GetUnitKinds() const;
    bool ResolvePlace(FName Place, FVector& OutLocation) const;      // resolved FVectors stay GAME-SIDE, never in the prompt
};

// AncientGround.h — AAncientGround, public static (the AGoldNode::FindBestMineFor idiom)
static AAncientGround* FindNearestAncientGround(UWorld* World, const FVector& From);
```

#### 9a. ⚖️ THE v1 PLACE VOCABULARY — PINNED CHARACTER-FOR-CHARACTER (added 2026-08-02)
**Nothing pinned a symbol for the mine, so TASK-426's corpus author had to choose one and flagged it rather than assuming.** They were right to flag: **a mismatch here fails the go/no-go for a NAMING reason rather than a MODEL reason, which is the worst way to fail a gate** — it reads as "the model is bad" when the model was fine. The seven symbols, exact spelling, lowercase, snake_case:

```
enemy_castle · own_castle · mid · ancient_ground_near · ancient_ground_far · nearest_mine · hero
```

- **⚠️ THIS LIST PINS THE *SET* AND THE *SPELLING*. IT DOES NOT PIN THE *ORDER* — AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING SOMEWHERE ELSE. `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp`'s `PlaceVocabulary[]` IS THE ORDER OF RECORD (added 2026-08-03, `qa/TASK-433.md` WARN-6).** The code states its own contract in as many words: Zone A prints in `PlaceVocabulary[]`'s order, `GetPlaceNames()` returns in that order, and TASK-417's grammar generates its `where` alternation from that array — so **reordering rewrites Zone A and throws away every cached prefix**, which §8 calls a QA FAIL. **The listing above is in a DIFFERENT sequence from the array** (it opens `enemy_castle`; the array opens `own_castle`). ⛔ **Anyone who "conforms" the code to this listing destroys the KV prefix, and no log line names the cause.** ⚠️ **It has already been misread once, on disk:** `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` says *"the seven pinned place symbols, **in the pinned order** (CONVENTIONS section 9a)"* and then prints the **code's** order — it is correct, and it is correct **by having read the code instead of this section.** ✅ **All seven spellings match character-for-character, so the pin itself is intact** — only the sequence was never this document's to give, and saying so is cheaper than the one reader who takes it literally.
- **⚠️ IT IS `own_castle`, NOT `my_castle` — AND THE STORY IS THE LESSON, SO IT IS RECORDED.** A `my_castle` spelling circulated briefly in an orchestrator message as a **transcription error** and was **retracted by the orchestrator the same day**. It was caught twice independently, both times the same way: **by verifying against the artifact on disk instead of trusting the message.** TASK-416's agent checked the corpus files and shipped `own_castle`; the manager checked and overruled the pin. `my_castle` **appears nowhere on disk.** The sealed corpus asserts `own_castle` in BOTH files — `DEV-16` and, decisively, **`HOLD-06`, which is in the holdout set that scores bar #5** — and under §11 the holdout is **frozen**, so a rename would either void the accuracy number or fail the gate for a **naming reason rather than a model reason.** `own_castle` is also what §5/§8 already said *before* the corpus was authored. **Standing lesson: a pinned symbol is verified against the file that asserts it, never against the message that quotes it.** `own_castle` stands; do not "modernize" it.
- **`nearest_mine`** — adopted from TASK-426 verbatim; it matches the shipped `AGoldNode::FindBestMineFor` idiom and the near/far derivation style. Asserted by `DEV-23` and `HOLD-12`.
- **`hero`** — pinned for completeness (Follow and Rally both anchor on the hero). ⚠️ **NO corpus row asserts it**, so it is *not* exercised by the go/no-go gate; it is forward-looking vocabulary, not measured coverage. Recorded so nobody reads its presence as tested.
- **This list is CLOSED for v1.** Widening it is a later pass **with its own accuracy re-measurement** — adding a place silently changes Zone A, which invalidates both the KV-prefix claim and the measured accuracy.
  - ⚖️ **AND, ADDED 2026-08-03, THAT LATER PASS ALSO OWES A CONTEXT-BUDGET RE-CHECK — THIS SENTENCE IS THE SANCTIONED DOOR INTO §8's ZONE-A SIZE BOUND, WHICH IS WHY THE DUTY IS WRITTEN HERE AND NOT ONLY THERE.** Widening the vocabulary **grows Zone A**, Zone A is bounded in size by `n_ctx_actual` (§8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND), and today's slack is **~413 tokens** — finite, and this is the clause most likely to spend it. So a vocabulary-widening pass re-runs **`Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt` for the new Zone-A token count** and re-checks the startup budget assertion **before** it is considered done. ⚠️ **An accuracy re-measurement alone would not catch an overflow** — it would catch its *symptom*, mis-attributed to the model, which is exactly the failure the size bound exists to prevent.

#### 9c. ⚠️ THE ZONE-A ↔ GRAMMAR SEAM — THE PROMPT'S FEW-SHOTS AND THE GBNF ARE ONE CONTRACT, AND NOTHING ENFORCES IT (added 2026-08-02)
**This is a SILENT-FAILURE seam, and silent-failure seams are what have cost this project most.** The few-shot examples in Zone A teach the model a JSON shape; the GBNF *forces* a JSON shape. **If they disagree, constrained decoding fights the few-shots on every token and accuracy collapses for a reason NO LOG LINE NAMES** — the model looks stupid, the grammar looks fine, and the go/no-go bar fails for a cause nobody can see. Today the entire tie between them is a code comment.

**How we know: TASK-416 wrote Zone A from the approved plan's GBNF sketch, then read TASK-417's LANDED files and found the sketch wrong in four ways.** The plan's sketch is **not** the schema — the landed grammar is. The four, recorded so nobody re-derives them from the sketch:
| Sketch said | Landed truth |
|---|---|
| selection key `select` | **`who`** |
| per-pair count key `count` | **`n`** — `count` is the GBNF *rule* name, never a JSON key |
| army-wide verb emits `[]` | **`"who":"none"`** |
| (absent) | a **`{"ask":ASK}`** question branch the few-shots must teach |

- **THE LAW:** the JSON key names are **owned by the landed grammar (TASK-417) and by nothing else.** Zone A's few-shots, the parser, and any documentation **conform to it** — a disagreement is resolved by changing the prompt, never by changing the grammar to match a doc.
- **⚖️ QA CRITERION, EXPLICIT, AT TASK-412 AND TASK-419: every few-shot example in Zone A must be VALIDATED AGAINST THE LANDED GBNF** — parse each few-shot's JSON with the same grammar/parser the runtime uses and show it accepts. **"It looks like the schema" is not evidence.** A few-shot the grammar would reject is a **BLOCKER**, not a warning.
- **The plan's GBNF sketch is superseded on sight by the landed grammar.** If a spec, a handoff or this document quotes a key name that the landed grammar does not emit, the landed grammar wins and the quote is a defect to fix.
- **⚠️ THE SAME SEAM ONE LEVEL DOWN — THE SHAPE IS PINNED, THE SYMBOLS ARE NOT: ZONE A TEACHES THREE UNIT KINDS THE GRAMMAR MAY FORBID (added 2026-08-02, `qa/TASK-412.md` NIT-4).** Everything above governs the *shape* — which keys exist. The **values** have the identical failure and, until now, no owner: the landed `BuildZoneA` names **`footman` / `sorcerer` / `archer` unconditionally** (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp:666-669`), while the landed grammar generates its `kind` alternatives **from the live roster** (`SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp:250-262`, whose own comment is *"every alternative here is a unit that actually exists right now; anything else is unreachable for the sampler"*). **On a board where any one of those three kinds is absent, Zone A demonstrates — in its only two command few-shots — a symbol the sampler physically forbids.**
  - **What makes it dangerous is an absence, not an event: THERE IS NO LOG LINE.** Nothing throws, nothing refuses, nothing warns, and the JSON that does come back is well-formed. The model is pulled toward a token constrained decoding silently declines to allow, so the cost arrives as **diffuse accuracy loss with no cause attached** — spread across rows that have nothing to do with the missing kind, and read as *"the model is bad"*. That is the §9c failure shape exactly, which is why it is recorded here rather than as a footnote elsewhere.
  - **⚠️ THE SPIKE CANNOT SEE IT — THE FIXTURE MASKS IT.** TASK-410's bench fields **13 kinds including all three**, so every few-shot symbol is always present in the generated grammar. **No measurement in this batch can surface this, and a green bar #5 is NOT evidence that this seam is closed.**
  - **The obvious fix is BARRED.** Generating the few-shots from the live roster would make **Zone A state-dependent** — §8 calls that a defect and it destroys the KV prefix outright, a strictly worse failure than the one being repaired. The close therefore has to come from the other side: either the demonstrated symbols are guaranteed admissible, or the few-shots demonstrate with kinds that cannot be absent. **Whoever owns the fix picks; whoever "fixes" it by making Zone A dynamic has broken the larger law.**
  - **🔍 THE SIGNATURE SHARED BY ALL THREE SEAMS RECORDED IN THIS BATCH** — this one, §8's `MaxRosterKinds` collapse, and §10's requested-vs-actual context — **is: no crash, no error, no log line, and in two of the three a number that still looks fine.** That defect class has dominated this batch, and every instance was found by reading the landed artifact rather than the document describing it.

- **⛔ A DUMP IS NOT A PARSE — AND THIS SECTION'S OWN VERIFICATION METHOD IS WHAT MISSED IT (added 2026-08-03; `handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` PART 2 §13).**
  > **String-comparing two generators can never prove either is valid — only the target parser can.** Any grammar shipped to llama.cpp must be **PARSED BY llama.cpp** before it is believed. Mirroring, diffing and by-derivation review are **necessary and NOT SUFFICIENT**.

  `llama_sampler_init_grammar` returned **NULL on every generation of all six bench runs.** The cause is one character: **`at_least` is not a legal GBNF rule name.** llama.cpp reads a rule name as **letters, digits and hyphens only — an underscore TERMINATES the name** — so the parser takes `at`, demands `::=`, finds `_least`, and rejects the entire grammar (`parse: error parsing grammar: expecting ::= at _least ::= "1" | "2" | ...`). llama.cpp's own grammars are kebab-case throughout. ⚠️ **The defect is in the AUTHORITY this section names — `SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp:312` (the rule) and `:392` (the reference) — not merely in the spike's mirror.** The rename is `at_least` → **`at-least`**, rule identifier only.
  - **THE JSON KEY `"at_least"` IS CORRECT AND STAYS.** It lives inside a **string terminal** (`"\"at_least\":"`), where the rule-name charset does not apply. It is the wire format, and TASK-426's **sealed** corpus asserts it — renaming the key to "match" would be a corpus breach dressed as a fix. **Rule names and JSON keys are different alphabets; that is the whole trap.**
  - **THE BUILDER NOW CARRIES A VALIDATOR THAT REJECTS AN ILLEGAL RULE NAME AT CONSTRUCTION.** The class is closed in the authority rather than the instance fixed once — a future rule named with an underscore fails loudly where it is written, not silently where it is loaded.
  - **⚠️ WHY THIS BELONGS IN §9c AND NOWHERE ELSE: §9c exists to police this seam, and its verification method until now was *diff the two generators*. QA did exactly that, correctly, and reported them identical rule-for-rule. THEY ARE IDENTICAL — THEY ARE IDENTICALLY UNPARSEABLE.** Part 1 of the same spike diffed the runtime dump against the landed generator and blessed the seam as holding at runtime. **Two of the most careful reviews of this batch both passed a grammar that had never once loaded.** A method that compares an artifact against another artifact of the same authorship **cannot detect an error they share** — and a shared error is the likely one, because they were written from the same head.
  - **THE FAILURE WAS SILENT IN EXACTLY THE SHAPE THIS BATCH KEEPS PRODUCING — AND NOTE PRECISELY WHAT IT COST.** A NULL sampler is not a refusal: generation **continues, unconstrained**. So bars #2 (latency for ~60 *constrained* tokens) and #5 (accuracy) were **NOT MEASURED — not measured wrongly.** In-engine with the grammar dead, **every warm CPU iteration emitted `<think>` prose and burned the whole budget to the 10 s abort** (206–480 output tokens, zero JSON, every tier); with the fixed grammar the same prompt at `--temp 0` returned `{"intent":"fallback","who":"none","where":"mid","when":"now"}`. ⇒ **The grammar is LOAD-BEARING, not a safety net:** it structurally forbids `<think>` from token 0 (both `root` alternatives open with a `{` literal). **The only reason this run is reportable at all is that the harness logged the NULL at `Error` level and refused to score against it.**
  - **⚖️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, from here on: any task that authors or edits the GBNF pastes a PARSE into its handoff** — a clean load by the **vendored build** (b10235, the same binaries the plugin links; `llama-completion.exe` offline or a non-NULL `llama_sampler_init_grammar` at runtime). **A dump is not evidence. A diff is not evidence. "It matches the generator" is not evidence.** A grammar that parses under some *other* llama.cpp release is not evidence either.
  - **⚖️ AND IT REFINES THE SIGNATURE ONE BULLET ABOVE — PROMOTED 2026-08-03 TO GENERAL LAW. THIS IS NOT COMMENTARY ON ONE GRAMMAR.**
    > **A method that compares an artifact to another artifact of the SAME AUTHORSHIP cannot detect an error they SHARE — and a shared error is the LIKELY one, because both were written from the same head.**
    > **Reading the artifact is the FLOOR, not the ceiling. Where an artifact has a PARSER, THE PARSER IS THE REVIEWER OF RECORD.**

    The three seams recorded one bullet above were each found **by reading the landed artifact** instead of the document describing it. **This one could not have been, and that is the whole lesson: THE LANDED ARTIFACT READ CORRECTLY.** It was well-formed, self-consistent, and matched every document describing it — so **re-reading could never have found it**, however careful the reader and however many readers there were. Two of this batch's most careful reviews both passed it. **It took EXECUTION.**
    - **The law binds every artifact on this project that has a runnable consumer, not just GBNF:** GBNF → llama.cpp · the eval CSVs → TASK-410's runner · `.uplugin`/`.uproject`/`.ini` → their loaders · `.gitignore` → **`git check-ignore -v`** (§7's *"ACCEPTANCE, NOT ASSERTION"* is this same law already written down for one file — it is the pattern being generalised, not a separate rule) · Python → import/compile · C++ → the compiler. **`FString::Printf` format strings and `DataTable` row schemas have consumers too.**
    - **⚖️ WHAT A REVIEWER OWES WHEN THE PARSER WAS NOT RUN: say so.** "Reviewed by reading" is a legitimate, useful, INCOMPLETE review — and it must be **reported as incomplete**, naming the parser that was not run. **Reporting it as a PASS is how a grammar that had never once loaded reached a measurement run**, and the only reason that run is reportable at all is that the harness logged the NULL at `Error` and refused to score against it.

#### 9b. ⛔ NAMED ALIAS PROHIBITION — `mage` STAYS UNALIASED, PERMANENTLY
**`mage` must resolve to NOTHING.** `sorcerer` and `wizard` are **different cards** — the Sorcerer is the non-attacking ley-warden empowerer, the Wizard is the AoE fireball caster — and they must share **no alias whatsoever**. The sealed corpus carries `the mage` in **both** files (`DEV-06`, `HOLD-09`) as a **`Clarify`** row.

**Aliasing `mage` to either card converts an honest clarification into a confident wrong command — the exact valid-shaped-wrong-command failure this feature's entire architecture exists to prevent.** This is written as a **named prohibition rather than a note** because *"add the obvious alias"* is precisely what a future task will do, and it would look like a helpful improvement while silently deleting a gate row's meaning. **Adding a `mage` alias is an automatic QA FAIL**, in this batch and in every later one.

### 10. TUNABLES (all `EditDefaultsOnly` / named constants, ALL flagged for Jonathan's feel pass)
`SoftTimeoutSeconds` **4.0** · `HardTimeoutSeconds` **10.0** · `MaxOutputTokens` **96** · `MaxSnapshotChars` **1085** (⚠️ **governs ZONE B + ZONE C ONLY, never Zone A** — = the ≤400-token cap at the **MEASURED 2.71** chars/token of that region, `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M`. ⚖️ **RULED DOWN FROM 1440 on 2026-08-03 from `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s printed counts — §8's TRIGGER fired: 1440 admitted 531 tok against a 400-token budget, over-admitting by ~33 %. CONDITIONAL AND THE CONDITION IS PART OF THE RULING: it holds only while the truncation the tighter cap now performs is OBSERVABLY LOGGED** — WARN-5 already showed this builder's collapse path is silent, and a tighter cap that silently degrades the prompt is worse than a loose one that does not. ⛔ **"Corrected from the spike's real measurement" stood here falsely until 2026-08-02 and was WITHDRAWN; that history stands — the phrase is true only from 2026-08-03, and only for this tokenizer.** As-built B+C = **955 chars = 352 tok — BOTH MEASURED — i.e. 88 % of the token budget while the char reading advertised 66 %**; see §8 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE, and note **2.13 ⇒ ~850** is the stricter bound if Zone C ever becomes as symbol-dense as Zone B. ⚖️ **ROLE-LABELLED 2026-08-03 — VALUE UNCHANGED AT 1085: THIS IS THE *AUTHORITY* VALUE, AND IT IS RETIRED, NOT RE-POINTED, AT TASK-423's COMMIT.** It is correct because it assumes the **smallest** plausible ratio; a **pre-filter** must assume the **largest**, so the safe direction inverts with the role. TASK-423 enforces the budget by **tokenizing** (`MaxSnapshotTokens` **400**) and introduces a **separate** constant **`SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` = 3000 chars** (floor **1424** = `400 × 3.56`, the measured whole-prompt ratio; generous by ruling — erring high costs one wasted tokenize, erring low silently truncates). **After that commit `MaxSnapshotChars` does not exist** — ⚠️ **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (WARN-9): read that as "NO CODE AND NO LOG-STRING REFERENCE", NOT as "the string appears nowhere."** ✅ **FOUR doc-comment mentions correctly SURVIVE** (`SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h:206`, `:208`, `:210`, `:352`) — the retirement notice itself plus one dated parenthetical — and ⛔ **they must NOT be deleted.** ⚖️ **This is the file's established dead-spelling idiom (the `llama_kv_cache_seq_rm` precedent) and it is the §14-CORRECT behaviour: a reader arriving from an older doc and grepping the dead name lands on *"retired, here is the successor"* instead of on SILENCE — and a negative search cannot distinguish silence from absence.** ⇒ **The wording was what needed to move, not the code.** ⛔ **Do NOT carry 1085 into the pre-filter under any name — full ruling: §8 resolution (2).** ⇒ ✅ **CONDITION STATUS + RE-ANCHORING (2026-08-03, §8's EIGHTH SEAM).** The observable-truncation condition above **is LANDED — the post-TASK-413 pass shipped the log in the same pass as the cap and closed WARN-5's `<`-boundary bug (it now fires on *"did any kind fail to print in full"*, names the cause bit, and escalates on every deepening) — UNDER QA, NOT VERIFIED, AND NOT EXECUTED (no caller — §12g).** ⛔ **AND THE CONDITION DOES *NOT* DIE WITH THIS CONSTANT.** It was written as a rider on the cap; it is **re-anchored onto THE ACT**, and now binds **any enforcement of the snapshot budget, by any mechanism, under any name** — `MaxSnapshotTokens`, `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars`, or anything that later replaces them. **A subsystem that truncates the snapshot without an observable log has not implemented the clause, regardless of which bound it enforced** — and TASK-423's **new** cut sites are not covered by the snapshot builder's existing log. ⚠️ **TASK-423 ALSO OWES §10 THE TOKEN CONSTANT ITSELF: `MaxSnapshotTokens` = 400 is pinned in §8 as "value already law, §10", but §10 carries the 400 only as an OPERAND of the char derivation above — so retiring `MaxSnapshotChars` would otherwise delete the budget's only home in this section. It lands here as a real named tunable in the same pass**) · `GrammarCountMax` **30** (⚠️ NOT the live roster max — see §1) · `SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds` **3** (⚠️ a `static constexpr`, NOT a tunable — it is pinned in §9 and enforced in BOTH the grammar and the parser) · `DeferredIntentTTLSeconds` **120** · `DeferredIntentPollHz` **1** (armed only while latched) · `ContextTokens` **2048** (⚖️ **FROZEN AT 2048 UNTIL AFTER THE RE-RUN — ruled 2026-08-03; raising it would invalidate bars #1/#3/#4, which have already PASSED at this value. See §8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND: the guardrail lands first, the number moves later, on measurement, on Jonathan's call**) · `GpuLayers` **-1 = auto-tier** (full-offload / partial / CPU-only, chosen from the spike's numbers).
- **⚠️ `ContextTokens` — AND ANY BATCH SIZE — IS A *REQUESTED* VALUE. THE CONTEXT IS ENTITLED TO USE A DIFFERENT ONE, AND THE VENDORED HEADER SAYS SO IN AS MANY WORDS (added 2026-08-02; `llama.h:551-556`, `qa/TASK-412.md` WARN-2).** *"After creating a llama_context, it is recommended to query the actual values using these functions … the requested values via llama_context_params may differ from the actual values used by the context."* The actuals are **`llama_n_ctx(ctx)` / `llama_n_batch(ctx)` / `llama_n_ubatch(ctx)`**, and **anything that sizes work reads those, never the tunable.** **Why this earns a clause instead of a comment: if `n_batch` comes back clamped, a prefill slice sized from the requested value is bigger than the context will accept, `llama_decode` rejects it outright, and EVERY run reads "inference failed" — a total-failure mode that presents as a model problem while the `.ini` value that caused it looks perfectly correct.** The same applies to a clamped `n_ctx` against §8's prompt-budget pre-check: the budget is checked against the **actual** context size or it is not checked. **The spike's copy is being corrected under WARN-2 — ⚠️ but TASK-423 INHERITS THE IDIOM:** the shipped subsystem queries both actuals at load, budgets from them, and **logs requested and actual side by side once** so a clamp is visible rather than inferred. **The tunable stays the request; the actual is what is enforced against.** ⇒ **MEASURED 2026-08-03: the assembled prompt is 1504 tok, so with `MaxOutputTokens` 96 reserved the feature occupies 1600 of a REQUESTED 2048 — 78 %.** ⇒ ⚖️ **THE HEADROOM QUESTION THAT FIGURE RAISED IS NOW DECIDED (2026-08-03), AND IT WENT TO THE GUARDRAIL, NOT TO THE NUMBER: `ContextTokens` STAYS 2048 before the re-run — bars #1/#3/#4 already passed at it and `n_ctx` drives KV-cache size and prefill cost, so moving it would silently invalidate three passing measurements to fix a problem that is not yet biting.** The bound instead is **TASK-423's MANDATORY startup budget assertion** — `ZoneA_tokens + MaxSnapshotTokens + MaxOutputTokens + safety_margin ≤ llama_n_ctx(ctx)`, computed **after the context exists**, failing **loudly at load** and naming which operand overflowed. **Full ruling, operands and headroom arithmetic: §8 ⚖️ THE ZONE-A SIZE BOUND.** ⚠️ **This clause is the reason the assertion may not read the tunable:** an assertion that checks the *requested* 2048 is not a check at all on the exact machine where a clamp makes it matter.

**Compile traps — restate in every dispatch touching these files:** no literal `*/` inside doc comments · `FString::Printf` format strings must be literal/`constexpr` (UE 5.8 `TCheckedFormatString`, the TASK-268 C7595 lesson) · the no-shadowing law · the complete-type include law.

**⛔ TOOLING TRAP — `Grep` MANGLES COMMENT SYNTAX ON THIS MACHINE. THREE OCCURRENCES, TWO FILES, TWO LANES ⇒ IT IS A PROPERTY OF THE TOOL, NOT OF ONE FILE'S ENCODING (promoted to law 2026-08-03; `qa/TASK-416.md` NOTE-1 ×2 on `SiegeAssistantGrammar.cpp`, `qa/TASK-433.md` §4 on `SummonedUnit.cpp`).** `Grep`'s output renders `//` — **and a bare inline `/`** — as `\`, so a shipped predicate's ordinary line comment comes back looking like a malformed line-continuation inside executable code.
- ⚠️ **IT CUTS BOTH WAYS, AND BOTH ARE EXPENSIVE.** It **MANUFACTURES** findings — twice a reviewer held a plausible BLOCKER that did not exist — and it **MASKS** them: *"no literal `*/` inside a `//` comment"* is the compile trap one line above, and it is exactly the shape this rendering hides.
- ⇒ ⚖️ **COMMENT-SYNTAX AND STRUCTURAL ANALYSIS IS DONE ON RAW `Read` OUTPUT, NEVER ON `Grep` OUTPUT. `Grep` LOCATES; IT DOES NOT READ.** A reviewer who reports a comment-level finding from `Grep` output has not verified it, and must say which tool produced the text they are quoting.
- 📌 **The count is three occurrences across TWO files** — counted off the two reports rather than off the relay that carried them, per the relayed-diagnosis law. The tell that generalised it was the **second file**, not the third occurrence.

### 11. ⚠️ THE EVALUATION CORPUS IS SEALED, SPLIT, AND AUTHORED BY A DIFFERENT OWNER THAN THE PROMPT
Added 2026-08-02 at the orchestrator's direction, closing a self-grading risk the manager escalated. **Accuracy bar #5 is the go/no-go number for a feature that could consume weeks — it must not be scored against a test set written by the person tuning the prompt.** This project has been burned by exactly this shape three times already: `Build.bat` returning exit 0 on a failed build, `rig_character.py` silently ignoring an override that did not exist, and MCP readback passing on visually-broken UMG. **Every one produced a confident green that was not real.**

- **DIFFERENT OWNER, STRUCTURALLY.** The corpus is authored by a **different task and a different role** than the prompt/few-shot author. Not an honour system — two dispatches, two contexts.
- **SEALED BEFORE THE PROMPT EXISTS.** The corpus task lands **before** the prompt is written or tuned, so the prompt cannot be fitted to it and the corpus cannot be narrowed to what the prompt already handles. **Fitting direction matters:** corpus → vocabulary/prompt *coverage* is legitimate requirements flow; vocabulary/prompt → corpus is the cheat.
- **⚖️ SPLIT 40 = 25 DEV + 15 HOLDOUT, TWO FILES.** The plan's own remediation ladder for a missed accuracy bar begins *"better few-shots"* — which **is** tuning against the eval. So few-shot tuning happens against **DEV only**; the **HOLDOUT is opened exactly once, at the measurement run, and the HOLDOUT number is the one reported against the ≥ 85 % bar.** A dev-set number quoted as the bar is a QA FAIL.
- **THE FEW-SHOTS MUST BE DISJOINT FROM BOTH FILES** — no corpus sentence may appear as a Zone-A few-shot. QA checks this by literal string comparison, not by assertion.
- **ADVERSARIAL BY REQUIREMENT, not by good intentions.** Mandatory coverage: ambiguous quantities · units that do not exist in the live roster · **the Sorcerer/Wizard collision** (different cards; a shared alias makes the assistant confidently command the wrong unit) · phrasings that mix a **selection-bearing** verb with an **army-wide** one · multi-kind selections · deferred phrasings · and rows whose correct outcome is **Clarify** or **Refuse**, not Execute.
- **CANONICAL HOME + SCHEMA (pinned):** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_dev.csv` and `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout.csv`, siblings of `cards.csv` (the project's data-source-of-truth home). Identical header, character-for-character:
  ```
  Id,Sentence,ExpectOutcome,ExpectKinds,ExpectCounts,ExpectWhere,ExpectTriggerKind,ExpectTriggerAtLeast,Notes
  ```
  `ExpectOutcome` ∈ `Execute` | `Clarify` | `Refuse`. `ExpectKinds` / `ExpectCounts` are **`|`-separated and index-aligned** (the `FSiegeAssistantCommand` shape). Empty cell = the field is not asserted for that row.
- **⚖️ INTENT IS ENCODED IN `Notes`, AND THAT ENCODING IS ACCEPTED LAW (ruling 2026-08-02).** The pinned header has **no `ExpectIntent` column**, but bar #5 scores `{intent, kinds, counts, where}` — and rows like `charge with the footmen` vs `send the footmen at their castle` differ in **nothing but intent**. TASK-426 hit this and **did the right thing: it did NOT unilaterally add a 10th column** (that would have broken the contract TASK-410's parser is written against, silently, after the seal). Instead every `Notes` cell is prefixed **`intent=<value>; `**, matched by **`^intent=([a-z]+); `** — the 7 intents plus **`none`** (Refuse rows) and **`unasserted`** (where the verb itself is the ambiguity). **Accepted because the runner scores it with a one-line regex and ZERO corpus edit, and — decisively — the values were authored before any prompt existed, so the seal holds.** The regex is part of the pinned contract: TASK-410's runner parses it, and the `Notes` prefix may not be reformatted.
- **🚩 KNOWN LIMITATION, v1-ACCEPTED, RECORDED SO NOBODY MISREADS IT: an empty cell is OVERLOADED.** It means *"not asserted"*, so a row whose correct answer is *"this field IS empty"* (`rally`, `fallback` — no kinds, no place) **cannot be positively asserted**; it is carried in `Notes` prose today. ⚠️ **Never read an empty cell as a passing assertion** — it is scored as *skipped*, not as *matched*. Fixing it properly means a sentinel value (e.g. `<none>`) in a future corpus revision, which is a **new** corpus, never an edit to the sealed one.
- **⚠️ THE CORPUS IS LOADED BY PATH AT RUNTIME, NEVER `#include`d INTO THE PLUGIN.** Baking Siegebound sentences into `Plugins/SiegeLlama/` would give the plugin a Siegebound dependency it is architecturally forbidden to have (§8's lane split). The paths are passed on the console command line.
- **REACTIVE COVERAGE IS DECLARED, NOT HIDDEN.** If a later vocabulary or prompt task adds an alias or a few-shot *because a corpus row needed it*, its handoff **lists them** — legitimate work, but the reader must know the coverage was reactive.
- **🔍 AND THE SAME INSTINCT GOVERNS EVERY CLAIM IN THIS BATCH: a relayed diagnosis is a LEAD, not a finding.** See **"🔍 THE RELAYED-DIAGNOSIS LAW (2026-08-02)"** above.
