This batch alone produced three relayed root causes that were wrong or incomplete — the `my_castle` pin, the `C2228`/`C2737` "include law" reading, and the obvious-but-shallow `C4172` read. **Every one was caught by checking the artifact instead of the message.** The corpus's whole reason for existing is the same principle applied to a benchmark: **do not grade yourself from your own report.**

### 12. ⚖️ THE ACCURACY LADDER WAVE — the post-spike remediation law (added 2026-08-03, manager, on Jonathan's TASK-415 rulings)

**Context, stated once so no clause below has to re-argue it.** The spike measured all six bars (`handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` PART 3). Bars #1/#3/#4/#6 PASS. **Bar #5 FAILED at 66.7 % against 85 %**, with `parse_failures = 0` on all 40 rows and `STRICT == LENIENT` — ⇒ **the grammar provably works and the failure is COMPREHENSION, not FORMAT.** Constrained decoding was never going to fix comprehension.

**Jonathan's two rulings (DECIDED 2026-08-03 — no agent re-opens them):**
1. **ITERATE THE PROMPT LADDER FIRST** — the plan's own rungs **1 (better few-shots)** then **2 (vocabulary)**, then **re-measure**. ⛔ **Not a bigger model, not fine-tuning, not a kill.** Rung 3 and beyond are HIS call, at a gate, on a number.
2. **DIAGNOSE THE CPU ABORTS BEFORE ANYONE CHANGES THE CPU-FALLBACK REQUIREMENT.** His ruling (i) — CPU fallback required — **stands until he moves it.** The question to answer is whether the 2-of-5 truncated CPU generations are a **TUNABLE** (a deadline / token budget cutting generation short) or a **WALL**. ⛔ **Diagnose, then bring a recommendation. Do not quietly redefine the requirement.**

#### 12a. 🔒 THE SPENT-SEAL LAW + HOLDOUT GENERATIONS

> **A holdout is SPENT the moment its rows are scored AND any per-row result reaches a report, a handoff or the board. A spent holdout is NEVER scored again — every subsequent score against it would be fitted.**

- **`assistant_eval_holdout.csv` (generation 1) IS SPENT as of 2026-08-03.** Its five failing rows are printed in `handoffs/TASK-413-buildmaster.md` §21a and on the board, and they are therefore **burned as tuning signal**. It is **never re-scored, never edited, and never deleted** — it stays in git as the record of what generation 1 measured.
- **THE DEV SPLIT IS *NOT* SPENT AND IS NOT GENERATIONAL.** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_dev.csv` is what tuning is scored against, freely and repeatedly. **A dev number is a fitted number by construction and is never the go/no-go figure** (unchanged from §11).
- **NAMING LAW — HOLDOUT GENERATIONS:** `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout<N>.csv`, `N` incrementing from **2**. Generation 1 keeps its historical un-suffixed name. Each generation:
  - is authored by **an owner who did no tuning** (§11's structural rule, and see 12b);
  - ⚖️ **READS THE DEV SPLIT IN FULL — AND *DECLARES* THAT IT DID. THE READ IS A SHOULD; THE DECLARATION IS A MUST (ruled 2026-08-03, from the generation-2 pass).** The author's whole duty is **non-collision**, and non-collision cannot be asserted about a file you did not open. **The dev split is not sealed** (bullet above) and it is precisely **the file the tuner is fitting to** — so a fresh holdout row that lands on a dev sentence's *shape* is **a FITTED row wearing a sealed file's authority**: the one failure this entire apparatus exists to prevent, arriving from the direction nobody was guarding. ⚠️ **Measured, not hypothesised: the generation-2 author read all 25 dev rows and moved FOUR sentences off dev frames. QA priced the un-read version at a ~27 % inflation risk on the go/no-go number** — four rows of a fifteen-row split, i.e. **larger than the margin any recent gate has turned on.** ⛔ **The declaration is separate from the doing and is not optional: *"I read the dev split in full, and here is the collision table"* is the artifact.** A holdout handoff that omits it has not discharged the duty, **because a reader cannot tell *"checked and clear"* from *"never looked"* — and both produce the same silent, confident file.**
  - carries the **pinned 9-column header character-for-character** and the `^intent=([a-z]+); ` `Notes` prefix contract, unchanged;
  - is **15 rows**, authored against the **FROZEN `t0` fixture** in `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` (a row whose truth depends on a live count is only true at t0; **t0's bytes may not be changed to suit a corpus row** — that silently rewrites every earlier premise and moves bar #3's zone boundaries);
  - is **SEALED at handoff and opened exactly once**, by the measurement task, on `tier=full` (the only tier with zero aborts — an aborted generation scores as a wrong answer and corrupts the number).
- **ZERO CODE CHANGE IS REQUIRED TO ADOPT A GENERATION.** `Siege.Llama.SpikeEval` takes `holdout=<path>` and **deliberately has no default**. A new generation is a new file and a new command line.
- 🔒⛔ **THE SEAL PROTECTS THE INSTRUMENT TOO — ADDED 2026-08-03, AND IT IS A HOLE THIS CLAUSE DID NOT SEE.** Everything above guards against a holdout being **scored twice** or **scored by its tuner**. **A holdout is equally lost if it can never be scored ONCE** — and gen 2 is authored against the **`t0` fixture inside `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp`** and scored by **`Siege.Llama.SpikeEval`, in the same file.** ⇒ ⛔ **Deleting that file spends Jonathan's one honest measurement by destroying the only thing that can take it.** **Full ruling, the four capabilities that must be replaced first, and the misclassification lesson: "Settings screen + the assistant CONFIRM STEP…" §16.**
- 🔒⛔ **A SCORE IS ONLY ABOUT THE LANE IT MEASURED — AND ON 2026-08-03 THE TWO LANES WERE MEASURED TO DIFFER (added at the first live run; this is the THIRD hole in this clause and the most consequential).** **`DA_AssistantVocabulary` DOES NOT RESOLVE, so the SHIPPED synonym table is EMPTY**, while **every eval number this project has — 18 → 19 → 20/25, the entire ladder — came from the SPIKE lane, which carries that table BAKED INTO THE C++.**
  - ✅⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-04 — THE EMPTY-TABLE HALF OF THIS SENTENCE IS *STALE* AND DESCRIBES THE PRE-TASK-463 STATE. IT IS STRUCK, NOT DELETED, BECAUSE IT WAS STILL BEING QUOTED AS CURRENT A DAY AFTER IT WAS FIXED.** ~~the SHIPPED synonym table is EMPTY~~ ⇒ **TASK-463 is `qa-passed` (`qa/TASK-469.md`, PASS / 0 blockers): the `USiegeAssistantVocabulary` C++ CONSTRUCTOR DEFAULTS ship and are used, `sorcerer` is among them (`SiegeAssistantVocabulary.cpp`), and the component carries an explicit fallback when the asset does not resolve (`SiegeAssistantComponent.cpp`, the `NewObject` fallback path).** ⇒ ⛔ **DO NOT CITE "the shipped synonym table is empty" AS A CAUSE OF ANY DEFECT FROM 2026-08-04 ONWARD** — it was cited exactly that way while diagnosing the Sorcerer defect and it sent the first pass at the wrong file.
  - ✅ **THE OTHER HALF OF THE SENTENCE IS UNAFFECTED AND STAYS LOAD-BEARING: every eval number still came from the SPIKE lane.** ⚖️ *A clause with two claims decays one claim at a time, and striking the dead one is what keeps the live one credible.*
  ⇒ ⛔ **THE SHIPPED ASSISTANT MAY PERFORM WORSE THAN 20/25 AND NOBODY HAS MEASURED IT.** ⚠️ **This is not cosmetic and it is not a content bug: the synonym table is Zone A, Zone A is the static prefix, and the prompt the player's model sees is therefore NOT the prompt any number describes.**
  ⇒ ⛔ **BINDING: NO EVAL NUMBER MAY BE REPORTED AS DESCRIBING THE SHIPPED FEATURE UNTIL (a) THE SHIPPED LANE RENDERS THE SAME ZONE A AS THE MEASURED LANE — *HOWEVER ACHIEVED* — AND (b) `ZoneA.TwoLaneByteEquality` HAS *PASSED*.**
  - ⚖️ **(a) AMENDED 2026-08-03 — I PINNED THE MECHANISM WHERE I MEANT THE PROPERTY, AND THE WRONG WORDING IS STRUCK.** ~~`DA_AssistantVocabulary` RESOLVES~~ **I wrote *"the asset resolves"* believing the table LIVED in the asset. TASK-463 proved otherwise: the C++ defaults were NEVER empty — 27 rows, populated since TASK-417 — and the defect was a SEAM (`GetVocabulary()` returned the raw `LoadSynchronous()` result, so `BuildZoneA(nullptr)` took its documented `else` branch).** ⇒ **The asset was never the source of truth; it is an OVERRIDE.**
  - ✅ **THE FIX SATISFIES (a) IN SUBSTANCE AND IS STRONGER THAN ASSET-RESOLUTION WOULD HAVE BEEN.** Constructing `NewObject<USiegeAssistantVocabulary>(this)` uses **deliberately the same construction `TwoLaneByteEquality` uses**, so that test's PASS becomes a statement about **the object the shipped lane actually renders**, not merely about one of the same class. ⚠️ **Asset-resolution would have proved LESS: an asset can differ from the defaults, and would have needed its own equality proof.**
  - ⚖️ **THIS IS §18a/§19/§23's PATTERN IN THIS CLAUSE'S OWN TEXT: A PROXY PINNED AS IF IT WERE THE PROPERTY.** *"The asset resolves"* was a mechanism I assumed; *"the shipped lane renders the measured Zone A"* is the thing that actually matters. ⛔ **The clause is amended rather than waived — a letter that outlived its reason is repaired, never granted an exception**, because the next reader obeys the letter.
  - ⛔ **AND (b) GETS *STRONGER*, NOT WEAKER: WITH NO ASSET IN PLAY, `TwoLaneByteEquality` IS NOW THE ONLY THING STANDING BETWEEN US AND SILENT DIVERGENCE.** ⚠️ **The sting that proves it: `ZoneA.NullVocabularyIsNotTheMeasuredLane` ALREADY ASSERTS THE EXACT IDENTITY THAT BROKE. The test that would have caught this was written — and simply never run.** ⇒ **A written test is not a guardrail; a RUN test is.** ⚠️ **That test exists and HAS NEVER RUN — 33 automation tests COMPILE, and compiling is not passing.** ✅ **The seal itself is INTACT** (nothing was scored, nothing leaked) **— what is impeached is the SUBJECT of any future score, not the corpus.**
  ⚖️ **AND IT IS §9c's SAME-AUTHORSHIP TRAP IN ITS LAST AVAILABLE COSTUME: the two lanes agree on everything a reader can check and differ in CONTENT the reader never sees.** The holdout was authored against the spike's fixture; **if the lanes' Zone A differ, the holdout is no longer authored against the thing it would score.** ⛔ **Byte-equality between lanes is therefore a PRECONDITION of the go/no-go number, not a tidiness test.**
- ⛔ **GENERATIONS ARE NOT ROW-COMPARABLE, AND A GENERATION-TO-GENERATION DELTA IS NOT EVIDENCE.** Each generation is authored to fix the weaknesses the last one revealed about *itself* (generation 1's own printed degenerate floor was **60.0 %**, leaving its 66.7 % result just **one row** above a model with no translation ability at all). **The bar is ABSOLUTE — ≥ 85 % — and the split's OWN printed leniency floor travels beside the number, every time.** Printing "66.7 % → X %" as an improvement measure is a category error and is a QA FAIL.

#### 12b. ⚠️ THE FAILURE-SHAPE TRAP — self-grading, one level up

> **Nobody tunes against a burned failure list and then scores on a holdout containing those same shapes. That is the self-grading trap one level up, and it defeats a fresh seal completely.**

The five burned rows are **legitimate signal about a CLASS** — they are already spent, so reading them costs nothing. They are **never a licence to memorize a STRING.** Both halves of this are law:

- **THE TUNER'S DUTY: fix the CLASS, not the STRING.** An exemplar, alias or rule that only works on the exact burned sentence is a **QA FAIL**. Each taught class must be argued in the handoff: *what generalizes, and why.*
- **THE HOLDOUT AUTHOR'S DUTY: carry the same failure CLASSES, and NONE of the burned SURFACE FORMS.** A fresh holdout that repeats *"trebuchets"*, *"spend my gold"*, *"bowmen"*, *"the wizard"* or *"attack with the archers"* is testing memorization. **Different non-existent unit, different economy verb, different colloquial, different determiner, different dropped-selection phrasing** — and the handoff carries a table proving each new form is not a paraphrase of the burned one.
  - ⚠️ **THE STANDING BURNED LIST GAINS ONE MORE, AND IT IS DECLARED RATHER THAN CONTAINED (2026-08-03): THE RETIRED COLLISION NOUN OF THE LADDER WAVE'S LOOP-1 BLOCKER-1** — the unit word a few-shot was re-authored to remove **because it collided with a corpus row**. ✅ **It is absent from the source tree, nothing is keyed to it, no exemplar/alias/rule/note mentions it, and it is NOT signal — it must stay unused.** ⛔ **And it is recoverable in PLAINTEXT** from `handoffs/TASK-428-programmer.md`'s two sweep tables and from `qa/TASK-430.md`'s line anchors, **one paragraph away from the sentence saying it collided.** ⇒ ⚖️ **A LINE-ANCHORED REVIEW PLUS VERSION CONTROL LEAKS A RETIRED TOKEN BY CONSTRUCTION. There is no containment available here, and pretending otherwise is the actual failure** — the git history cannot be unwritten and the reviewer was right to anchor. **So it is BURNED, in writing, on this list, where the next holdout author already looks.** ⚠️ **The recovery is a CONVENTION, not a code change: declaring the burn converts an uncontainable leak into the one thing this pipeline already enforces** — and the same move is owed for any future retired-because-it-collided token, on the pass that retires it.
- **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION, NOT AN HONOUR SYSTEM.** The holdout task and the tuning task are **dispatched in the same wave, in the same message**, so *neither agent's output exists when the other starts.* The tuner may not read the fresh holdout; the holdout's author may not read the tuner's few-shots, synonym table, Zone A, or handoff.
- **QA IS THE ONLY ROLE THAT MAY OPEN A SEALED HOLDOUT, AND ONLY FOR THE DISJOINTNESS CHECK** (§11's literal-string rule, extended to near-paraphrase after the TASK-412 WARN-1 precedent). ⛔ **A QA report may not quote or paraphrase a single holdout sentence, Id or expected field** — QA reports are read by the tuner, and one quoted row leaks the seal. The `qa/TASK-416.md` §2c idiom is the model: **learn that a string is absent; learn nothing else.**
  - ⚖️ **THE LINE, DRAWN PRECISELY, BECAUSE THE GENERATION-2 PASS RAN RIGHT UP TO IT AND STOPPED — THE CERTIFICATE / RECOMMENDATION SPLIT (ruled 2026-08-03; it WORKED, and it is REUSABLE).** A reviewer holding a sealed file **MAY issue a purely NEGATIVE fact stated against publicly checkable criteria** — *"this word occurs in no corpus, in any generation"* — because **the recipient can verify it against files they already hold**, and it therefore transfers **no information about what the sealed file contains.** ⛔ **The moment that same reviewer explains why one candidate is pedagogically better than another, it has crossed from CERTIFICATE into RECOMMENDATION and must stop:** a preference between candidates **is a function of the sealed rows**, and it leaks their shape without quoting one of them. ⇒ ⚖️ **THE TELL IS THE DIRECTION OF THE CLAIM, NOT ITS LENGTH: an ABSENCE certificate is checkable by the recipient; a QUALITY judgement is not — and anything the recipient cannot check is, by construction, something only the seal could have supplied.**
- **REACTIVE COVERAGE IS DECLARED** (unchanged from §11): if an alias or few-shot was added *because a corpus row needed it*, the handoff lists it.

⚠️ **AND THE GRAMMAR CANNOT CLOSE THIS CLASS — BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY OMISSION.** Both refusal failures were **structurally valid commands that were semantically wrong**. The grammar admits all 13 kinds and all 7 intents regardless of board state or policy (**§1: constrain identity hard, leave quantity soft** — deliberately), so an out-of-scope sentence always has a legal-looking production. **`{"ask":"unsupported"}` already exists in the grammar and the model simply did not route to it** — `HOLD-14` proves it can. ⇒ **The fix is few-shots and vocabulary. A task that responds to this by tightening the grammar is introducing the defect §1 names, not fixing one.**

#### 12c. ⚖️ THE ZONE-A GROWTH BUDGET IS IN TOKENS, AND `ContextTokens` STAYS FROZEN

Rungs 1 and 2 **grow Zone A** — more few-shots, a bigger synonym table. That is expected and is the point. It is also the one way this wave can silently break three bars that already PASSED.

- **CEILING FOR THIS WAVE: `zoneA_tok ≤ 1389`** (measured 1139 **+ 250**). Derivation, from measured operands: `1389 (Zone A) + 400 (MaxSnapshotTokens, the B+C cap) + 96 (MaxOutputTokens) + ~13 (chat-template wrapper) = 1898 of 2048`, leaving **~150 tok of margin**. **HARD GATE at the measurement task: over budget ⇒ STOP, do not score anything against an over-budget prompt.**
- ⛔ **`ContextTokens` STAYS 2048 THROUGH THIS WAVE.** Bars **#1, #3 and #4 already PASSED at this value**, and `n_ctx` drives KV-cache size and prefill cost — **raising it to buy Zone-A room would silently invalidate three passing measurements to solve a problem that is not yet biting.** Any request to raise it comes to Jonathan **with a re-measure of those three bars attached.** It is not a tuner's lever.
- **A CHAR-DERIVED TOKEN FIGURE IS `DERIVED`, NEVER `MEASURED`** (§8's instance-1 and instance-6 classes, both of which cost this batch a correction). The tuning agent has no model and **cannot measure tokens** — it states chars and the assumed ratio and labels the result DERIVED. **Only `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s printed counts are MEASURED.** ⇒ ⚠️ **AND FROM 2026-08-03 THE RATIO IT DERIVES WITH IS PINNED: DERIVE AT THE MEASURED WORST *MARGINAL* RATE — ~1.3 chars/token — NEVER AT THE WHOLE-STRING ~3.77 (§12g).** The wave measured those two rates differing by **2.8× on the same prompt**, and the whole-string rate erred **in the unsafe direction on the smaller edit.**
- **STATE THE TWO-SIDED EFFECT, DO NOT LEAVE IT IMPLIED.** Zone A is the **static prefix**: growing it **IMPROVES bar #3's reuse ratio** and **WORSENS turn-1 cold prefill**, which is bar #2's TTFT and the first sentence of every match. Both move; a handoff that names only the favourable one is not reporting.
- **ZONE B AND ZONE C BYTES DO NOT MOVE IN THIS WAVE.** The `t0` tripwire (`zoneB_chars = 68`, `zoneC_chars = 887`) must still hold at the measurement, or bar #3's reproduction is void and the corpus's premises have moved.

#### 12d. ⚖️ MANAGER RULING — BAR #2 IS A **WALL-CLOCK** BAR, AND ITS TOKEN COUNT IS RESTATED

Build-master correctly refused to resolve this in its own favour: the board's bar read *"TTFT **+** wall-clock for ~60 constrained tokens … ≤ 2 s partial · ≤ 6 s cpu"* — **one threshold, two quantities.** Bar text is the manager's, so I rule it, and I rule it in the conservative direction:

- **THE THRESHOLD GOVERNS WALL-CLOCK TO THE COMPLETE PARSED ORDER.** ⚠️ **TTFT is not player-visible for this feature and cannot be**: §3 says the model emits **symbols only** and every player-facing string is a game-authored template, so **there is no streaming surface** — nothing exists to show the player until the whole JSON has been emitted and parsed. A latency bar measured at a moment the player cannot observe is not a latency bar.
- **"~60 constrained tokens" IS RETIRED FROM THE BAR.** Real constrained output is **19–57 tokens (mean ~39)** — it stops at EOS after valid JSON. The bar is restated against **the real constrained output length**. ⚠️ **`wall60_equiv_ms` is an EXTRAPOLATION printed by the harness and MUST NEVER be quoted as a measurement.**
- ⇒ **UNDER THIS READING BAR #2 FAILS: partial 6472 ms vs ≤ 2000 · cpu 8730 ms vs ≤ 6000.** Full offload — not a named tier on the bar — measures **2106 ms with zero aborts.**
- ⛔ **AND THE UNAMBIGUOUS FACT OUTRANKS THE WORDING EITHER WAY: the CPU tier did not finish 2 of 5 generations and emitted truncated JSON. A truncated command is unusable, not slow.** No reading of the bar makes that a pass.
- ⚠️ **THE NUMBER THAT ACTUALLY MATTERS TO JONATHAN: the BEST tier is 2.1 s** — which is exactly the *"2-second pause mid-battle"* tolerance he named himself at TASK-415. **This ruling is flagged for his overturn at the next gate**: the bar's text is mine, the tolerance is his.
- **Decode is the whole cost** (ms/token: full ~36-49 · partial ~117-131 · cpu ~156-182; TTFT is nearly flat within a tier). 🚩 **RECORDED, NOT TASKED:** a more compact JSON schema would cut the full-offload wall roughly proportionally — that is **rung 3 ("tighter grammar") and is Jonathan's call**, not a tuning task's.

#### 12e. STANDING PROCEDURE ITEMS THAT TRAVEL WITH EVERY EDITOR TASK IN THIS LANE

- ⚠️ **THE GITIGNORED `Saved/Config/WindowsEditor/Engine.ini` PYTHON REMOTE-EXEC BLOCK IS DROPPED BY THE EDITOR'S SHUTDOWN REWRITE, EVERY TIME.** Confirmed across two sessions — **standing behaviour, not a one-off.** Any task needing that channel **re-appends it after every editor close.**
- ⚠️ **THE USER-GLOBAL `EditorSettings.ini` `bThrottleCPUWhenNotForeground=False` LINE IS LOAD-BEARING FOR EVERY BENCH.** A headless-driven PIE session is always background and idles at **exactly 3.0 FPS forever** with the throttle on — **a bar taken under it is meaningless, not conservative.** Verify measured FPS **≥ 58 live** immediately before benching; the run-twice rule is necessary and never sufficient.
- ⛔ **WARN-R2's LINE MUST NEVER BE QUOTED AS A LAYOUT FINDING.** `bound=BEST_CASE reused=0 DROP=0.9%` + *"THE PROMPT LAYOUT IS WRONG AND MUST BE FIXED BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IS BUILT"* has fired **twice**, been checked twice, and been a **decode abort both times** — with the same run's `SHIPPED_WORST_CASE` bound healthy at 77.1 %. **A diagnostic that has misled every time it fired is a defect, and it is fixed, not merely warned about:** the layout verdict may only be emitted for a bound that **completed both turns**.

#### 12f. ⛔ THE PROMPT RUNG IS CLOSED — AND THE REASONING IS THE PART THAT OUTLIVES THE RESULT (ruled 2026-08-03)

> ⛔ **PROMPT-LEVEL TUNING ON `Qwen3-4B-Q4_K_M` TOPS OUT AT 20/25 ON THE DEV SPLIT, AGAINST A GATE OF 22/25 *AND* ZERO REFUSE-CLASS FAILURES. THE RUNG IS EXHAUSTED. THE NEXT RUNG IS JONATHAN'S AND HE RESERVED IT.**
> ⛔ **HOLDOUT 2 STAYS SEALED. Nothing in this ruling spends it; only a gate PASS or Jonathan's own instruction can.**

**Three waves, one row each: 18 → 19 → 20.** Sources, all measured: `handoffs/TASK-431-buildmaster.md` §LOOP 2 · `qa/TASK-430-gate-loop2.md` §L9 · `qa/TASK-430-gate-loop3-ruling.md`. **This clause records WHY the rung closed, because the reasoning is reusable on the next model and the score is not.**

- ⛔ **BUDGET WAS NEVER THE CONSTRAINT — AND THAT IS WHAT CLOSES THE RUNG RATHER THAN PAUSING IT.** Wave 1 spent **+797 chars** (18.5 % of Zone A) to buy one row; wave 2 spent **+8 chars NET** to buy one row. **A 100× difference in spend bought the same yield**, and the wave ended with **27 of the allowed tokens still unspent.** ⇒ ***"Give it more room"* is not an available argument, and neither is *"try harder"* — the cheap wave bought exactly what the expensive one did.**
- ⚠️ **23 OF 25 OUTPUTS CAME BACK BYTE-IDENTICAL between loop 1 and loop 2.** Five rule lines rewritten, one rule added, two `[notes]` lines deleted, an exemplar reframed — and **92 % of the corpus did not move by a single character.** **That is the shape of a lever at its stop, not of a lever being pulled wrong.**
- ⚖️ **THE RUNG CLOSED UNDER A *PRE-REGISTERED* CONDITION, NOT BY EXHAUSTING A LOOP BUDGET — and the distinction is the process result.** The gate ruled *before* loop 2 ran that **a loop 3 is refused unless loop 2's run itself produces a NEW COUNTABLE MECHANISM** — a fact read off an artifact, never a hypothesis about the model. Loop 2 produced **three** countable facts, and **every one retired a lever instead of aiming one**: the two mechanisms that had funded loop 2 (the **4-to-1 competing-instruction count** and the **character-for-character slot-fill substring**) were both **measured falsified**. ⇒ **The ledger of qualifying mechanisms did not fail to grow from 2 — it went from 2 to 0.** **The condition was missed in the WRONG DIRECTION, which is a far stronger stop than a narrow miss.**
- ⚖️ **AND THE STEELMAN WAS BUILT AND KILLED ON ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A RULING AND NOT A MOOD.** The best loop 3 available — delete the four `who="none"` consequents that the new gate rule could not out-evaluate — reaches, **if it works PERFECTLY on both target rows, 22/25 with ZERO margin** and leaves the categorical half untouched: **the one row that must refuse is not on its path, so half (ii) still fails, the holdout stays sealed, and NOTHING UNLOCKS.** ⇒ ⛔ **A PERFECT LOOP 3 ON THE HIGHEST-VALUE LEAD CHANGES NO STATE.** Any future proposal on this rung is measured against that sentence **first**, before its mechanism is even read.

> ⛔ **THE LAW — IT BINDS EVERY FUTURE PROMPT-LEVEL TASK ON THIS MODEL: NO SUCH TASK MAY BE JUSTIFIED BY NAMING THE ROWS IT WILL FIX.**
> **Both levers move ~1 row per wave, and NEITHER STEERS WHICH ROW.** Wave 2 targeted five named rows, moved **none** of them, and moved a **sixth that had already been written off** — and it moved that one on an **ADDITIVE +13-char clause**, after the additive lever had been declared dead on three measurements. ⇒ ⚖️ **RATE IS UNDER PROMPT CONTROL; SELECTION IS NOT.** A task whose case is *"this fixes row X and row Y"* is a lottery ticket with a name on it, and **it is failed at the gate.**

**⚖️ THE WORKED EXAMPLE — QA's SUBTRACTION THESIS: FALSIFIED, AND WITHDRAWN BY ITS OWN AUTHOR ON ITS OWN PRE-REGISTERED TEST. RECORDED AS A METHOD RESULT, NOT AS A FOOTNOTE TO A FAILED WAVE.**

The thesis replaced *"class-level prediction is at chance"* with a better variable — **was a competing mechanism DELETED?** — and it **fit 4 of 4 outcomes retrospectively.** Loop 2 was built as its clean test and the test was clean: deletion-based, net **+8 chars**, the mechanism **verified physically present in the rendered prompt**, the competitor **deleted rather than out-shouted**, the exemplar using the **identical noun phrase**. **It produced ZERO byte change on both rows it was designed for — 0 of 2 prospectively.**

- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE BATCH'S CLEANEST INSTANCE OF A HYPOTHESIS THAT EXPLAINED EVERYTHING AND PREDICTED NOTHING.** 4 of 4 backwards, 0 of 2 forwards ⇒ **an overfit to four data points.** ⚖️ **It was believed BECAUSE it was more sophisticated than the thing it replaced** — and **retrospective fit is not evidence; it is the null result of a search over explanations.** A model that fits every past outcome and has never been asked for a future one **has not been tested once.**
- ✅ **THE BEHAVIOUR THE PIPELINE WANTS, NAMED SO THE NEXT REVIEWER COPIES IT RATHER THAN RE-INVENTS IT: the author PRE-REGISTERED the falsifying condition, the run met it, and the author RETIRED THE THESIS INSTEAD OF DEFENDING IT** — in writing, on the record, as the party with the most to lose from saying so, and in the same pass that also stated the thesis's replacement was *"measured wrong, and I do not get to keep it."* ⇒ ⚖️ **A REVIEWER WHOSE MODEL IS FALSIFIED AND WHO SAYS SO HAS DONE THE MOST VALUABLE THING AVAILABLE AT THAT GATE.** ⛔ **Defending a thesis past its own pre-registered test is the failure. Being wrong is not, and a pipeline that punishes the second gets the first.**
- ⚖️ **AND PRE-REGISTRATION IS WHAT MADE BOTH RESULTS CHEAP.** The stopping condition, the *"if row X flips, the cause is rule R3 and NOT rule R2"* diagnosis, and the *"if this row returns to its old answer, clause order does not steer this model"* falsification were **all written before the run** — so **ONE measurement settled a stopping question, a regression question and a thesis, with nothing fitted afterwards.** ⇒ **Every measurement task on this lane pre-registers, in its handoff and before the run, what result would falsify its own case.**
- ⚠️ **§1's THIRD OWNER IS ASSERTED IN THIS DOCUMENT AND NOT IMPLEMENTED IN THE CODE — MEASURED, NOT INFERRED.** §1 says *"grammar guarantees existence, EXECUTOR guarantees legality"*; the failing categorical row emitted a **live, well-formed order for a kind the roster line itself prints as `0 orderable`.** **Orderability is per-match STATE, not identity** — §1's own law says the grammar must not encode it, and four prompt attempts have not taught it — ⇒ **only the command layer can refuse it, and that layer does not check.** ⛔ **This is a SHIPPED-SAFETY gap, not a route to the gate** (the eval scores emitted JSON, not executed actions) and **nobody may report closing it as progress on bar #5.**
- 📌 **CARRIED AS CROSS-TASK LAW, so the next toucher does not undo what worked:** ⛔ **the economy/card-play rule stays BYTE- AND POSITION-FROZEN** (it is the one class that landed, it protects Jonathan's standing *"never spends my gold, never plays my cards"* ruling, and it held byte-identically across both loops) · ⛔ **the counts rule is not reworded again** (its own pre-registered falsification fired) · ⛔ **`gold` is not re-added to `nearest_mine`** (TASK-421) · ⛔ **the retired collision noun stays unused** (§12b, and it is now declared BURNED).

✅ **WHAT THE RUNG BOUGHT — QUOTE THIS BESIDE THE 20/25, NEVER THE 20/25 ALONE:** **zero regressions across two loops** · the **gold / card-play refusal FIXED**, the one failure that broke a standing Jonathan ruling · the **exemplar slot-fill route measurably CLOSED** · **the sealed holdout NEVER OPENED and still unspent.** ⚠️ **A truthful *"this rung is done"*, bought with three measured mechanisms, is a RESULT and not a defeat** — it tells Jonathan exactly what the reserved rung is for, which is the thing a third loop could not have bought at any price.

#### 12g. ⚠️ THE TOKENISATION LAW — THE WAVE'S ONE GENUINELY NEW MECHANISM IS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT, NOT THE MODEL (ruled 2026-08-03)

**The marginal chars/token rate COLLAPSES on a small edit, and a derivation that uses the whole-string rate is wrong in an unpredictable direction.** Both loops, measured:

| loop | edit | derived cost | **measured cost** | error | **marginal chars/token** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **+797 chars** | 1371 | **1356** (`1139 → 1356` = **217 tok**) | **−15 · SAFE** (over-predicted) | **3.67** — tracks the whole-string 3.77 |
| 2 | **+8 chars** | 1358 (`8 ÷ 3.767 ≈ 2`) | **1362** (`1356 → 1362` = **6 tok**) | **+4 · UNSAFE** (under-predicted) | **1.33 — 2.8× WORSE** |

- ⚖️ **THE MECHANISM, AND IT IS COUNTABLE RATHER THAN A STORY: A LARGE ADDITION RE-TOKENISES MOSTLY ITSELF, SO ITS MARGINAL RATE TRACKS THE WHOLE-STRING RATE. A SMALL EDIT IS DOMINATED BY *BOUNDARY RE-TOKENISATION OF TEXT IT DID NOT TOUCH*, so its added characters land far denser than the average.** `+8 chars` cost **6** tokens, not the derived 2, and **nothing about the 8 characters explains it** — the cost was paid by their neighbours.
- ⛔ **THE LAW: BUDGET ADDED PROMPT TEXT AT THE MEASURED WORST MARGINAL RATE — ~1.3 chars/token — NEVER AT THE WHOLE-STRING ~3.77.** That is a **2.8× safety factor and it is MEASURED, not assumed.** ⚠️ n = 1 for a small edit and boundary effects can push either way, **so it is a floor on caution, not a constant.**
- ⛔ **OBSERVED ERROR BAND: −15 to +4 TOKENS, SIGN UNPREDICTABLE, ON n = 2.** ⇒ ⚖️ **ANY EDIT WITH UNDER ~20 TOKENS OF DERIVED HEADROOM IS *UNDECIDED* UNTIL MEASURED — it is not "probably fine".** ⚠️ **Had loop 2's 2.8× marginal error landed on a ~100-char edit it would have cost ~19 tokens against 27 of slack: the NEXT edit of ordinary size is the one this bites.** **Re-fit the band on every future measurement** — loop 2 already widened it once *and reversed its sign*.
- ✅ **NOTHING IN THE WAVE IS INVALIDATED, and that is stated before anything else: no decision in either loop rested on a derived figure.** The standing rule — ***read the printed `zoneA_tok~=` before scoring; DERIVED IS NOT A PASS*** — was honoured both times and both loops were scored on a **measured** number. **The sign flip CONFIRMS that rule rather than breaching it, and the rule does not change.** ⚠️ **The instructive part is who came closest to breaking it: the gate report that told build-master not to trust the derivation quoted *"~31 slack"* in the same pass, where the truth was 27** — 4 optimistic, in the unsafe direction, by its own author.

**📌 SPEC INPUT FOR TASK-423's BUDGET ASSERTION — recorded here because the task does not exist yet, and this is the shape it must land in:**

- ✅ **THE ASSERTION IS ON CHARACTERS (equivalently BYTES), NEVER ON TOKENS.** Chars are **exact, countable offline with no model resident**, and the repo's **verified ASCII-clean prompt-literal property** makes char count **== byte count**. Both lanes' char counts have now matched a hand-derivation **to the byte, twice** (5108, 5116). **Chars are the reliable quantity in this system; tokens are a runtime reading.**
- ✅ **AND IT MUST ALSO ASSERT THE TWO-LANE EQUALITY** — the shipped `USiegeAssistantSnapshot::BuildZoneA(V)` equals the spike's `AppendZoneA` output. **That single test converts this wave's most load-bearing claim from *reviewed* to *tested*.**
- ⛔ **A DERIVED TOKEN CONSTANT MUST NOT BE BAKED INTO ANY TEST.** Its error **sign is unknown** — which is the same defect class this section has already paid for twice, **in automated form, where it is worse because it looks authoritative.** A test asserting a number nobody measured is a guardrail that reports safe.
- ✅ **THE TOKEN CEILING STAYS A *RUNTIME* GATE, read off the printed `zoneA_tok~=` at the measurement run.** ⇒ ⚖️ **TWO ARTIFACTS, TWO QUANTITIES: the TEST asserts lane equality and an exact CHAR count; the RUN reads the TOKEN figure and stops on it.** Neither may be asked to do the other's job.

**⚠️ AND THE STANDING WARN THAT MUST NOT READ AS DISCHARGED: `zoneA_chars=5116` PROVES THE *SPIKE* LANE ONLY.**

- ⛔ **NO COMMAND PRINTS THE SHIPPED `BuildZoneA`.** The measurement everyone quoted came from the spike's `AppendZoneA`; **the shipped lane's byte count remains a READING-LEVEL CLAIM**, carried across two loops and two gates.
- ⛔ **TWO BYTE-EXACT HAND-DERIVATIONS DO NOT CLOSE IT — BOTH WERE ON THE SPIKE LANE.** ⚠️ **An agreement between a derivation and a measurement of the *same* lane says nothing about the other lane**, which is §9c's same-authorship law wearing its plainest costume.
- ⛔ **AND THE CLASS HAS ZERO CALLERS AND ZERO TESTS.** `Capture`, `BuildZoneA`, `BuildZoneB`, `BuildZoneC` and `ResolvePlace` are called from **nowhere** in `Source/`; the only test file in the lane never constructs a snapshot. ⇒ **Every §8/§10 sentence about what the shipped builder DOES is an argument about code that has never executed** — which is why *"landed"*, *"under QA"* and *"observable"* are now written with **"unexecuted"** beside them throughout this section. **TASK-423 is the first caller and owes the first observation.**

#### 12h. ⚖️ THE REPEAT LAW — ONE RUN IS NOT A MEASUREMENT (ruled 2026-08-03, from Jonathan's own hands-on eval)

**Jonathan ran the eval himself in PIE on 2026-08-03.** Two runs completed; a 3rd and 4th were **refused by the harness** — *"a spike job is already running. Queue depth is 1 by design."* ✅ **Record that refusal as a PASS, not an obstacle:** it is §9's queue-depth-1 contract working exactly as designed, observed live by the user, and it is the first time that path has been exercised by a human.

- ✅ **THE 20/25 REPRODUCED EXACTLY — SAME SCORE, SAME FIVE FAILING ROWS (`DEV-01` · `DEV-04` · `DEV-07` · `DEV-16` · `DEV-20`).** The overnight pipeline's headline number was **honest**, and it was confirmed by an independent operator on a separate run. ⚖️ **This is the single most reassuring result of the batch and it is stated first**: after a wave whose whole theme was *"figures that were labelled measured and were not"* (§8 🔢 TOKEN PROVENANCE, §12g), the number that actually gated the feature held up under someone else's hands.
- ⚠️ **EXACTLY ONE ROW IS UNSTABLE, AND IT IS `DEV-11`** — it flips between `n:2` and `n:"all"`. **19/25 at 10:44, then 20/25 at 11:00: same binary, same corpus, same device, same fixture.** One row, one field, both runs otherwise byte-stable.
- ⛔ **AND IT IS NOT SAMPLING RANDOMNESS — THE SAMPLER IS `llama_sampler_init_greedy()`, WITH NO TEMPERATURE AND NO SEED.** The obvious explanation is therefore **excluded by construction**, which is why this earns a clause instead of a shrug.
  - 🚩 **THE LEADING HYPOTHESIS IS GPU / KV-CACHE NUMERICAL NONDETERMINISM** (the prefill reuses ~1706 cached tokens, and a reused cache is exactly where non-associative float reduction order can move a near-tie logit). ⛔ **IT IS NOT DIAGNOSED, AND NO TASK MAY SPEC A FIX AS THOUGH IT WERE A FINDING.** That is the RELAYED-DIAGNOSIS LAW turned on our own guess: *a plausible root cause we produced ourselves is still a lead, and a wrong root cause ships a wrong fix plus a wrong lesson.* Whoever eventually diagnoses it does so **against the artifact** — greedy determinism is testable offline, with no corpus, by generating the same prompt N times.
- ⛔ **THE BINDING CONSEQUENCE — THIS IS THE LAW: NO FUTURE WAVE MAY CLAIM A "+1 ROW" GAIN FROM A SINGLE RUN. REPEATS ARE REQUIRED, AND THE SPREAD TRAVELS WITH THE NUMBER.** A one-row delta is now **measured to be inside the instrument's own noise band**, so a single-run *"18 → 19"* is not evidence of anything. Report **N runs, min/median/max, and which rows moved** — the *identity* of the unstable row is the diagnostic, not the score.
  - ⚖️ **AND NOTE PRECISELY WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT DO TO §12f, BECAUSE THE TEMPTATION RUNS BOTH WAYS.** §12f's ledger (*"three waves, one row each: 18 → 19 → 20"*) **was built from single runs.** Its conclusion is ***the rung is CLOSED*** — and a ±1 noise band makes that conclusion **STRONGER**, not weaker: if the per-wave yield is indistinguishable from noise, the lever is even more clearly at its stop. ⛔ **The reverse move is barred: nobody may cite this clause to argue that a wave "actually gained more than it reported" and therefore deserves another loop.** Noise is symmetric; a claim that needs it to point one way is not using it as noise.
  - ⚠️ **IT APPLIES TO THE HOLDOUT TOO, AND §12a's ONE-SHOT IS NOT A CONTRADICTION — IT IS THE CONSTRAINT ON *HOW* THE REPEAT IS TAKEN.** The holdout is opened **once**; the repeat therefore happens **inside that single opening** — N generations against the same file in the same session, reported as a distribution — **never as a second opening on a later day.** A holdout re-opened to "check" a number is spent twice, which is spent.

## Settings screen + the assistant CONFIRM STEP + the non-orderable-kind guard (2026-08-03)

Added 2026-08-03 on Jonathan's directive, verbatim: *"I'd shrug and re-order, but lets go ahead and add the confirm step, and make it a toggle in settings. We do not have a settings option so go ahead and add it to the main menu"* (batch **SETTINGS+CONFIRM**, TASK-436..449). Three things ship: a **settings screen that does not exist today**, reachable from the main menu and persisted across sessions; the **confirm-before-execute step** for the assistant, with a toggle in that screen; and the **non-orderable-kind guard**, which is model-independent and is not a settings item at all.

### 1. ⚖️ THE UN-GATE IS A DELIBERATE OVERRIDE BY JONATHAN, AND IT IS RECORDED AS ONE — IT IS NOT A BYPASS

> **Jonathan was asked explicitly whether Wave 1 should stay gated on bar #5, and he chose: UN-GATE WAVE 1, BUILD IT ALL. That overrides his own standing gate. It is his to override, he made it with the evidence in front of him, and it must never be written up as a bypass, a lapse, or an agent's initiative.**

- ⛔ **BAR #5 ITSELF NEVER CLEARED, AND THAT STAYS ON THE RECORD, UNSOFTENED.** Prompt-level tuning topped out at **20/25 against a gate of 22 *and* zero refuse-class failures** (§12f). Nothing here retro-grades that number, and **no artifact may describe Wave 1 as unblocked *because the ladder succeeded*.** It was unblocked **because the user weighed the failure modes and accepted them.** Those are different sentences and only one of them is true.
- ✅ **HIS STATED GROUNDS, FROM HIS OWN HANDS-ON PLAYTEST, AND THEY ARE MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Of the five stable failures, **four are wrong-place / wrong-count on orders the model otherwise understood** — the class he described as *"I'd shrug and re-order"*:

  | row | utterance | what came back | the defect |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `DEV-01` | *send 10 footmen with a sorcerer to the nearest ancient ground* | `{"who":[{"footman",10},{"sorcerer",1}],"where":"nearest_mine"}` | **units RIGHT, place WRONG** |
  | `DEV-16` | *infantry back to our castle now* | `{"intent":"fallback","who":"none"}` | **whole-army retreat** |
  | `DEV-20` | *clerics follow me* | `{"cleric","n":1}` | **1 instead of all** |
  | `DEV-07` | *charge with the footmen* | `{"intent":"charge","who":"none"}` | **selection silently dropped** |
  | `DEV-04` | *send the catapults at the enemy base* | `{"sapper","n":1}` at `enemy_castle` | ⛔ **INVENTS A UNIT HE DOES NOT OWN** |

- ⚖️ **THAT TABLE IS THE CONFIRM STEP'S ENTIRE DESIGN RATIONALE, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS IS A REASONABLE CALL RATHER THAN A GAMBLE. 4 OF THE 5 ARE EXACTLY WHAT A CONFIRM STEP CATCHES** — a wrong place and a wrong count are **visible on the ground before anything moves**, which is precisely what §1 already said constrained decoding could never guarantee (*"confirm-before-execute with ghost circles on the ground is a requirement, not polish"*). **The feature that closes the gap was in the design from day one; the eval measured how much it is needed.**
- ⛔ **`DEV-04` IS DIFFERENT IN KIND, 3/3 REPRODUCIBLE, AND PROMPT WORK WILL NOT FIX IT** (§12f's categorical half). **It gets a CODE GUARD, not a model** — see §5 below.

### 2. NAMING + FOLDER LAW (the cross-task contract for the settings lane)

| Thing | Exact name | Location |
|---|---|---|
| Settings persistence | `USiegeSettingsSaveGame` (`USaveGame`) | game — `SiegeSettingsSaveGame.h/.cpp` |
| SaveGame slot name | **`"SiegeSettings"`**, user index **0** | ⇒ `Saved/SaveGames/SiegeSettings.sav` |
| Settings owner | `USiegeSettingsSubsystem` (`UGameInstanceSubsystem`) | game — `SiegeSettingsSubsystem.h/.cpp` |
| Settings log category | **`LogSiegeSettings`** | declared/defined in `SiegeSettingsSubsystem.h/.cpp` |
| Settings widget C++ base | `USettingsMenuWidget` (`UUserWidget`) | game — `SettingsMenuWidget.h/.cpp` |
| Reserved widget asset name (see §3) | `WBP_SettingsMenu` | `/Game/UI/WBP_SettingsMenu` — **RESERVED, not authored in v1** |
| Main-menu entry button | `Btn_Settings` | additive on the existing `/Game/UI/WBP_MainMenu` |
| The setting | `bAssistantConfirmBeforeExecute` — **default `true`** | on `USiegeSettingsSaveGame` |
| Orderability accessors | `IsKindOrderable(FName) const` · `GetOrderableCount(FName) const` | game — `USiegeAssistantSnapshot` |
| The guard | `ValidateCommandAgainstSnapshot(...)` | game — `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h/.cpp` |

- **`USaveGame` AND NOT `UGameUserSettings` — RULED, WITH THE REASON, BECAUSE THE OTHER ANSWER LOOKS MORE IDIomatic.** `UGameUserSettings` is the engine's home for **video/audio/input** settings and adopting it means a `GameUserSettingsClassName` edit in `DefaultEngine.ini` — **a shipped-config change plus a new engine coupling, to store one gameplay bool.** `USiegeDeckSaveGame` + a fixed slot is **this project's proven, package-safe, runtime-writable idiom** (CONVENTIONS "Deck-builder & saved decks (M6)"), it needs no config edit, and it is what the next setting will also want. ⚠️ **If a genuine graphics/audio settings pass ever lands, `UGameUserSettings` is the right home FOR THOSE and the two coexist** — that is not a contradiction and does not re-open this.
- **THE SUBSYSTEM IS A `UGameInstanceSubsystem` BECAUSE THE SETTING CROSSES A LEVEL TRAVEL, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REQUIREMENT.** It is written in **`L_MainMenu`** and read in **`L_Arena`**, mid-match, at confirm time. A widget-owned or controller-owned value does not survive `OpenLevel` (the Input-mode ownership law is this same lesson in a different costume). Precedents: `USiegeSessionSubsystem`, `USiegeLlamaSubsystem`.
- ⚠️ **LOAD ONCE, SAVE ON CHANGE, AND NEVER READ THE DISK FROM A GAMEPLAY PATH.** `Initialize()` loads the slot (missing/unreadable ⇒ **C++ defaults**, logged once, never a crash — the `USiegeDeckSaveGame` null-safety contract). `SetAssistantConfirmEnabled` writes the in-memory value **and** saves the slot. **The confirm-time read is an in-memory getter.** A `LoadGameFromSlot` on the order path would be a disk hit inside a mid-battle interaction.

### 3. 🔨 RULING — THE SETTINGS WIDGET'S TREE IS CODE-AUTHORED. THIS IS A **SECOND, SEPARATE** NAMED EXCEPTION AND IT DOES NOT INHERIT FROM §6 RULING A

⚠️ **§6 ruling A is scoped to `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` ONLY and says in as many words that citing it for another widget is a misuse. So this is a NEW ruling, argued on its own facts** — the manager's to make, made here, and equally narrow.

`USettingsMenuWidget` builds its tree in `RebuildWidget()` via `WidgetTree->ConstructWidget<>` and ships with **no `.uasset`.** Reasoning:
1. **The `U<Name>Widget` ↔ `WBP_<Name>` law's PURPOSE is satisfied vacuously.** That law exists so an artist's asset name and a programmer's code reference cannot drift. **With no asset there is nothing to drift.** The law is not being broken; it has no subject.
2. ⛔ **THE ALTERNATIVE IS THIS PROJECT'S MOST EXPENSIVE UI FAILURE MODE.** A new `WBP_` starts as a **duplicate of a donor** under the template-donor rule, and **a duplicated+reparented WidgetBlueprint has silently broken RUNTIME repaint here — design-time fine, ~9 wasted fixes.** A code-authored tree cannot have that defect, and it cannot have the `K2Node_CustomEvent`-masquerading-as-a-BIE defect that hid the health-bar bug five times.
3. **The one route that avoids BOTH — authoring a fresh design-time tree by hand — is a JONATHAN HAND-STEP** (proven at TASK-355: `unreal.new_object` design-time widgets skip the designer's GUID registration, so the manual UMG step was what cleared that wall). **A human step on the critical path is legitimate but expensive, and it is not owed for a two-control panel.**

**The exception's five conditions — a ruling, not a waiver, and all five are QA criteria:**
- **(a) SCOPE.** `USettingsMenuWidget` **only**. Every other new widget on this project still obeys the `WBP_` law. Citing THIS clause for a third widget is the same misuse §6(a) names.
- **(b) THE ESCAPE HATCH IS BUILT IN, AT ZERO COST.** Every child is declared `UPROPERTY(meta=(BindWidgetOptional))` with the exact pinned names below, and `RebuildWidget()` **constructs a child only if that member is still null**. A `WBP_SettingsMenu` authored later wins automatically with **zero C++ change**.
  - ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-08-03 — THIS CLAUSE SAID *"after `Super::RebuildWidget()`"* AND THAT ORDERING WAS WRONG. IT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A BLANK WIDGET, AND THE WRONG TEXT IS STRUCK RATHER THAN DELETED SO NOBODY "RESTORES" IT.** ~~constructs a child only if that member is still null **after `Super::RebuildWidget()`**~~
  - ✅ **THE CORRECT ORDER, VERIFIED FIRST-HAND IN THE INSTALLED ENGINE (manager, raw `Read`): BUILD THE TREE AND SET `WidgetTree->RootWidget` **FIRST**, THEN `return Super::RebuildWidget();`.** `UserWidget.cpp:1214` is one line and it settles it: `TSharedRef<SWidget> UserRootWidget = WidgetTree->RootWidget ? WidgetTree->RootWidget->TakeWidget() : TSharedRef<SWidget>(SNew(SSpacer));` followed immediately by `return UserRootWidget;`. ⇒ **Super READS `RootWidget` and RETURNS. Anything constructed after it is discarded — the Slate widget already handed back is the `SSpacer`.**
  - ⚠️ **AND IT FAILS IN THE SIGNATURE THIS PROJECT KEEPS PAYING FOR: THE WIDGET RENDERS EMPTY WHILE PASSING EVERY PROPERTY READBACK.** The `UPROPERTY` members are all non-null and correctly typed, so a tree/property check reports a perfectly healthy widget — the same false green as MCP readback on visually-broken UMG and the 6/6 binding readback on TASK-355's controls stacked in a corner.
  - ✅ **THE SEMANTIC HALF SURVIVES UNCHANGED, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A ONE-WORD FIX AND NOT A REDESIGN:** `BindWidget*` members resolve inside `Initialize()` (`WidgetBlueprintGeneratedClass.cpp:275`), **long before `RebuildWidget()` ever runs** — so *"only if still null"* is **fully determined before Super would have been called.** The escape hatch works identically; only the ordering was wrong.
  - 📌 **TWO PROGRAMMERS INDEPENDENTLY REFUSED THIS TEXT AND BOTH WERE RIGHT** — TASK-437 departed deliberately and QA ruled the departure correct and **the spec wrong**; TASK-444 shipped `ConstructConsoleTree(); return Super::RebuildWidget();` with a code comment explaining exactly why. ⚖️ **No shipped widget is defective. The ONLY defective artifact was this law** — which is the strongest possible argument for correcting it in place: it was on track to break the *next* author, who would have had no reason to doubt it.
- **(c) THE ASSET NAME IS RESERVED NOW.** `/Game/UI/WBP_SettingsMenu` may be used by nothing else. Taking the fallback costs **one art task and zero C++ change.**
- **(d) THE CONTRACT IS UNCHANGED.** `BindWidgetOptional` members · `BlueprintCallable` wrappers · **FString/int32/bool/uint8-only `BlueprintImplementableEvent`s** — the `USessionMenuWidget` shipped contract, cloned exactly.
- **(e) VERIFICATION IS A HUMAN PIXEL CHECK.** There is no `.uasset` to read back, and **MCP readback has repeatedly passed on visually-broken UMG here.** Correctness closes on **rendered pixels or Jonathan's eyes**, never on a tree/property readback.

**Pinned child-widget member names** (the C++ and any future WBP both read this line): `RootPanel` (`UVerticalBox`) · `BackdropBorder` (`UBorder`) · `TitleText` (`UTextBlock`) · `ConfirmToggleCheckBox` (`UCheckBox`) · `ConfirmToggleLabelText` (`UTextBlock`) · `ConfirmToggleHintText` (`UTextBlock`) · `BackButton` (`UButton`) · `BackLabelText` (`UTextBlock`).

- ⚠️ **`BackdropBorder` IS HIT-TEST **VISIBLE**, AND THIS IS A CORRECTNESS REQUIREMENT, NOT STYLING — IT IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE OF `WBP_SessionMenu`'s BACKDROP.** The settings panel is added **on top of `WBP_MainMenu`** (see §4), so a hit-test-**invisible** plate would let clicks fall through to Play / Sandbox / Deck Builder / Quit **while the panel is open and looks modal.** TASK-355's backdrop was deliberately `HIT_TEST_INVISIBLE` because it sat over nothing clickable; **copying that choice here ships a live click-through into "Quit".** ⇒ **QA criterion: with the panel open, a click on the backdrop reaches nothing behind it.**

### 4. NAVIGATION — THE PANEL IS ADDED **ON TOP** OF THE MAIN MENU AND `Back` REMOVES ONLY ITSELF

- `Btn_Settings` on `WBP_MainMenu` does **`CreateWidget(USettingsMenuWidget)` → `AddToViewport(ZOrder 10)`**. ⛔ **It does NOT `RemoveFromParent` the main menu.** `Back` calls `RemoveFromParent(self)` and the menu is simply revealed again, **already alive and already correct.**
- ⚖️ **WHY THIS AND NOT THE DECK-BUILDER'S REMOVE/RE-ADD PATTERN.** Remove-and-recreate requires the settings widget to hold a **soft class reference back to `/Game/UI/WBP_MainMenu`** and to re-create it on Back — **navigation state living in the leaf**, plus one more asset path to get wrong, plus a re-created menu that is not the object the player left. Overlay has **no state, no soft path, and no re-creation.** The deck-builder's pattern predates this and is not being changed.
- ⚠️ **THE MAIN-MENU EDIT IS ADDITIVE AND THE FOUR EXISTING ENTRIES ARE UNTOUCHABLE.** Order becomes **Play (vs Bot) → Sandbox (No Bot) → Deck Builder → Multiplayer → Settings → Quit** — spliced **before the Quit block so Quit stays last**, exactly as TASK-355 spliced Multiplayer. Follow the shipped idiom **character-for-character** (font 28, `MakeMargin(24,12,24,12)`, `HAlign_Fill`), **granular ops only, never `write_graph_dsl`**, and the **`add_event`-first pattern** that defeats the `AssignOnClicked` auto-rename trap. **Prove the five pre-existing entries are character-identical by full-graph DSL diff** (ruling-3 idiom).
- **V1 SCOPE: THE SETTINGS SCREEN IS REACHABLE FROM THE MAIN MENU ONLY.** No in-match pause entry — Jonathan named the main menu and nothing else. The subsystem is the level-crossing part, so an in-match entry later is **one widget task and zero C++ change**; recorded so nobody builds a pause menu on spec.

### 5. ⚖️ THE CONFIRM STEP + THE TOGGLE — AND THE ONE LAW THAT MAKES THE TOGGLE SAFE

**The confirm step is the `AwaitConfirm` state that Wave 1's B2 already owed** (§1: *"confirm-before-execute with ghost circles on the ground is a requirement, not polish"*). It is not new architecture; **what is new is that it is now user-visible and user-controllable.**

- **WHAT `AwaitConfirm` SHOWS:** (i) a **game-authored** one-line summary of the parsed order, filled from reason codes and the template table — ⛔ **never a model-produced string** (§3), and (ii) the **ghost circles on the ground** via the shipped `ASiegePlayerController::SpawnGroupCircleDecal`. **Accept executes; Cancel discards and returns to `Idle` with NOTHING partially executed.**
- **THE TOGGLE'S DEFAULT IS `true` — ON — AND THE DEFAULT IS ARGUED FROM THE MEASUREMENT, NOT FROM CAUTION.** §1's table shows **4 of 5 stable failures are wrong-place / wrong-count**, i.e. precisely the class a ground preview makes obvious in one glance. **A player who turns it off has chosen to trade that; a player who never saw it did not.**
- ⛔ **THE LAW THAT MATTERS MOST HERE — THE TOGGLE REMOVES A **HUMAN REVIEW STEP** AND NEVER A **MACHINE CHECK**.** With the toggle OFF, the FSM skips `AwaitConfirm` and executes. **It does NOT skip the parse, the `Kinds.Num() == Counts.Num()` invariant, the shortfall/clarification path, the eligibility checks, the authority refusal, or the §5-below non-orderable guard.** ⚠️ **A future task that "simplifies" the off-path by short-circuiting any of those is turning a UX preference into a safety regression, and it is an automatic QA FAIL.** The tell is simple: **every check that runs with the toggle ON runs with it OFF.**
- ⛔ **AND THE TOGGLE TOUCHES NOTHING OUTSIDE THE ASSISTANT.** §2 is unchanged and unchangeable: every keyboard command still works byte-identically, the console is never a requirement, and **no setting may ever gate a key.** A settings entry that can disable a keyboard command is out of scope by law.

### 6. ⛔ THE NON-ORDERABLE-KIND GUARD — MODEL-INDEPENDENT, AND EXPLICITLY **NOT** A ROUTE TO THE GATE

> **The authoritative command path REJECTS any order whose `who[].kind` is not orderable in the live snapshot — BEFORE execution, independent of the model — surfaced as the existing unsupported-ask outcome.**

- **THIS IS §1's THIRD OWNER FINALLY BEING IMPLEMENTED.** §1 says *"grammar guarantees existence, **executor guarantees legality**, FSM owns the conversation"* — and §12f measured that **the executor half was asserted in this document and absent from the code.** `DEV-04` emitted a live, well-formed order for a kind the roster line itself prints as **`0 orderable`**. **Orderability is per-match STATE, not identity**; §1 forbids the grammar from encoding it and four prompt attempts have not taught it ⇒ **only the command layer can refuse it, and that layer did not check.**
- ⛔ **IT DOES NOT MAKE `DEV-04` PASS THE EVAL, AND NOBODY MAY REPORT IT AS PROGRESS ON BAR #5.** **The eval scores EMITTED JSON, not EXECUTED ACTIONS.** The guard changes what the game *does*; it changes nothing about what the model *emits*. This is **shipped safety**, full stop. ⚠️ **Any handoff, QA report or board entry that lists this guard under an accuracy heading is factually wrong and is failed at the gate.**
- ✅ **IT IS UNIT-TESTABLE WITH NO MODEL RESIDENT, AND THAT IS THE REASON IT IS CHEAP.** The predicate reads the roster the snapshot already computes (`KindOrderable`, live today at `SiegeAssistantSnapshot.cpp:490/559/565/1131` and currently **private**). Expose `IsKindOrderable(FName) const` / `GetOrderableCount(FName) const`, add a **pure** validator, and test it against a hand-populated roster. **No `UWorld`, no GGUF, no PIE.**
- ⚠️ **THE REFUSAL REUSES THE EXISTING UNSUPPORTED-ASK OUTCOME AND INVENTS NO NEW PLAYER-FACING SURFACE.** `{"ask":"unsupported"}` already exists in the grammar and already has a template. **The guard routes to it; it does not author a new one** (§3 — every player-facing string is a game-authored template).

### 7. ⚠️ THE FIRST-EXECUTION AUDIT OF `USiegeAssistantSnapshot` IS OWED AT THIS BATCH, AND IT IS A REAL DELIVERABLE

⛔ **`USiegeAssistantSnapshot` HAS ZERO CALLERS AND ZERO TESTS TODAY** (§12g). `Capture`, `BuildZoneA`, `BuildZoneB`, `BuildZoneC` and `ResolvePlace` are called from **nowhere** in `Source/`. **Its truncation logging has never emitted a line** — every *"now observable"* clause in §8/§10 describes code that **has never run at all.**

- ⇒ **WAVE 1 IS ITS FIRST EXECUTION, AND AN AUDIT IS OWED AT THAT MOMENT** (`handoffs/TASK-434-buildmaster.md` §6 item 5). ⚠️ **"Observable" is not "observed", and "reviewed" is not "executed"** — this is §9c's promoted law (*where an artifact has a parser, the parser is the reviewer of record*) applied to a class whose parser is **the running game**.
- **WHAT THE AUDIT MUST PRODUCE, at first live capture:** the **real captured snapshot pasted in full with its character count** · **`zoneB_chars` / `zoneC_chars` read off the live builder** and compared to the 68 / 887 fixture figures and the 738 shipped-builder figure · **whether the truncation log fired, and if not, that it was not supposed to** · and **whether `MaxRosterKinds = 8` truncated on a real board.** ⛔ **An audit that reports "no problems" without stating which of these it actually observed has not been performed.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE STANDING CONDITION TRAVELS WITH EVERY NUMBER:** the measured accuracy is **the number for a board that FITS the cap.** A live match can exceed 8 kinds. **Shipped behaviour on a wider board is WORSE, never better** — state it beside the number, every time.

### 8. PINNED CROSS-TASK SIGNATURE REGISTRY (this batch's link contract)

⚠️ **UBT compiles the whole module.** Every task in this batch compiles against this list **character-for-character**; "improving" a pinned signature breaks the link and is an **automatic QA FAIL.** Several of these **cannot compile alone and are not expected to** — they compile together at the batch's integration gate (the TASK-395/396 and TASK-416/417 precedent). **Do not open a QA loop over it.**

```cpp
// ── SiegeSettingsSaveGame.h ───────────────────────────────────────────────
UCLASS()
class USiegeSettingsSaveGame : public USaveGame
{
public:
    // Assistant: show the parsed order + ghost circles for review before executing.
    UPROPERTY() bool bAssistantConfirmBeforeExecute = true;
};

// ── SiegeSettingsSubsystem.h ──────────────────────────────────────────────

UCLASS()
class USiegeSettingsSubsystem : public UGameInstanceSubsystem
{
public:
    static const TCHAR* SettingsSlotName;   // == TEXT("SiegeSettings")

    UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure,     Category = "Siegebound|Settings") bool IsAssistantConfirmEnabled() const;
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintCallable, Category = "Siegebound|Settings") void SetAssistantConfirmEnabled(bool bEnabled);

    UPROPERTY(BlueprintAssignable, Category = "Siegebound|Settings") FOnSiegeSettingsChanged OnSettingsChanged;

    virtual void Initialize(FSubsystemCollectionBase& Collection) override;   // loads slot; missing ⇒ defaults, logged once
};

// ── SettingsMenuWidget.h ──────────────────────────────────────────────────

class USettingsMenuWidget : public UUserWidget
{
public:
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintCallable, Category = "Siegebound|Settings") void BackPressed();
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintCallable, Category = "Siegebound|Settings") void ConfirmTogglePressed(bool bChecked);

};

// ── SiegeAssistantSnapshot.h — ADDITIVE to the §9 pin, nothing renamed ────
bool  IsKindOrderable(FName Kind) const;        // reads the roster Capture() already tallies
int32 GetOrderableCount(FName Kind) const;      // 0 == not orderable

// Free function, NOT a member. PURE: no UWorld, no UObject state beyond the
// two arrays. This is what makes the guard unit-testable with no model resident.
enum class ESiegeAssistantRejectReason : uint8 { None, KindNotOrderable, KindUnknown };
bool ValidateCommandAgainstSnapshot(const FSiegeAssistantCommand& Command,
                                    const TArray<FSiegeAssistantRosterEntry>& Roster,
                                    ESiegeAssistantRejectReason& OutReason,
                                    FName& OutOffendingKind);
```

- ⚠️ **`ValidateCommandAgainstSnapshot` TAKES THE ROSTER ARRAY, NOT THE SNAPSHOT OBJECT — DELIBERATELY, AND FOR THE SAME REASON `USiegeAssistantGrammar::Build` TAKES `TArray<FName>`.** A pure function over plain data needs **no `UWorld`, no `Capture()`, and no engine state to test**, so its automation tests are genuinely cheap. **A later "tidy-up" that changes it to take a `const USiegeAssistantSnapshot*` deletes that property and is rejected on sight.**
- ⛔ **NO NEW REPLICATED SURFACE ANYWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** Every header and every handoff states it verbatim: **"adds no replicated property, no new replicated class, no new relevancy tier."** *"There is nothing to declare"* only counts when it is stated; **"tier not declared" is a QA FAIL.** The settings value is **client-local by construction** — it governs a local review step, never an authoritative outcome.

### 9. ⛔ THE QUIET-MODULE LAW BINDS THIS BATCH HARDER THAN USUAL — SAY SO ON THE BOARD

**This batch is game-module heavy: seven programmer tasks land in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/`.** Under the QUIET-MODULE LAW, **file-disjointness is not build-disjointness** — any in-flight `.cpp` in that module can fail any other lane's compile gate.

- **Single-owner-per-file is necessary and NOT sufficient.** The tasks are parallel-safe **to WRITE**; they are **never** safe beside a **BUILD**. ⇒ **Exactly one compile gate in this batch, and the module is quiesced before it runs** — every C++ task finished, handoff written, not merely non-conflicting.
- ✅ **`Plugins/SiegeLlama/` IS A SEPARATE UBT MODULE and is genuinely parallel-safe against a game-module gate.** Prefer plugin-side work when a game gate is imminent (§6 ruling B's now twice-demonstrated payoff).
- ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-03 — MY PREMISE ABOUT `SiegePlayerController.{h,cpp}` WAS WRONG, AND THE WRONG VERSION IS STRUCK RATHER THAN QUIETLY DELETED** (*"THE MANAGER IS NOT EXEMPT"* — correcting the record is cheap; leaving a wrong lesson in a law file is not). ~~The FOLLOW batch's uncommitted work is sitting in those files right now, so the Wave-1 controller task edits additively on top of it and the integration commit splits the two lanes BY ATTRIBUTION because they cannot be split by pathspec.~~
  - ✅ **THE FILES WERE CLEAN. Jonathan had already committed the FOLLOW lane himself (`e70f5ba`)** — the standing *"Jonathan self-commits"* pattern, which I failed to check for. ⇒ **The whole working diff in those files is the Wave-1 controller task's own work**, so ⛔ **THE ATTRIBUTION SPLIT IS RETIRED: the lanes are already separated BY COMMIT, which is strictly stronger than a split argued in a commit message.**
  - ⚖️ **THE LESSON THAT GENERALISES, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS CORRECTION IS WORTH THE SPACE: A "THIS FILE IS DIRTY" PREMISE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE WORKING TREE, AND IT DECAYS FASTER THAN ANYTHING ELSE A SPEC CAN ASSERT.** Board state, handoffs and even a `git log` reading go stale the moment Jonathan touches the repo — and **he commits and pushes his own work without telling the pipeline.** ⇒ **A spec may say *"run `git status --porcelain` and branch on the answer"*; it may NOT say *"it will be dirty"*.** The pre-flight stays mandatory; the **prediction** is what was wrong.
  - 📌 **PROVENANCE, STATED BECAUSE THE RELAYED-DIAGNOSIS LAW BINDS ME TOO: this correction was verified against git BY THE ORCHESTRATOR, first-hand. The manager has no git tool and did NOT independently re-derive it** — recorded as a first-hand verification by the party holding the instrument, not as a manager finding.

### 11. ⛔ THE CODE-AUTHORED-TREE LIFECYCLE TRAP — `NativeOnInitialized` IS TOO EARLY, AND IT FAILS IN COMPLETE SILENCE (added 2026-08-03; hit TWICE in one wave)

> **In a widget whose tree is built in `RebuildWidget()`, child-widget wiring MUST NOT live in `NativeOnInitialized()`. `Initialize()` runs LONG BEFORE `RebuildWidget()`, so every `BindWidgetOptional` member is still NULL — the bindings bind to nothing and no-op FOREVER, with no error, no log, no ensure and no crash.**

- ⚠️ **THIS BINDS *BOTH* CODE-AUTHORED RULINGS — §6 ruling A (`USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget`) and §3 above (`USettingsMenuWidget`) — so it is written ONCE here rather than duplicated into each condition list.** ⇒ **Both rulings now carry SIX conditions, not five: (f) child wiring happens in `NativeConstruct()` (or later), never in `NativeOnInitialized()`, and QA checks it explicitly.**
- ⚖️ **WHY IT EARNS A LAW INSTEAD OF A CODE COMMENT: IT IS THE EXACT SIGNATURE THIS PROJECT KEEPS PAYING FOR.** *No crash, no error, no log line* — the same shape as the duplicated+reparented WidgetBlueprint's silent runtime-repaint break (~9 wasted fixes), the `K2Node_CustomEvent` masquerading as a BIE (hid the health-bar defect 5×), `Build.bat`'s exit 0 on a failed build, and MCP readback passing on visually-broken UMG. **A widget that renders perfectly and responds to nothing looks like a gameplay bug, and it is a lifecycle bug.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE ORDINARY `WBP_` INSTINCT IS PRECISELY WHAT WALKS INTO IT.** For an asset-backed widget the designer-authored tree exists by `Initialize()`, so `NativeOnInitialized` **is** the idiomatic place — which is why a competent author reaches for it. ⇒ **The trap is not carelessness; it is correct habit applied to the one case that inverts it, which is why declaring the exception is cheaper than expecting people to re-derive it.**
- ⛔ **THE ASYMMETRY, NAMED EXPLICITLY, BECAUSE THE SHIPPED PRECEDENT IN THIS REPO POINTS THE WRONG WAY (added 2026-08-03 at QA's request — this is the sentence that stops the recurrence).** **`USessionMenuWidget` uses `NativeOnInitialized` (`SessionMenuWidget.cpp:13`) AND IT IS CORRECT TO DO SO — because it is a WBP**, whose designer tree is already resolved by `Initialize()`. ⇒ ⚠️ **COPYING THAT SHIPPED PRECEDENT INTO A CODE-AUTHORED WIDGET IS THE BUG.** The two cases look identical in the header and differ only in **where the tree comes from**:

  | | tree exists by `Initialize()`? | wire children in |
  |---|---|---|
  | **WBP-backed** (`USessionMenuWidget`, any `WBP_*`) | ✅ yes — the designer tree is loaded | **`NativeOnInitialized`** ✅ |
  | **Code-authored** (`USettingsMenuWidget`, `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget`) | ⛔ **no — the tree does not exist until `RebuildWidget()`** | **`NativeConstruct`** ✅ |

  ⚖️ ***"The existing widget does it this way"* IS EXACTLY THE REASONING THAT WILL REINTRODUCE THIS**, and it is the most reasonable-sounding argument available to whoever hits it next — which is why the precedent is disqualified here **by name** rather than left to be re-derived. ⚠️ **Both rulings' condition (b) is the same trap one step earlier** (`Super::RebuildWidget()` ordering) — **the two failures share a cause: assuming the tree exists earlier than it does.**
- ✅ **THE ESCAPE-HATCH CONDITION MAKES THIS STRICTLY WORSE IF IGNORED, WHICH IS THE PART TO NOTICE.** Both rulings require *"construct a child only if it is still null after `Super::RebuildWidget()`"* so a future `WBP_` wins for free — **so the null-child state is DESIGNED and permanent-looking, not transient.** A binding taken against it will never be repaired by a later frame.
- **QA CRITERION:** for any code-authored tree, **name the function each child binding is made in.** *"Bindings are wired"* is not a review; **`NativeConstruct`** is.

### 12. ⛔ `SlateCore` IS LOAD-BEARING AND STAYS — ONLY THE COMMENT'S EXAMPLE WENT STALE (added 2026-08-03)

`GitClaudeUnrealTest.Build.cs:30` justifies the `SlateCore` dependency by naming **`SiegeAssistantInputProbe.cpp`** as the first file to reference a real SlateCore *symbol* — and **that file was deliberately DELETED** with the console widget's arrival (§6 ruling A / the throwaway-probe ruling).

- ⛔ **DO NOT REMOVE `SlateCore` BECAUSE THE FILE IT NAMES IS GONE.** The dependency is still required: **`USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget` is now the module's only `ETextCommit`-in-a-`UFUNCTION` signature and its only `FSlateApplication` user.** Removing it reproduces **the exact 16-unresolved-externals link failure led by `Z_Construct_UEnum_SlateCore_ETextCommit`** that the comment exists to prevent.
- ⚠️ **THE MECHANISM, RESTATED SO THE FIX IS NOT RE-DERIVED WRONG: `Slate` PUBLICLY DEPENDS on `SlateCore`, so it propagates SlateCore's INCLUDE PATHS — which is why the module compiled clean for a long time without it — but a dependency DOES NOT propagate the IMPORT LIBRARY.** ⇒ **The gap is invisible until some file references an actual symbol rather than merely a type.** A "cleanup" here compiles fine and fails at LINK.
- ⇒ **THE OWED EDIT IS ONE SENTENCE OF COMMENT, NOT A DEPENDENCY CHANGE:** re-point the example from the deleted probe to `SiegeAssistantConsoleWidget.cpp`. ⚖️ **Generalised: when a justifying COMMENT names an artifact that is later deleted, re-point the comment — never delete the thing it justifies.** A stale rationale is a documentation defect; acting on it is an outage.

### 13. ⛔ `TestEqual` ON `FString` IS CASE-INSENSITIVE — A BYTE OR CASING CLAIM MUST USE `TestEqualSensitive` (added 2026-08-03)

> **`FAutomationTestBase::TestEqual(const TCHAR*, const FString&, const FString&)` compares case-INSENSITIVELY. `TestEqualSensitive` exists precisely because it does. ⇒ ANY assertion whose CLAIM is byte-identity, casing, determinism or "character-for-character" MUST use `TestEqualSensitive`. Using `TestEqual` for those makes the test VACUOUS — it cannot fail for the property it names.**

- ⚠️ **THIS IS THIS REPO'S OWN NAMED FAILURE CLASS — AN AUTOMATED GUARDRAIL THAT REPORTS SAFE** — alongside `Build.bat` returning exit 0 on a failed build, `rig_character.py` silently ignoring an override that did not exist, and MCP readback passing on visually-broken UMG. **Three confident greens that were not real.** ⛔ **This instance is worse in one specific way: it LOOKS automated, it is committed, and it PASSED A HUMAN QA GATE.**
- **THE WORKED EXAMPLE, VERIFIED AT THE ARTIFACT** (`Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/Tests/SiegeAssistantGrammarTest.cpp`): `:464` asserts *"Symbol casing does not change the emitted grammar"* **with `TestEqual`** — and the comment immediately above it (`:456-459`) explains that the guarantee under test is that `Build` **lower-cases every symbol**, *"asserting the guarantee rather than the habit."* ⇒ ⚖️ **The test states its own intent in prose and then uses the one comparator that cannot check it.** `:454` does the same for a **byte-identity** claim.
- ⚖️ **THE TELL, GENERALISED, BECAUSE IT IS REUSABLE BEYOND THIS FUNCTION: READ THE ASSERTION'S OWN MESSAGE STRING AND ASK WHETHER THE COMPARATOR CAN DETECT WHAT THE MESSAGE PROMISES.** The words *byte-identical*, *casing*, *character-for-character*, *deterministic* and *unchanged* are all **stronger than equality-ignoring-case**. **A test whose name is a stronger claim than its comparator is a documentation of intent, not a test.**
- ✅ **INTEGER, BOOL AND `.Num()` COMPARISONS ARE UNAFFECTED** — do not churn them.
- ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-08-03 — THIS CLAUSE SAID THE DEFECT IS *"CONFINED TO `FString` OVERLOADS"*. THAT IS FALSE, AND IT IS FALSE IN THE DIRECTION THAT MAKES THE LAW UNDER-PROTECT.** ~~The defect is confined to `FString` overloads.~~ **The insensitivity spans `const TCHAR*`, `FString`, `FStringView`, `FUtf8StringView`, `FText` AND `FName`.** ⚠️ **No site in the sweep's scope was affected, so nothing was narrowed on this basis — but this law is read by people whose sites are NOT in that scope, and a law that under-states its own reach is worse than one that over-states it.**
- ✅ **THE MECHANISM, VERIFIED FIRST-HAND IN THE INSTALLED ENGINE (manager, raw `Read`) — cite these, not this paragraph:** `AutomationTest.h:1997-2000` the `FString` overload is a thin forward to the `TCHAR*` one · **`AutomationTest.cpp:2163` — `TestEqual` compares with `FCString::Stricmp`** · **`:2295` — `TestEqualSensitive` uses `FCString::Strcmp`.** One character of difference between the two functions, and it is the whole defect.
- ⛔ **THE REMEDY DOES NOT EXIST FOR `FText` OR `FName`, AND THAT GAP IS WHAT THIS CLAUSE MUST GOVERN — BECAUSE AN AUTHOR WHO HITS IT WILL IMPROVISE.** Verified at `AutomationTest.h:2014-2020` + `:2093-2119`: **`TestEqualSensitive` ships overloads for `const TCHAR*`, `FString`, `FStringView`, `FUtf8StringView`** (plus mixed-view and `FString What` variants) — **and NONE for `FText` or `FName`.**
  - ⇒ ✅ **THE RULING: convert EXPLICITLY at the assertion site and assert on the conversion** — `TestEqualSensitive(TEXT("…"), Actual.ToString(), Expected.ToString())`. ⛔ **Never reach for `TestEqual` because the `*Sensitive` overload is missing; the missing overload is the warning, not the permission.**
  - ⚠️ **`FName` CARRIES A SECOND, DEEPER CAVEAT AND IT MUST TRAVEL WITH EVERY `FName` CASING ASSERTION: `FName` COMPARISON IS CASE-INSENSITIVE *BY DESIGN*, AND `FName` ONLY REMEMBERS ITS CASE AT ALL WHEN `WITH_CASE_PRESERVING_NAME` IS COMPILED IN** (it follows `WITH_EDITORONLY_DATA`, = 1 under `EditorContext` automation, **0 in a packaged/Shipping build**). ⇒ ⚖️ **A casing claim about an `FName` is only meaningful through `.ToString()`, and only in an editor-context test. It is NOT a property that survives to a shipped build**, so a test asserting it is testing the editor, not the game. **State that beside any such assertion rather than letting a green editor run imply a shipped guarantee.**
- 📌 **`TestNotEqual` SHARES THE ROOT AND FAILS IN THE *SAFE* DIRECTION — recorded so nobody mis-diagnoses it when it fires.** `AutomationTest.cpp:2219` uses `Stricmp` and `:2329`'s `TestNotEqualSensitive` uses `Strcmp`. ⇒ Two strings differing **only in case** are read as EQUAL, so a `TestNotEqual` asserting they differ **FAILS LOUDLY** rather than passing silently. ⚖️ **Same defect, opposite blast radius: this one costs you an afternoon; `TestEqual` costs you a guarantee you never had.**
- **QA CRITERION, ADDED TO EVERY REVIEW OF A TEST FILE FROM HERE ON:** for each `FString` assertion, **name the claim shape and confirm the comparator matches it.** ⛔ *"The tests pass"* is not a review of a test file — **a vacuous test passes hardest.**

### 17. ⛔ TOOL CALLS THAT REPORT SUCCESS (OR FAIL SILENTLY) AND DO NOTHING — THE STANDING LEDGER (2026-08-03)

**This project's most expensive defect class has ONE shape: an operation that did not happen, reported in a way nobody reads as failure.** Kept as one ledger because the individual entries keep being re-discovered as if they were novel.

| tool / call | what it does | the tell |
|---|---|---|
| **`ObjectTools.set_properties`** | ⛔ **silently returns `false` unless `values` is a JSON *STRING*. Passing an OBJECT — the exact shape `get_properties` RETURNS — mutates NOTHING**, with no error and no log | **a read→modify→write round-trip that looks perfectly correct and changes nothing** |
| `Build.bat` | returns **exit 0 on a FAILED build** (and exit 1 on a success) | parse `Result:`, never `$LASTEXITCODE` |
| MCP UMG readback | passes on **visually broken** widgets (6/6 bindings green while stacked in a 165×48 px corner) | **a readback cannot see geometry** — pixels or a human |
| `TestEqual` (`FString`) | **case-insensitive**; a casing/byte claim cannot fail | §13 |
| `rig_character.py` | silently ignored an override that did not exist | — |
| `git grep` | **blind to untracked files** — every new file in a batch | §14 |
| **`find_nodes(title=…)`** (MCP) | ⛔ **returns 0 for nodes that demonstrably EXIST — a false negative from the tool itself** | **§14 instance 5** — prove the POSITIVE (a terminal pin's `connected = 0`), never a search that found nothing |

- ⚖️ **THE UNIFYING RULE: A TOOL'S RETURN VALUE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE CALL, NOT ABOUT THE WORLD. VERIFY THE EFFECT, NOT THE CALL.** ⇒ **After any MCP mutation, READ BACK the property you believe you changed** — and note the readback's own limits (row 3). ⛔ **"The call returned" is not "the thing happened."**
- ⚠️ **`ObjectTools.set_properties` IS THE WORST OF THESE BECAUSE THE FAILING SHAPE IS THE ONE THE SISTER FUNCTION HANDS YOU.** `get_properties` → edit → `set_properties` is the obvious idiom, and it is the broken one. **Serialise to a JSON string.**

### 16. 🔒⛔ `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` IS RECLASSIFIED FROM **THROWAWAY** TO **LOAD-BEARING TEST INFRASTRUCTURE**. THE DELETION IS **UNSCHEDULED**, NOT DEFERRED (2026-08-03) — amends §12a and Wave-0 manager ruling 4

> ### 🔒 **DELETING `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` WOULD SPEND JONATHAN'S ONE HONEST MEASUREMENT BY DESTROYING THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT CAN TAKE IT.**
> **`Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout2.csv` — committed UNSPENT at `21f7e01` — is authored against the `t0` fixture that lives INSIDE that file, and is scored by `Siege.Llama.SpikeEval`, also inside it.** ⇒ **Delete the file and the sealed corpus becomes UNMEASURABLE.**

- ⚖️ **§12a PROTECTS THE SEAL AGAINST A *FITTED* SCORE. THIS IS THE SAME ONE-SHOT LOST A DIFFERENT WAY — BY DESTROYING THE SCORER — AND THE SPENT-SEAL LAW DID NOT SEE IT COMING BECAUSE IT ONLY EVER LOOKED AT WHO READS THE ROWS.** A holdout is equally lost whether it is graded twice or can never be graded once. ⇒ **The seal's protection now explicitly covers its INSTRUMENT: the fixture it was authored against and the command that scores it.**
- ⛔ **THE DELETION MAY NOT BE RE-SCHEDULED UNTIL A NAMED REPLACEMENT EXISTS FOR *EVERY* CAPABILITY BELOW.** Four, measured, not assumed:
  1. **the frozen `t0` fixture** — the board every gen-2 row asserts against (§12a: *"a row whose truth depends on a live count is only true at t0"*, and **t0's bytes may not move**);
  2. **`Siege.Llama.SpikeEval`** — the only scorer for either corpus generation;
  3. **`Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`** — ⛔ **the ONLY command that prints `zoneA_tok`**, which §12c's ceiling is a **runtime gate** against;
  4. **the partial-tier sampler** — ⛔ **it exists ONLY here**, so the frame-time hitch re-measure is **impossible from the shipped subsystem** (see the TASK-447 correction).
- ⚖️ **AND THE REAL LESSON IS THE PATTERN, NOT THE FILE — THIS IS WHY IT IS LAW RATHER THAN A THIRD DEFERRAL:** the deletion has now been scheduled **three times and blocked three times, ON A DIFFERENT REASON EACH TIME** — (i) *"with no subsystem, deleting leaves ZERO load paths"*; (ii) *"do not delete the instrument that prints `zoneA_tok` before taking the reading"*; (iii) *"it is the sealed corpus's only scorer."*
  > ⛔ **A DELETION THAT KEEPS ACQUIRING NEW REASONS NOT TO HAPPEN IS NOT "BLOCKED" — IT IS AN ARTIFACT THAT WAS MISCLASSIFIED, AND THE CLASSIFICATION IS WHAT MUST CHANGE.**
  **Each deferral was individually correct and each was discovered LATE, by the task standing over the file with the axe.** ⚠️ **Three independent discoveries is not luck holding; it is a warning that the next one may not be found in time.** ⇒ **Wave-0 manager ruling 4 called this file *"throwaway by construction"* and that classification is now FALSIFIED BY MEASUREMENT. It is deleted only by a task whose SOLE deliverable is migrating all four capabilities — never as a step inside a task that is doing something else.**
- ✅ **THE ORIGINAL CONCERN THAT MOTIVATED RULING 4 IS DISCHARGED ON ITS OWN TERMS AND IS NOT RE-OPENED:** *"two model-load paths must never coexist"* was about **shipping** ambiguity.
