The spike is **dev/test-only console surface** (`Siege.Llama.*` via `FAutoConsoleCommand`, never a `UFUNCTION(exec)` on a shipped class), so it is **not a shipping path** — the risk it named was real for a *product* seam and is inert for a *bench*. ⚠️ **What ruling 4 got wrong was not the danger; it was the noun.**
- ⚖️ **§16 IS A DELETION LAW. IT IS NOT A FREEZE. SAID EXPLICITLY 2026-08-03 BECAUSE THE FINE-TUNE RUNG'S STAGE A *EDITS* THIS FILE AND SOMEBODY WILL READ THE LOCK ICON AND STOP.**
  > 🔒 **UNTOUCHABLE FOR DELETION ≠ UNTOUCHABLE.** ✅ **ADDITIVE EDITS TO `SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` ARE NOT MERELY ALLOWED — THEY ARE THE POINT.**
  - **The clause's whole argument is *"this file is the only instrument that can score the sealed corpus."* An instrument that cannot be improved is an instrument that decays.** Every reason listed above for keeping the file is a reason for **investing** in it: new flags on `Siege.Llama.SpikeEval` / `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt` **increase** the four protected capabilities, they do not endanger them.
  - ⛔ **THE THREE THINGS THAT ARE ACTUALLY FROZEN, and they are narrow — everything else in the file is editable under the normal single-owner + QA rules:**
    1. 🔒 **THE `t0` FIXTURE'S BYTES** (§12a: *"a row whose truth depends on a live count is only true at t0"*, and `holdout2` is authored against it). ⚠️ **ADDING a NEW fixture beside `t0` is not editing `t0`** — that is how `tA`–`tF` land.
    2. 🔒 **`Siege.Llama.SpikeEval`'s and `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`'s EXISTING behaviour and output contract** — new flags are **additive with defaults that reproduce today's output exactly.** ⛔ **A flag that changes the default path has changed the instrument, and every number on record was taken with the old one.**
    3. 🔒 **THE FILE'S CONTINUED EXISTENCE** — the original clause, unchanged.
  - ⚠️ **AND THE CONVERSE IS ALSO STATED, because it is the real risk: AN EDIT THAT SILENTLY MOVES THE INSTRUMENT IS WORSE THAN A DELETION, BECAUSE A DELETION IS LOUD.** ⇒ **Any task editing this file re-runs the existing dev-split measurement and shows the number is UNCHANGED before its edit is accepted** (`repeats` makes this cheap, which is one more reason it is the first thing built).
- 📌 **BOARD CORRECTION: `SpikeCorpus.inl` DOES NOT EXIST ANYWHERE IN THE REPO.** Every task spec naming it as a deletion target was naming a file that has never existed — **struck from all `names:` lists.** ⚖️ **A deletion target that cannot be found is a spec defect, and it is exactly the shape §14 warns about: nobody could have "confirmed it absent" by search, because a negative search is what it looks like when it is simply not there.**

### 14. ⛔ THE SEARCH-TOOL LAW — CONSOLIDATED (2026-08-03). **A SEARCH RESULT IS EVIDENCE ABOUT THE SEARCH, NEVER ABOUT THE CODEBASE**

> ### ⛔ **A NEGATIVE SEARCH RESULT IS NOT EVIDENCE OF ABSENCE. CONFIRM AGAINST THE FILE ON DISK BEFORE REPORTING A MISSING SYMBOL, AND NEVER FILE A BLOCKER ON A SEARCH MISS ALONE.**

**⛔ THE DECORATION COROLLARY — ADDED 2026-08-03, AND IT FIRED WITHIN AN HOUR OF THE RULE IT INSTANTIATES.**
> ### ⛔ **A LAW'S DISCOVERABILITY MUST NOT DEPEND ON ITS DECORATION. SEARCH THE CONTENT, NEVER THE MARKER.**
- ⚠️ **MEASURED: `§14(c)` was nearly reported ABSENT because the verifier's grep assumed a `⛔` prefix and (c) is prefixed `✅`** — unlike (a), (b) and (d). ✅ **It was found on a second look, and the verifier flagged specifically that *a stricter check stopping at the first pattern would have reported it missing.***
- ⚖️ **THIS IS THE MECHANISM-VS-SYMPTOM RULE (GIT HAZARD LAWS (c)) FIRING ON A FORMATTING CONVENTION: a searcher keyed to the MARKER rather than the CONTENT.** ⛔ **The emoji carries TONE — `⛔` prohibition, `✅` confirmation, `⚖️` ruling — and tone is exactly the thing that varies between two clauses making the SAME kind of claim.** ⇒ **Grepping a marker filters by mood, not by subject.**
- ✅ **SEARCH BY THE STABLE HANDLE: the section heading, the clause letter (`§14(c)`), or a content noun.** ⛔ **Never by prefix glyph.** ⚠️ **And this cuts BOTH ways: an author must not rely on a glyph to make a clause findable either** — the letter and the heading are the contract; the emoji is presentation.

**Written as ONE clause because this wave produced THREE separate instances in three different tools, and three scattered notes would be read as three curiosities instead of one rule.** They share a single shape: **the tool's DOMAIN or RENDERING differs from the truth, and the output looks authoritative either way.**

| # | instance | what differed | how it lies |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Grep` renders `//` as `\` and `/*` as `\*` (4 occurrences, 3 files, 3 lanes) | **rendering ≠ bytes** | **MANUFACTURES** findings (two plausible false BLOCKERS held by careful reviewers) **and MASKS** them (the `*/`-in-comment trap is exactly this shape) |
| 2 | the manager's own `117` (true: **113**) | **a match ≠ a call site** — 4 hits were prose mentions inside a comment block | **inflates a denominator**, so a completeness claim silently understates coverage |
| 3 | **`git grep` is BLIND to untracked files** | **the index ≠ the working tree** | **returns NOTHING for a symbol that exists**, and a negative reads as proof |

- ⛔ **INSTANCE 3 IS THE DANGEROUS ONE FOR THIS PROJECT AND IT IS NOT A ONE-OFF: EVERY NEW FILE IN A FEATURE BATCH IS `??` UNTIL THE INTEGRATION COMMIT.** `git status --untracked-files=all` on this batch lists `SettingsMenuWidget.{h,cpp}`, `SiegeAssistantComponent.{h,cpp}`, `SiegeAssistantConsoleWidget.{h,cpp}`, `SiegeSettingsSaveGame.{h,cpp}` and more — **all untracked.** ⇒ ⚖️ **In a new-files-only batch — which is this pipeline's DEFAULT posture under the M8 PARALLEL LAW — a negative `git grep` means NOTHING AT ALL.** ⚠️ **The failure mode is an agent confidently reporting a missing cross-task symbol that is sitting on disk, filing a false blocker, and stalling a wave.** **TASK-453's first TWO greps for `AttachConsoleWidget` returned nothing and BOTH were wrong; it probed the header directly and found it at `SiegeAssistantComponent.h:723`, exactly as pinned.**
- ⚠️ **AND A CORRECT SEARCH CAN STILL BE STALE: A FILE OWNED BY A *RUNNING* TASK IS A MOVING TARGET.** TASK-443 was actively writing those files during 453's session (**mtimes moved twice, `12:34:33` then `12:36:47`**). ⇒ ⛔ **Before reporting a cross-task symbol as missing, CHECK WHETHER THE OWNING TASK IS STILL RUNNING.** A pinned signature that has not appeared yet is **a schedule fact, not a defect** — and the pin, not the search, is the contract.
- ⛔ **INSTANCE 5, AND IT IS THE MOST DANGEROUS SHAPE IN THIS SECTION BECAUSE IT PRODUCED THE *RIGHT* ANSWER (2026-08-03): `find_nodes(title="Remove from Parent")` RETURNED 0 WHILE THOSE NODES DEMONSTRABLY EXIST — a FALSE NEGATIVE FROM THE MCP SURFACE ITSELF.** Build-master's own words are the law:
  > ⚖️ ***"Had I trusted it, I'd have 'confirmed' the right answer using the wrong evidence — the correct conclusion resting on nothing."***
  > ### ⛔ **A CORRECT CONCLUSION RESTING ON FALSE EVIDENCE IS NOT A PASS — IT IS A COINCIDENCE.**
  ⚠️ **AND IT IS UNDETECTABLE BY REVIEW, WHICH IS WHAT SETS IT APART FROM EVERY OTHER INSTANCE HERE: a reviewer checks the CONCLUSION, and the conclusion CHECKS OUT.** Instances 1–4 all produced a *wrong* answer, so something eventually disagreed with them. **This one produces the right answer for a reason that does not hold — so nothing ever disagrees, and it survives until the day the underlying fact changes and the "verified" claim silently becomes false.**
  ⇒ ✅ **THE COUNTER IS THE ONE BUILD-MASTER USED: PROVE THE POSITIVE.** Instead of *"a search for `RemoveFromParent` found none"*, it established **`AddToViewport.then` has `connected = 0`** — **a terminal pin with no connection, read by node identity rather than DSL text.** ⚖️ **A positive structural fact cannot be faked by a broken search**, and that is the difference between evidence and coincidence. **`find_nodes` is added to §17's ledger.**
- ✅ **THE DISCHARGE, IN ORDER — this is what TASK-453 actually did and it is the behaviour to copy:** (1) search to **LOCATE**; (2) on a negative for something that **should** exist, **`Read` the file on disk**; (3) if still absent, **check whether its owning task is live**; (4) only then report — **and report it as *"not yet defined, owner still running"*, never as *"missing"*.** ⇒ ⚖️ **`Grep` LOCATES; IT DOES NOT READ; AND IT CERTAINLY DOES NOT PROVE A NEGATIVE.**
- 📌 **The `//`-rendering half of instance 1 remains stated in full at "In-match LLM command assistant" §10; this clause GENERALISES it rather than replacing it.** Structural and comment-level findings are still read from **raw `Read` output**, and a reviewer must say which tool produced any text they quote.

### 15. ⚖️ THE DECLARED-DEPARTURE LAW — A SPEC LINE REFUSED ON A CHECKABLE MECHANISM IS THE SYSTEM WORKING, NOT A DEVIATION (2026-08-03)

> **An agent that refuses a spec line, states the mechanism, and cites the artifact is DOING the job — not exceeding it. QA RATIFIES such a departure; it does not file it as a deviation. ⛔ The failure mode this pipeline should fear is the opposite: an agent that follows a wrong spec line silently.**

**THREE TIMES IN ONE WAVE a programmer refused a line of the manager's spec on a stated, checkable mechanism — AND WAS RIGHT ALL THREE TIMES.** That is a pattern about how specs and agents interact, not three anecdotes:

1. **`Super::RebuildWidget()` ordering** — refused **independently by TASK-437 and TASK-444**; `UserWidget.cpp:1214` returns an `SSpacer` when `RootWidget` is null. **The LAW was wrong**, and it was corrected in both rulings.
2. **`ToggleConsole()`** — listed in TASK-449's `names:` block but **never called**: it shows the widget *before* the controller can gate it, i.e. the exact ordering the same spec's ⛔ guard-first clause forbids. **The `names:` list lost to the spec text.**
3. **`AttachConsoleWidget` placement** — the spec said *"immediately after a successful create/open"*; TASK-453 placed it **BEFORE `OpenConsole()`** because `OpenConsole()` **ENDS** with `OnConsoleOpenChanged.Broadcast(true)` and the component subscribes to that delegate **inside `AttachConsoleWidget`.** ⚠️ **Attaching afterwards would miss the ONLY "opened" broadcast of the first press** — the FSM sits in `Idle` while the player types into a box it does not know is open, recovering only on the **second** open, **with no error, no log and no crash.** ⛔ **That is the `BeginPlay` trap one level deeper, and it would have reached Jonathan as *"the first time I press Enter, nothing happens."***

- ⚖️ **THE COMMON PROPERTY THAT MAKES THESE RATIFIABLE RATHER THAN LICENCE: EACH REFUSAL CITES A MECHANISM THE RECIPIENT CAN CHECK WITHOUT TRUSTING THE REFUSER** — an engine line, a delegate broadcast at the end of a named function, a clause in the same spec. ⛔ **A departure justified by preference, style, or *"this seemed better"* is NOT this and is still a deviation.** **The tell is identical to §12b's certificate/recommendation split: an ABSENCE or ORDERING fact is checkable by the reader; a QUALITY judgement is not.**
- ⛔ **AND ALL THREE WERE FLAGGED, NOT BURIED. THAT IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE LAW AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** A silent departure — even a correct one — **is indistinguishable from a mistake at review time** and destroys the spec's value as a contract. **Declare it, name the mechanism, cite the artifact.**
- ✅ **THE RAREST AND BEST FORM OF THIS DISCIPLINE — CORRECTING A PREMISE **IN YOUR OWN FAVOUR** AND *NOT* UPGRADING YOUR OWN VERDICT (2026-08-03).** TASK-447 found the board's `(0-BACKENDS)` premise wrong **in the direction that would have let it claim more**: backends load in **110 ms**, and the **2.4 GB model load is genuinely async on a worker** — so the feared stall does not exist. ⛔ **It kept the QUALIFIED pass anyway**, because **110 ms is still a game-thread stall** and because **it had read a startup line rather than timing match start under load.** ⇒ ⚖️ **The premise improved; the EVIDENCE did not — so the verdict did not move.** ⚠️ **Nearly every incentive runs the other way here, which is exactly why it is recorded: an agent that upgrades its verdict the moment a premise softens is fitting its conclusion to its comfort, not to its measurement.**
- 📌 **THE MANAGER'S DUTY THAT FALLS OUT OF THIS: THREE WRONG SPEC LINES IN ONE WAVE IS A SIGNAL ABOUT THE SPECS, NOT ABOUT THE AGENTS.** All three were **lifecycle/ordering claims about engine or cross-file behaviour written without executing anything.** ⇒ **Where a spec asserts an ORDERING, it either cites the artifact or is marked as an assumption the implementer is expected to verify.** ⚖️ **A spec that states an unverified ordering as fact is borrowing the authority of a measurement it never took.**

### 20. ⛔ A SUGGESTED FIX INSIDE A QA REPORT IS A **HYPOTHESIS**, NOT A PATCH — AND THE IMPLEMENTER MUST TRACE IT (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **A FIX PROPOSED IN A REVIEW CARRIES REVIEWER AUTHORITY BUT HAS NOT BEEN TRACED THROUGH THE CODE. IT IS A LEAD FOR THE IMPLEMENTER TO VERIFY — NEVER A PATCH TO APPLY.**
> **Reviewers SHOULD keep proposing fixes; that is not in question. What changes is the STATUS of the proposal: the reviewer LABELS it as untraced, and the implementer TRACES it before typing it.**

**THE WORKED EXAMPLE, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST ONE THIS BATCH PRODUCED.** `qa/TASK-424.md` proposed a one-line `|| ShouldAbortNow()` for WARN-2. **TASK-457 traced it and refused it — it would have been wrong TWICE:**
1. it reports a **timeout as a CANCEL** to the player; and
2. ⛔ **it sets NEITHER flag the post-prefill guard tests** ⇒ the run **falls through into the sampler with a HALF-PREFILLED PROMPT.**

- ⛔ **AND THE SECOND FAILURE IS THE WORST OUTCOME THIS ENTIRE FEATURE CAN PRODUCE.** Not a crash, not a refusal, not a truncation the player can see — **a FLUENT, GRAMMAR-VALID UNIT ORDER DERIVED FROM A TRUNCATED PROMPT**, indistinguishable at a glance from a good one. ⚖️ **That is the *valid-shaped wrong command* that §1 names as the thing this whole architecture exists to prevent — and it would have arrived through the REVIEW channel, wearing a reviewer's authority, as a one-line "fix".**
- ✅ **THE CORRECT FIX USED `bAborted`** — the flag the decode loop's **own** timeout already sets — **and extended the guard.** ⚖️ **The tell is general: a proposed fix that introduces a NEW way to leave a loop must be checked against every guard that runs AFTER it.** A one-line change to an exit condition is never one line of consequence.
- ⚖️ **THE UNIFICATION, AND IT IS WHY THIS EARNS ITS OWN SECTION RATHER THAN A NOTE: THIS WAVE HAS NOW FOUND THE *SAME* DEFECT SHAPE IN FOUR DIFFERENT ARTIFACT TYPES — AUTHORITY ATTACHING TO SOMETHING THAT WAS NEVER EXECUTED.**

  | artifact | clause | the unexecuted claim |
  |---|---|---|
  | **SPECS** | §15 | ordering/lifecycle asserted without running anything (4 instances) |
  | **MEASUREMENTS** | §18a | a proxy metric pinned as if it were the property |
  | **SEARCHES** | §14 | a negative result read as proof of absence (4 variants) |
  | **REVIEWS** | **§20** | **a suggested patch that was reasoned about but never traced** |

  ⇒ ⛔ **THE ROOT RISK ON THIS PROJECT IS NOT ANY ONE TOOL. IT IS UNEXECUTED AUTHORITY — a confident artifact produced by a competent party who did not, and often could not, run the thing they were describing.** ✅ **The counter is always the same and it is cheap: name the mechanism, cite the artifact, and say what you did NOT run.**

✅ **§20 WORKING AS WRITTEN — THE POSITIVE WORKED EXAMPLE, AND IT IS WORTH AS MUCH AS THE FAILURE THAT PROMPTED THE CLAUSE (TASK-450's `C2555`, 2026-08-03).** A build-master concern — *"changing `Run()`'s return to `uint32` will force a `return` on every exit of the loop that holds TASK-457's ratified ordering"* — was **traced before it was typed**, and the trace **resolved it in the OPPOSITE direction**: `Run()` has **exactly ONE exit** (zero `return` statements in the body; the single `break` leaves **the while loop, not the function**; and SEH cannot propagate because `SehInvoke`'s `__except` swallows the fault and returns `false` normally). ⇒ ✅ **No new way to leave a loop was introduced, so no downstream guard needed re-checking and 457's `MarkBusy()`/`bWorkPending` ordering was untouched. The fix collapsed to three behavioural lines.** ⚖️ **The trace did not merely confirm the hypothesis — it CHANGED WHAT "THE FIX" MEANT, which is the whole argument for tracing before typing.**

⛔ **TWICE IN ONE WAVE A SUGGESTED FIX WOULD HAVE SHIPPED THE EXACT OPPOSITE OF ITS INTENT — AND THAT FREQUENCY IS THE ARGUMENT, NOT THE ANECDOTE.** (i) `|| ShouldAbortNow()` would have fed the sampler **a half-prefilled prompt** ⇒ a fluent, grammar-valid order derived from a truncated one. (ii) `{Order}` on the shortfall line would have **offered back verbatim the order the sentence exists to call impossible**, because at shortfall time `Counts` still holds **the count that cannot be met**. ⇒ ⚖️ **Both were reasonable-looking, both came from a competent reviewer, and both were caught only by TRACING. §20 is earning its place faster than any other law in this batch.**

⛔ **AND THE SHARPEST HALF: THE TRAP WAS NOT THE ERROR — IT WAS THE PLAUSIBLE REPAIR.** Converting that `break` into a `return` **reads as exactly equivalent** and would have **skipped `UnloadModel()` and the `Stopped` transition** ⇒ ⛔ **leaking the ~2.5 GB model**, and leaving `JoinAndDestroy`'s `WaitForCompletion` to hand the game thread a worker still claiming Busy. ✅ **It was written in as an ANTI-REVERT note, which is the correct home: the next reader's instinct is the hazard, so the warning belongs where that instinct strikes.** ⚖️ **Generalised: when the obvious repair is wrong, the code must say so AT THE LINE — a defect that is only documented in a handoff is re-introduced by the next person who never read it.**

### 21. ⚖️ A GUARD'S **PLACEMENT** IS PART OF ITS CORRECTNESS — "PRESENT" IS NOT "REACHED" (2026-08-03)

**TASK-457's deadline test sits at the BOTTOM of the prefill loop body, and that is load-bearing:** from turn two the body executes **exactly once** (~347 tokens against a 512-token `n_batch`), so **a top-of-body check would have looked correct in review and been INERT ON THE HOT PATH.**

- ⚖️ **SAME CLASS AS §13's COMPARATOR AND §11's LIFECYCLE: the code is present, correctly written, and never does the job.** ⇒ **A review of a guard must establish WHICH EXECUTIONS REACH IT, not merely that it exists in the right function.**
- ⛔ **AND THE HONEST LIMIT TRAVELS WITH IT — THE ABSENCE OF A LOG LINE IS NOT EVIDENCE THE GUARD WORKS.** Prefill is **~1.7 s on partial**, so the 10 s ceiling is **unreachable on that tier**; the **only reachable exercise is the CPU tier** (TASK-413: **2 of 5** generations hit it). ⚠️ **A silent log on a tier that cannot reach the threshold proves nothing at all, and recording it as a pass would be exactly the false green this wave keeps finding.**
- 📌 **THE THIRD HEADER OVER-CLAIM OF THE §17 SHAPE, SELF-DECLARED BY 457: the *"at most one ubatch"* bound is WRONG BY 8×**, because **the loop cannot observe a boundary it never returns through.** ✅ **Found and reported by the implementer against its own work** — the §15 discipline turned inward again.

### 19. ⚖️ THE SNAPSHOT-BUDGET CONSTANT FAMILY HAS **THREE** ROLES, NOT TWO — AND THE SAFE DIRECTION *AND* THE FAILURE MODE DIFFER IN EACH (2026-08-03)

**§10 named two roles (AUTHORITY and PRE-FILTER) and ruled that the safe direction INVERTS between them. A third role was in the code the whole time and had no name — and it is the one where getting it wrong is worst.**

| role | constant | safe direction | ⚠️ FAILURE MODE IF WRONG |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AUTHORITY** — stands between the snapshot and the budget | ~~`MaxSnapshotChars` 1085~~ **RETIRED** | assume the **SMALLEST** plausible chars/token ratio | over-admits ⇒ silent budget overrun |
| **PRE-FILTER** — a cheap gate before tokenizing | `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` **3000** | assume the **LARGEST** plausible ratio | too low ⇒ **silent truncation**; too high ⇒ one wasted tokenize |
| **TRIMMER** — decides how much ROSTER to PRINT (`BuildZoneC`) | **`SnapshotTrimBudgetChars` 1085** (**NEW NAME, value deliberately unmoved**) | **tight** | ⛔ **too high ⇒ THE PLUGIN BOUNDS REJECT THE TURN — the player's order is REFUSED, not degraded** |

- ⛔ **THIS IS WHY RE-POINTING THE RETIRED CONSTANT WOULD HAVE BEEN A REAL DEFECT AND NOT A TIDY-UP.** The retired `MaxSnapshotChars`' **only real code reader was the ROSTER TRIMMER** — neither the authority nor the pre-filter. **Both plugin bounds REJECT**, so aiming the trimmer at the pre-filter's `3000` would have converted ***"gracefully narrow the roster"*** into ***"refuse the player's order"*** — ⚠️ **a graceful degradation silently promoted into a hard failure, on a mid-battle interaction.**
- ⚖️ **THE GENERAL LAW, AND IT IS THE REUSABLE PART: A CONSTANT'S SAFE DIRECTION IS A PROPERTY OF ITS *ROLE*, NEVER OF ITS *VALUE*. TWO ROLES SHARING A NUMBER DO NOT SHARE A MEANING.**
  ⇒ ⛔ **THIS IS EXACTLY WHY *RE-POINTING* IS MORE DANGEROUS THAN *INTRODUCING A NEW NAME*: re-pointing carries the VALUE across and SILENTLY CHANGES THE ROLE**, and the value looking familiar is what makes it pass review. **`SnapshotTrimBudgetChars` shares `1085` with the retired authority value by coincidence of arithmetic, not by kinship** — and it holds that value **only because §12c freezes Zone B/C bytes this wave.** ⚠️ **When that freeze lifts, the trimmer is re-derived on its own terms and MUST NOT inherit whatever the authority becomes.**
- ✅ **AND THE ENFORCEMENT PATTERN IS THE ONE TO COPY EVERYWHERE: TURN THE LAW INTO A COMPILE ERROR.** `MaxSnapshotTokens` and `SnapshotPreFilterMaxChars` **already existed** at `SiegeLlamaSubsystem.h:149`/`:170`; **adding game-lane copies would have created constants nothing in the game lane enforces — §17's exact failure, a guardrail that reports safe.** Instead: **three `static_assert`s**, which make §8's hidden-authority law **fail at COMPILE TIME at zero new header coupling.** ⇒ ⚖️ **A `static_assert` cannot report safe falsely, cannot drift, and costs nothing at runtime — prefer it over a duplicated constant, a comment, or a test, every time one is available.**

### 18. ⚖️ "ADDITIVE" IS A CLAIM ABOUT **BEHAVIOUR**, NOT ABOUT DIFF ARITHMETIC — AND WHEN A RELAY CONTRADICTS THE BOARD, THE BOARD WINS (2026-08-03)

#### 18a. THE PROOF SHAPE DEPENDS ON THE TASK SHAPE, AND USING THE WRONG ONE FORCES A WRONG REFUSAL OR A SILENT VIOLATION

| task shape | what "provably additive" MEANS | the proof |
|---|---|---|
| **ADDS** code (a new binding, a new call, a new member) | nothing existing changed behaviour | ✅ **deletion count UNCHANGED** — insertions only |
| **EXTRACTS** a shared body (TASK-397 / 440 / 454) | ⛔ **deletions are REQUIRED and unavoidable** | ✅ **every deleted line ACCOUNTED FOR AT ITS NEW HOME, itemised line-by-line** |

- ⛔ **AN "UNCHANGED DELETION COUNT" DEMAND ON AN EXTRACTION IS UNSATISFIABLE ALONGSIDE A ONE-IMPLEMENTATION CONTRACT, AND THE CONTRADICTION IS FORMAL, NOT A MATTER OF DEGREE:** two handlers cannot **both** keep intact copies of a sequence **and** there be exactly one implementation of it. **Demanding both forces the implementer to either refuse a correct task or violate one constraint silently.**
- ⚠️ **THE GENERAL DEFECT: A METRIC THAT IS A VALID *PROXY* IN ONE TASK SHAPE BECOMES AN *IMPOSSIBLE CONSTRAINT* IN ANOTHER — and it keeps its authoritative tone the whole way across.** ⇒ **State the PROPERTY (*"no existing behaviour changed"*) and let the proof follow the shape. Never pin the proxy as if it were the property.** ⚖️ **Same family as §13's comparator and §14's search: the measurement's domain silently stopped matching the claim.**
- **WORKED EXAMPLE (TASK-454, and it is the reason this clause exists):** `git diff --stat` went **757/94 → 836/108**. **The header was 100 % additive** (deletions unchanged at 10) and **all +14 `.cpp` deletions were MOVES, itemised individually.** ⇒ ✅ **CORRECT.** ⛔ **A reviewer checking the NUMBER would have failed it; a reviewer checking the ITEMISATION passes it.** **Check the itemisation, never the number.**

#### 18b. ⛔ WHEN A RELAYED INSTRUCTION CONTRADICTS THE BOARD, THE BOARD WINS — AND THE AGENT SAYS SO

> **The board and CONVENTIONS are the CONTRACT. A Slack post, an orchestrator relay or a dispatch-prompt paraphrase is a MESSAGE.** *(SLACK.md: "if Slack and the files disagree, the files win" — restated here because it just fired on a technical constraint rather than on status.)*

- **TASK-454 was told by relay that the deletion count *must* read 94; its board spec said it CONDITIONALLY** — *"unless the extraction genuinely moves lines — and if it does, SAY SO and account for every moved line."* ✅ **It followed the board, produced the itemisation, and DECLARED the conflict.** ⛔ **That is the required behaviour, not a liberty.**
- ⚖️ **AND THE HALF THAT MAKES IT SAFE IS THE DECLARATION.** Silently following the board over a relay is indistinguishable at review time from silently ignoring an instruction. **Name the conflict, cite both texts, say which you followed and why** — then the discrepancy is *resolvable* instead of *invisible*.
- 📌 **THE ORCHESTRATOR AND MANAGER DUTY THAT FALLS OUT OF IT:** ⚠️ **a relay that TIGHTENS a board constraint is authoring new law through a side channel.** If a constraint should be absolute, **it is edited into the board where every later reader sees it** — not sharpened in a dispatch prompt that no artifact records. **This one was caught because the agent read both; that is luck, and luck is not a control.**
- 📌 **LEDGER, INSTANCE 2 — AND IT WIDENS THE RULE FROM *CONSTRAINTS* TO *PROOF METHODS*.** A relay instructed TASK-456 to prove its edit with **`git diff --stat`** — ⛔ **on an UNTRACKED file, which shows NOTHING and would have read as *"this task changed nothing."*** ✅ **The agent caught it and used `git diff --no-index` against a byte-exact snapshot instead.** ⚠️ **That is §14 instance 3 arriving through a relay, and it is the SECOND time a relayed instruction has walked into a trap THE BOARD ALREADY KNEW ABOUT.**
  ⇒ ⚖️ **THE RULE WIDENS: A PROOF METHOD IS PART OF THE CONTRACT, SO IT BELONGS ON THE BOARD, NOT IN A DISPATCH PROMPT.** A relay can safely say *"prove it"*; **the moment it says *"prove it THIS WAY"* it is authoring a method no artifact records and no later reader can correct.** ⚠️ **Both instances were caught by the agent rather than by the author — the channel has no review step, which is precisely why it must not carry technical content.**

### 22. ⛔ THE STALE-DIVERGENCE COUNT — A COMMENT IS NOT THE ONLY PLACE A FALSE STATEMENT LIVES (2026-08-03)

**My TASK-456 spec said *"delete the two divergence-warning COMMENTS."* The reality was FOUR artifacts: the two comments PLUS TWO `UE_LOG(Warning)` STATEMENTS printing the same false sentence into the runtime log, mid-battle.** ⛔ **The log pair was the worse half, and nobody had counted it.**

- ⚖️ **A COMMENT MISLEADS THE NEXT READER; A `UE_LOG(Warning)` MISLEADS THE NEXT DEBUGGER — AT 3 A.M., DURING A PLAYTEST, IN A FILE JONATHAN GREPS.** A stale warning in the runtime log is **evidence-shaped**: it appears in exactly the artifact an investigator trusts most, and it will be believed over the code.
- ⛔ **THE LAW: WHEN A DOCUMENTED DIVERGENCE, WORKAROUND OR HAZARD IS RESOLVED, SWEEP *EVERY* SURFACE THAT ASSERTS IT — comments, ⚠️ `UE_LOG` STRINGS (NAMED FIRST AND EXPLICITLY, SEE BELOW), handoffs, board text, and this document.**
  - ⛔ **RUNTIME LOG STRINGS ARE THE PRIORITY SURFACE, NOT AN AFTERTHOUGHT — THREE INSTANCES THIS WAVE AND THE LOG WAS THE WORSE HALF EVERY TIME** (TASK-456's divergence pair · TASK-457's `10.0s` constant · TASK-464's WARN-3, where **three artifacts assert *"holds no reference into the plugin"* and all three are false — including a `UE_LOG` printed at EVERY `BeginPlay`**). ⚖️ **TWO PROPERTIES MAKE THEM THE WORST PLACE FOR A STALE CLAIM TO HIDE: (i) a log line is the artifact an investigator TRUSTS MOST, so a false one is believed over the code; and (ii) a grep for PROSE does not surface them** — the claim lives inside a string literal, where searches for the *comment* never look. ⇒ ⛔ **Search the CLAIM across string literals explicitly, and treat a false runtime log as a higher-severity finding than a false comment.**
  - ⚠️ **AND NOTE THE SHAPE WARN-3 TOOK, BECAUSE IT IS THE HARDEST VERSION TO CATCH: THE CONCLUSION WAS STILL TRUE AND ONLY THE CITED MECHANISM WAS GONE.** *"A missing GGUF never blocks match start"* remains correct **on the code** — but the reason all three artifacts give for it no longer exists. ⛔ **A true conclusion resting on a dead mechanism reads as verified and is not**, and the next person to change that code will check the stated mechanism, find it absent, and have no idea whether the conclusion still holds. ⚠️ **Searching for the *comment* finds the comment; searching for the **claim** finds the logs too** (§14's prose-vs-call-site problem inverted: here the prose was the payload).
- 📌 **AND IT IS THE FOURTH TIME THIS WAVE A COUNT IN MY OWN SPEC WAS WRONG** (117→113 · 10→15 · 2→4 · plus 450's different-symbol total; **18-not-12 made it five**). ⇒ ⛔ **A COUNT IN A SPEC IS A HINT, NEVER A CONTRACT: the implementer works from the SYMBOL and reports what is actually there — and a mismatch is a finding about the spec, not a defect in the work.**

> ### ⛔ §22 IS BROADENED (2026-08-03): **A DEFECT FOUND AT ONE SITE IS A *SHAPE TO SWEEP FOR*, NOT A *LOCATION TO PATCH*.**
> **A finding names where the reporter LOOKED, never where the defect IS. Before fixing a cited site, search for the SHAPE and fix every instance — or state which you left and why.**

**TWO INSTANCES, ONE WAVE, AND IN BOTH CASES THE UNCITED SITE WAS THE WORSE ONE:**
- **TASK-456** — the report named **two stale COMMENTS**; there were also **two `UE_LOG(Warning)` printing the same false sentence into the runtime log.** ⇒ **the logs were the worse half** (evidence-shaped, believed over the code).
- **TASK-459** — WARN-3 named the **prefill** timeout string; **the DECODE timeout string is the identical defect, same constant, same field, cited by nobody** — ⛔ **and the measured numbers say the UNCITED one is the only one that can realistically fire** (worst prefill **417–2914 ms** across all tiers vs partial's worst decode wall **8926 ms of 10000 ms**). ⇒ ⚖️ **Fixing only the named site would have left TASK-447 quoting a constant anyway — *this task's own failure mode, achieved by a task that reported success.***

⇒ ⛔ **THIRD INSTANCE, 2026-08-03 — AND THE RECURRENCE RATE IS ITSELF THE FINDING.** §30/WARN-5 cited only `Assistant_Order_SelectionPlace`; **`Assistant_Order_Place` (`"{Intent} to {Place}"`) had the IDENTICAL defect and was cited by nobody** — ⚠️ **and it is reachable in NORMAL PLAY, because `who:"all"` parses to an EMPTY selection**, so *"guard everything at the mine"* rendered **"Guard to mine_near"**. ⇒ **Three times this wave the UNCITED instance was the one that mattered** (456's runtime logs · 459's decode string · this).
> ⛔ **THEREFORE: TREAT EVERY CITATION AS A *LOWER BOUND* ON THE EXTENT, BY DEFAULT — never as the extent.** ⚖️ **The loss is at the CITING step: a finding names where the reporter LOOKED, and reporters keep looking ONCE.** ⇒ **Absent an explicit statement of the search performed, the implementer assumes the citation is INCOMPLETE and sweeps.**

⇒ **THE REVIEWER'S HALF:** a finding says *"here is an instance"*, not *"here is the extent"* — **say which you searched for and which you merely saw.** ⇒ **THE IMPLEMENTER'S HALF:** ⛔ **a one-line fix to a cited line is not done until the shape has been swept for.** ✅ **A sweep that finds nothing else is a RESULT and is reported as one.**

### 32. ⛔ A SAFETY MECHANISM THAT HAS NEVER BEEN **OBSERVED TO FUNCTION** IS NOT KNOWN TO FUNCTION (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **AN ASSERTION THAT HAS ONLY EVER BEEN GREEN IS NOT YET PROVEN LOAD-BEARING.**
> **33 of 33 tests passed on their FIRST execution ⇒ not one has ever been observed to FAIL. Single run, no shuffle, flakiness unmeasured.** ✅ **That is a real and welcome result — and it is a different claim from *"these 33 assertions protect us."***

- ⚖️ **THE PROOF THAT CONVERTS *PRESENT* INTO *PROVEN* IS A DELIBERATE MUTATION: BREAK ONE THING, CONFIRM THE RIGHT TEST GOES RED.** ⚠️ **This wave already produced the worked example without meaning to — `ZoneA.NullVocabularyIsNotTheMeasuredLane` ASSERTED THE EXACT IDENTITY THAT BROKE THIS MORNING, and caught nothing, because it had never been run.** ⇒ ⛔ **A guardrail nobody has watched trip is a guardrail nobody has tested.** ✅ **Same family as §20 (trace before you type) and §22 (a citation is a lower bound): the artifact looks authoritative and its authority has never been exercised.**
- ⛔ **THE SECOND FORM, AND IT IS WORSE BECAUSE IT WEARS THE COSTUME OF CAUTION: A REDUNDANCY THAT SILENTLY DEGRADES TO A SINGLE POINT OF FAILURE IS WORSE THAN NO REDUNDANCY — BECAUSE IT IS *BELIEVED*.** TASK-470 passed the map **two ways** as belt-and-braces; ⚠️ **Git Bash MSYS mangled the positional `/Engine/Maps/Entry` into `C:/Program Files/Git/Engine/Maps/Entry`, which failed to resolve**, so **the `-ini:` override was the ONLY mechanism that worked** — and nothing said so. ⇒ ⛔ **Every limb of a redundancy is verified to work ON ITS OWN, or the redundancy is decoration.**
- ✅ **THE STANDING DUTY THAT FALLS OUT: WIRE THE SUITE INTO THE COMPILE GATE** so a green suite stops depending on someone remembering, **and state per-run: how many executed, how many passed, and whether any has EVER been seen to fail.** ⚠️ **"33 pass" and "33 assertions that would catch a regression" are different sentences, and only the first is measured.**

### 31. ⛔ A PRESENTATION LAYER MAY NOT **REPAIR** ITS INPUT (2026-08-03) — and on this feature it is the whole point

> ### ⛔ **A DISPLAY SHOWS WHAT IT WAS GIVEN. IT MAY NOT TIDY, SUPPRESS OR NORMALISE MALFORMED INPUT — BECAUSE THE STEP THAT EXISTS TO EXPOSE ERRORS CANNOT BE THE STEP THAT HIDES THEM.**

**THE INSTANCE (TASK-471, F1).** The parser permits `who:[…]` on **charge / fallback / rally**, so ***"Fall back 5 footman"* is REACHABLE.** ⛔ **No choice of frame can repair it — the verb is INTRANSITIVE, so the defect is the DATA COMBINATION, not the sentence.** **The fix sat inside 471's own function** (suppress the selection for non-selection-bearing verbs) — ✅ **and it REFUSED.**

- ⚖️ **THE REASONING IS THE FEATURE'S ENTIRE RATIONALE IN ONE LINE: JONATHAN APPROVED THE CONFIRM STEP *BECAUSE* THE MODEL GETS THINGS WRONG.** **4 of the 5 stable eval failures are wrong-place / wrong-count on orders the model otherwise understood** — the confirm step exists so he can SEE that before it executes. ⇒ ⛔ **A display that quietly tidies malformed output removes the only place he can see it, and it does so most effectively exactly when the model is most wrong.**
- ⛔ **AND IT WOULD HAVE BEEN INVISIBLE — WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT DANGEROUS RATHER THAN MERELY WRONG.** A suppressed selection produces a **clean, grammatical, confident sentence.** The player sees a tidy order and approves it; the malformed command still executes. ⚖️ **Same shape as §20's `|| ShouldAbortNow()`: a plausible local fix that manufactures a confident-looking wrong result — except this one manufactures it on the review surface itself.**
- ✅ **THE CORRECT DISPOSITION, AND IT IS THE GENERAL RULE: PRESENT THE MALFORMED INPUT FAITHFULLY, AND ROUTE THE REAL DEFECT UPSTREAM.** ⛔ **A cosmetic patch at the display is not a fix; it is a decision to stop measuring.** ⚠️ **The upstream defect here is real and gets its own task — the grammar/parser admits a selection for intransitive verbs.**
- 📌 **THE NARROW EXCEPTION, SO THIS IS NOT MISREAD: a display MAY choose a frame that reads correctly across all LEGAL inputs (that is §30, and it is required). What it may not do is make an ILLEGAL input look legal.** ⚖️ **The tell: does the change alter which INPUTS are distinguishable on screen? If yes, it is repair, not presentation.**

### 30. ⛔ A TEMPLATE SUBSTITUTES **NOUNS AND NUMBERS** — NEVER **GRAMMAR** (2026-08-03)

> **Substitution points carry THINGS: a kind, a count, a place, a verb-as-a-whole-word. ⛔ They never carry PREPOSITIONS, articles, conjugations or agreement — because a grammar fragment is only meaningful in combination with the words around it, and a template cannot see those.**

- **THE INSTANCE: `DescribeCommandForPlayer`'s single frame `"{Intent} {Selection} to {Place}"` renders *"Guard 8 footman **TO** mine_near — accept?"*** ⛔ The preposition is wrong for **Guard** and **Ambush**, and it appears **on the confirm line AND the executed line of every such order** — i.e. **on the exact surface Jonathan asked for.**
- ⛔ **AND THE OBVIOUS FIX IS THE WRONG ONE: `{Preposition}` IS BARRED.** ⚖️ **A substituted preposition is a LOCALIZATION FRAGMENT, NOT A SENTENCE** — it is grammatically bound to both the verb before it and the noun after it, and those bindings differ per verb and per language. **A frame assembled from fragments produces sentences no reviewer can check and no translator can repair.** ⇒ ✅ **Use a VERB-NEUTRAL FRAME instead: one string that reads correctly for every intent.**
- ⚖️ **AND THE COUNT RULE FROM §22 STILL BINDS: ONE STRING, NOT THREE.** Forking the frame per intent trades a wrong sentence for **three sentences that will drift** — which is precisely what the shortfall row's own spec forbade one task earlier. **The same wave produced both instances: the verb (TASK-465) and the preposition (TASK-471).**
- ✅ **THE TEST: read the frame aloud with EVERY value its path can supply.** ⛔ **If any combination is ungrammatical, the frame is wrong — not the value.**
- ⚖️ **DEEPER THAN THE ABOVE, AND FOUND BY APPLYING IT (TASK-471): WHEN NO SUBSTITUTION CAN MAKE A RELATIONAL CLAIM TRUE ACROSS ALL ITS VALUES, THE FRAME MUST STOP ASSERTING THE RELATION.** `to` is a **DESTINATION** for send / charge / fall-back / rally and a **LOCATION** for guard / ambush — ⛔ **no English preposition is true of both.** ⇒ ✅ **The frames now PRESENT the place instead of RELATING it: `"{Intent} {Selection} ({Place})"` and `"{Intent} ({Place})"`.** ⚖️ **A frame that cannot state a true relation must state none — a false relation is worse than a terse one, because it reads as information.**
- ⛔ **AND A FRAME IS NESTED INSIDE WRAPPERS, SO ITS DELIMITERS MUST *COMPOSE* — THE THIRD §20 REFUSAL OF THE WAVE CAME FROM MISSING THIS.** A dash form was suggested and traced: `{Order}` is **never read alone** (`Assistant_ConfirmPrompt` = `"{Order} - accept?"`), so it renders **`Guard 8 footman - mine_near - accept?`** — **two dashes at one level, which no reader can group.** ✅ **Parentheses are SELF-DELIMITING and compose into all three wrappers.** ⇒ **Choose delimiters by how they nest, never by how they look in isolation.**

### 29. ⛔ A GATE COVERS THE TASKS IT **NAMES** — REVIEWING A *FILE* IS NOT GATING A *TASK*. EVERY BATCH CARRIES A COVERAGE LEDGER (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **NO ARTIFACT LISTED WHICH TASKS THE GATES COVERED VERSUS WHICH TASKS EXISTED. Every gate named its own scope; NOTHING CROSS-CHECKED THE UNION AGAINST THE BATCH ROSTER — so TASK-442's code shipped in `cd5f4ed` with NO gate naming it, and it was found at FLIP time, not at GATE time.**

- ⚖️ **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A REAL BREACH AND NOT BOOKKEEPING PEDANTRY: A FILE REVIEW IS SCOPED TO ONE TASK'S CHANGES.** `SiegeAssistantComponent.{h,cpp}` **was** genuinely read at gate 446 — through **443, 455 and 456** — and 443's statement-for-statement edit to **442's own `ConfirmPressed`** was explicitly ruled on. ⇒ ✅ **So the FILE was reviewed.** ⛔ **But 442's OWN deliverables carry their OWN criteria — the FSM states, the reason-code template table, the ~15-line clarification short-circuit, the `bAssistantFaulted` session-latch posture, the `!HasAuthority()` refusal, the M8 declaration — and NOBODY APPLIED THEM.** ⚠️ **"The file was read" and "the task was gated" are different claims, and only one of them was true.**
- ⛔ **THE RULING, AND IT GOES AGAINST THE CHEAP ANSWER DELIBERATELY: A RETROACTIVE SCOPED GATE IS OWED — *"it was probably covered"* IS THE EXACT UNEXAMINED-AUTHORITY PATTERN §15/§20 EXIST TO STOP.** ⚖️ **Consistency decides it: at TASK-461 and TASK-462 the rule was *code that changed after a PASS needs a verdict, no exception for "it only fixes the build."* A task whose review was NEVER SCOPED has a WEAKER claim than one whose review merely predated a diff — so it cannot receive a MORE generous ruling.** ✅ **The real mitigation SCOPES the remediation; it does not replace it: the gate covers only what no prior gate named, and it is small precisely because the file was genuinely read.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE BREACH IS RECORDED AS A BREACH.** The hard gate is *"nothing is committed to Git without a PASS QA report (code)"*, and by the letter **442's FSM skeleton was committed without one.** ⛔ **Do not write this up as "covered in substance."** The commit stands (reverting green, reviewed-in-file code would be worse), **the remediation is the retroactive gate, and the record says what happened.**

> ### ✅ **THE FIX IS ONE LINE PER TASK: EVERY BATCH CARRIES A COVERAGE LEDGER — `task → gate` — AND THE UNION IS CROSS-CHECKED AGAINST THE ROSTER *BEFORE* THE INTEGRATION COMMIT.**

- **The manager writes it at decomposition and updates it as gates land; build-master REFUSES TO COMMIT until every code task in the batch names a gate** (or names an explicit, reasoned exemption). ⚖️ **This is the cheapest possible control for the most expensive possible miss, and it would have caught 442 at gate 446 rather than at the flip.**
- ⚠️ **NOTE WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE: EVERY INDIVIDUAL GATE WAS CORRECT AND COMPLETE FOR ITS OWN SCOPE.** ⛔ **The defect existed only in the UNION, which no artifact expressed** — the same shape as the QUIET-MODULE LAW (*"each batch's own board looks safe in isolation"*). **A property of a set cannot be checked by inspecting its members one at a time.**

### 27. ⚖️ A COMPILE-FIX DIFF IS **CODE** — IT NEEDS A VERDICT, AND THE GATE IS SCOPED TO **THE DIFF**, NOT TO THE TASK (2026-08-03)

> **Any code change made AFTER a PASS needs its own verdict before commit — the hard gate admits no exception for *"it only fixes the build."* ⛔ BUT THE GATE COVERS THE DIFF, NOT A RE-REVIEW OF THE ALREADY-PASSED TASK.**

- ⚖️ **BOTH HALVES ARE LOAD-BEARING AND THEY PULL AGAINST EACH OTHER, WHICH IS WHY THE RULE HAS TO SAY BOTH.** Skip the gate and an unreviewed change rides in on an old PASS — the exact thing the hard gate exists to stop. **Re-review the whole task and the gate becomes expensive enough to skip next time, AND it dilutes attention away from the only thing that actually moved.** ⇒ **Scope it to the diff, name the fences, and keep it cheap enough that nobody is tempted to argue it away.**
- **IN SCOPE:** the changed lines · **the fences the fix must not have disturbed**, verified at the artifact · whether the fix introduced **a new way to leave a function or loop** (§20) · and whether the **same shape exists elsewhere** (§22).
- ⛔ **OUT OF SCOPE:** everything the earlier PASS already covered. **A build-fix gate that re-litigates a passed design is not being thorough; it is being unable to stop.**
- 📌 **CONSISTENT WITH CLAUDE.md's routing rule** (*a build failure routes back to the programmer and **counts as a QA loop***) — this clause just says what that returning loop reviews. **Precedents: TASK-461 over TASK-459; and the TASK-444/450 compile-fix gate.**

> ### ⛔ 27b. A COMPILE-GATE PASS ATTACHES TO A **COMMIT**, NEVER TO A **LANE** (2026-08-03)
> **A green build describes the exact tree that was built. It does NOT extend to code that lands afterwards in the same files, the same lane, or the same batch.**

- **THE LIVE INSTANCE: TASK-447's ✅ describes `cd5f4ed`.** Build-master flagged `SiegeAssistantComponent.{h,cpp}` as **modified and unstaged** at hand-off and warned **its PASS does not extend to them.** ✅ **The writer was known to the orchestrator (TASK-463) and invisible from inside the task — flagging it was CORRECT rather than paranoid**, and it is the behaviour to copy: **report the tree you actually left, not the tree you were asked about.**
- ⇒ ⛔ **ANY CODE LANDING AFTER THE COMMIT NEEDS ITS OWN QA *AND* A SECOND COMPILE+COMMIT RUN.** ⚠️ **Board that second gate AS A PLANNED STEP at the moment the first one closes** — not as something remembered at the end, and **never as an assumption that "the batch already compiled."**
- ⚖️ **THIS IS §29's LESSON ON THE TIME AXIS: §29 says a gate covers the tasks it NAMES; 27b says a build covers the TREE IT BUILT.** ⛔ **Both failures look identical from the outside — a green mark standing next to code nobody checked.**

### 28. ⚖️ REVIEW EXPOSURE IS NOT UNIFORM ACROSS A CODEBASE — **LOOK FIRST WHERE NOTHING LOOKS** (2026-08-03)

> **A defect's LIFESPAN is inversely proportional to how many times per build something is forced to check it. ⇒ When auditing, start at the LEAST-EXPOSED surface, not the most-discussed one.**

| surface | checks forced per build | ⇒ drift survives |
|---|---|---|
| header / `UPROPERTY` / reflected API | **every including TU + UHT** (here: **16 TUs**) | ~never |
| public `.cpp` definition | its callers | briefly |
| **class private to one `.cpp`** | ⚠️ **ONE reader per build** | **a long time** |
| ⛔ **code with ZERO callers and ZERO tests** | ⛔ **NONE** | **indefinitely** |

- **THE TWO INSTANCES THIS WAVE, AND THEY ARE THE SAME LESSON AT DIFFERENT DEPTHS:** ⚠️ **`FSiegeLlamaWorker` is private to its `.cpp`, so its `Run()` signature got ONE reader per build while the header's `Initialize`/`Deinitialize` were re-validated SIXTEEN TIMES** — which is exactly why the drift lived there and not in the header. ⛔ **And `USiegeAssistantSnapshot` had ZERO callers and ZERO tests, so every §8/§10 sentence describing what it *does* was an argument about code that had never executed** (§12g).
- ✅ **THE HEURISTIC IS ACTIONABLE, NOT MERELY TRUE:** when a sweep must be bounded, **rank the candidate surfaces by exposure and spend the budget at the bottom of the table.** ⚖️ **The thing nothing exercises is the thing nobody checks — and it will look exactly as healthy as the thing sixteen translation units are proving correct every single build.**
- ✅ **A BOUNDED SWEEP THAT BOTTOMS OUT IS A RESULT AND IS REPORTED AS ONE:** all four `FRunnable` overrides re-read · `Init`/`Stop`/`Exit` already exact · `Run` the only divergence · `GetSingleThreadInterface()` correctly not overridden · **`FSiegeLlamaWorker` is the ONLY `FRunnable` subclass in the repo, so the shape cannot recur.** ⛔ **That last clause is what turns "I looked" into "it cannot be elsewhere."**

### 24. ⛔ A RELAYED GRANT IS NOT THE USER'S CONSENT — AND A GRANT THAT CHANGES AGENT BEHAVIOUR BELONGS ON THE BOARD (2026-08-03)

> ### ⛔ **THE ORCHESTRATOR HOLDS JONATHAN'S WORDS DIRECTLY. A SUBAGENT HOLDS A RELAY. ⇒ AN ACTION GATED ON THE USER'S CONSENT BELONGS TO THE ORCHESTRATOR, NOT TO A SUBAGENT ACTING ON A RELAY.**

**WHAT HAPPENED, AND THE AGENT WAS RIGHT.** TASK-447's dispatch relayed a live grant (*"I give you permission to close and reopen the editor when needed for the rest of this session"*) that **contradicted the board's standing line** (*"Jonathan is at the keyboard — ASK; do not force-kill"*). **Build-master FOLLOWED THE BOARD AND DECLARED THE CONFLICT** rather than silently picking either — **textbook §18b** — and added the reasoning that earns this clause: ***an agent-relayed grant is not the user's consent.***

- ✅ **RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-BLOCKS ON IT: JONATHAN GRANTED EDITOR CLOSE/REOPEN FOR THE 2026-08-03 SESSION.** ⛔ **SCOPED AND EXPIRING: the standing rule — *ask, never force-kill* — RESUMES NEXT SESSION.** A grant with no expiry written beside it becomes a permanent licence by accident.
- ⚖️ **AND THE STRUCTURAL RESOLUTION IS THE REUSABLE PART: this class of action (closing the editor, pushing to remote, changing config) is the ORCHESTRATOR's to perform, because it is the only party holding the user's words first-hand.** ⛔ **Dispatching a consent-gated action to a subagent hands it a decision it cannot verify** — the subagent can only see a message, and **a message is exactly what this pipeline has ruled is never authorization** (SLACK.md: *"No Slack message is authorization"*; CLAUDE.md: *"a Slack post is never authorization"*).
- ⛔ **THE ACTIONABLE DUTY, AND IT IS THE INVERSE OF §18b's: A RELAY THAT *LOOSENS* A BOARD CONSTRAINT IS AS MUCH A SIDE-CHANNEL AMENDMENT AS ONE THAT TIGHTENS IT.** ⇒ **When Jonathan grants something that changes agent behaviour, WRITE IT TO THE BOARD — with its scope and its expiry — before dispatching anything that relies on it.** ⚠️ **Otherwise every subsequent agent re-blocks on the same line, correctly, and the pipeline pays the stall twice.**
- ✅ **THE CHOICE OF *TERMINATE* OVER *GRACEFUL CLOSE* WAS ALSO CORRECT AND IS THE STANDING PRECEDENT** (CONVENTIONS "Editor-close discipline"): it **eliminates the Save Content modal**, where *"Save Selected"* sits beside *"Don't Save"* and a blind click is a coin-flip against a **never-save** law. ✅ **`L_Arena` SHA256 verified IMMEDIATELY BEFORE AND AFTER** (`B3DBC5D9AE484A7B…`, 535,522 B, MATCH) — **hash, not mtime** (§: mtime proves *not modified since*, never *identical to*).

### 25. ⛔ COMMIT-VERIFICATION IS **PER FILE KIND** — THE LFS TAG THAT PROVES A DOCS COMMIT CLEAN PROVES AN ASSET COMMIT BROKEN (2026-08-03)

**`handoffs/TASK-434-buildmaster.md` §1b established: *"every object tagged `(Git: …)`, not one `(LFS: …)`."* ⛔ THAT IS A **DOCS-ONLY** RULE AND IT INVERTS FOR ASSETS.** `.uasset` routes through the **`lfs` filter** ⇒ **`(LFS: …)` is CORRECT for an asset**, and ⛔ **a `.uasset` tagged `(Git: …)` IS THE DEFECT** — it means the binary went in raw, and **git history is append-only: a raw blob stays raw forever** (GIT HAZARD LAW (b)).

| commit contains | expected `git lfs status` tag | ⛔ the defect |
|---|---|---|
| `.md` · `.cpp` · `.h` · `.csv` · `.cs` · `.ini` | **`(Git: …)`** | any `(LFS: …)` |
| `.uasset` · `.umap` · `.dll` · `.lib` · `.png` | **`(LFS: …)`** | **any `(Git: …)`** |

- ⚖️ **SAME FAMILY AS §19 AND §23: a check whose correctness depends on a property of what it is checking, applied uniformly, silently inverts.** ⇒ **State the file kind, then the expected tag — never carry a tag expectation across commit kinds.**
- ✅ **VERIFY WITH `git check-attr filter -- <path>` PER KIND BEFORE COMMITTING**, and paste it (§7's *"acceptance, not assertion"*).

### 26. 📌 LIVE CODING CANNOT INTRODUCE NEW REFLECTED TYPES — A FULL RESTART IS MANDATORY, NOT PREFERRED (2026-08-03)

**`Ctrl+Alt+F11` is NOT an escape hatch when a batch adds UCLASSes.** This batch added **6 new `UCLASS`es + 3 test TUs**, and **Live Coding cannot introduce new reflected types** ⇒ **a full editor restart was MANDATORY.** ⛔ **Offering Live Coding as a way to avoid an editor bounce in that situation wastes a gate.**
- ⚠️ **AND ITS PRESENCE IS ITSELF A BUILD BLOCKER:** `Unable to build while Live Coding is active … Result: Failed (OtherCompilationError)` in **6.44 s**. ⛔ **Neither of the two standing false-attribution traps applies to that line — rule them out BY MEASUREMENT, as this gate did: it is NOT a code error** (UBT never reached a compiler — **zero `error C…`, zero `LNK…`**) **and NOT Smart App Control** (`VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState = 0`). ✅ **`$LASTEXITCODE` was 6 and was correctly NOT consulted — the log was parsed** (§17).

### 23. ⚖️ A MEASUREMENT'S **BASE** IS PART OF THE MEASUREMENT — A VARYING VALUE IN THE RIGHT FIELD IS STILL WRONG IF ITS BASE IS WRONG (2026-08-03)

**TASK-459's check was deeper than its fix, and the depth is the lesson.** Substituting *any* elapsed-time expression would have produced a **varying** number and looked correct. **`PrefillStart` and `DecodeStart` were both in scope at both sites, both vary, and both would have been WRONG** — because the ceiling being tested is `HardDeadlineSeconds = StartSeconds + HardTimeoutSeconds`, so **only a value based on `StartSeconds` can be compared against it.** ⇒ ✅ **`printed − 10.0` IS the overshoot; `now − PrefillStart` is a number that varies and means nothing at that field.**

- ⚖️ **THE GENERAL FORM, AND IT IS §19's PRINCIPLE ONE LEVEL UP: CORRECTNESS IS A PROPERTY OF THE *RELATIONSHIP*, NOT OF THE SURFACE FORM.** §19: a constant's safe direction comes from its **role**, not its value. **§23: a measurement's meaning comes from its base, not from the fact that it moves.** ⛔ **"It varies now" is not evidence a metric is correct — that is the same false comfort as "the call returned" (§17) and "the test passes" (§13).**
- ✅ **AND IT WAS PROVED EMPIRICALLY RATHER THAN ARGUED:** the substituted expression is the **same subtraction over the same two symbols** that already compute the published `TtftMs` / `WallMs`, and TASK-413 printed **both varying** (TTFT 417.3 / 1655.4 / 2716.1 ms; wall worsts 2937.5 / 8926.3 / 10000.6 ms). **The proof that the new value varies is a citation, not an assurance.**
- ⚠️ **DECLARED LIMIT, KEPT RATHER THAN SMOOTHED: `%.1f` FLOORS AT ~50 ms, so a tiny overshoot still renders `10.0s`.** ⇒ ⚖️ **It is now *a measurement that ROUNDS to the ceiling* rather than *a constant that IGNORES it* — a real improvement, and not the same as exact.** ✅ **The format was deliberately NOT changed, because that was not the argument** — a fix that quietly widens its own scope is harder to review than one that states its residue.

#### 18c. ⚠️ LINE NUMBERS IN ANY GATE CRITERION ARE AS-OF-AUTHORING AND ROT ON EVERY SUBSEQUENT EDIT — **VERIFY BY SYMBOL, NEVER BY OFFSET**

**TASK-453's `AttachConsoleWidget` call moved `4324 → 4348` when TASK-454 inserted ~72 lines above it. Nothing about it changed.** ⇒ ⛔ **A QA criterion, handoff or law clause citing an absolute line is a POINTER THAT DECAYS**, and this batch has already been bitten once (**WARN-4: 7 of 7 game-lane `file:line` anchors stale**).
- ✅ **Cite `file:symbol` and use the offset only as a hint.** **A criterion that fails ONLY because a number moved has found nothing** — and worse, it manufactures a finding, which is §14's manufacture-vs-mask problem in a different tool.
- ⛔ **AND IT APPLIES TO THE BOARD ITSELF, WHICH IS NOW THE WORST OFFENDER: `TASKBOARD.md` IS ~2.2 MB WITH MANY WRITERS, AND TASK-465's OWN HEADER MOVED `6252 → 6331` BETWEEN TWO READS, MID-TASK (~79 lines).** ⇒ ⚠️ **ANY BOARD LINE NUMBER IS ALREADY STALE WHEN IT IS WRITTEN.** ✅ **Cite the `#### TASK-###` HEADING, never an offset** — every agent survived today because they verified by symbol, and that is the only reason.
- ✅ **RELATIVE ORDER IS THE DURABLE ASSERTION AND IT IS WHAT THESE GATES ACTUALLY MEAN:** *"`AttachConsoleWidget` precedes `OpenConsole()`"* survives every insertion; *"`:4324` precedes `:4357`"* does not. **Write the ordering, not the addresses.**

### 20. ⚖️ EXCLUSION SELECTIONS · THE "ALL UNITS" REPAIR · THE CONFIRM-UX CHANGE (2026-08-04) — batch **ASSISTANT-EXCLUDE**, on Jonathan's three rulings

**His directive, verbatim:** *"I want to improve the AI model that takes commands. First of all, sometimes you may want to send all units except for some. For example (All units except miners), it doesnt seem to handle that very well. Also, whenever I say all units, it doesn't seem to include sorcerers even when they were spawned. Also, lets change the command confirmation, instead of it being a cancel button and an accept button, lets make it to where there is no cancel button(they just simply close the chat box), and instead of an accept button they press "z"("z" button for QWERTY, make it that same button location for other keyboards)"*

**⛔ THREE BINDING RULINGS, GIVEN VIA `AskUserQuestion`, RECORDED, NOT RE-OPENABLE:** **(1)** `MaxRosterKinds` **8 → 13** — the D2 escalation, spent by its owner (written into the FINE-TUNE §3 D2 clause, where the escalation lives). **(2)** `Escape` **stays exactly as it is** — written into `AS-§6` RULING A-2, which is now **CLOSED**. **(3)** **Real exclusion in the schema** — *"all except X (and Y)"* becomes a genuine executable order; ⛔ **he DECLINED the count-controlled variant (*"all except 5 archers"*), so it is WHOLE-KIND EXCLUSION ONLY** and that limit is made **structural** below, not documentary.

#### 20.1 ⚖️ THE EXCLUSION SCHEMA — RULED: A NEW `who` **SHAPE**, ⛔ NEVER A NEW TOP-LEVEL KEY

> ### **`"who": {"all_except": ["miner"]}` — a third shape for an existing key. The top-level key set does NOT change.**

**The alternative was a fourth top-level key (`"except": …`) and it is REJECTED, on this schema's own rules:** the parser validates an **EXACT key set** (`ValidateExactKeySet`) and the prompt law says **every key is always emitted** — so a new top-level key would have to appear in **every** emission, rewriting all seven few-shots, every corpus row's expected JSON, and the parser's key set **at once**. ⇒ **A `who` shape is STRICTLY ADDITIVE AT THE WIRE: every JSON the model emits today stays valid, byte-for-byte, and nothing that already passes starts failing.** That is the same property `AS-§2` demands of the feature as a whole, applied to its own schema.

- ⭐ **THE DECLINED FEATURE IS MADE *INEXPRESSIBLE*, NOT MERELY UNIMPLEMENTED.** The exclusion list is an array of **bare kind symbols**, ⛔ **never `{"kind":…,"n":…}` items.** ⇒ *"all except 5 archers"* has **no shape in the grammar to be sampled into** — the model physically cannot produce it. ⚖️ **That is §1's "grammar guarantees existence" doing the product's work: a declined feature enforced by a shape cannot be re-introduced by a tired author, a prompt tweak, or a model that guessed.**
- ⛔⭐ **`all_except` IS LEGAL *ONLY* WHERE `SiegeAssistantIntentTakesSelection(Intent)` IS TRUE — i.e. `send` / `guard` / `ambush` / `follow`. THE PARSER REFUSES IT FOR `charge` / `fallback` / `rally` WITH `ExcludeConflict`.** ⚠️ **This is not conservatism, it is a hard constraint read off the executor:** `Charge` and `Fallback` are executed by calling **`ASiegePlayerController::ApplyArmyWideStance(...)` — the SAME shipped API the `T` and `E` keys call, release law included** — and `Rally` calls `AHeroCharacter::Rally()`. **None of the three goes through the selector.** ⇒ Honouring an exception for them would mean **either editing a shipped controller API that two keys depend on, or building an assistant-side "army-wide except" path — and the second is precisely the *"never a parallel implementation, never a better one"* that `AS-§2` forbids by name.**
- ⛔⛔ **AND THE FAILURE THIS PREVENTS IS THE ONE THIS WHOLE DESIGN EXISTS TO PREVENT: PARSING AN EXCEPTION AND THEN DROPPING IT.** The parser's existing comment records that for an army-wide verb *"the executor ignores the selection"* — **harmless for a selection, CATASTROPHIC for an exception**, because *"fall back, except the miners"* would execute as *"fall back, including the miners"*: **a valid-shaped wrong command that looks obeyed.** ⇒ **REFUSE AT THE PARSER. ⛔ Silently ignoring `ExcludeKinds` on any path is an automatic QA FAIL.**
- ✅ **JONATHAN'S OWN EXAMPLE IS FULLY SERVED BY THIS: he wrote *"send all units except for some"* and *"(All units except miners)"* — `send` is selection-bearing.** ⚠️ **`DEV-08` (*"everyone fall back except the miners"*) is the CORPUS AUTHOR's framing, not his, and it STAYS `Clarify`** — see 20.6 for the Notes rewrite, because the *reason* it clarifies changes completely even though the verdict does not.
- **SEMANTICS, PINNED:** `ExcludeKinds` is meaningful **only** alongside the `all` selection (`Kinds` empty). ⛔ **`Kinds` non-empty AND `ExcludeKinds` non-empty is a parse FAILURE (`ExcludeConflict`), never a merge** — *"send 10 footmen except miners"* is a confused sentence and the FSM must ask, not guess. **`who:"none"` with an exclusion is the same failure.**
- **EMPTY-AFTER-EXCLUSION:** if every eligible unit is excluded, the executor **refuses the whole order** and reports it through the **existing unsupported-ask outcome**, carrying the precise arithmetic **in the log** — ⛔ **exactly as the shipped shortfall path already does, and citing it.** ⛔ **NO NEW `ask` SYMBOL** — the ask alternatives are part of the grammar the model samples from, and widening them is a schema change nobody asked for. ⛔ **And it is NEVER a silent no-op:** "nothing happened and nothing was said" is the shape that reads as *"the assistant ate my order"*.

**📌 PINNED CROSS-TASK REGISTRY (`AS-§20`) — every task compiles against this character-for-character; UBT builds the whole module and "improving" a pinned name breaks the link:**

```cpp
// ── SiegeAssistantCommand.h ───────────────────────────────────────────────

/** Whole KINDS subtractable from an "all" selection. Mirrors SiegeAssistantMaxSelectionKinds
 *  deliberately: bounded in the GRAMMAR as an alternation, and re-checked in the PARSER
 *  because M8 P2 takes this struct over the wire from a peer that passed no grammar.
 *  ⛔ Widening it is a MANAGER RULING (the ruling-15 precedent), not a tuner's edit. */
static constexpr int32 SiegeAssistantMaxExclusionKinds = 3;

struct FSiegeAssistantCommand
{
    // ... the five shipped fields, UNCHANGED, IN THEIR SHIPPED ORDER ...

    /** Kinds SUBTRACTED from an "all" selection. Non-empty ONLY when Kinds is empty,
     *  and ONLY when SiegeAssistantIntentTakesSelection(Intent). No counts, by design. */
    UPROPERTY()
    TArray<FName> ExcludeKinds;
};

namespace SiegeAssistantJsonKeys { inline constexpr const TCHAR* AllExcept = TEXT("all_except"); }

namespace SiegeAssistantReason
{
    inline constexpr const TCHAR* ExcludeArity    = TEXT("exclude_arity");     // 0 or > SiegeAssistantMaxExclusionKinds
    inline constexpr const TCHAR* ExcludeConflict = TEXT("exclude_conflict");  // with a selection, with "none", or on an army-wide intent
}
```

- **GBNF (generated, and generated the same way `selection` is):** `except ::= "{" "\"all_except\"" ":" except_list "}"` · `except_list` = a **BOUNDED ALTERNATION of exactly 1, 2 and 3 bare kind strings**, generated from `SiegeAssistantMaxExclusionKinds` · `who ::= selection | except | "all" | "none"`. ⛔ **Emitted ONLY when the roster has kinds** (`bHasKinds`) — an empty roster has nothing to exclude, exactly as it has nothing to select. ⚠️ **If `_` is not a legal character in this emitter's rule names, use `exceptlist` and DECLARE the departure (`SC-§15`) — do not silently pick a third name.**
- **`SiegeAssistantValidateSelection` KEEPS ITS NAME AND GAINS THE INVARIANTS:** `ExcludeKinds.Num() <= SiegeAssistantMaxExclusionKinds` · no repeated symbol (reuse `DuplicateKind`) · empty whenever `Kinds` is non-empty. **Symbol validation reuses `ParseKindSymbol`** — ⛔ **no second kind-validation path.**
- **EXECUTOR:** the filter goes in the **existing `Command.Kinds.Num() == 0` branch**, comparing `Candidate.Unit->GetCardID()` against `ExcludeKinds` — ⛔ **`FName` comparison is already case-insensitive and the snapshot already owns the symbol transform; do NOT re-lower-case and do NOT re-derive the mapping** (the shipped comment at that seam says why).
- 📌 **M8: `ExcludeKinds` is a `TArray<FName>` — the struct stays `uint8`/`int32`/`FName`-only, so §3's *"M8 P2 takes `FSiegeAssistantCommand` as-is over the wire"* property SURVIVES.** ⛔ **This feature adds no replicated property, no new replicated class, and no new relevancy tier.**

#### 20.2 ⛔ THE SORCERER DEFECT — THE CAUSE IS THE **PROMPT**, AND THE CAP RAISE ALONE DOES NOT CLOSE IT

**Exonerated, at the artifact, so nobody re-investigates them:** ✅ the executor's `who:"all"` branch walks the world itself and **does** include Sorcerers · ✅ `IsGroupCommandEligible()` keys on `CanTakeZoneOrders()` and the Sorcerer **deliberately keeps `Standard`** for exactly this reason · ✅ `CanEverAttack()` is read only inside the attack machine, **never by any selection path** · ✅ the vocabulary table is **NOT empty** (§12a's stale claim is struck, above) and lists `sorcerer`.

**The actual cause is three stacked prompt layers:** **(i)** `MaxRosterKinds = 8` collapsed the tail into `other_kinds: N kinds, M units` — **counts only, NO SYMBOLS** — and roster order is fixed `DT_Cards` row order in which **`Sorcerer` is the LAST of the 13 commandable rows**, so it was **always the first kind collapsed** and the token `sorcerer` never reached the model. **(ii)** Zone A then instructs *"if the unit named is not a kind in [FORCES], answer unsupported"* — a rule the author had already flagged as hazardous three lines above it. **(iii)** **No exemplar or rule ever demonstrated the bare `who:"all"`**; the only *"everyone"* few-shot maps to `who:"none"`.

- ⛔⭐ **AND THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE FIX: RAISING THE CAP TO 13 DOES *NOT* GUARANTEE 13 ROWS PRINT.** The roster is **elastic** — `RosterBudget = SnapshotTrimBudgetChars − ZoneBCharReserve − Head − Tail`, and **the `order:` line is IN the Tail** and may run to `MaxUtteranceBytes`. **At 13 kinds the block measures ~887 against ~893, so roughly SEVEN CHARACTERS of a longer sentence re-collapses a kind — and the kind it collapses is the Sorcerer, every time, because it is last.** ⇒ **A player who types a long order gets the ORIGINAL BUG BACK, silently.**
- ⇒ ⭐⭐ **THEREFORE THE ROOT-CAUSE FIX IS NOT THE CAP — IT IS THAT `other_kinds:` MUST PRINT THE COLLAPSED KINDS' **NAMES**.**
  > ### ⛔ **`other_kinds: sorcerer, cleric (5 units)` — NAMES, NOT JUST COUNTS. A COLLAPSE MAY HIDE A KIND'S *NUMBERS*; IT MAY NEVER HIDE ITS *NAME*.**
  - ✅ **This closes the class at ANY cap and at ANY board size**, it is **cheaper in characters than the row it replaces** (a name is ~8 chars; a full roster row is ~46), and — decisively — **it makes Zone A's refusal rule TRUE again**, because a name the model can see is a name it can check `[FORCES]` membership against. ⚠️ **The grammar has ALWAYS admitted every collapsed kind** (`GetUnitKinds()` is never truncated), so before this change the sampler could name a unit the prompt had not shown it. **That gap is what is actually being closed.**
  - ⚖️ **JONATHAN'S RULING 1 SHIPS AS HE GAVE IT AND IS NOT SUBSTITUTED FOR.** The cap goes to 13. **This is an ADDITIONAL repair he was not offered because it had not been found** — the two compose, and together they mean the token `sorcerer` is in the prompt **whether or not the trimmer bites.**
  - ✅ **`t0` IS UNAFFECTED BY THE FORMAT CHANGE:** at 13 kinds on a 13-kind board **nothing collapses**, so both lanes still print `other_kinds: none`, byte-identically. **The new format is only reachable on a board this project has never measured.**
- ⛔ **THE THREE LEGS ARE ALL REQUIRED AND ARE BOARDED SEPARATELY SO A PARTIAL LANDING IS VISIBLE:** the cap (**his ruling**) · the `other_kinds:` names (**the root cause**) · Zone A's `who:"all"` rule (**the behaviour he actually complained about**). ⚠️ **A handoff that lands one and reports the defect fixed has not measured it.**

#### 20.3 ⚠️ NAMED, BOARDED RISK — **THE 6-CHARACTER HEADROOM** (`MaxRosterKinds = 13`)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | At 13 kinds Zone C's roster measures **~887 chars against a ~893-char budget**. ⇒ **~6 chars of headroom.** A **14th commandable unit kind**, a longer `pending:` line, or a longer typed `order:` re-collapses the tail. |
| **Owner-accepted** | ✅ **Jonathan was told this number and accepted it**, including that *"a future unit kind forces a re-tune."* ⛔ **This is not an open question and is not re-escalated.** |
| **Why it is survivable** | ⭐ **`other_kinds:` now prints NAMES (20.2), so a collapse degrades the roster's NUMBERS and never hides a kind's EXISTENCE.** ⇒ **The failure mode drops from *"the model refuses a unit that is alive"* to *"the model does not know how many of them there are"* — a clarification, not a refusal.** |
| **The one honest lever** | **`ZoneBCharReserve = 192` is a documented ~124-char OVER-CHARGE** (measured Zone B readings: 68 / 71). **Lowering it can only WIDEN the roster and is safe in the only direction it will ever move** — it buys **~20× the current headroom.** |
| ⛔ **The condition on that lever** | **IT MUST BE SET FROM A *PRINTED* `zoneB_chars` OFF A LIVE `FIRST LIVE CAPTURE` LINE, FROM *THIS* BUILDER — NEVER FROM A DERIVED WORST CASE, AND NEVER FROM THE SPIKE'S `AppendZoneB`.** §12g's whole subject. ⇒ **Boarded as a MEASURE-FIRST task, blocked on a reading that does not exist yet. ⛔ If the reading does not happen, the task does not proceed — it does not fall back to arithmetic.** |
| ⛔ **Also banned** | **Raising `SnapshotTrimBudgetChars` to buy the room** — the constant's own header forbids it, and `AS-§19`'s three-role table is why: 1085 is an **authority** value and must assume the smallest plausible chars/token ratio. |

#### 20.4 ⛔ THE ZONE-A CHANGE BUDGET, AND **NO NEW FEW-SHOT SENTENCES** — THE SEAL DECIDES THIS, NOT TASTE

- ⛔ **HARD CEILING, CARRIED FROM §12c AND RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: `zoneA_tok ≤ 1389`.** The arithmetic is structural, not wave-scoped — `1389 + 400 (B+C) + 96 (output) + ~13 (template) = 1898 of ContextTokens 2048`. ⛔ **`ContextTokens` STAYS 2048.** ⇒ **250 tok of room, derived at §12g's pinned WORST MARGINAL RATE (~1.3 chars/tok) = ⛔ A TOTAL ZONE-A GROWTH BUDGET OF ~325 CHARACTERS FOR THIS ENTIRE BATCH.** ⛔ **Over budget ⇒ STOP and escalate. Do NOT trim an existing few-shot to make room** — every one of them is a measured lesson, and the `:940` economy/card-play line is **byte-frozen** and must not be touched at all.
- ⛔⭐ **RULED: ZONE A GAINS **RULE LINES ONLY**. NO NEW FEW-SHOT *SENTENCES* IN THIS BATCH.** Three reasons, and the first is the binding one:
  1. 🔒 **THE SEAL. `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout2.csv` IS SEALED AND THIS BATCH MAY NOT OPEN IT** — and the few-shot law requires **literal disjointness from all three corpus files.** ⚠️ **TASK-430 proved a new sentence CANNOT be cleared without opening them: two agents given the same public brief converged on the same invented noun and NEITHER COULD SEE IT.** ⇒ **A new few-shot sentence would require spending the seal for an absence certificate. A rule line requires nothing** — the disjointness law binds **example sentences**, not instructions. ✅ **So the seal is not spent, and this is the cheapest possible way to keep it unspent.**
  2. ⭐ **THE WAVE ALREADY MEASURED THAT A DECISION-ORDERING RULE BEATS AN EXEMPLAR HERE.** The `who:"none"` collapse (DEV-07 / DEV-16) was fixed by **a rule with a testable antecedent**, after the exemplar-and-emphasis approach was measured *losing* 4-to-1 against the prompt's own competing instructions. **A rule is the instrument this seam responded to.**
  3. **Chars.** Two rule lines fit the 325-char budget with room; two few-shot pairs do not.
- ⚠️ **THE COST, STATED SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED LATER: a rule line is a WEAKER teaching signal than an exemplar for a brand-new output SHAPE the model has never seen.** ⇒ ⛔ **This is the batch's #1 accuracy risk and it goes on the human gate, not in a footnote.** **If Jonathan's playtest shows exclusion or "all units" still missing, the next pass may spend a few-shot — but only with HIS instruction to run the absence certificate, and the certificate is a mechanical boolean per candidate string, never a read.**

**⛔⛔ AND THE TRAP THAT ANY ZONE-A EDIT WALKS INTO — NAMED HERE BECAUSE THE OBVIOUS FIX IS THE FORBIDDEN ONE:**

> **Editing `BuildZoneA` BREAKS TWO SHIPPED, PASSING AUTOMATION TESTS: `Siegebound.Assistant.ZoneA.TwoLaneByteEquality` and `Siegebound.Assistant.ZoneA.MeasuredCharCount` (5116). The equality test's own failure message says *"do NOT edit the frozen spike fixture to silence this"*.**

- ⛔ **THAT MESSAGE IS RIGHT FOR AN *ACCIDENTAL* DIVERGENCE AND WRONG FOR A *DELIBERATE, RULED* ONE — SO THE PROCEDURE IS WRITTEN HERE RATHER THAN LEFT TO WHOEVER HITS THE RED BAR AT 2 A.M.**
- ⛔ **`Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/SiegeLlama/Private/SiegeLlamaSpike.cpp` IS NOT TOUCHED BY THIS BATCH.** It is **another batch's in-flight instrument** (`FT-§16` reclassified it as load-bearing test infrastructure; TASK-481 is mid-flight against it). ⇒ **The shipped lane moves and the spike lane does not: that is a NEW, DECLARED divergence — call it D4 — and it is RECORDED, not hidden and not "fixed" by editing the test's transcription until it agrees.**
- ⛔⭐ **THE TEST MUST NOT BE "UPDATED TO PASS". Its subject — *"the shipped prompt is the prompt the numbers were measured on"* — becomes FALSE the moment we deliberately change the shipped prompt, and no edit can make it true again.** ⇒ **Re-purpose it honestly: it asserts the shipped lane against a NAMED, DATED shipped baseline, and it states in its own message that the spike lane has diverged and why.** ⚠️ **A test whose transcription is silently re-copied from the thing it is testing is a guardrail that reports SAFE — the exact failure this test file's own comments rail against.**
- ⛔ **NUMBERS: the CHAR count is RE-COUNTED (chars are countable without a model — §8 says so). ⛔ THE TOKEN COUNT IS NOT.** ⇒ **`zoneA_tok = 1139`, the `77.1 %` KV-reuse figure and every prefill number derived from them are RELABELLED **STALE — PENDING RE-MEASUREMENT ON THE MODEL**, ⛔ NOT recomputed by arithmetic and ⛔ NOT deleted.** **They are re-measured by `Siege.Llama.SpikePrompt`, which is the only instrument that prints them, at the FINE-TUNE batch's own re-measurement step.** ⚖️ **A stale number that says it is stale is safe; a stale number wearing a fresh label is how §8 lost two days.**

#### 20.5 THE CONFIRM UX — NO CANCEL BUTTON, NO ACCEPT BUTTON, **`Z` ACCEPTS**

- ✅ **PURE C++, ZERO ASSET WORK** — the tree is code-authored under `AS-§6` RULING A, so there is no `.uasset` to edit. **The pin and the close-route contract are amended in `AS-§6` (above), not here**, because that is where the next reader will look.
- ⛔⭐ **THE ONLY VIABLE INTERCEPT IS `UUserWidget::NativeOnPreviewKeyDown` ON `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget`. THE TWO ALTERNATIVES ARE REJECTED HERE SO THEY ARE NOT RE-PROPOSED:**
  - ⛔ **`NativeOnKeyDown` — NOT VIABLE ALONE.** It bubbles **UP** from the focused leaf, so for a printable key the `SEditableText` has already handled it never fires. **Preview runs DOWN the focus path from the root, before the leaf** — that is the entire reason it is the answer.
  - ⛔ **AN ENHANCED INPUT `IA_AssistantAccept` — REJECTED.** It almost certainly does not fire while the box holds Slate focus (the same reasoning that explains why `Enter` never reached the controller toggle), and it collides with `AS-§2`. ⚠️ **Recorded as rejected WITH the reason, because it is the first thing an input-shaped mind reaches for.**
  - 📌 **THERE IS NO PRECEDENT IN THIS REPO: zero `NativeOnKeyDown` / `NativeOnPreviewKeyDown` / `OnKeyChar` / `FReply` / `SEditableTextBox` overrides exist anywhere in `Source/`.** ⇒ **The implementer VERIFIES from installed UE 5.8 source that `SObjectWidget` forwards preview key events to the `UUserWidget` while a DESCENDANT holds focus, and QUOTES what they verified.** ⛔ **Do not assume it; this is a mechanism nobody here has used.**
- ⛔⛔ **SCOPE THE INTERCEPTION AS NARROWLY AS IT CAN POSSIBLY BE SCOPED: `Z` IS CONSUMED *ONLY* WHILE THE CONSOLE IS OPEN **AND** `bConfirmPromptVisible` IS TRUE. IN EVERY OTHER STATE IT MUST TYPE A NORMAL `z`.** ⚠️ **Typing is NOT blocked during `AwaitConfirm` today** — the box stays editable and submissions are refused at the FSM — **so a global `Z` grab would break typing.** ⇒ **The handler returns `FReply::Unhandled()` for every key and every state except that one, and it returns `Handled` only after `ConfirmPressed()` has actually accepted.**
- ✅ **`AS-§2` IS SATISFIED, AND ONLY UNDER THAT NARROW SCOPE: `Z` IS CURRENTLY BOUND TO NOTHING** (the bound letters are W/A/S/D/Q/T/R/E/F/C). **No shipped key changes behaviour.**
- ⛔ **`Escape` IS NOT TOUCHED BY THIS HANDLER** — see `AS-§6` A-2's now-CLOSED clause. **Returning `Handled` for `Escape`, even harmlessly, overturns a Jonathan ruling.**
- **THE ACCEPT KEY IS RESOLVED POSITIONALLY** via `USiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem::GetPositionalKey(EKeys::Z)` (`KBD-§8`, pin amended above) — ⛔ **never a hard-coded `EKeys::Z` comparison**, and ⛔ **the player-facing prompt still says `Z` on every layout** (the reason is in the `KBD-§8` amendment).
- ⚠️ **ACCEPTED CONSEQUENCE, RECORDED NOT DISCOVERED: while a confirm prompt is up, the player cannot type the letter `z` into the box.** ⚖️ **Judged acceptable because a submission during `AwaitConfirm` is ALREADY refused by the FSM** — the box is editable but useless in that state. **It goes on the human gate as a feel item.**

#### 20.6 THE EVAL CORPUS — ⛔ NO TENTH COLUMN, AND THE SEAL IS NOT SPENT

- 🔒⛔ **`Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout2.csv` IS SEALED AND THIS BATCH DOES NOT OPEN IT, DOES NOT SCORE AGAINST IT, AND DOES NOT QUOTE A ROW.** ✅ **20.4's no-new-few-shot ruling is what makes that free rather than merely obeyed.** **`assistant_eval_dev.csv` is the regression instrument and IS fair game — ⛔ it is never trained on.**
- ⛔ **DO NOT ADD AN `ExpectExcludeKinds` COLUMN.** The header is **pinned character-for-character**, the runner parses it, and **both corpus files share it** — a tenth column in one file makes the two structurally different and breaks the contract silently, after the seal. ⇒ ✅ **Use the accepted `Notes`-prefix mechanism, exactly as `intent=` did: a pinned `except=<kind>|<kind>; ` prefix, matched by `^…except=([a-z|]*); `.** ⛔ **NO RUNNER CHANGE IN THIS BATCH** — teaching `SpikeEval` to score the new field is boarded as a follow-up, **and until it lands the exclusion rows are DOCUMENTATION, not a score.** ⚠️ **Say that in the handoff: a row that is not scored has not measured anything.**
- ⚠️⛔ **`DEV-08` KEEPS ITS `Clarify` VERDICT AND ITS `Notes` ARE REWRITTEN — because the verdict staying the same for a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT REASON is the most misleading state a corpus row can be in.** ~~*"inexpressible; must ask rather than silently drop the exception"*~~ ⇒ **the exception IS expressible now; what is not expressible is an exception on an ARMY-WIDE STANCE VERB, because `charge`/`fallback`/`rally` execute through the shipped `ApplyArmyWideStance` / `Rally` APIs the `T`/`E`/`R` keys call (20.1).** **A reader who leaves the old note in place will later "fix" a row that is already right.**
- ⚠️ **THE MEASUREMENT LIVES IN THE AUTOMATION TESTS, NOT IN THE CSV — AND THE REASON IS THE SCHEMA'S OWN KNOWN LIMITATION: AN EMPTY CELL MEANS *"NOT ASSERTED"*, SO A ROW CANNOT POSITIVELY ASSERT `who:"all"` AT ALL.** ⇒ **`DEV-13` never scored the Sorcerer and could not have.** ⛔ **A repair with no failing test to close is not a repair**, so the batch's actual instruments are: **(a)** a test that a **>8-kind board prints `sorcerer`** in Zone C, and **(b)** a test that the collapse path prints collapsed **NAMES**, and **(c)** parser tests for every exclusion accept/refuse case. **Those are the claims; the CSV rows are behavioural documentation beside them.**
- ⛔ **ANY SCORE MEASURED AFTER THIS CORPUS EDIT IS NOT COMPARABLE TO A SCORE MEASURED BEFORE IT.** The handoff records the exact rows changed and the date. **A ladder graph that spans the edit without saying so is a false trend.**
