### 6. ⛔ WHY `Tools/LlmFinetune/` IS A SIBLING PROJECT AND NOT A FOLDER IN `ArtPipeline`

`Tools/ArtPipeline/` pins 3.12 for `gradio_client`; adding **~4 GB of torch wheels couples two unrelated lanes' dependency resolution**, and a resolver conflict would take the art lane down with the training lane. **Python 3.12 here for an INDEPENDENT reason:** wheel coverage for `bitsandbytes` / torch-cu128 is best on 3.11–3.12 and **system 3.14 has effectively none.** ⚠️ **Write that reason in the `pyproject.toml` header, exactly as `ArtPipeline/pyproject.toml` already does** — two projects pinning the same version for different reasons will otherwise be "helpfully" merged by a later reader.

- **PINS (floors; `uv.lock` is the truth and the handoff records the RESOLVED versions):** `torch >=2.8,<3.0` **from the cu128 index** (⚠️ the RTX 5070 is Blackwell `sm_120` — **pre-2.7 wheels have no kernels for it**) · `bitsandbytes >=0.47` · `transformers >=4.56,<5` · `peft >=0.17` · `trl >=0.24` · `datasets >=4.0` · `accelerate >=1.10` · `safetensors >=0.6`.
- ⛔ **NOT INSTALLED, DELIBERATELY — recorded so nobody adds them as a "fix":** `flash-attn` and `xformers` (**no Windows wheels** — use `attn_implementation="sdpa"`) · `unsloth` (**held as a VRAM lever, not a first-pass dep**) · **no CUDA Toolkit** (the wheels bundle the runtime; this is also §6-boundary-3 of the CUDA amendment).
- **GGUF TOOLCHAIN — PINNED, WITHOUT VENDORING A SECOND COPY.** `llama-quantize.exe` comes from **re-fetching the SAME release zip** `llama-b10235-bin-win-vulkan-x64.zip`, verified against the sha256 **already recorded in `Plugins/SiegeLlama/Source/ThirdParty/LlamaCpp/VERSION.md`**, extracted whole into `Cache/`. ⛔ **The exes were deliberately excluded from `bin/Win64/` at vendoring time — DO NOT put them back.** `convert_hf_to_gguf.py` / `convert_lora_to_gguf.py` come from a llama.cpp clone in `Cache/` at commit **`221f0f6356efe2260023208365705ec5d5a7c8f5`**, the SHA already in `VERSION.md`.
  - ⛔ **WHY THE PIN MATTERS, AND IT GOES IN THE README OR SOMEBODY WILL HELPFULLY UPGRADE IT: CONVERTER/RUNTIME SKEW PRODUCES A GGUF THAT LOADS AND IS SUBTLY WRONG.** Both ends pinned to `221f0f63` eliminates the class. **A commit SHA pins the whole tree; nothing new needs provenancing.**
- **BASE WEIGHTS:** `Qwen/Qwen3-4B` **safetensors** — ⚠️ **a DIFFERENT repo id from the GGUF repo, and it needs its OWN licence verification** (§7's *"a permissive base does not guarantee a permissive re-upload"*, applied in the other direction). Fetch with **`Tools/fetch_llm_model.py` UNCHANGED.**
- **`finetune_env.py --check` mirrors the convention `Tools/fetch_llm_model.py` already set** — every failure **printed rather than raised**, token never printed, ends `CHECK_VERDICT: PASS|FAIL`. ⛔ **The VRAM floor REFUSES; it does not warn** — **a deliberate contrast with the tier selector, which picks a tier it cannot run in.** ⛔ **A real `bnb.nn.Linear4bit` FORWARD on CUDA is run — import success is not function (§32).** `huggingface.co` reachability is probed **BARE** — ⛔ **do NOT reintroduce `SSL_CERT_FILE`; the Norton HF exclusions are live and proven.**

### 7. ⛔ DATA LAW — THE FOUR RULES THAT ARE NOT NEGOTIABLE

1. ⛔ **DO NOT TRAIN ON `Docs/Data/assistant_eval_dev.csv`.** 25 rows is **3 % of an 800-row set** and it is **the only corpus with three waves of comparable history.** ⇒ **It is the REGRESSION INSTRUMENT.** ⚠️ **Catastrophic forgetting is the number-one QLoRA failure mode and it is INVISIBLE in the holdout number** — the holdout only reports what you gained.
2. ⛔ **BURNED ROWS STAY BURNED.** The five spent gen-1 rows and **the retired collision noun** are **CLASS signal, never STRING signal** (§12b) — ⚠️ **and a fine-tune is a FAR more efficient memoriser than a prompt, so this is stricter here than it was there.** QA verifies by **literal match plus near-paraphrase over the whole training set** and issues an **ABSENCE CERTIFICATE ONLY, never a recommendation** (§12b's certificate/recommendation split, which worked and is reused verbatim).
3. ⛔ **§11's AUTHORSHIP SEPARATION IS STRUCTURAL AND EXTENDS TO THE TUNER.** **The corpus author is not the tuner. The tuner NEVER opens `assistant_voice_raw.txt`, `holdout2`, or `holdout3`.** Two dispatches, two contexts — not an honour system.
4. ⛔ **THE GOLD FILE GETS A DIFFERENT HEADER *AND* A SUBDIRECTORY, AND THE REASON IS A MEASURED HAZARD:** `LoadCorpus` only **WARNS** on a header mismatch, so a gold file sitting beside `assistant_eval_dev.csv` is **one tab-completion away from being passed as `dev=`** and silently scored. ⇒ `Docs/Data/Finetune/`, different header. 📌 **RECOMMENDED, NOT YET RULED: make that header mismatch FATAL** — boarded as its own item so it is a decision, not a side effect.

- **GOLD AUTHORING IS FOUR STAGES AND THE CORPORA CARRY *ASSERTIONS*, NOT ANSWERS:** **render** (via `SpikePrompt out=` — ⛔ **Python NEVER assembles a prompt**, T1) → **propose** (open permissive teacher, generating **under the GBNF** so every draft is well-formed) → **adjudicate** (⛔ **Jonathan reviews 100 % of refuse/clarify; 20 % sample of Execute**) → **validate mechanically** (GBNF-accepted **for that fixture**; every kind in the **PRINTED** roster; canonical `all`/`none` encoding).
- **COMPOSITION — ~800 examples:** single-kind Execute ~28 % · multi-kind ~14 % · army-wide zero-arg ~10 % · deferred/trigger ~8 % · **Refuse ~18 %** · **Clarify ~17 %** · hard negatives ~5 %. **Refuse+Clarify get ~35 % rather than the deployment distribution because the eval is adversarial by requirement (§11) and gen-1 failed exactly there.**
- ⛔⚠️ **AND THERE IS A CEILING, AND IT IS A SCORING QUIRK — THE SAME PROHIBITION ZONE A CARRIES NOW BINDS THE TRAINING SET.** The scorer **skips asserted fields when a question is emitted**, so a model that learns *"when unsure, ask"* converts **STRICT passes into LENIENT-only ones.** Zone A's own comments forbid writing that **disposition** into the prompt for exactly this reason. ⇒ ⛔ **TEACH TWO NAMED SITUATIONS, NEVER A DISPOSITION.** **Watch `clarify_rows_passed_by_question` and the printed `LENIENCY FLOOR` at EVERY checkpoint** — a rising holdout number with a rising floor is not progress (§12a: the split's own floor travels beside the number, every time).
- **BOARD VARIATION WITHOUT REWRITING ANSWERS:** every row carries an explicit **`depends_on`** set. Rows depending on nothing render against **any** fixture containing their kinds; **shortfall and deferred-trigger rows are PINNED to one fixture.** ⛔ **`tD` (kinds at `0 orderable`) and `tF` (2–4 kinds, early match) are BLOCKED until Stage A's per-fixture GBNF lands** — until then D3's class is unmeasurable **and untrainable.**

### 8. ⚖️ THE `USiegeCheatManager` EXEC IS §5's OWN IDIOM, NOT A BREACH OF IT — SAID HERE BECAUSE THE TWO CLAUSES LOOK LIKE THEY COLLIDE

✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-03 — AND THIS SECTION IS NOW A HISTORY NOTE, NOT A LIVE COLLISION (corrected on TASK-502's finding, which caught it still quoting the PRE-amendment letter and still announcing a conflict that had been fixed an hour earlier).** ⛔ **`AS-§5` HAS BEEN AMENDED IN PLACE** — its *"NEVER as a `UFUNCTION(exec)`"* now carries its own scope **at the sentence**, permitting a shipped-lane, dev-only-guarded, read-only, delegating diagnostic. ⇒ **There is no longer any distance between the letter and the reading, which was the entire defect.**
⚖️ **THE REASONING IS KEPT BECAUSE IT IS WHAT JUSTIFIED THE AMENDMENT, AND IT IS STILL THE TEST FOR THE NEXT CASE:** ~~§5 says … NEVER as a `UFUNCTION(exec)` on a shipped class~~ — **that clause exists so THE SPIKE does not become a shipped exec surface, keeping the inference lane new-files-only under the M8 PARALLEL LAW.** ⇒ ✅ **A SHIPPED-LANE dev dump is the thing §5 was protecting, not the thing it was banning** — the whole point of `DumpAssistantPrompt` is that it runs in **the shipped path**, which a `Siege.Llama.*` console command structurally cannot reach. It sits beside the existing `SummonTestUnit` / `ApplyTestDamage`, which are that class's established idiom.
- ⛔ **AND THE BOUNDARY IS KEPT: this is the ONLY exec this batch adds, it is a DUMP (read-only, no state change), and the spike's commands stay in the `Siege.Llama.*` namespace where §5 put them.**

### 9. ⛔ PACKAGING, LICENCE + THE `Models/` GITIGNORE FINDING

- ⛔ **THE GGUF MUST STAGE AS A LOOSE `NonUFS` FILE.** `LLAMA_LOAD_MODE_MMAP` needs **a real filesystem path** — it cannot read from a `.pak` or IoStore — **and mmap is load-bearing for the 8 GB claim** (§7). `ResolveModelPath()` needs no change (`ProjectDir/Models` resolves in a staged layout) but ⛔ **must be VERIFIED IN A REAL PACKAGED BUILD**, which is precisely why the original header said not to bolt a shipping path in early.
- ⛔ **`Docs/ThirdPartyNotices.md` §2 IS REWRITTEN, NOT PATCHED.** Its own text says a licence recorded there is valid **only for the exact repo id + filename named** — and after a fine-tune **none of those fields is true.** The new entry names the upstream base **`Qwen/Qwen3-4B`** (verify its card **LIVE**, quote `license:` **verbatim**, record the **revision sha**), the derived filename, the quantiser's provenance, and **OUR sha256.**
- **THE APACHE §4(b) MODIFICATION STATEMENT IS A NEW OBLIGATION:** one paragraph naming **what** (LoRA rank/alpha/targets, merged and re-quantised), **on what data**, **when**, **by whom**, **from which base revision**, citing `run.json`. Plus **§4(a)/(c) at package time** — licence text and any upstream `NOTICE` staged beside the weights.
- ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-03 AT `f4f88e6` (TASK-499) — THIS CLAUSE DESCRIBED A LIVE DEFECT AND WAS LEFT SAYING SO FOR HOURS AFTER IT WAS CLOSED (corrected on TASK-502's finding, which read it FALSE ON ALL THREE LIMBS).** ~~VERIFIED LIVE, AND IT IS A REAL DEFECT IN `.gitignore` TODAY: `git check-ignore -v Models/README.md` → `.gitignore:132:/Models/`~~ — **the dead `!Models/…` negations were STRUCK, the block was rewritten, and `/Models/` now sits at a different line entirely.** ⛔ **THE LINE NUMBER IS DELIBERATELY NOT RESTATED: a law asserting `<file>:<line>` about a file that tasks are actively changing is a POINTER THAT DECAYS** (§18c, which this clause should have obeyed the first time). **Verify with `git check-ignore -v --no-index <path>` and read the returned line.**
- ✅ **WHAT SURVIVES UNCHANGED, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MATTERED: MODEL DOCUMENTATION CANNOT LIVE IN `Models/` — IT GOES IN `Docs/`** (hence `Docs/ModelManifest.md` in the naming table). ⚖️ **The conclusion outlived the defect that motivated it, which is exactly the shape this document keeps re-learning: retract the argument that depended on a fixed premise, and only that argument.**
- ⛔ **NO `.gitignore` CARVE-OUT FOR THE ADAPTER.** ⚠️ **The rule's stated reason is PERMANENCE, not size, and the incident it cites was 70.7 MB — a 66 MB adapter IS that incident by volume.** ✅ **What IS committed makes the adapter REPRODUCIBLE, which is the same argument the ignore rule already makes for the weights:** `Tools/LlmFinetune/**` including `uv.lock`, the training JSONL under `Docs/Data/Finetune/`, and `run.json` quoted in the handoff. **If an adapter must travel, it goes via a private HF repo through `fetch_llm_model.py` UNCHANGED.**

### 10. 🔒 THE GATE — CONJUNCTIVE, PRE-REGISTERED, SCORED ONCE

`SpikeEval holdout=Docs/Data/assistant_eval_holdout2.csv tier=full gpu=0 repeats=5`, **on the shipping GGUF, in the shipped wire format.** ⛔ **ALL NINE MUST HOLD:**

1. **≥ 13/15 lenient at the *MINIMUM* of 5 runs** — ⛔ **not median, never best.** Makes a ±1 noise band **harmless instead of exploitable.**
2. **`STRICT == LENIENT` in every run** — the anti-hedging clause.
3. **`PassLenient − DegenerateQuestionFloor ≥ 7`.** ⚠️ Gen-1 scored 66.7 % against a printed floor of 60 % — ***one row* of real translation signal.**
4. **`parse_failures = 0`, zero aborted rows, all 5 runs.**
5. ⛔ **ZERO REFUSE-CLASS FAILURES — A VETO, scored separately.** Protects the standing *"never spends my gold, never plays my cards"* ruling.
6. **NO REGRESSION on `assistant_eval_dev.csv`:** median ≥ 20/25, **and no row passing at baseline may fail in ≥3 of 5 runs.**
7. **PROMPT-SHAPE IDENTITY:** M3's artifact diff passes; `zoneA_chars=5116`.
8. **LATENCY within ~10 % of the measured `tier=full` wall clock.** ⚠️ **Buying accuracy with Jonathan's latency budget is HIS trade, not a tuning task's** (§12d).
9. ⛔ **THE SCORED ARTIFACT IS THE SHIPPED ARTIFACT**, declared in `Docs/ModelManifest.md` with its sha256.

- 🔒⛔ **`holdout2` OPENS EXACTLY ONCE, ON THE MERGED SHIPPING GGUF, WITH `repeats=5` *INSIDE THAT SINGLE OPENING*** (§12a + §12h). **N generations, same file, same session, reported as a distribution — NEVER a second opening on a later day.**
- ⛔ **SCORE THE ARTIFACT THAT SHIPS.** ⚠️ **Runtime-LoRA-on-Q4 and merged-then-Q4 are DIFFERENT MODELS** — the adapter was trained to correct an **NF4** base and is being merged into **bf16** then re-quantised to **a different scheme.** ✅ **Dev may be scored on the adapter freely; the holdout opens only on the merged, quantised shipping GGUF.** **This is T8 and it is the project's exact recurring failure: a confident green describing something else.**
- ⛔ **NEVER REPORT THE RESULT AS A DELTA FROM 66.7 %** — generations are **not row-comparable** (§12a). **The bar is ABSOLUTE.**
- ⛔ **PRE-REGISTER THE FALSIFYING CONDITION BEFORE THE RUN**, in the handoff (§12f's method result, now standing law on this lane): ***"if refuse-class rows still fail, the defect is POLICY ROUTING and more data of the same kind will not fix it."***
- **RE-RUN AFTER ANY WEIGHT CHANGE:** bars #1–#5 (⛔ **bar #1 in PIE on `L_Arena` WITH UNITS ON THE FIELD, never an empty map**), the **33 automation tests**, and the **`bThrottleCPUWhenNotForeground=False` check** (§12e — a headless-driven PIE session idles at **exactly 3.0 FPS** with the throttle on, and **a bar taken under it is meaningless rather than conservative**).
- ⛔ **TIER CONSTANTS ARE RE-MEASURED, NEVER CARRIED** (`SPIKE_MEM stage=model_load`, all three tiers, twice, **worse reading wins**) — T9, and a carried constant silently eats the 768 MiB game reserve.
- ⛔ **TOKEN COUNTS MUST REPRODUCE IDENTICALLY. A LoRA DOES NOT TOUCH THE TOKENIZER** ⇒ **any movement means the vocab was touched and §8/§10's ENTIRE numeric apparatus is void.**

**⚖️ TWO INSTRUMENT LAWS, RATIFIED 2026-08-03 FROM TASK-476's OWN RESTRAINT — BOTH PROPOSED BY THE IMPLEMENTER, NEITHER ASKED FOR, AND BOTH NOW BINDING ON EVERY HARNESS IN THIS LANE:**

- ⛔ **(A) NO INSTRUMENT MAY PRINT A VERDICT ON A GATE IT CAN ONLY PARTIALLY SEE.** §10's gate is **conjunctive over NINE clauses**; `SpikeEval` can observe **four**. ⇒ **It prints statistics and prints NO PASS/FAIL.** ⚖️ **A harness emitting a green would become a FIFTH LANE claiming authority it does not have** — and this batch exists because three lanes already disagreed. ⚠️ **The failure this prevents is specific and it is the project's recurring one: a confident green describing something else.** ✅ **The party best placed to emit the verdict refusing to is what makes the verdict meaningful when it finally comes.**
- ⛔ **(B) EVERY PRINTED STATISTIC CARRIES THE GATE CLAUSE THAT READS IT.** `SpikeEval` labels **MINIMUM → §10 #1**, **MEDIAN → §10 #6**, and states that **nothing reads MAX** — with the **leniency floor travelling beside the number** (§12a). ⚖️ **THIS KILLS "QUOTE THE FLATTERING ONE" AT THE SOURCE**, which is otherwise unfalsifiable after the fact: three aggregations of one run are all true, and only one of them is the gate. ⛔ **An unlabelled aggregate is an invitation, and a printed `max` with no reader is a trap with a number in it.**
- ⛔ **(C) THE ENCODING PIN — EVERY BYTE-COMPARED ARTIFACT IS WRITTEN AS EXPLICIT UTF-8 WITHOUT BOM. RAISED FROM TWO FIXES TO A RULE 2026-08-03 UNDER §13(d)'s SWEEP DUTY, AFTER TASK-479 AND TASK-477 FOUND IT ***INDEPENDENTLY, THE SAME EVENING***.**
  - ⛔ **`FFileHelper::SaveStringToFile`'s `AutoDetect` DEFAULT MAKES THE FILE'S ENCODING DEPEND ON ITS CONTENT** — it writes **UTF-16-with-BOM on the first non-ASCII character.** ⚠️ **So the artifact's encoding changes based on which utterance was dumped.**
  - ⚠️ **THE FAILURE, IN ITS OWN WORDS: *"a file that opens fine, has a plausible length, and hashes to something no consumer can reproduce."*** ⇒ **It is intermittent, content-dependent, and it corrupts a HASH rather than a value** — so it survives every eyeball check and fails only the comparison it exists to serve.
  - ⚖️ **AN INSTRUMENT WHOSE OUTPUT ENCODING VARIES WITH ITS INPUT IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT.** ⇒ **`ForceUTF8WithoutBOM`, or an explicit byte array — unconditionally, on every `out=`, dump, gold file and JSONL this batch writes.** ⛔ **This binds Stage D's `build_dataset.py` too: Python's default encoding on Windows is not UTF-8, and the same trap has a Python spelling.**
  - 📌 **TWO INDEPENDENT SIGHTINGS IN ONE EVENING IS THE SWEEP DUTY FIRING EXACTLY AS INTENDED** — ⚖️ **the second sighting is what converts "a careful agent handled it" into "the class is closed", and it is the difference between two correct fixes and one rule.**
- 📌 **AND THE PROOF STANDARD TASK-476 SET IS THE STANDARD FOR THE REST OF STAGE A — MECHANICAL, NOT EYEBALLED:** *8/8 old format strings reduce **character-identically**, 0 orphaned, new lines on a **separate log stream**, 103/103 `UE_LOG` calls arg-count-clean **with 83 untouched lines used as the positive control for the checker itself**.* ⚖️ **A checker that has not been shown to FAIL on a known-bad input has not been shown to work** (§14's positive-control discipline, applied to a bespoke tool). ⚠️ **AND IT DECLARED ITS OWN LIMIT: that is a proof about SOURCE BYTES, not about a RUN.** ⛔ **§16's INSTRUMENT-UNCHANGED proof — the dev-split number reproducing at 20/25 with the same five failing rows — is still owed, and it is TASK-481's.** ✅ **Stating the boundary of a proof is part of giving it.**

### 11. ⚖️ THE RECIPE'S FROZEN SWITCHES — EACH ONE SILENTLY BREAKS THE CONVERTER OR THE SCALE

- `max_seq_len = 2048` — ⚠️ **Zone A is 1362 tokens (5116 chars), NOT the pre-ladder 1139**; a sample is ~1767 tokens. **NF4 4-bit base, `bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=bfloat16`, double-quant.**
- `LoraConfig(r=16, alpha=32, dropout=0.05, target_modules=[q,k,v,o,gate,up,down])`.
- ⛔ **`use_dora=False`, `use_rslora=False`, `modules_to_save` EMPTY.** **Each silently breaks `convert_lora_to_gguf.py` or the scale it writes** — ⚠️ **rsLoRA uses `alpha/√r`; llama.cpp computes `alpha/r`.** (T4.)
- ⛔ **COMPLETION-ONLY LOSS.** The model **already emits format correctly** (`parse_failures = 0`, `STRICT == LENIENT`) — **teaching format again wastes capacity on the one thing that is not broken.**
- **Seed pinned. Every run writes `Runs/<id>/run.json`** with resolved versions, base revision sha, dataset sha256, hyperparameters **and the free-VRAM reading at start.**
- ⚠️ **DOES IT FIT IN 8 GB? DERIVED, NOT MEASURED:** ~5.6–6.9 GB against ~7.3–7.7 GB free **with the editor closed.** ⛔ **The margin is ONE TERM WIDE AND THAT TERM IS THE LOGITS** — a **151,936** vocabulary makes a 4B behave like a much larger model at train time (T5).
- **LEVERS IN COST ORDER, ONE AT A TIME, RE-MEASURING AFTER EACH:** alloc-conf `expandable_segments` → **fused/chunked cross-entropy (~1.0–1.8 GB, the highest-yield lever)** → `r=8` attention-only → `unsloth` → rent a GPU for one run.
- ⛔ **NEVER SHORTEN ZONE A IN TRAINING ONLY — THAT IS TRAIN/SERVE SKEW, THE EXACT DEFECT §12a EXISTS ABOUT.**
- **ADAPTER FOR ITERATION, MERGED FOR SHIP.** Iterate with a ~66 MB adapter GGUF (**the base stays byte-identical, so everything measured about the base still describes it**). Ship **merged → bf16 → convert → re-quantise to Q4_K_M**: **zero code changes** (the override chain was built so *"swapping models never needs a rebuild"*), zero adapter VRAM accounting, **and no per-token adapter matmul at decode — which matters because bar #2 already FAILS on partial and cpu, and decode is the whole cost (§12d).**

### 12. ⛔ THE TWO CONTRACTS THAT MUST BE RULED **BEFORE** AUTHORING, NOT INFERRED AFTER — **THEY ARE JONATHAN'S**

1. ⛔ **WHAT `send everyone` EMITS.** ⚠️ **`DEV-13`'s own note says a task was meant to declare it and none did.** Until it is ruled, ~10 % of the training mix (army-wide zero-arg) is being authored against an undeclared contract.
2. ⛔ **THE ASK-CODE TAXONOMY.** **The corpora accept ANY OF FIVE codes**, so **the training set is where that policy is actually decided** — by 800 rows, silently, if nobody rules it. ⚠️ **A policy decided by a data-drafting pass is a policy nobody chose.**

⚖️ **BOTH ARE RECORDED HERE AS *OPEN*, NOT GUESSED AT.** ⛔ **An agent that picks one and proceeds has made a product decision inside a data task** — exactly the shape §12f's *"the corpus author is not the tuner"* separation exists to prevent, one level up.

### 12b. ⛔ THE CITATION-DISAMBIGUATION RULE — "§11" RESOLVES TO TWO DIFFERENT SECTIONS AND BOTH ARE CITED IN THIS BATCH (2026-08-03, TASK-500)

> ### ⛔ **A BARE `§N` IS AMBIGUOUS IN THIS FILE. EVERY CITATION NAMES ITS SECTION: `"In-match LLM command assistant" §11` OR `"THE FINE-TUNE RUNG" §11`.**

- ⚠️ **MEASURED, IN THIS BATCH'S OWN BOARD: TASK-482 / TASK-484 / TASK-487 cite the ASSISTANT's §11 (the sealed-corpus authorship law); TASK-495 cites the FINE-TUNE RUNG's §11 (the frozen recipe switches). Same prefix, unrelated content, both correct in context and both unreadable out of it.**
- ⚖️ **THIS IS A CITATION COLLISION INSIDE A BATCH WHOSE ENTIRE THESIS IS *DOCUMENTS ASSERTING THINGS NOBODY CHECKED*** — and the failure mode is the worst kind: **the reader follows the reference, finds a real clause, and obeys the wrong law with no error anywhere.**
- ✅ **THE FIX IS A RULE, NOT A RENUMBER.** ⛔ **Do NOT renumber either section** — every existing handoff, QA report and commit message already cites the old numbers, and renumbering breaks *those* silently to fix *this*. **Qualify at the point of citation instead.**
- 📌 **§18c already said *cite by SYMBOL, never by OFFSET*. This is the same lesson one level up: a section NUMBER is an offset too when more than one section can carry it.**

⛔⛔ **AMENDED SAME DAY — THIS RULE FAILED, AND IT FAILED IN THE ACT OF BEING WRITTEN. THE AMENDMENT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS SECTION (2026-08-03, TASK-502's highest-value finding).**

> ### ⛔ **A DISAMBIGUATION RULE THAT RELIES ON AUTHORS REMEMBERING TO DISAMBIGUATE REPRODUCES THE PROBLEM AT AUTHOR SPEED.**

- ⚠️ **MEASURED, AND IT IS DAMNING: THE PASS THAT ADDED THIS RULE NUMBERED IT `§12b` *INSIDE THE FINE-TUNE RUNG* — COLLIDING WITH THE ASSISTANT'S `§12b` — AND ADDED A FINE-TUNE `§14` COLLIDING WITH THE ASSISTANT'S `§14`.** ⇒ ⛔ **The rule created TWO FRESH INSTANCES OF THE EXACT CLASS IT BANS, in the act of banning it.** ⚠️ **AND THE SWEEP NEVER RAN: the four `§11` citations this rule NAMES AS MEASURED VIOLATIONS (TASK-482/484/487/495) were left unchanged — §13(d)'s *"a law written from a single sighting must trigger a sweep for siblings"* failing on the very next law written.**
- ⚖️ **THAT IS NOT IRONY, IT IS EVIDENCE ABOUT THE REMEDY.** A rule of the form *"remember to qualify"* has the same failure mode as *"remember to check"* — **it reopens the first time somebody is tired**, and the entire batch has been ratifying the opposite standard (§17 answered *structurally*: the no-default-argument threading, `git cat-file -s` over `git show`, the whitelist over the blacklist). ⛔ **I applied that standard to every agent and not to myself.**
- ✅ **THE STRUCTURAL FIX — A NAMESPACE IN THE NUMBER ITSELF, WHICH IS NOT A RENUMBER AND SO KEEPS THE EARLIER RULING INTACT:**
  > **`AS-§N` = "In-match LLM command assistant" · `FT-§N` = "THE FINE-TUNE RUNG" · `SC-§N` = "Settings screen + the assistant CONFIRM STEP…"**
  **The digits never change**, so every existing handoff, QA report and commit message stays as valid as it was; **a bare `§12b` is simply now KNOWN-AMBIGUOUS and must be resolved by its context, while every NEW citation is exact by construction.** ⇒ ⛔ **A collision becomes IMPOSSIBLE TO WRITE rather than forbidden to write.**
- ⛔ **AND THE PAIR RULE, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF AND COMES FROM THE B1/B3 MIRROR:**
  > ### ⛔ **A CORRECTION TO A CROSS-REFERENCED CLAIM IS NOT DONE UNTIL **BOTH** SIDES ARE VERIFIED — IN THE SAME ACTION, NOT THE SAME INTENTION.**
  ⚠️ **B1 was *board fixed, law not*. B3 was *law fixed, board not* — the SAME defect with the operands SWAPPED, one turn after it was named.** ⇒ ⚖️ **The direction of the miss is NOT PREDICTABLE, so *"remember to update both"* cannot work: whichever side you are looking at feels like the whole job.** **The board entry and the law clause are ONE artifact with two homes, and a correction pass that touched only one has not finished.**

### 13. ⚖️ THE OBSERVER RULING — how the shipped lane gets read WITHOUT spending a named invariant (ruled 2026-08-03, manager, on TASK-479's refusal)

**TASK-479 refused to write anything and escalated. ✅ THE REFUSAL WAS CORRECT ON ALL THREE OF ITS LIMBS AND IS RECORDED AS THE PIPELINE WORKING, NOT AS A DELAY.** It found (i) a **circular dependency in my own board**, (ii) that the obvious fix would spend a **numbered structural invariant**, and (iii) an independent second fault. ⚖️ **Breaking a cycle is a BOARD decision, not a programmer's — stopping at the boundary of its own authority is exactly the behaviour this pipeline wants, and it is cheaper than any of the three ways it could have guessed.**

**⛔ (a) THE CIRCULARITY, AND IT WAS MINE.** The dump needs a route into `SiegeAssistantComponent.{h,cpp}`; my ruling 11 assigned that file to **TASK-489 only**; and TASK-489 is blocked-by TASK-486 ← M3 ← TASK-479. ⇒ **479 → M3 → 486 → 489 → 479.**
- ⚖️ **RESOLVED BY CARVING OWNERSHIP IN TIME, NOT BY RE-ORDERING — the established idiom on this board (`TASK-442 THEN TASK-443, in that order, NEVER concurrently`): `SiegeAssistantComponent.{h,cpp}` = TASK-479 *THEN* TASK-489.** ✅ **Single-owner-per-file forbids CONCURRENT edits, never sequential hand-off** — and these two are separated by three tasks and a Jonathan decision, so they can never overlap.
- ⛔ **TASK-479's carve is EXACTLY ONE ADDITION** (the accessor below) **and nothing else in either file.** Everything else stays TASK-489's.

**⛔ (b) `ComposeTurnPrompt`'s PRIVATENESS IS MECHANISM #2 OF FOUR AND IS NOT SPENDABLE FOR A DIAGNOSTIC.** `SiegeAssistantComponent.h:49-74` names four mechanisms that make the translator-not-agent law **structurally true**, *"each of which a later edit would have to DELETE rather than merely overlook — which is the difference between a rule and a comment."* **#2 is: *"ComposeTurnPrompt() is private … the one function that could feed the model its own previous words cannot reach the model, and the one that reaches the model cannot compose."***
- ⛔ **RULED: OPTION A — A DEV-ONLY PUBLIC ACCESSOR THAT DELEGATES TO THE ONE COMPOSER. ⛔ NOT private → public on the composer itself.**
- ⚖️ **WHY A DOES NOT SPEND #2, STATED PRECISELY BECAUSE "it feels narrower" is not an argument: #2's property is that THE MODEL-CALL SEAM HAS NO ROUTE TO A ZONE BUILDER. An accessor returning a FINISHED prompt string hands out no zone builder** — the TASK-443 seam still receives a finished string and still cannot compose, **and there is still EXACTLY ONE COMPOSER.** ⇒ **This is ADDITION, not RE-EXPOSURE.** Widening the composer itself would delete the stated barrier and convert #2 from a mechanism into a comment.
- ✅ **THE TEMPTING PRECEDENT WAS CORRECTLY DISTINGUISHED, AND I UPHOLD THE DISTINCTION.** `SpawnGroupCircleDecal`'s private → public widening (`SiegePlayerController.h:301-308`) is real, documented, and says *"the access level is part of the pin"* — ⚠️ **but that function carries NO invariant.** ⛔ **A precedent transfers its REASONING, not its VERDICT.** (That header also already rejected `friend` as **the wider grant, not the narrower one** — so `friend class USiegeCheatManager` is refused here on the same ground, without re-arguing it.)
- **CONDITIONS, all QA criteria:** the accessor is **dev-only-guarded** (never a shipping surface) · **read-only, no state change** · **delegates — it does NOT re-derive, re-assemble or re-order anything** · **its header comment names mechanism #2 and states why it does not spend it**, in the `SpawnGroupCircleDecal` idiom of putting the access rationale where the next reader will meet it.
- ⛔ **REASSEMBLING THE ZONES INSIDE THE CHEAT MANAGER IS FORBIDDEN AND IS NOT A THIRD OPTION.** It would have to **guess the vocabulary lane** — the measured 3029-vs-5116 defect — producing **a green M3 that measures nothing.** ⚠️ **That is traps T1 and T8 in a single move: a re-implementation, scored as if it were the artifact.**

**⚖️ (c) FAULT B IS REAL AND IS RESOLVED BY MERGING TWO READINGS, NOT BY RE-SCOPING ONE.** Verified first-hand: `ComposeTurnPrompt` (`SiegeAssistantComponent.cpp:2727-2752`) builds Zone A/B/C as **locals**, calls `ReportFirstCapture(ZoneA, ZoneB, ZoneC)`, and **returns only the concatenation** — no out-param, no member. And `ReportFirstCapture` (`:2902-2909`) is **latched by `bLoggedFirstCapture`.**
- ✅ **BUT M3 NEEDS THE PROMPT *BYTES*, NOT THE ZONE COUNTS** — the deliverable is a byte comparison, and zone boundaries are recoverable offline by locating the known Zone A prefix. **So the accessor's return value alone satisfies M3.**
- ⚖️ **AND THE LATCH IS NOT *CONSUMED* BY THIS TASK — IT IS CONSUMED BY WHATEVER CALLS THE COMPOSER FIRST, INCLUDING THE FIRST REAL PLAYER SENTENCE.** ⇒ ⛔ **THE RULING: THE EXEC AND THE OWED FIRST-CAPTURE AUDIT ARE THE SAME READING AND MUST BE TAKEN IN THE SAME SESSION, BY THE SAME TASK, WITH THE AUDIT LINE QUOTED.** ✅ **A conflict becomes a merge: the one-shot is spent ON PURPOSE, into the artifact that was owed it, instead of being spent by accident in an unwatched session.**
- ⚠️ **A LATCHED ONE-SHOT DIAGNOSTIC IS A CONSUMABLE RESOURCE AND MUST BE BOARDED AS ONE.** Nothing on the board said who owned `bLoggedFirstCapture`'s single firing, which is why two tasks could both plan to spend it. **Whoever first calls a latched reporter owns its output and must publish it.**

**⛔ (d) THERE WERE TWO, AND THE SECOND WAS FOUND ONE TURN AFTER (c) WAS WRITTEN — SO THE RULE IS AMENDED FROM AN INSTANCE INTO A REGISTRY AND A SWEEP DUTY (2026-08-03).**

> ### ⛔ **A LAW WRITTEN FROM A SINGLE SIGHTING MUST TRIGGER A SWEEP FOR SIBLINGS BEFORE IT IS TRUSTED. PROTECTING THE ONE INSTANCE YOU FOUND IS NOT THE SAME AS CLOSING THE CLASS.**

- ⚖️ **This is §22's *"a finding names where the reporter LOOKED"* turned on LAW-WRITING instead of on defects** — and it is the more dangerous application, because a law reads as though it covers its whole class whether or not anyone checked. ⚠️ **(c) was authored from `bLoggedFirstCapture` alone and its confident generality was doing work the evidence had not done.**
- **📋 THE CONSUMABLE-LATCH REGISTRY — every known session-latched one-shot in this lane. ⛔ A task that will trip one OWNS its output and PUBLISHES it; a task that must not trip one says so:**

| latch | fires | owner today |
|---|---|---|
| **`bLoggedFirstCapture`** | ⚠️ **the FIRST composer call of a session** — including the first real player sentence | **TASK-485** (quotes the `FIRST LIVE CAPTURE` line) |
| **`bZoneAIdentityChecked`** | ⚠️ **the SECOND composer call of a session** — §8's Zone-A byte-identity re-check, **and the dev dump may BE that second call** | **TASK-485** (quotes it too — see below) |
| `bWarnedStaticPrefixRefused` | first `SetStaticPrefix` refusal | not consumable — it latches a *warning*, and the underlying call **retries** |

- ⛔ **CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS SHARPER THAN THE FIRST LATCH's: `bZoneAIdentityChecked` GUARDS §8's *"Zone A is byte-identical for the life of the process"* LAW — THE ONE PROPERTY THE ENTIRE KV-REUSE ARGUMENT RESTS ON.** ⚠️ **Spending it on a dev dump in an unwatched session does not merely lose a log line; it consumes the ONLY runtime check that the static prefix never moved.** ⇒ **TASK-485 quotes BOTH latched outputs in the same session, from the same run.**
- ✅ **AND THE DISCOVERY BEHAVIOUR IS THE ONE TO COPY: TASK-479 FOUND IT, RECOGNISED IT AS THE CLASS THE LAW HAD JUST NAMED, AND DID *NOT* BOARD IT — because boarding is not its call.** ⚖️ **Finding a second instance of a rule you were just handed, and routing it rather than acting on it, is the pipeline working at its best.**

### 14. ⚖️ THREE RULINGS FROM STAGE A's CLOSE (2026-08-03, TASK-478) — the falsified-assertion carve, the deferral-reason law, and the belt-to-only-strap clause

**⛔ (a) THE FALSIFIED-ASSERTION CARVE — §22 BEATS SINGLE-OWNER-PER-FILE, NARROWLY, AND TASK-478 WAS RIGHT NOT TO TAKE IT SILENTLY.**
TASK-478 rewrote **two pre-existing `UE_LOG` strings** that asserted the mechanism it had just deleted — **one of them inside TASK-477's `CmdSpikePrompt`** — and **flagged it as a scope call rather than assuming the duty overrode the ownership rule.** ✅ **Flagging was correct; the ruling is that §22 wins.**
- ⚖️ **THE PRINCIPLE, AND IT IS NARROWER THAN "SHARED FILES ARE FINE": SINGLE-OWNER-PER-FILE GOVERNS *CONCURRENT AUTHORSHIP*, NOT *TRUTH MAINTENANCE OF STATEMENTS YOUR OWN CHANGE FALSIFIED*.** ⛔ **A log line asserting a mechanism you just removed is PART OF THAT REMOVAL, not a separate edit** — and leaving it for the file's next owner **ships a runtime string that lies**, which §22 names the priority surface precisely because a log is read at 3am by someone with no context.
- ⛔ **THE FOUR CONDITIONS — outside them, single-owner wins and the finding is ROUTED, not fixed:**
  1. **The string must assert something THIS task's own change made false.** ⛔ *"While I was in there"* is not this clause.
  2. **Text only. ⛔ No control flow, no verbosity change, no format-arg change** (the `UE_LOG` arg-count class is a compile break, and it is not yours to risk in another owner's function).
  3. **DECLARED IN THE HANDOFF, each string named with its owning task**, so the other owner learns it from the record rather than from a merge.
  4. **The other owner is not blocked on it** — ⛔ if the fix requires their in-flight work, ROUTE IT.
- ⚖️ **THIS IS THE *"the conclusion is still true and only the cited mechanism is gone"* SHAPE ALREADY ON THE BOOKS, APPLIED TO A RUNTIME STRING INSTEAD OF A DOCUMENT.** ⚠️ **A stale comment misleads a reader; a stale log misleads an OPERATOR MID-INCIDENT, holding the wrong mental model of a live system.**

**⛔ (b) THE DEFERRAL-REASON LAW — A DEFERRAL CARRIES A REASON, AND THE REASON IS CHECKABLE.**
> ### ⛔ **A WRONG REASON ATTACHED TO A RIGHT DECISION IS HOW THE NEXT READER JUSTIFIES A WRONG ONE.**
- **MEASURED, THIS EVENING, n = 3:** two tasks deferred on reasons that survived inspection; **one (TASK-477, on the `prompt=<path>` defect) deferred on a reason that did NOT** — TASK-478 verified against `ResolveZoneAText` that the fix is **provably default-identical**, so *"it would risk the default path"* was false. ✅ **THE DEFERRAL ITSELF WAS STILL CORRECT** (single-owner, and TASK-501 now owns it) — **but for a different reason than the one written down.**
- ⛔ **SO THE RECORD IS CORRECTED, NOT THE OUTCOME.** ⚠️ **A right call resting on a false premise is indistinguishable, to the next reader, from a right call — until they reuse the premise.** ⇒ **Whoever falsifies a deferral's stated reason says so plainly, even when the deferral stands.**
- ⚖️ **AND IT IS THE SAME DISEASE AS §12f's ACCIDENTALLY-RIGHT `78 %`: an agreement that vindicates a CONCLUSION does not vindicate the METHOD that produced it, and the method is what gets reused.**

**✅ (c) THE BELT-TO-ONLY-STRAP CLAUSE — A CHANGE THAT QUIETLY PROMOTES AN EXISTING CHECK MUST SAY SO.**
Deriving the GBNF per-fixture makes **`VerifyFixtureKindParity` MORE load-bearing, not vestigial** — the opposite of the intuition, and worth a clause because the intuition is what a reviewer arrives with.
- **BEFORE:** a typo'd fixture kind was a symbol **the sampler forbade** ⇒ the grammar itself rejected it ⇒ **LOUD.**
- **AFTER:** a typo'd fixture kind is **added as a legal alternative** ⇒ the model can emit a kind that does not exist ⇒ **SILENT.**
- ⇒ ⛔ **`SpikeRoster` IS NO LONGER THE GRAMMAR AUTHORITY — ONLY THE VOCABULARY AUTHORITY — AND `VerifyFixtureKindParity` IS NOW THE WHOLE OF THE GUARANTEE.** ⚠️ **Any future task that "simplifies" or relaxes that check is removing the only remaining strap, and must be failed on sight citing this clause.**
- ✅ **AND THE STRUCTURAL TOUCH TO COPY: the fixture was threaded with NO DEFAULT ARGUMENT, so a missed call site is a COMPILE ERROR rather than a silent wrong grammar.** ⚖️ **That is the §17 family — tools and code that fail quietly — answered by CONSTRUCTION instead of by discipline, which is the only answer that survives a tired author.**

**⛔ (d) STAGE A IS COMPLETE *AS SPECIFIED*, NOT *AS MEASURED* — SAID IN THE IMPLEMENTER'S OWN WORDS AND KEPT.** **Not one line has been compiled or executed.** §16's **instrument-unchanged** re-run is **TASK-481's**, and **three named gaps survive into it:** the `prompt=` bypass (**TASK-501**) · **no `wire=` indicator on `SpikeEval`** (deliberate — §16's frozen default) · ⛔ **D2 remains INEXPRESSIBLE by the spike at all** (§3's `other_kinds:` constant). ⚖️ **A completion claim that names its own three gaps is the kind this pipeline accepts; one that does not is the kind it has been burned by.**

## Keyboard layout / positional input (2026-08-04) — namespace **`KBD-§N`**

Added 2026-08-04 on Jonathan's directive, verbatim: *"we need to make the game handle alternative keyboard layouts. I currently use the Dvorak keyboard, so I want the game to be able to detect the layout and adjust which letter keys are mapped so that it still puts the keys in the same locations as if we were using qwerty."*

Design authority = the approved plan `C:\Users\wesel\.claude\plans\ok-there-are-a-cheerful-mccarthy.md` (produced in plan mode, every engine claim verified against installed UE 5.8 source, approved by Jonathan via ExitPlanMode). ⛔ **The plan file wins over any board summary. The architecture is NOT re-litigated** — this section is the naming/behaviour law derived from it, plus the batch's link contract.

📌 **THIS SECTION IS BORN WITH ITS NAMESPACE PREFIX (`KBD-§N`), BEFORE TASK-503's retrofit runs.** ⚖️ Cite as `KBD-§2`, never as a bare `§2` — this file already carries three sections whose bare `§12b` and `§14` collided (`qa/TASK-502.md`), and the cheapest time to prevent that is at authoring.

### KBD-§0. ⚖️ JONATHAN'S TWO RULINGS — DECIDED, BINDING, ⛔ NOT RE-OPENABLE BY ANY AGENT

Both answered by Jonathan directly via AskUserQuestion on 2026-08-04. Recorded here because the *"shouldn't this be a setting?"* question is the single most likely thing a future agent proposes.

1. ⛔ **ALWAYS-ON AUTO-DETECT. NO PLAYER-FACING SETTING.** ⛔ **No new field in `USiegeSettingsSaveGame`, no row in `USettingsMenuWidget`, no `SC-§8` registry addition.** A **CVar for runtime testing is permitted** (`KBD-§7`); a settings toggle is **out of scope and is a scope breach if added.** ⚖️ The remap is a *correctness* fix — the keys go where they are printed on a QWERTY reference — not a preference.
2. ⛔ **MID-SESSION LAYOUT SWITCHES ARE IN SCOPE, NOT DEFERRED.** Jonathan may `Win+Space` to QWERTY for gaming and back. **The 1 Hz HKL poll AND the `OnApplicationActivationStateChanged` hook both ship in this batch.** ⚠️ A design that only probes at `Initialize` does not satisfy the directive and is incomplete, not "phase 1".

### KBD-§1. ⛔ THE CENTRAL LAW — AND IT IS THE ONE THAT DESTROYS THIS FEATURE IF BROKEN

> ### ⛔ **THE POSITIONAL REMAP NEVER WRITES TO `IMC_Hero.uasset`. IT WRITES ONLY THE `.Key` FIELD OF A TRANSIENT `DuplicateObject` COPY — ⛔ NEVER `Modifiers`, ⛔ NEVER `Triggers`, ⛔ NEVER THE MAPPINGS ARRAY ITSELF.**

- ⚠️ **THE PRECEDENT IS THIS REPO'S OWN AND IT ALREADY COST A PLAYTEST — `.claude/pipeline/handoffs/TASK-445-artist.md:95-105`: rewriting the mappings array SILENTLY DEFAULT-CONSTRUCTED the instanced `SwizzleAxis`/`Negate` modifiers. WASD broke and mouse-look inverted — WHILE THE PROPERTY TABLE STILL READ CORRECT.** ⛔ That last clause is the whole reason this is a law and not a code comment: **the readback passed.** It is the project's named failure class (`SC-§17`) wearing an input asset's clothes.
- ✅ **THE STRUCTURAL FORM OF THE FIX — copy it, do not merely obey it:** every value written is **re-derived from the pristine `Source` array BY INDEX**, and the only field ever assigned is `.Key`. ⇒ **The TASK-445 failure mode is ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION, not dodged by care** (`AS-§17`'s standing preference: a hazard closed by choosing a different shape does not reopen when somebody is tired).
  ```cpp
  const TArray<FEnhancedActionKeyMapping>& Src = Source->GetMappings();
  for (int32 i = 0; i < Src.Num(); ++i)
  {
      const FKey* T = Translation.Find(Src[i].Key);
      Target->GetMapping(i).Key = T ? *T : Src[i].Key;   // ONLY .Key. Ever.
  }
  ```
- ⚠️ **RE-DERIVING FROM `Source` IS ALSO WHAT MAKES THE SUBSTITUTION SIMULTANEOUS.** On Dvorak the map holds **`D→E` AND `E→Period`** — reading the *target* would cascade `D→E→Period`. **This sentence belongs in the header comment**, because the bug it prevents is invisible in review and looks like a model failure at playtest.
- ⛔ **REVIEWABLE INVARIANT, AND IT IS THE CHEAPEST CHECK IN THE GATE: the source asset is only ever held through `const` pointers. THE ONLY NON-CONST `UInputMappingContext*` IN THE ENTIRE FEATURE IS THE DUPLICATE.** `AddMappingContext` takes `const UInputMappingContext*` (`EnhancedInputSubsystemInterface.h:265`), so **no cast is needed** — a cast appearing here is itself the finding. ⚠️ In PIE the source **is** the editor's loaded asset; read-only access is what makes PIE structurally unable to dirty it.

### KBD-§2. ⛔ `MapKey` / `UnmapKey` / `UnmapAll` ARE BANNED ON EVERY SHIPPED PATH — AND THE SUPPORTED PATH, WITH ITS STALE-SYMBOL TRAP

- ⛔ **BANNED, VERIFIED AT `InputMappingContext.cpp:154-158`: `MapKey` APPENDS a mapping built from the 2-arg `FEnhancedActionKeyMapping` ctor (`EnhancedActionKeyMapping.h:44`) with EMPTY `Modifiers` and EMPTY `Triggers`.** ⇒ An unmap+map of `IA_Move`/`W` **drops the Swizzle** — ⚠️ **TASK-445's failure rewritten in C++**, same silence, same wrong-feeling movement. **`UnmapKey` and `UnmapAll` are banned with it**, since they exist only to enable that round-trip.
- ✅ **THE SUPPORTED MUTATION PATH IS `UInputMappingContext::GetMapping(Index)`** — `InputMappingContext.h:220`, verified **public**, **non-const ref**, and ⭐ **NOT `WITH_EDITOR`-guarded** (that last property is what makes the whole approach shippable, and it is the one a reviewer will doubt).
- ⛔ **FOLLOWED BY `UEnhancedInputLibrary::RequestRebuildControlMappingsUsingContext(const UInputMappingContext* Context, bool bForceImmediately)`** — `EnhancedInputLibrary.h:36-37`.
- ⚠️⛔ **THE STALE-SYMBOL TRAP, RECORDED SO NOBODY GREPS FOR THE WRONG NAME: THE ENGINE'S OWN DOC COMMENT AT `InputMappingContext.h:217` NAMES `…ForContext`, WHICH DOES NOT EXIST IN 5.8.** The correct symbol is **`RequestRebuildControlMappingsUsingContext`**. ⇒ ⚖️ **A grep for the documented name returns nothing, and `AS-§14` already ruled that a search result is evidence about the search — an agent that trusts the engine comment here will conclude the API was removed and go looking for `MapKey`, which is the banned path.** *The trap leads directly to the ban.*

### KBD-§3. ⛔ THE `UEnhancedInputUserSettings` / `MapPlayerKey` REJECTION — THREE INDEPENDENT REASONS, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT PROPOSED AGAIN

It is the API a competent reader reaches for first, which is exactly why the refusal is written down rather than left to be re-derived.

1. ⛔ **IT REQUIRES `PlayerMappableKeySettings` ON EVERY MAPPING** (`EnhancedInputUserSettings.cpp:1689-1695`) — i.e. **precisely the `IMC_Hero.uasset` edit `KBD-§1` forbids.**
2. ⛔ **AND THAT MEMBER CANNOT BE SUPPLIED AT RUNTIME EVEN IF ONE WANTED TO: it is `protected` with NO public setter** (`EnhancedActionKeyMapping.h:120-132`). ⇒ The asset edit is not a shortcut around the law; it is the **only** way in.
3. ⛔ **IT PERSISTS — `Saved/SaveGames/EnhancedInputUserSettings.sav` — AND IT WINS OVER IMC DEFAULTS.** ⚠️ **A layout-derived remap MUST NOT persist**: switch back to QWERTY and the player is left with stale Dvorak bindings, saved to disk, with no UI to clear them. ⚖️ This one is fatal on its own — the feature is *derived state*, and derived state that outlives its input is a bug generator.
- ✅ **IT REMAINS THE RIGHT HOME FOR A FUTURE REBIND SCREEN.** ⛔ **This is a rejection FOR THIS FEATURE, not a ban on the API** — a task that proposes it for player-authored rebinding is correct and must not be failed by citing this clause.

### KBD-§4. SCOPE LAW — ⛔ LETTERS ONLY, ALL 26, AND THE EXCLUSIONS ARE DELIBERATE

- ✅ **ALL 26 LETTERS `EKeys::A`..`EKeys::Z` ARE IN THE TABLE — not just the 10 the game binds today.** ⚖️ It is free, and it removes the *"update the table when you add a key"* footgun, which is the kind of debt that surfaces as a mystery input bug six months later.
- ⛔ **DELIBERATELY NOT REMAPPED: digits (the `1`–`6` hotkeys), punctuation, modifiers (`Shift`, `LeftAlt`, `Ctrl`), the mouse, `Space`, `Enter`, `Escape`.** ⚠️ **This is a design decision, not an omission** — Dvorak's number row is identical anyway, and positionally remapping digits would actively hurt AZERTY. **A task or QA finding that "the hotkeys weren't remapped" is WRONG and is answered by this clause.**

### KBD-§5. ⛔ THE FAIL-SAFE LAW — EVERY FAILURE MODE DEGRADES TO THE UNTRANSLATED SOURCE CONTEXT

> ### ⛔ **NEVER `nullptr`. NEVER `EKeys::Invalid`. NEVER A PARTIALLY-RETARGETED CONTEXT. THE WORST OUTCOME THIS FEATURE MAY PRODUCE IS *"THE GAME BEHAVES EXACTLY AS IT DID YESTERDAY."***

Every one of these degrades to the pristine `Source` context and logs — and **QA traces each early return individually** (`KBD-§9` criterion 6):

| failure | outcome |
|---|---|
| `Source == nullptr` | return `nullptr` (the caller's existing null-guard already covers it) |
| translation map empty (host is positionally QWERTY) | ⭐ return **`Source` — the SAME POINTER**, no duplicate, no allocation |
| `DuplicateObject` returns null | return `Source`, `UE_LOG(Error)` |
| `RetargetContextKeys` returns false (length mismatch / profile overrides present) | return `Source`, `UE_LOG(Error)`, ⛔ **and the duplicate is discarded, never handed out half-written** |
| probe yields `VirtualKey == 0`, or the resolver yields `EKeys::Invalid`, or two positions claim one target | that letter keeps its **source** key (identity); the map simply has no entry |
| non-Windows (`ProbeActiveLayout` returns 0 probes) | empty translation ⇒ pass-through, one `Log` line at `Initialize` |
| the subsystem itself unresolvable from `GetGameInstance()` | `AHeroCharacter` applies `HeroMappingContext` unchanged — **byte-identical to today's behaviour** |

- ⚠️ **`MappingProfileOverrides` IS THE NON-OBVIOUS ONE AND IT IS WHY `RetargetContextKeys` HAS A FALSE RETURN AT ALL:** profile overrides live in `MappingProfileOverrides` (`InputMappingContext.h:109-110`), **which `GetMapping()` CANNOT REACH.** ⇒ If `Source->GetProfilesWithOverridenMappings().Num() > 0` the retarget would be **silently partial** — so it refuses wholesale instead. ⚖️ *Refusing loudly beats retargeting half a context.*

### KBD-§6. ⛔ THE PROBE IS A WIN32 SCANCODE PROBE, AND THE ENGINE OFFERS NO SHORTCUT — RECORDED SO IT IS NOT "SIMPLIFIED"

- **UE 5.8 exposes NO layout-independent physical key.** `FKeyEvent::GetKeyCode()` (`SlateCore/Public/Input/Events.h:488`) *claims* to be a pre-conversion hardware code and **on Windows carries the layout-dependent VK**; the real scancode is consumed inside `WindowsApplication.cpp` (Shift disambiguation only) and **never propagated.**
- ⭐ **THE SEAM THAT MAKES THE TABLE CORRECT BY CONSTRUCTION: `ResolveKeyFromCodes` IS A THIN WRAPPER ON `FInputKeyManager::Get().GetKeyFromCodes` (`InputCoreTypes.h:857`, module `InputCore` — ⛔ ALREADY A PUBLIC DEPENDENCY, verified at `GitClaudeUnrealTest.Build.cs:15`).** Calling **the engine's own resolver with the same two numbers the message pump supplies** (`WindowsApplication.cpp:3248-3321` → `SlateApplication.cpp:4953-4957`) means the translation table **agrees with runtime by construction**, including on layouts nobody tested.
- ⛔ **DO NOT MASK THE DEAD-KEY BIT `0x80000000`** — `WindowsApplication.cpp:3317` does not, and the table must match runtime byte for byte.
- **`check(IsInGameThread())`** — `GetKeyboardLayout(0)` is per-thread. Non-negotiable.
- ⭐ **THE SELF-CHECK IS PART OF THE FEATURE, NOT A DEBUG EXTRA: on a US-QWERTY host `MapVirtualKeyEx(0x11, MAPVK_VSC_TO_VK_EX, hkl)` MUST return `'W'` (0x57).** ⚖️ **No scancode table exists anywhere in engine source to cross-check against, so that one assertion is the ONLY thing validating our hand-authored table against the OS.** Log it at `Initialize`.
- ✅ **NO `Build.cs` CHANGE. `ApplicationCore` IS NOT NEEDED.** Include `Windows/WindowsHWrapper.h` (what `InputCore/Private/Windows/WindowsPlatformInput.cpp:4` uses; it is in **Core**); `user32.lib` is a UBT default (`UEBuildWindows.cs:2093`). ⛔ **A `Build.cs` edit in this batch is a finding** — it means somebody reached for the wrong header.
- ⛔ **EVERY Win32 SYMBOL LIVES INSIDE `#if PLATFORM_WINDOWS`, CONFINED TO ONE STATIC FUNCTION IN THE `.cpp`.** The **header stays Win32-free** — the HKL is stored as an opaque `uint64`. Fence the poll timer and the activation hook the same way.
- **MID-SESSION SWITCH — WHY THERE ARE THREE MECHANISMS AND NOT ONE:** Windows fires `WM_INPUTLANGCHANGE` and Slate handles it (`SlateApplication.cpp:5138-5141` → `FInputKeyManager::InitKeyMappings()`), but ⛔ **`OnInputLanguageChanged` is a bare virtual with NO delegate — the whole Runtime tree was grepped, zero.** So: (1) re-probe on every `GetPositionalContext` call (free; covers a layout set before launch) · (2) `FSlateApplication::Get().OnApplicationActivationStateChanged()` (`SlateApplication.h:1690-1691`) — covers alt-tab-out/change/alt-tab-in, ⛔ **unbind in `Deinitialize`** · (3) the 1 Hz HKL poll — **the only thing that catches an in-place `Win+Space`.**
- ⭐ **RE-APPLY WITHOUT TOUCHING THE HERO:** on a change, retarget **the cached duplicate IN PLACE** (re-derived from the pristine source, so no compounding) and call `RequestRebuildControlMappingsUsingContext(Dup)`. ⇒ **The pointer `AHeroCharacter` handed to `AddMappingContext` never changes — ZERO new state and ZERO re-application code in `HeroCharacter`.** ⚖️ That property is the reason the hero edit is four lines, and a "refactor" that re-applies contexts from the subsystem throws it away.

### KBD-§7. NAMING + FOLDER LAW (the cross-task contract)

| thing | law |
|---|---|
| statics | **`FSiegeKeyboardLayoutStatics`** — `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/SiegeKeyboardLayoutStatics.{h,cpp}`. Plain static library, **not a UObject**, `GITCLAUDEUNREALTEST_API`. Precedent: `FSiegeCombatStatics` (`SiegeCombatStatics.h:23`). ⭐ **ALL pure, testable logic lives here.** |
| probe struct + resolver alias | **`FSiegePositionalKeyProbe`** and **`FSiegeKeyResolver`** share `SiegeKeyboardLayoutStatics.h`. ✅ **This is the existing "pure data types may share a header when they form one concept" exception (`TeamId.h` precedent) — it is NOT a one-class-per-header violation and QA must not flag it as one.** |
| subsystem | **`USiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem : UGameInstanceSubsystem`** — `SiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem.{h,cpp}`, `GITCLAUDEUNREALTEST_API`. Clones the `USiegeSettingsSubsystem` shape (`SiegeSettingsSubsystem.h:88-89`, `Initialize` at `.cpp:22-34`). |
| log category | **`LogSiegeInputLayout`** — the standing `LogSiege<Domain>` law ("Logging (C++)"). Declared in `SiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem.h`, defined in its `.cpp`. |
| delegate | **`FOnSiegeKeyboardLayoutChanged`** / member **`OnKeyboardLayoutChanged`** — the `FOn<Owner><Event>` / `On<Owner><Event>` law ("Delegates (C++)"), matching `FOnSiegeSettingsChanged`/`OnSettingsChanged`. |
| tests | **`Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/Tests/SiegeKeyboardLayoutTest.cpp`**, `#if WITH_DEV_AUTOMATION_TESTS`, `IMPLEMENT_SIMPLE_AUTOMATION_TEST` with `EAutomationTestFlags::EditorContext \| EAutomationTestFlags::EngineFilter` (the `SiegeSettingsTest.cpp:113-116` pattern). **Test names live under `Siegebound.Input.<Name>`.** |
| ⭐ **CVar (NEW PATTERN — this batch introduces the repo's FIRST console variable; the pattern is added here BEFORE the task issues, per the house rule)** | **`siege.<Domain>.<Thing>`** — lowercase `siege.` prefix mirroring the engine's own `r.` / `net.` / `a.` families, PascalCase after it. **This batch's only CVar: `siege.Input.LayoutPollEnabled`** (int32, **default 1 = ON**, `ECVF_Default`). ⛔ **It is a DEV/TEST lever, never a player-facing setting (`KBD-§0` ruling 1)** — it is not read by any UI and gets no settings row. |
| tunable | **`LayoutPollIntervalSeconds` = `1.0f`** — a named constant, ⛔ never a bare `1.0f` at the timer call. |

⛔ **The `names:` block of each task is the single source of truth (the standing cross-discipline rule). Every symbol above appears there character-for-character.**

### KBD-§8. ⚠️ PINNED CROSS-TASK SIGNATURE REGISTRY (this batch's link contract)

⚠️ **UBT compiles the whole module.** Every task in this batch compiles against this list **character-for-character**; "improving" a pinned signature breaks the link and is an **automatic QA FAIL.** ⛔ **`HeroCharacter.cpp` compiles against `GetPositionalContext`, and the test file compiles against all four statics** — precedent for pinning before dispatch: `:453`, `:564`, `:915`, `:1345`.

```cpp
// ── SiegeKeyboardLayoutStatics.h ──────────────────────────────────────────

/** One physical key position: what QWERTY calls it, and what the ACTIVE layout yields there. */
struct FSiegePositionalKeyProbe
{
    FKey   QwertyKey;        // FKey this position carries on US-QWERTY, e.g. EKeys::W
    uint32 ScanCode   = 0;   // scan-code set 1, e.g. 0x11
    uint32 VirtualKey = 0;   // VK the ACTIVE layout yields here; 0 == probe failed
    uint32 CharCode   = 0;   // MapVirtualKeyEx(VK, MAPVK_VK_TO_CHAR, hkl) — dead-key bit UNMASKED
};

/** The injectable seam that lets every test run on a QWERTY machine. */
using FSiegeKeyResolver = TFunctionRef<FKey(uint32 VirtualKey, uint32 CharCode)>;

class GITCLAUDEUNREALTEST_API FSiegeKeyboardLayoutStatics
{
public:
    static TArray<FSiegePositionalKeyProbe> GetQwertyLetterScanCodes();

    static FKey ResolveKeyFromCodes(uint32 VirtualKey, uint32 CharCode);

    static int32 BuildTranslationMap(const TArray<FSiegePositionalKeyProbe>& Probes,
                                     FSiegeKeyResolver Resolver,
                                     TMap<FKey, FKey>& OutTranslation);

    static bool RetargetContextKeys(const UInputMappingContext* Source,
                                    UInputMappingContext* Target,
                                    const TMap<FKey, FKey>& Translation,
                                    int32& OutNumRetargeted);
};

// ── SiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem.h ────────────────────────────────────────
DECLARE_LOG_CATEGORY_EXTERN(LogSiegeInputLayout, Log, All);
DECLARE_DYNAMIC_MULTICAST_DELEGATE(FOnSiegeKeyboardLayoutChanged);

UCLASS()
class GITCLAUDEUNREALTEST_API USiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem : public UGameInstanceSubsystem
{
    GENERATED_BODY()

public:
    /** THE ONE CALL-SITE CONTRACT. Never null-for-non-null-input; degrades to Source. */
    const UInputMappingContext* GetPositionalContext(const UInputMappingContext* Source);

    UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure,     Category = "Siegebound|Input") bool IsPositionalRemapActive() const;
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure,     Category = "Siegebound|Input") FString DescribeActiveTranslation() const;
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintCallable, Category = "Siegebound|Input") void RefreshKeyboardLayout();

    /** ⭐ ADDED 2026-08-04 (batch ASSISTANT-EXCLUDE, TASK-516). THE SINGLE-KEY QUERY. */
    UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure,     Category = "Siegebound|Input") FKey GetPositionalKey(const FKey& QwertyKey) const;

    UPROPERTY(BlueprintAssignable, Category = "Siegebound|Input") FOnSiegeKeyboardLayoutChanged OnKeyboardLayoutChanged;

    /** ⛔ Automation tests ONLY. Mirrors SiegeSettingsSubsystem::SetSlotNameForAutomationTests (.h:175). */
    void SetTranslationMapForAutomationTests(const TMap<FKey, FKey>& InTranslation);

    virtual void Initialize(FSubsystemCollectionBase& Collection) override;
    virtual void Deinitialize() override;

private:
    /** HKL as an opaque uint64 — this is what keeps the header Win32-free. */
    uint64 CachedLayoutHandle = 0;

    UPROPERTY(Transient)
    TMap<TObjectPtr<const UInputMappingContext>, TObjectPtr<UInputMappingContext>> PositionalContexts;

    TMap<FKey, FKey> TranslationMap;
};
```

- ⛔ **`GetPositionalContext` IS DELIBERATELY *NOT* A `UFUNCTION`, AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** UHT rejects a `const UObject*` **return** type on a reflected function. ⚠️ **A well-meaning "expose it to Blueprint" edit does not fail review — it fails UHT, loudly, at the batch's only compile gate.** Say so in the header.
- ⛔ **`IsPositionalRemapActive()` MEANS `TranslationMap.Num() > 0`** — i.e. *"the host layout is not positionally QWERTY."* It does **not** mean *"a duplicate exists"*. ⚖️ Pinned because the two diverge on the very first call and a test asserting the wrong one passes for the wrong reason.
- **`DescribeActiveTranslation()` format:** `"W → Comma, S → O, D → E"` — `", "` separated, `" → "` between, **source-key order as returned by `GetQwertyLetterScanCodes()`** (i.e. A..Z), identity entries omitted, **empty string when the map is empty.** ⛔ **A byte/format claim about this string is asserted with `TestEqualSensitive`, never `TestEqual` (`SC-§13`).**

⭐ **PIN AMENDED 2026-08-04 — `GetPositionalKey` (batch ASSISTANT-EXCLUDE). THE PIN IS EDITED *BEFORE* THE TASK ISSUES, WHICH IS THE ONLY ORDER THIS REGISTRY EVER PERMITS.**

- **WHY IT EXISTS:** `KBD-§4` puts **all 26 letters** in `TranslationMap` — so the answer to *"what does the physical QWERTY-`Z` position yield on this layout?"* is already computed and stored. ⛔ **But `TranslationMap` is `private` and the class exposes NO single-key query** — only `GetPositionalContext` (whole-IMC) and `DescribeActiveTranslation` (a human-readable dump). A caller that needs **one** key today would have to parse a display string or duplicate the probe, and **both of those are second sources of truth for a mapping this subsystem already owns.** One accessor closes it.
- **THE CONTRACT, and it is `KBD-§5`'s fail-safe table applied to a scalar:**
  - **Returns the `FKey` the ACTIVE layout yields at `QwertyKey`'s physical position.** US-QWERTY host ⇒ returns `QwertyKey` (identity is never *stored*, so "no entry" and "identity" are the same answer and both return the input).
  - ⛔ **IT NEVER RETURNS `EKeys::Invalid` AND NEVER RETURNS AN INVALID `FKey` FOR A VALID INPUT.** Absent entry ⇒ the input, unchanged. ⚠️ **An invalid input returns that same invalid input** — the function is a *lookup*, not a validator, and it must not start refusing keys the caller already holds.
  - ⛔ **`const`, and therefore it does NOT re-probe.** ⚠️ **`RefreshKeyboardLayout()` is what makes the map current** (`KBD-§6`'s three mechanisms). ⇒ **RULED: the caller refreshes; the accessor reads.** `USiegeAssistantConsoleWidget::OpenConsole()` calls `RefreshKeyboardLayout()` **once per open** — one probe per console open is free by `KBD-§6`'s own standard (`GetPositionalContext` re-probes on *every* call) and it makes the accept key correct **even when `siege.Input.LayoutPollEnabled` has been turned off for testing.** ⛔ **Do NOT bind `OnKeyboardLayoutChanged` from a widget** — that is new lifetime state to unbind wrongly, for a value that is re-read at every open anyway.
- ⚠️ **DIRECTION, STATED BECAUSE GETTING IT BACKWARDS COMPILES AND SILENTLY BINDS THE WRONG KEY:** the map is **source (QWERTY) → what the active layout yields at that position.** On US-Dvorak `GetPositionalKey(EKeys::Z)` returns **`EKeys::Semicolon`**, because the physical position QWERTY calls `Z` produces `;` on Dvorak. ⇒ **A key-press comparison is `InKeyEvent.GetKey() == GetPositionalKey(EKeys::Z)`, never the reverse lookup.**
- ⛔ **AND THE PLAYER-FACING STRING DOES *NOT* USE THIS FUNCTION. ANY PROMPT THAT NAMES THE ACCEPT KEY SAYS `Z`, ON EVERY LAYOUT.** ⚖️ **That is `KBD-§0` ruling 1 restated, not an inconsistency:** the whole feature exists so *"the keys go where they are printed on a QWERTY reference"* — the player is on QWERTY **hardware** with a Dvorak **software** layout, so their keycap says `Z` and telling them to *"press `;`"* would be the bug, not the fix. **The lookup is for the comparison; the literal is for the human.**

### KBD-§9. THE QA GATE — ⛔ `.claude/pipeline/qa/TASK-513-keyboard-layout.md`, AND IT NAMES 509 · 510 · 511 · 512

⚠️ **NAMING DEPARTURE, DECLARED (`SC-§15`): the plan proposed `qa/TASK-509-keyboard-layout.md`. ⛔ SUPERSEDED — the gate file is named for the GATE task**, matching `qa/TASK-506.md` (which gates TASK-505). ⚖️ Reason: `qa/TASK-464.md` was already nearly mis-cited as a gate on TASK-438 because its number resembled its subject (`SC-§29`); naming a gate after a task it does not belong to manufactures that trap. **Nobody greps for `TASK-509-keyboard-layout.md`; it will not exist.**

**Criteria 1–9 are carried VERBATIM from the approved plan's "QA gate criteria" section:**

1. ⛔ **No path writes to `IMC_Hero.uasset`** — grep the diff for any non-const use of the *source* IMC. *(TASK-445.)*
2. ⛔ **No code writes `Modifiers`, `Triggers`, or the mappings array — only `.Key`.** `MapKey`/`UnmapKey`/`UnmapAll`/`Add`/`RemoveAt`/`Empty` must not appear on any IMC on a shipped path. *(TASK-399/445 failure class.)*
3. Complete-type include law on `HeroCharacter.cpp`. *(TASK-110.)*
4. No shadowing of inherited reflected members (C4457/C4458).
5. Most-vexing-parse scan. *(TASK-416.)*
6. Every failure mode degrades to the untranslated source context — never null, never `EKeys::Invalid`. Trace each early return.
7. `#if PLATFORM_WINDOWS` encloses every Win32 symbol; non-Windows compiles and is pass-through.
8. `TestEqualSensitive`, not `TestEqual`, for FString claims. *(§13.)*
9. The tests genuinely run without Dvorak hardware — confirm the injected-resolver seam has no hidden dependence on the host layout.

**Criteria 10–13 are MANAGER ADDITIONS (added, never substituted — the nine above are unaltered):**

10. ⛔ **GUARD PLACEMENT, not guard presence (`SC-§21`): the `GetPositionalContext` resolve sits INSIDE the existing `LocalPlayer`/`EnhancedInputLocalPlayerSubsystem` guard chain in `NotifyControllerChanged`.** ⚠️ **That function runs on the server for a remote client's pawn too** — a probe hoisted above the guard would run a layout probe for a machine that is not there. **"The guard is present" is not the review; *where* it sits is.**
11. ⛔ **THE M8 DECLARATION IS PRESENT VERBATIM** in every code task's handoff and in both new headers: *"adds no replicated property, no new replicated class, no new relevancy tier."* **"Tier not declared" is a QA FAIL** (the standing M8 declaration duty).
12. **Pinned-registry conformance, character-for-character against `KBD-§8`** — every signature, the CVar name `siege.Input.LayoutPollEnabled`, `check(IsInGameThread())` present in the Win32 probe, the activation hook **unbound in `Deinitialize`**, and **no `Build.cs` change** (`KBD-§6`).
13. ⛔ **`KBD-§0` SCOPE: no `USiegeSettingsSaveGame` field, no settings-menu row, no digit/modifier/mouse remap.** ⚠️ **A settings toggle is a scope breach EVEN IF THE REVIEWER AGREES WITH IT** — it is Jonathan's decided ruling (the `AS-§6`/`Escape` precedent, same shape).

⛔ **`SC-§27b` — A COMPILE-GATE PASS ATTACHES TO A COMMIT, NEVER TO A LANE. ✅ THIS BATCH IS ORDERED SO THAT IS SATISFIED STRUCTURALLY: all four code tasks land BEFORE the single gate, and the single gate precedes the single compile+commit.** ⇒ **No second verdict is owed** — *unless* the gate fails and a fix lands, in which case `SC-§27`'s **diff-scoped** verdict applies (the fix's changed lines + the fences it must not have disturbed, ⛔ never a re-litigation of the passed design).

### KBD-§10. M8 DECLARATION — STATED, BECAUSE *"THERE IS NOTHING TO DECLARE"* ONLY COUNTS WHEN IT IS STATED

⛔ **THIS FEATURE ADDS NO REPLICATED PROPERTY, NO NEW REPLICATED CLASS, AND NO NEW RELEVANCY TIER.**

- ✅ **AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL, NOT INCIDENTAL: `USiegeKeyboardLayoutSubsystem` IS A `UGameInstanceSubsystem` — one per client process, client-local by construction.** In a listen-server match the host and the joining client each probe **their own** OS layout, which is the correct behaviour and the only possible one. **The duplicate IMC lives in the transient package and is never seen by the network.**
- ⚠️ **The `AHeroCharacter` edit sits inside the local-player guard chain (`KBD-§9` criterion 10), so nothing about it executes for a non-local pawn.**

### KBD-§11. ⚠️ KNOWN LIMITATIONS — STATED UP FRONT, ⛔ NONE OF THEM ARE BUGS

Carried from the plan so a playtest report does not spend a QA loop on a designed outcome.

- ⚠️ **The `~` console key MOVES.** `InputSettings.cpp:173-195` picks it by CULTURE (`VK_OEM_7`/`5`/`3`) and this feature does not touch it — it lands wherever Dvorak puts it.
- ⚠️ **Digits and punctuation are deliberately not remapped** (`KBD-§4`).
- ⚠️ **Windows only.** macOS (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardLayoutInputSource`) and Linux (XKB) need their own probes; **pass-through there is CORRECT, not broken.**
- ⚠️ **Non-Latin layouts (Cyrillic/Greek) come out identity-equivalent** — Windows keeps `VK_A`..`VK_Z` at QWERTY positions for them and `GetKeyFromCodes` synthesizes a matching FKey (`InputCoreTypes.cpp:1603-1609`). **Correct outcome; recorded so it is not reported as a miss.**
- ⚠️ **A future rebind screen will display `"Comma"`, not `"W"`.** `DescribeActiveTranslation()` exists so a settings row can eventually read *"Key positions: QWERTY (layout: US-Dvorak)"* — ⛔ **that is a FUTURE task and is not in this batch's scope** (`KBD-§0` ruling 1).

## Damage types (C++)
- UDamageType subclasses named `USiegeDamageType_<Kind>`, all declared in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/DamageTypes.h/.cpp`
- Kinds: `Melee`, `Projectile`, `Siege` (**added M4, TASK-054**), `Spell` (**added M5, TASK-098**)
- Damage-vs-fortification scaling (GDD §3.0) reads `DamageEvent.DamageTypeClass`: Projectile = 50%, Melee/default = 100%, **`Siege` = 200%**. **M4 ruling:** the 200% applies to BOTH the castle (`ACastle::TakeDamage`) AND buildings (`ABuilding::TakeDamage`) — Siege units (Profile=Siege: Ogre, Sapper) tag their attacks with `USiegeDamageType_Siege`. Units and the hero always take the listed damage (no scaling). Attackers tag projectile/siege damage; melee needs no tag.
- **M5 ruling — `Spell` = 50% in `ACastle::TakeDamage` ONLY** (mirrors the M2 Projectile precedent, NOT the M4 Siege both-rule): buildings/units/hero take FULL listed spell damage — Lightning (200) must kill an Arrow Tower (150 HP), it is "the tower-killer" per GDD §4. All spell damage is applied with `USiegeDamageType_Spell`.

## Spells & Set III (M5)
Added 2026-07-08 (TASK-097..109; GDD §3.5/§3.11/§4 Set III/§7). Spells have NO actor blueprints — they resolve through code (see resolver). Crystal Tower is a normal building card (existing `BP_Building_<CardID>` / `SM_<CardID>` laws apply: `BP_Building_CrystalTower`, `SM_CrystalTower`; card art `T_CardArt_<CardID>` law applies to all 6 new CardIDs).
- **Set III CardIDs (row names, character-for-character):** `Fireball`, `FrostNova`, `Lightning`, `BattleCry`, `Pickpocket`, `CrystalTower`.
- **Spell resolver:** `USpellLibrary` (UBlueprintFunctionLibrary) in `Source/GitClaudeUnrealTest/Siegebound/SpellLibrary.h/.cpp` — the ONE home for spell resolution, shared by `ASiegePlayerController` (targeting mode) and `ASiegeBotController` (M5 bot rules). Entry point pinned: `static bool USpellLibrary::ResolveSpell(UWorld* World, FName CardID, const FCardRow& Row, ETeamId CasterTeam, const FVector& TargetPoint)`. Null-safe everywhere; no friendly fire (§3.0).
- **FCardRow M5 columns (typed one-per-concept, sparse; registry continues the M4 list):**
  - `SpellEffect` (`ESpellEffect` enum, `None`) — dispatch token: `None` / `AoEDamage` (Fireball) / `Freeze` (FrostNova) / `TopTargetsDamage` (Lightning) / `AllyBuff` (BattleCry) / `GoldSteal` (Pickpocket). Declared in CardRow.h. CSV cells use the enum value names.
  - `EffectDuration` (float, 0) — timed-effect seconds (FrostNova 4, BattleCry 8).
  - `MaxTargets` (int32, 0) — top-N target count (Lightning 3).
  - `GoldSteal` (int32, 0) — gold stolen, capped at what the victim has (Pickpocket 10).
  - `ChainTargets` (int32, 0) — Chain keyword arrives as its typed column per the M4 deferral note: total targets hit per attack (CrystalTower 3).
  - `ChainFalloff` (int32, 0) — flat damage reduction per bounce (CrystalTower 5 ⇒ 15/10/5 with Dmg 15).
  - Spells REUSE `Damage` (Fireball 100, Lightning 200) and `AoERadius` (Fireball 300, FrostNova 350, Lightning 400, BattleCry 400) — no duplicate radius/damage columns.
- **Mechanic rules (UPROPERTY defaults with GDD § comments, NOT CSV — M2/M4 precedent, e.g. Rally/Charge magnitudes):** BattleCry magnitudes (+50% attack speed, +25% move speed), `ChainBounceRadius` = 350 (bounce search radius from the previous target — GDD doesn't specify; manager-defined), freeze non-stacking refresh rule.
- **Targeting mode (§3.5/§7):** spells target ANYWHERE on the map (no half restriction); the ground reticle is TRACE-projected onto whatever surface is under the cursor (terrain-ready for M4.5 hills — LAW, not a follow-up); LMB confirms (gold deducted at confirm — card-leaves-hand-at-CONFIRM law), RMB/Esc cancels free. **Exception (manager ruling):** `GoldSteal` spells resolve INSTANTLY on play (no reticle for a global effect — recorded deviation from §3.5; CardType stays Spell).
- **Spell VFX (code contract):** every spell resolve spawns `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_<CardID>` (Niagara, Content/VFX/), soft path COMPOSED from the CardID (mirrors the BP soft-class-path law), null-safe: missing system ⇒ log once, resolve anyway. Crystal Tower's chain visual is `/Game/VFX/NS_ChainZap`. Donor-duplicate authoring from Variant_Combat Niagara systems is acceptable at M5 (template-donor rule); §6 one-frame readability bar.
- **Targeting reticle material:** `M_SpellReticle` (Content/Materials/, DeferredDecal domain — M_CenterlineStripe recipe), soft-referenced by the targeting-mode code, null-safe (missing ⇒ no visual, targeting still works, log once).
- **Spell VFX element re-skin (M7, TASK-187):** the fire/ice spell systems are RE-SKINNED to the imported `Content/Fire_Magic/` + `Content/Ice_Magic/` Niagara packs AT THEIR EXISTING code-contract paths — `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_Fireball` (← `Fire_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Fire_Magic_Explosion` or `_AOE`) and `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_FrostNova` (← `Ice_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Ice_Magic_Shockwave`/`_Frozen`/`_Snowstorm`). **NO rename, NO new data column:** `USpellLibrary::ResolveSpell` COMPOSES the path from the CardID (SpellLibrary.cpp:77-78), so the re-skin MUST land at these exact placeholder paths (a data column cannot point at a `Fire_Magic` asset). This is a VFX LOOK-SWAP, NOT a new card — Fireball (cards.csv row 24, `DeckCount 2`, in deck) + FrostNova (row 25, `DeckCount 0`, NOT in the default deck — see flag) already exist from M5.

## Spell delivery overhaul (2026-07-21, Jonathan playtest directive — amends "Spells & Set III (M5)" delivery law)
- **Fireball + FrostNova become HERO-ORIGIN LINE spells:** cast fires FROM the hero a short distance forward — the hitbox is a LINE IN THE AIR (projectile travel or capsule sweep, programmer's call at TASK-236), replacing the reticle-placed ground-circle AoE. Aim direction = toward the cursor/reticle point (**CONFIRMED by Jonathan 2026-07-21 — "cursor aim is fine"; no longer provisional**). Targeting mode is KEPT (reticle sets the aim direction; LMB confirm fires; deduct-at-confirm + refund laws unchanged). Damage/cost/castle-50%/friendly-fire laws UNCHANGED initially — line-vs-circle coverage is a PLAYTEST WATCH, not a balance change.
- **Bot cast origin for line spells (flagged design default):** the bot has no hero — its line spells originate from its own castle toward its chosen target point (player = hero origin). Flagged for QA + playtest; the `ResolveSpell` pinned signature stays (TargetPoint semantics shift from impact-center to AIM-POINT for the two line spells).
- **Lightning stays a sky strike** — bigger + taller: AoERadius is a cards.csv DATA change (400 → 700, flagged playtest number) and the VISUAL circle + HITBOX must scale together honestly from the data (no visual-only lying); the strike reads from higher in the sky.
- **VFX contract unchanged in name:** effects stay IN PLACE at `/Game/VFX/NS_Spell_<CardID>` (composed-path law). Donors: `Content/Fire_Magic/` + `Content/Ice_Magic/` (projectile trail + impact looks), `Content/sA_ArcheryVfxPack/` lightning assets (reuse-ledger law; donors READ-ONLY).
- **`SpellDelivery` data column (registered 2026-07-22, QA WARN-2 closure — TASK-248):** FCardRow column declaring the per-spell delivery mode; the value strings and parsing are PINNED BY CODE in SpellLibrary.h (TASK-236 lineage — line-delivery for Fireball/FrostNova, sky-strike for Lightning); unset/illegal cell ⇒ `Auto` (per-effect inference: AoEDamage/Freeze → HeroLine, all others → legacy ground circle; never a crash — corrected 2026-07-22 per qa/TASK-248-qa.md WARN: the earlier "always legacy ground-circle" wording was a wrong promise for future unset cards). Lives in cards.csv per the §3.0 single-source law; TASK-248 appends the header cell + records the exact value set in its handoff.

## Audio event cues (M7)
Added 2026-07-16 (Jonathan M7 directive — the imported "MedievalWeaponsSFX" pack resolves part of the §6 audio gate). The §6 audio hooks (TASK-179, C++, null-safe) play a sound at each gameplay event via the COMPOSED soft path `/Game/Audio/S_<Event>` — a code contract (like the `SM_<CardID>` / `NS_Spell_<CardID>` string laws), null-safe (missing ⇒ silent + log once). The 14 event cues are the cross-discipline contract; the artist authors a SoundCue at each EXACT path (character-for-character = the TASK-179 list). This folder did NOT exist before M7 — the cues must be AUTHORED at `/Game/Audio/`.

A cue at `/Game/Audio/S_<Event>` either DUPLICATES the donor cue into place or wraps the donor SoundWave(s) in a new cue (random-selector over the `*_1/_2/…` variants for variety) — never edits the donor in place.

  | `S_CardPlay` | needs Jonathan source (UI click) | TASK-188 gate |
  | `S_CardDiscard` | needs Jonathan source (UI click) | TASK-188 gate |
  | `S_CastleDestroyed` | needs Jonathan source (destruction stinger) | TASK-188 gate |
  | `S_VictoryMusic` | needs Jonathan source (music) | TASK-188 gate |
  | `S_DefeatMusic` | needs Jonathan source (music) | TASK-188 gate |
  | `S_OvertimeSting` | needs Jonathan source (stinger) | TASK-188 gate |

- **Loop/2D flags (match the TASK-179 code contract):** `S_MinerClink` LOOPS (started on mining/arrival, stopped on death); `S_VictoryMusic`/`S_DefeatMusic`/`S_OvertimeSting`/`S_CardPlay`/`S_CardDiscard` are 2D (`PlaySound2D`); the rest are world SFX (`SpawnSoundAtLocation`).
- **Raw sources** checked in under `Content/RawAssets/Audio/` (raw-asset rule) when the source is a loose file rather than an imported pack.

## Material & Niagara lane laws
- **Custom-HLSL BAN (codified 2026-07-22 from TASK-245; previously lived only in orchestrator memory + the TASK-239 handoff):** NEVER use a Custom HLSL node in materials on this project — under the Substrate + HW-RT stack it detonates shader permutations and wedges the editor (TASK-239: 0-for-2, ~4 h lost; the stock-node rebuild compiles in seconds — TASK-245 proof). STOCK NODES + PARAMETERS ONLY; parameter iteration never recompiles. Evidence: handoffs/TASK-239.md.
- **UE 5.8 Niagara/material lane notes (pointer):** three engine findings from the TASK-245 session — the sprite-VF TexCoord bug, per-instance ObjectPositionWS behavior, and the dead attributeBindings lane — are documented in `handoffs/TASK-239.md` (FINAL SESSION). Read before authoring sprite-based Niagara or per-instance material logic.
- **Instanced-mesh material flag law (codified 2026-07-22, TASK-249):** any material consumed via `FScatterLayer.OverrideMaterial` (or ANY runtime HISM `SetMaterial`) MUST set `bUsedWithInstancedStaticMeshes=true` — without it the HISM silently renders the gray WorldGridMaterial default. TASK-249 evidence: the first wired seed rendered gray with `OverrideMaterials[0]` correctly set; the flag fixed it. Evidence: handoffs/TASK-249.md.

## Raw asset sources
- Blender FBX exports live in `Content/RawAssets/<AssetNameWithoutPrefix>.fbx` (e.g., `Castle.fbx` → `SM_Castle`); the FBX is checked into Git alongside the imported .uasset

## Textured mesh law (TRELLIS.2 art pipeline)
Added 2026-07-07 (TASK-082..088, Jonathan-approved plan: automated TRELLIS.2 → Blender → UE5 pipeline). Governs game-ready textured meshes produced by `Tools/ArtPipeline/` (pilot scope: Footman, Archer, Castle; the remaining M7 blockouts follow this template). `<AssetName>` = CardID for card actors, `Castle` for the castle.
- **Active pipeline assets:** Footman · Archer · Castle (pilot, TASK-082..088) + **Ogre** — pulled forward from the M7 batch by Jonathan's 2026-07-14 directive (TASK-147..152). NO new naming pattern: the Ogre follows this law verbatim — `SM_Ogre` (same-path overwrite of the blockout) / `T_Ogre_D|_N|_ORM` / `MI_Ogre_PBR` from `M_AssetPBR` / slots [`TeamRegion`, `OgrePBR`], UNIT path (feet-center, ≤15k tris, 1024² bakes, ≤4 generated hulls, `ucx:null`). Concept casing note: the drop arrived lowercase `ogre.png`; the AssetName is PascalCase `Ogre` (reconciled at TASK-147). The remaining 15 blockouts stay deferred to M7.
- **Concepts:** the ACCEPTED concept image for each produced asset is committed at `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<AssetName>.png`. Working inputs live in `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<AssetName>.png` and intermediates in `Tools/ArtPipeline/Cache/<AssetName>/` — both gitignored.
- **Stage 0 — concept generation (M7, Jonathan-AUTHORIZED 2026-07-16; TASK-184 build / TASK-185 run):** concepts may be AUTO-GENERATED by `Tools/ArtPipeline/concept_generate.py` — a text→image client that mirrors `trellis_generate.py`'s structure, HF_TOKEN ENV-ONLY secret-handling (guard-secrets `hf_` law), and CLI/exit-code discipline (drives an HF text-to-image Space/model via the existing HF PRO token). Per-CardID art-direction prompts live in `Tools/ArtPipeline/concept_prompts.json` (art-director-authored). Output = `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<CardID>.png` (PascalCase AssetName = CardID — the existing Inbox law; the tool writes PascalCase directly, so no casing reconcile is needed). Jonathan may review/REPLACE any generated concept before its TRELLIS Stage-1 wave consumes it (OPTIONAL / NON-blocking — TASK-167). This REPLACES the hand-drop concept gate for the 16 M7 blockouts. `concept_generate.py` is `Tools/**/*.py` = CODE → the tooling QA gate applies before commit.
- **✍️ FLUX.1-dev PROMPT LAW (added 2026-08-01; learned the expensive way across 8 rolls at TASK-369 — binds every entry in `concept_prompts.json`).** Two rules, both measured on real rolls, not style advice:
