  - **(1) WRITE IN POSITIVE FORM ONLY — IN-PROMPT NEGATIONS *SUMMON* THEIR TOKENS.** `negative_prompt` is **dropped entirely** by FLUX.1-dev (it is guidance-distilled, so there is no CFG channel to carry it), which means every defence has to live in the positive prompt — and negating there **backfires**. Demonstrated on roll 2: *"not rounded pauldrons"* → **got rounded pauldrons**; *"absolutely NO branching antler rack"* → **got a branching antler rack**. Rolls 3+ were rewritten with **zero** negations and immediately behaved. **Never write "no X" / "not X" / "without X" — describe the thing you DO want instead** ("flat slab mantle" beats "no rounded pauldrons"). The `negative_prompt` field stays authored in `concept_prompts.json` for a future CFG-model swap; **it is inert today and must never be relied on.**
  - **(2) HARD STYLE CLIFF AT ≈1.6k CHARACTERS — KEEP ENTRIES ≲1,600.** The shared style block (*"Warcraft Rumble tier, 2.5–3 heads tall, hand-painted"*) sits at the **END** of every entry, so a long prompt dilutes exactly the tokens that keep the roster coherent. At **2,007 chars** (roll 3) the tail washed out and the roll lost the roster style, the chunky proportions **and** two identity props at once. Back at **~1,570** (rolls 4–6) the style returned. The other **18 shipped concepts are 700–900 chars**, which is why this never surfaced before — the cliff is only reachable on an unusually specified subject. If an identity genuinely needs more than ~1,600 chars, **cut description, not the style tail** (or move the style block to the front and re-validate).
  - **Reproducibility:** a shipped concept entry pins its winning **seed** so `concept_generate.py <Asset> --force` reproduces it byte-for-byte (Sorcerer = seed 71022, 1,573 chars). Record the roll count and both gate verdicts in the handoff.
- **Mesh swap:** the refined FBX exports to the EXISTING blockout path `Content/RawAssets/<AssetName>.fbx` and imports OVERWRITING `/Game/Meshes/SM_<AssetName>` in place — same-path swaps keep every code/BP soft reference (including the placement-ghost `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` string contract) intact. NEVER delete+recreate the SM asset.
- **Textures:** `/Game/Textures/T_<AssetName>_D` (sRGB) · `T_<AssetName>_N` (normal) · `T_<AssetName>_ORM` (packed Occlusion/Roughness/Metallic, LINEAR — sRGB off). PNG sources checked in at `Content/RawAssets/Textures/<AssetName>/`. Bake sizes: 1024² units / 2048² buildings.
- **Two-slot material contract (requires ZERO C++ changes):** every pipeline mesh has EXACTLY two material slots, in order — **slot 0 named `TeamRegion`** (a minority face-set: trim/banners/accents; the BeginPlay team recolor hardcodes `MI_TeamColor_<Team>` onto slot 0 — SummonedUnit.cpp:146 / Building.cpp:80 / Castle.cpp:125 — and keeps working unchanged; author `MI_TeamColor_Blue` as the design-time placeholder), **slot 1 named `<AssetName>PBR`** assigned `MI_<AssetName>_PBR` (Content/Materials/Instances/), an instance of the master **`/Game/Materials/M_AssetPBR`** whose texture parameters are named exactly `BaseColor`, `Normal`, `ORM`. The placement ghost tints ALL slots (already true today).
- **Mesh settings:** **Nanite OFF** on pipeline meshes (ruling). UV layer named exactly `UVMap`. Tri budgets: units ≤15k, castle ≤40k (per `pipeline_manifest.json`). Origins: units feet-center, buildings ground-center. Export axis contract: `axis_forward='-Z'`, `axis_up='Y'`, `apply_unit_scale`, FACE smoothing. Collision: units ≤4 simple hulls; buildings author explicit `UCX_SM_<AssetName>` geometry — the castle UCX must be wall-footprint-exact (M1 plinth ~410-unit dead-zone lesson) with bounds within ±10% of the blockout.
- **Tooling law:** `Tools/**/*.py` is CODE — the full QA gate applies before commit. **`HF_TOKEN` is ENV-ONLY**: read from the environment at runtime; never written to any file, never passed on argv, never echoed/logged (guard-secrets hook carries the `hf_` pattern). Heavy Blender refine runs HEADLESS via `blender.exe --background --python` through Bash — the live Blender MCP bridge has a 30 s socket cap and is for <30 s inspection/preview only.
- **Fab quarantine:** marketplace packs land in `Content/Fab/<Pack>/` and are READ-ONLY donors (the Variant_* rule applies: soft-reference or duplicate-into-/Game/, never edit in place). Acquisition is HUMAN-ONLY via `.claude/pipeline/fab/FAB-REQUESTS.md` (FAB-### entries: requested → approved → fulfilled → integrated; Jonathan fulfills via the Epic Launcher). Agents never browse/buy/download Fab.
- **Lane isolation:** this pipeline NEVER writes `Content/RawAssets/CardArt/` or `/Game/UI/CardArt/` — those belong to the card-art chain (TASK-077..081, "Card artwork (hand UI)" law).
- **M7 batch scope (16 remaining blockouts, TASK-166..173):** the M7 art pass upgrades ALL remaining un-textured blockouts through this law verbatim. UNIT path (feet-center, ≤15k tris, 1024² bakes, two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]`, ≤4 hulls, `ucx:null`): `Knight`, `Miner`, `Cavalry`, `Cleric`, `Longbowman`, `MilitiaMob`, `Pikeman`, `Sapper`. BUILDING path (ground-center, 2048² bakes, authored `UCX_SM_<CardID>` wall-footprint-exact hulls, two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]`): `ArrowTower`, `Wall`, `BombTower`, `BallistaTower`, `Barracks`, `DeepMine`, `CrystalTower`. Each same-path overwrites `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` (soft-ref parity, never delete+recreate). Concepts arrive at `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/<CardID>.png` (PascalCase AssetName = CardID; casing reconciled at TASK-166) — the source-of-concepts gate (TASK-167) precedes all Stage-1 generation. Footman/Archer/Castle/Ogre are already shipped and are NOT re-run.
- **GoldNode emissive economy-prop VARIANT (M7 ruling, TASK-166/171):** `SM_GoldNode` IS in the M7 batch (all 16 upgraded) but is the ONE exception to the two-slot TeamRegion contract — it is the §"Team contract" team-color EXCEPTION (gold nodes carry emissive glow regardless of team; `AGoldNode` never runs the BeginPlay slot-0 recolor and is a level prop, not a card actor). It therefore takes a SINGLE-slot PBR+EMISSIVE material with NO `TeamRegion` slot: slot 0 named `GoldNodePBR` assigned `MI_GoldNode_PBR` (from `M_AssetPBR`) with the warm-yellow emissive PRESERVED — either keep the existing authored `M_GoldGlow` emissive on a dedicated slot OR bake a `T_GoldNode_E` emissive channel wired into `MI_GoldNode_PBR` (art-director's call at TASK-171; §6 "gold glows warm yellow (emissive)" is the bar). Manifest `team_region:null`, BUILDING path (2048²/ground-center). Everything else follows the law (same-path overwrite of `/Game/Meshes/SM_GoldNode`, Nanite off, `UVMap`). Concept `Inbox/GoldNode.png`. **⚠️ AS-SHIPPED CORRECTION (2026-07-27, TASK-309 audit): the `MI_GoldNode_PBR` option was NOT what shipped — `SM_GoldNode` slot 0 carries `M_GoldGlow` directly and `T_GoldNode_*` are ORPHANED (referenced by nothing on screen). Same for CrystalTower / `MI_CrystalGlow` / `T_CrystalTower_*`. See "GoldNode / CrystalTower glow materials" below before touching either — a texture re-bake for these two changes NOTHING.**

## Meshy second engine — Stage-1.5 retexture & image-to-3D alternative (M7.5)
Added 2026-07-18 (TASK-191; Jonathan-approved Art Quality Upgrade directive, plan `C:\Users\wesel\.claude\plans\i-am-a-bit-sunny-bird.md`). Meshy Pro is a SECOND ENGINE inside the existing TRELLIS.2 pipeline — it changes only the mesh/texture SOURCE. It NEVER replaces the Stage-2/Stage-3 laws above.
- **Secret law:** the canonical env var is **`MESHY_TOKEN`** (AMENDED 2026-07-18 — TASK-197 DONE: Jonathan created the Meshy premium account and set the key as user env var `MESHY_TOKEN`, not the originally-specced `MESHY_API_KEY`; his actual setup is law. `MESHY_API_KEY` survives only as a DEPRECATED alias — tools read `MESHY_TOKEN` and must never require the alias). ENV-ONLY — exactly the `HF_TOKEN` discipline: read from the environment at runtime, never written to any file, never passed on argv, never echoed/logged. TASK-198 adds the observed Meshy key prefix to the guard-secrets hook once seen. HKCU User scope — note the Windows-rollback-wipes-HKCU precedent (uv/HF machine-config memory). Norton: NO exclusion pre-added — run BARE first (the proven playbook); TASK-198's live run adjudicates, and only a surfaced-VERBATIM TLS/cert failure justifies adding `meshy.ai` / `*.meshy.ai` exclusions. Never disable verification.
- **Tool:** `Tools/ArtPipeline/meshy_generate.py` (TASK-198) mirrors `trellis_generate.py` in structure, CLI, secret-handling, and exit-code discipline (0 ok/`--check` · 2 key unset · 3 quota/credits exhausted, expected pause · 4 API drift · 5 input missing · 64 usage). Two modes: `--mode retexture` (Stage 1.5) and `--mode image3d` (Stage-1 alternative). `Tools/**/*.py` is CODE → the tooling QA gate applies.
- **Stage 1.5 — RETEXTURE (the dark-albedo fix):** input = the DENSE DONOR `Cache/<CardID>/trellis_raw.glb` (or `meshy_raw.glb`) + the per-CardID concept `Inbox/<CardID>.png` as style reference; output `Cache/<CardID>/meshy_retex.glb`. Stage 2 then consumes the retextured donor UNCHANGED (cleanup → conform → remesh/decimate → UV → bake D/N/ORM → two-slot TeamRegion split → UCX → FBX) — only the texture source improves.
- **Stage 1 ALTERNATIVE — IMAGE-TO-3D:** the same concept PNG in → `Cache/<CardID>/meshy_raw.glb` out. Stage 2 consumes whichever raw GLB the asset's state/manifest selects. TRELLIS.2 stays the DEFAULT engine (speed + free quota); Meshy is per-asset/per-class per the A/B ruling (hero assets are the expected Meshy/1536³ candidates).
- **Provenance:** `Cache/<CardID>/state.json` records `engine` (`trellis` | `meshy-retex` | `meshy-i23d`), the Meshy task id(s), and the input-GLB sha; `refine_report.json` carries the `engine` field forward. `pipeline_manifest.json` MAY pin a per-asset `engine` preference (absent = `trellis`) and an optional per-asset `albedo_delight` override (the Stage-2 de-light step, TASK-193, defaults conservative-ON in-script).
- **INVARIANT (load-bearing):** Stage-2 + Stage-3 laws are UNTOUCHED regardless of engine — two-slot `[TeamRegion, <CardID>PBR]` (GoldNode emissive variant excepted), tri budgets, `UVMap`, Nanite OFF, same-path `SM_<CardID>` reimport (never delete+recreate), UCX rules. Meshy output NEVER lands directly in Content/ — everything re-enters through Stage 2.
- **A/B fleet gate (law):** NO fleet Meshy rollout (retexture or image-to-3D) before a Jonathan-approved A/B on a recorded-dark asset (Ogre; Knight optional second sample), judged from the Stage-2 refine_report preview renders. One asset proves the lane, then the fleet — the Ogre/rig-spike precedent. **GATE SATISFIED 2026-07-21 (TASK-200 ruled in substance by Jonathan's playtest feedback — see the board).**
- **Color-fidelity acceptance bar (added 2026-07-21, Jonathan playtest directive):** every retexture/image-to-3D output is accepted per-asset against its concept — the baked result must READ AS THE CONCEPT PNG'S PALETTE (`Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<CardID>.png`), not merely brighter. Meshy runs use `Inbox/<CardID>.png` as the style reference + the delight defaults; the eyeball comparison vs the concept is part of each wave's acceptance.
- **Animation (auto-rig + preset clips):** Meshy auto-rig FBX + preset clip library (idle/walk/attack/death — clips are credit-free) enter via the UE5 IK Retargeter: IK Rig assets `IK_<SkeletonName>` + retargeter `RTG_<Source>_to_<Target>` in Content/Characters/ (prefix table). Retargeted output clips OVERWRITE the existing `A_<CardID>_<Action>` sequences AT THEIR EXISTING PATHS (`/Game/Characters/Anims/`) — the same-path law preserves the TASK-189 composed-soft-path triggers and all ABP wiring; `SK_<CardID>`/`SKEL_SiegeBiped` contracts unchanged. Raw Meshy rigged/clip FBX checked in under `Content/RawAssets/Characters/Meshy/<CardID>/`. Pre-approval spike output goes to SCRATCH paths (never overwrite live clips before the eyeball gate).
- **UE-5.8 retarget-export defect + SANCTIONED export path (added 2026-07-19, TASK-229 ruling):** ALL THREE IK-Retargeter export doors (commandlet, `IKRetargetBatchOperation` editor-python API, and the UI "Export Selected Animations" button) route into the same `FIKRetargetBatchOperation`, which exports ROOT-ONLY motion in UE 5.8 (preview is correct; evidence handoffs/TASK-221-222.md §7 — Jonathan-eyewitnessed). The SANCTIONED export path for Meshy clips is therefore the BLENDER-SIDE retarget tool `Tools/ArtPipeline/retarget_meshy_to_siegebiped.py` (maps the Meshy 24-bone Mixamo-style rig onto the 21-bone SiegeBiped via the IK_MeshyBiped 9-chain map incl. the spine-reversal quirk; exports per-anim FBX for DIRECT import onto the existing SK skeleton). The `IK_`/`RTG_` assets remain for in-editor PREVIEW/verification only — never as the export route. Amplitude acceptance floor (HEIGHT-NORMALIZED, amended 2026-07-19 TASK-232 ruling): a retargeted Walk must show foot amplitude ≥ **40 uu × (unit height / 1.75 m)** — 40 uu at the standard-biped reference height, scaling linearly for short/tall units (MilitiaMob @ 1.49 m ⇒ floor ≈34 uu) — verified BOTH in Blender AND after UE import. The ~7 uu root-only signature is an ABSOLUTE failure regardless of height. Borderline passes still take a PIE visual verdict (conservative-rollout law).

## Skeletal rig & animation workstream (M7)
Added 2026-07-15 (TASK-153; Jonathan M7 directive — replace the blockout-tier procedural-lunge attack (GDD §3.8) with REAL skeletal-mesh animation). There is NO rig/anim tooling in the project today; it is stood up FROM SCRATCH and PROVEN on ONE character (the rig spike, TASK-160/161 — Jonathan-gated) BEFORE any batch rigging (TASK-163/164), exactly as the Ogre proved the static TRELLIS.2 pipeline before its batch. The HERO is ALREADY skeletal (template Manny + `ABP_Manny_Combat`/`AM_ComboAttack` donors) and is UNCHANGED by this workstream — it covers the SUMMONED UNITS only (buildings do not animate; tower recoil stays the procedural static-mesh motion, TASK-155).
- **Skeletal mesh:** `SK_<CardID>` in Content/Characters/ (`/Game/Characters/SK_<CardID>`), e.g. `SK_Footman`. Rigged FROM the game-ready textured mesh (`SM_<CardID>` from the TRELLIS.2 batch) so the animated runtime mesh carries the SAME materials. Raw rigged FBX checked in at `Content/RawAssets/Characters/<CardID>.fbx` (raw-asset rule). The SK mesh KEEPS the same two-slot material contract as its SM source — slots ordered [0] `TeamRegion` → `MI_TeamColor_<Team>` (BeginPlay recolor drives slot 0, unchanged), [1] `<CardID>PBR` → `MI_<CardID>_PBR` — so team recolor works identically on the skeletal path. Nanite OFF; ≤15k tris (unit budget); feet-center origin; `UVMap`.
- **Skeleton:** PREFER a SHARED humanoid skeleton `SKEL_SiegeBiped` (`/Game/Characters/SKEL_SiegeBiped`) that humanoid units retarget onto (author locomotion once, share across units); a non-humanoid / oddly-proportioned unit MAY carry its own auto-created `SK_<CardID>_Skeleton` **only on an explicit manager ruling** — it is a NON-DEFAULT, never an assumption. The chosen skeleton per asset is RECORDED in the task handoff. **AS SHIPPED (corrected 2026-07-26): the planned `SKEL_SiegeBiped` name was never used — the shipped shared skeleton is `SK_Footman_Skeleton` (`/Game/Characters/SK_Footman_Skeleton`, auto-created from the Footman rig) and it is the SOLE skeleton asset in `/Game/Characters`. NO bespoke per-unit skeleton exists. In particular `SK_Ogre_Skeleton` DOES NOT EXIST AND NEVER DID — the Ogre (the unit this bullet once cited as the example) binds the shared `SK_Footman_Skeleton`; see the "Fleet Meshy remaster" shared-skeleton binding law below for the full evidence chain and the root cause of the myth.**
- **🪤 RIG-ANCHOR OVERSHOOT — THE DIRECTION IS **UP**, AND THE PROJECT WROTE IT BACKWARDS IN FOUR PLACES (added 2026-08-01; MEASURED at TASK-372, independently re-derived by QA in `qa/TASK-372-tooling-report.md` §4).**
  - **The mechanism:** `rig_character.py` fits every SiegeBiped anchor as `z(frac) = base + frac × H`, where `H` is the **measured mesh bbox height** and the `*_z` fractions are documented as *"fractions of measured mesh HEIGHT (0 = feet, 1 = head-top)"*. An **overshooting prop — antlers, a tall staff, a crown, a banner — inflates `H`**, so for a fixed fraction the anchor lands **higher in absolute Z, i.e. HIGHER ON THE BODY.**
  - **⚠️ THE RIG SLIDES **UP**, NEVER DOWN.** The plan text, the TASK-369 gate rationale, `rig_character.py:216-218`'s comment and `rig_manifest.json`'s `_doc.proportions_override` all state it as *"anchors land too LOW / slides the whole rig down the body"* — **that is backwards.** A maintainer reasoning from the stated direction could "fix" a *correct* override and break a shipped rig. **Worked example, Sorcerer:** inflated bbox 181.805 vs true body 172.42 ⇒ inflation factor **1.0545**; uncorrected `neck_top` = 0.865 × 181.805 = 157.27 UE = **0.9121 of the true body** — the neck anchor rides **up into the skull**.
  - **⇒ THE CORRECTION RULE (unchanged by this entry — the *divide* was always right, only the prose was wrong): `corrected_z = nominal_z / inflation_factor`,** where `inflation_factor = measured_bbox_height / true_body_height`. Sorcerer: `neck_top_z` 0.865 / 1.0545 = **0.8203**. QA recomputed all 16 shipped Sorcerer overrides this way and matched to 5 dp.
  - **ONLY THE HEIGHT-SCALED KEYS ARE CORRECTED.** The 15 `*_z` keys **and** `foot_forward_frac` multiply `H` and are divided; the `*_x_frac` keys (`hip_x/foot_x/clav_x/sho_x/elb_x/wri_x`) multiply the **measured half-widths** `hh`/`sh`, never `H` — dividing them would have narrowed the rig by 5.45% for no measured reason. **The rule: divide what multiplies `H`; leave what multiplies `sh`/`hh`.**
  - **PREVENTION BEATS CORRECTION:** the gate at CONVENTIONS "Ancient Grounds §3" still stands — check the prop against head height at the CONCEPT stage, before Meshy credits are spent. The proportions override is the escape hatch for a prop you decided to KEEP (Jonathan's antler ruling), not a licence to skip the gate.
- **🔗 THE EXPORTED FRONT AND `rig_character.py`'s ASSUMED FRONT MUST AGREE — AN UNDOCUMENTED COUPLING THAT SILENTLY CORRUPTED A SHIPPED RIG'S ANIMATION (added 2026-08-02; MEASURED at TASK-375-FACING-FIX §1c, `handoffs/TASK-375-facing-fix.md`).**
  - `rig_character.py` consumes **`Content/RawAssets/<CardID>.fbx`** (the Stage-2 export) and **hard-codes its own convention: line 383 `"Front is -Y"`** — then drives EVERY forward motion toward `−Y`: `:399` `foot_fwd  # -Y front` (toe offset), `:616` *"Character faces -Y, so a limb swings FORWARD with a NEGATIVE X-angle"* (leg/arm swing), `:749` `A.loc("root", p2, (0, -0.14*H, 0))  # lunge forward (-Y)` (the Attack lunge).
  - **Because the pre-fix `Sorcerer.fbx` fronted `+Y` in that space, the SHIPPED rig had its toe offset, leg-swing direction, Attack lunge AND `_l`/`_r` bone sides ALL INVERTED relative to the mesh.** That is a real animation defect, not a cosmetic yaw — and **no Blueprint or C++ yaw fix could ever have caught it** (a yaw offset rotates the whole visual; it cannot un-invert a limb swing or swap the left/right bone sides). **This is the single strongest argument on record for fixing facing AT THE SOURCE rather than at the consumers**, and the source fix corrected it for free.
  - **LAW: the FBX's exported front and `rig_character.py`'s assumed front (`−Y`) MUST agree, and ANY change to export handedness — `_ue_handedness_precomp`, `pre_rotate_z_deg`, `axis_forward`/`axis_up` — is CHECKED AGAINST THE RIGGER before it ships.** The check is the same `−Y`-camera + `Footman.fbx`-control render from the pre-comp law in "Fleet Meshy remaster". **A facing fix applied at ANY consumer (a `BP_Unit_<Unit>` yaw, `SkeletalVisualYawOffset`, `GhostYawOffset`, a card-render azimuth) leaves this defect live and undetected** — which is exactly what would have shipped had TASK-375's proposed consumer-side fixes been taken.
- **Animation sequences:** `A_<CardID>_<Action>` in Content/Characters/Anims/ (`/Game/Characters/Anims/A_<CardID>_<Action>`). Required actions per unit: `Idle`, `Walk`, `Attack`, `Death`. Shared-skeleton locomotion retargeted across several units may instead be authored once as `A_SiegeBiped_<Action>` (Idle/Walk) with per-card `A_<CardID>_Attack` / `A_<CardID>_Death` overriding the silhouette-specific actions — art-director's call, recorded per asset.
- **Attack montage:** the attack action is played as a montage `AM_<CardID>_Attack` (mirrors the hero's `AM_ComboAttack` donor) so the existing attack-cadence C++ triggers it on the unit's attack tick (replacing the procedural lunge). Null-safe: a missing montage falls back to the procedural lunge (TASK-020 behavior), never a crash.
- **AnimBlueprint:** `ABP_<CardID>` in Content/Characters/ (`/Game/Characters/ABP_<CardID>`) — the unit's anim state machine (Idle/Walk driven by velocity → Attack montage slot → Death). A shared `ABP_SiegeBiped` may back all humanoid units on `SKEL_SiegeBiped` (per-card only where movement/attack differ). Anim-notify / BIE params float/int/bool/byte only (MCP BP-param rule).
- **SkeletalMeshComponent swap contract (TASK-159 — ZERO change to the placement-ghost soft-ref law):** `ASummonedUnit` gains an OPTIONAL `USkeletalMeshComponent` named exactly `SkeletalVisualMesh` alongside the existing static `VisualMesh`. At BeginPlay the unit composes the soft path `/Game/Characters/SK_<CardID>` from its CardID (mirrors the existing `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` string law); if the SK asset RESOLVES it becomes the runtime visual (set on `SkeletalVisualMesh` + `AnimClass = /Game/Characters/ABP_<CardID>`, the static `VisualMesh` hidden) and the BeginPlay team recolor targets `SkeletalVisualMesh` slot 0; if it does NOT resolve, the unit keeps the static `VisualMesh` (`SM_<CardID>`) EXACTLY as today — the skeletal path is PURELY ADDITIVE and null-safe. **Parity rule (LOAD-BEARING):** the placement GHOST is UNCHANGED — it still resolves the static `/Game/Meshes/SM_<CardID>` (ghosts do not animate), so the textured static `SM_<CardID>` MUST remain at its path for every unit even after it gains a skeletal rig. The TRELLIS.2 static batch and this skeletal batch are COMPLEMENTARY: `SM_<CardID>` backs the ghost + the null-safe fallback; `SK_<CardID>` is the animated runtime. No CSV change (the SK path is composed from CardID, never a data column).
- **Unit mesh facing — the YAW half of the per-BP authoring trap that TASK-306/307 closed for Z (TASK-326/327).** RATIFIED by the manager 2026-07-27 from TASK-326's measured 12-unit sweep (`handoffs/TASK-326-programmer.md`), per FLEET-FACING ruling 5. Jonathan confirmed the defect in live play.
  - **THE BAKED FORWARD IS UE-LOCAL `+Y` — ONE value, ALL 12 units, no exceptions.** Derived three independent ways, deliberately not from any authored component value: **(a) one skeleton** — 12/12 `SK_<CardID>` bind `/Game/Characters/SK_Footman_Skeleton`, including the Meshy-new Wizard; **(b) bounds** — ref-pose extents put the LATERAL axis on **X** for every humanoid (Knight 2.04×, MilitiaMob 1.99×), with the **Cavalry decisive**: a mounted body is long in the direction of travel and its long axis is **Y at 2.16×** ⇒ X = left/right, Y = fore/aft, forward is ±Y and NEVER ±X; **(c) generator source** — `Tools/ArtPipeline/rig_character.py:337` authors the character front on Blender **−Y** (exported `axis_forward="-Z" / axis_up="Y"`), and the Blender→FBX→UE handedness flip lands that on UE-local **+Y** with left staying +X (self-consistent under `Right = Up × Forward`); **(d) empirical, in-engine** — raw `SkeletalMeshActor`s at actor yaw 0, spanning BOTH pipeline generations (old-pipeline Footman/Archer/Ogre + Meshy-new Wizard), ALL present their FACE to a camera parked on **+Y** (`handoffs/TASK-326-bakedforward-from-plusY.png`).
  - **⇒ The ONE correct relative yaw for `SkeletalVisualMesh` is `-90`, fleet-wide, with pitch and roll `0`.** Actor forward is `+X`; `Rot(θ)·(0,1,0) = (1,0,0) ⇒ θ = -90°`. At yaw `0` the mesh front lands on the actor's **RIGHT**, so the unit marches with its left shoulder leading — that is exactly Jonathan's "facing sideways when they walk" — and because facing is driven by `bOrientRotationToMovement` and attack aim is the same actor rotation, the identical 90° error applies to MARCHING, ATTACKING and DEATH alike. There is no state in which a yaw-0 unit looks right.
  - **The defect was THREE units — Archer, Ogre AND Wizard — not the two originally suspected.** All three measured yaw `0` **and** Z `0`: their `SkeletalVisualMesh` was **never authored at all** and sat at the C++ constructor default `(0,0,0)`. The other nine measure exactly `(0,-90,0)` with a hand-authored `-CapsuleHalfHeight` Z. The Z column is the fingerprint of the SAME trap — Archer/Ogre/Wizard are precisely the units that FLOATED before TASK-307. **This is not a new bug; it is the other half of TASK-306/307.** One trap, two properties: the Z half was closed systemically, the yaw half was left open.
  - **REUSABLE LESSON (the general law).** Any per-BP hand-authored component offset is a **LATENT TRAP**: it is invisible while every existing unit happens to carry it, and it fires the day a new unit is added by someone who does not know the ritual. Any visual offset that is really a property of the SHARED export contract — Z grounding (TASK-307), yaw facing (TASK-327) — is **DERIVED IN C++ AT THE SINGLE SWAP SITE** (`ASummonedUnit::ResolveSkeletalVisual`) with an `EditDefaultsOnly` exception hatch, and is **NEVER** hand-authored per `BP_Unit_<Unit>`. A per-BP transform value on `SkeletalVisualMesh` is a regression waiting for the next new unit.
  - **Owned by C++, never by a Blueprint.** `ResolveSkeletalVisual` writes the yaw at swap time from `UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, BlueprintReadOnly, Category="Siegebound|Unit") float SkeletalVisualYawOffset = -90.f`, preserving the component's authored pitch/roll exactly as the grounding fix preserves authored X/Y. A `BP_Unit_<Unit>` MUST NOT hand-author `SkeletalVisualMesh` rotation. The property is the documented **exception hatch** for a genuinely differently-baked mesh; overriding it is a **NON-DEFAULT requiring an explicit manager ruling**, exactly like a bespoke skeleton — and any unit that needs one has almost certainly left the shared rig pipeline by mistake.
  - **ABSOLUTE ASSIGNMENT, NEVER ADDITIVE — load-bearing, and it is the obvious WRONG implementation.** The fix **assigns** `.Yaw = SkeletalVisualYawOffset`. Writing `+=` (or any "add an offset on top of the authored value" formulation) drives the nine already-correct units to **−180** and regresses the entire fleet. Absolute assignment is the ONLY formulation that is simultaneously a provable no-op for the 9 and a fix for the 3. **`+=` is FORBIDDEN here — QA treats an additive form as an automatic FAIL.**
  - **SHIPPING PRECEDENT — the project already proved this pattern.** `ASiegePlayerController::GhostYawOffset = -90.f` (`SiegePlayerController.h:702`) is an `EditDefaultsOnly` float applied centrally in C++ that has solved the IDENTICAL problem for the placement ghost for months, its comment already stating that the whole family shares one export orientation. That is why the ghost has never mis-faced — not even the Wizard's. TASK-327 invents nothing; it applies the project's own shipped pattern to the one component that was left out.
  - **DEBUNKED — "0 / 180 / 270 all occur"** (the build-master screenshot observation of 2026-07-26 that opened this batch). That was a **camera-side artifact** of hunting a per-unit visual-front offset against a mixed population of −90 and 0 meshes. The measured data has only **TWO** component values (−90 and 0) and only **ONE** baked forward. There is no third convention and no per-unit mesh divergence — do not re-derive a per-unit camera-yaw table from it.
  - **STATIC `VisualMesh` — a SEPARATE, still-hand-authored contract (manager ruling 2026-07-27, TASK-334).** Everything above governs `SkeletalVisualMesh` ONLY. The static `VisualMesh` (the null-safe fallback when `SK_<CardID>` does not resolve) carries its own transform, **still hand-authored per BP with NO C++ owner**: yaw `-90` and Z `-CapsuleHalfHeight` — verified exact on 11/12 units (Ogre −145, Cavalry −104, Knight/Pikeman −95, Longbowman −92, Cleric −91, Footman/Archer −90, Miner −86.5, Sapper −84.5, MilitiaMob −74.5; the "half-height == 90" assumption stays BANNED, read the real capsule). **`BP_Unit_Wizard` is the ONE outlier at yaw `0` / Z `0`** — latent today (the static component is hidden the moment `SK_Wizard` resolves, and the ghost uses the controller's own offset actor), but it would render mis-faced **and** floating 88 cm if `SK_Wizard` ever failed to load. It is brought in line at **TASK-334**, which RESTORES the existing shipped convention on the one unit that missed it — it does not create a new hand-authored value. **RECORDED DEBT: the static path deserves the same C++ derivation treatment** (**TASK-335**, parked, diagnose-first — the derivation site is entangled with the lunge's `VisualMeshBaseRelativeLocation` caching order). Until it lands, the static `VisualMesh` transform remains a hand-authored ritual and EVERY new `BP_Unit_<Unit>` must set it explicitly.
  - **⏳ THE "BAKED FORWARD IS UE-LOCAL `+Y`" DERIVATION ABOVE IS GENERATION-SCOPED, NOT ETERNAL (added 2026-08-02, TASK-375-FACING-FIX).** All four of its independent lines were measured on **12 units that ALL predate the TASK-348 `_ue_handedness_precomp` MIRROR-FIX (2026-07-28)**. A unit exported AFTER that date lands **un-mirrored** and its baked forward is the OPPOSITE — see "Fleet Meshy remaster" → "THE UNIT LANE HAS NOW MET THE PRE-COMP". **`−90` still holds fleet-wide ONLY because every post-fix unit pins `pre_rotate_z_deg = 180.0` at the source.** Never re-derive the fleet constant from a single new asset, and **never "fix" a mis-facing new unit at the yaw** — that hides an inverted rig (see the rigger-coupling law above).
  - **📏 CAPSULE SIZE IS PART OF THIS RITUAL, AND THE SORCERER SHIPPED ON THE RAW DEFAULT — recorded 2026-08-02 (TASK-375 §3b), NOT fixed, no ruling.** `BP_Unit_Sorcerer` measures **`88 / 34`**, the untouched `ACharacter` constructor default (neither `ASummonedUnit` nor `ASorcererUnit` resizes the capsule, so that is what a fresh BP inherits). **11 of the 12 shipped units hand-author their capsule to match their mesh**; a mesh-matched Sorcerer would be ≈ **`91 / 40`** (`SM_Sorcerer` is 181.81 tall × 90.98 wide — the Cleric's exact profile, and the Cleric is the same class of robed humanoid). The BP is self-consistent either way because Z tracks whatever the capsule is, so this is a **collision/nav/spacing** question, not a rendering one. **If Jonathan wants it mesh-matched it is a one-line follow-up: set the capsule, then RE-DERIVE Z from the new READ value** — the "half-height == 90" assumption stays BANNED.

## Wizard unit — AoE fireball caster (2026-07-26)
Added 2026-07-26 (TASK-298..305; Jonathan feature request: a new ranged unit *"similar to the Archer but instead of arrows it shoots fireballs that deal Area-of-Effect (splash) damage — the fireball hits a target and damages everything within a radius"*). A STANDALONE unit-add batch (label **W-UNIT-1**), NOT part of M7.6/M7.7. The Wizard is the ARCHER behavioral template — a `Standard`-profile ranged `ASummonedUnit` (so it obeys the Shield-Wall ATTACK/HOLD/DEFEND commands exactly like the Archer) — whose homing projectile deals RADIAL splash on impact by REUSING the shipped AoE path, never a new one.

- **CardID / DisplayName:** `Wizard` / "Wizard" (PascalCase row name in `Docs/Data/cards.csv` → `/Game/Data/DT_Cards`). Referenced by code/BP as the `Wizard` FName.
- **AoE = REUSE, not reinvent (LOAD-BEARING):** the splash is the EXISTING TASK-056 projectile-AoE path. `AProjectile::InitProjectile(Team, Target, Damage, DamageTypeClass, InAoERadius)` ALREADY turns an impact into `FSiegeCombatStatics::ApplyRadialDamage` (enemies-in-radius only, no friendly fire, castle-side 50% projectile scaling preserved) when `InAoERadius > 0` — built + QA-passed for the Bomb Tower. The unit ALREADY binds `AoERadius = Row->AoERadius` at BeginPlay (`SummonedUnit.cpp` LoadStats). The ONLY gameplay code change is that `ASummonedUnit::FireProjectileAt` now PASSES the bound `AoERadius` into `InitProjectile` (the 5th arg, currently omitted ⇒ defaults 0). Every existing ranged shot (Archer / Longbowman / Arrow + Ballista Tower) has `AoERadius == 0`, so their single-target behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged; ONLY a `bRanged` row with `AoERadius > 0` (the Wizard) splashes. No new spell, no Siege/suicide reuse — the Wizard is `Standard` + `bRanged`, NOT a Sapper.
- **`AoERadius` property:** the EXISTING `FCardRow.AoERadius` float column (CONVENTIONS "M4 keyword/behavior columns") — NO new column. Wizard default `250` (moderate; the Bomb-Tower/Sapper-proven radius). FLAGGED tunable.
- **Optional per-unit projectile class (additive code hook):** `ASummonedUnit` gains `UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, BlueprintReadOnly, Category="Siegebound|Unit") TSubclassOf<AProjectile> ProjectileClass` (default null). `FireProjectileAt` spawns `ProjectileClass ? ProjectileClass : AProjectile::StaticClass()` (null-safe fallback = today's base projectile). `BP_Unit_Wizard` sets it to `BP_Projectile_Fireball`; every existing unit leaves it null ⇒ unchanged. This is the ONLY seam the fireball VISUAL needs — the AoE gameplay is data (the row `AoERadius`), the look is the BP subclass.
- **Static mesh (ghost + fallback):** `SM_Wizard` at `/Game/Meshes/SM_Wizard` — the placement-ghost + null-safe visual per the per-card-visual law (MUST exist even after the rig lands; ghosts don't animate). Game-ready textured static mesh from the TRELLIS.2/Meshy pipeline: two-slot material [0] `TeamRegion` → `MI_TeamColor_<Team>` / [1] `WizardPBR` → `MI_Wizard_PBR`, ≤15k tris, feet-center origin, `UVMap`, Nanite OFF. Raw FBX checked in under the raw-asset rule (`Content/RawAssets/Characters/Wizard.fbx`).
- **Skeletal runtime:** `SK_Wizard` at `/Game/Characters/SK_Wizard` (auto-resolved from CardID by `ASummonedUnit::ResolveSkeletalVisual`, purely additive per the "SkeletalMeshComponent swap contract"). SAME two-slot material contract as `SM_Wizard`. Humanoid ⇒ PREFER the shared `SKEL_SiegeBiped` + retargeted locomotion; anim clips `A_Wizard_{Idle,Walk,Attack,Death}` at `/Game/Characters/Anims/` (shared `A_SiegeBiped_{Idle,Walk}` allowed, with per-card `A_Wizard_Attack` / `A_Wizard_Death` for the silhouette-specific cast/death); AnimBP `ABP_Wizard` at `/Game/Characters/` (or the shared `ABP_SiegeBiped` fallback). **LOD chain per the M7.6 SK-unit LOD law (exact):** LOD1 50% @ screen 0.4 / LOD2 20% @ 0.15 (`rig_character.py` LOD step / `regenerate_lod`), plus `VisibilityBasedAnimTickOption = OnlyTickPoseWhenRendered` + `bEnableUpdateRateOptimizations = true`.
- **Unit blueprint:** `BP_Unit_Wizard` at `/Game/Blueprints/Units/BP_Unit_Wizard` (`ASummonedUnit` subclass) — CardID = `Wizard`, `VisualMesh` = `SM_Wizard`, `ProjectileClass` = `BP_Projectile_Fireball`. Code spawns it by the composed path `/Game/Blueprints/Units/BP_Unit_Wizard.BP_Unit_Wizard_C` (the per-card spawn law).
- **Fireball projectile blueprint:** `BP_Projectile_Fireball` at `/Game/Blueprints/BP_Projectile_Fireball` (`AProjectile` subclass — VISUALS ONLY). Wires the SHIPPED **Fire_Magic** pack (READ-ONLY donor, template-donor soft-reference rule — never edited): in-flight fireball/trail `NS_Fire_Magic_Projectile` (`/Game/Fire_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Fire_Magic_Projectile`), impact `ImpactEffect = NS_Fire_Magic_Explosion` (`/Game/Fire_Magic/VFX_Niagara/NS_Fire_Magic_Explosion`; `NS_Fire_Magic_AOE` is the radius-matched alternative if a wider blast reads better), OPTIONAL muzzle flash `NS_Fire_Magic_Muzzle`. Overrides the base engine-sphere visual to the fire look; gameplay (homing flight, impact reach test, AoE radial blast, terrain-death) is inherited C++, unchanged.
- **Concept / card art:** concept style-ref `Tools/ArtPipeline/Inbox/Wizard.png` (Meshy image-to-3D input) + acceptance concept `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/Wizard.png` (the M7.5 color-fidelity acceptance bar). Card art `T_CardArt_Wizard` at `/Game/UI/CardArt/T_CardArt_Wizard` (512×512, per the "Card artwork (hand UI)" law), source PNG `Content/RawAssets/CardArt/Wizard.png`; the CSV `CardArt` cell = the full object path `/Game/UI/CardArt/T_CardArt_Wizard.T_CardArt_Wizard`.
- **Deck-builder description = AUTO (no authoring):** the M7.7 `UDeckBuilderWidget::GetCardDescription` generator composes the Wizard's "how it works" from the row fields (`bRanged` ⇒ "homing projectile" delivery line + `AoERadius 250` ⇒ splash-radius line) — NO `Notes`/`Description` authoring. **TRUTH LAW:** the splash line is truthful ONLY once TASK-298 ships (the `FireProjectileAt` AoE pass-through); the whole batch ships together, so it is truthful when the Wizard becomes playable. QA on TASK-298 confirms the `bRanged`+`AoERadius` path actually splashes so the auto-description does not claim a rule the code lacks.
- **Default stats (modeled on Archer + a moderate AoE — ALL FLAGGED "tune at a later playtest," Jonathan default-the-numbers pattern):** Cost `24` (×3-economy multiple of 3; premium over Archer 12 for a repeat-fire AoE unit), MaxCopies `4`, HP `45` (Archer-frail caster), Damage `15` (per fireball, dealt to everything in the splash), Range `700` (Archer), Cadence `1.6` (slower than Archer 1.2 — the AoE is the payoff), Speed `350` (Archer), Profile `Standard`, `bRanged` true, `AoERadius` `250`, `DeckCount` `0` (NOT in the curated 50-card default deck — preserves the `sum(DeckCount)==50` invariant; add the Wizard to a deck at a later playtest). All other columns default / None.

## Fleet Meshy remaster — same-path overwrite of the 11 fleet units (2026-07-26)
Added 2026-07-26 (batch **FLEET-REMASTER**, TASK-306..321; Jonathan directive: the 11 fleet unit meshes render WASHED-OUT / almost colourless in-game and don't match their approved concepts because they were made with the OLD non-Meshy pipeline; the Meshy-built Wizard looks great by comparison; FULL Meshy image-to-3D REBUILD of all 11). The eleven fleet units: **Footman, Archer, Knight, Miner, Cleric, Ogre, Sapper, Pikeman, Cavalry, MilitiaMob, Longbowman**. This is the EXACT Wizard pipeline (TASK-300..305) run once per unit, MINUS the concept-gen and card-art stages (both already exist). **NO new asset names** — every rebuild OVERWRITES the existing asset AT ITS EXISTING PATH.
- **Same-path overwrite is LAW (preserves ALL references):** each rebuilt asset reimports OVER the existing one — `/Game/Meshes/SM_<Unit>`, `/Game/Characters/SK_<Unit>`, `/Game/Materials/Instances/MI_<Unit>_PBR`, `/Game/Textures/T_<Unit>_{D,N,ORM}`, raw `Content/RawAssets/Characters/<Unit>.fbx`. NEVER delete+recreate. This keeps every soft-ref intact: `BP_Unit_<Unit>`, `DT_Cards`, the placement-ghost `/Game/Meshes/SM_<Unit>` string contract, the CardID-composed `SK_<Unit>` runtime-resolve (`ASummonedUnit::ResolveSkeletalVisual`), and the ABP/anim bindings. Because references are preserved, the remaster touches NO C++, NO Blueprint, NO cards.csv, NO DT_Cards reimport, NO placement/BP re-author — it is a PURE art+build asset swap. The ONLY code change in the batch is the systemic float-fix below (unrelated to the swap).
- **Reuse the APPROVED concepts (NO concept-gen):** the acceptance concept for each unit ALREADY exists and is approved — `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<Unit>.png` (all 11 verified present 2026-07-26). Feed it straight to Meshy image-to-3D (`meshy_generate.py --mode image3d`). The M7.5 colour-fidelity acceptance bar applies: the baked result must READ AS the concept's palette — VIVID, not the washed-out old look. This batch does NOT run `concept_generate.py` and does NOT touch `Content/RawAssets/CardArt/` or `/Game/UI/CardArt/` (card art already exists and is out of scope — the wash-out is the 3D mesh, not the card face).
- **Shared-skeleton binding law (LOAD-BEARING — this is why the anims survive):** the rebuilt `SK_<Unit>` MUST rig onto the SAME skeleton the CURRENT `SK_<Unit>` binds to, so the EXISTING `A_<Unit>_*` sequences + the shared `ABP_<Unit>`/`ABP_SiegeBiped` still bind. For the humanoid fleet that skeleton is the SHIPPED shared **`SK_Footman_Skeleton`** (`/Game/Characters/SK_Footman_Skeleton`) — this is the asset the "Skeletal rig & animation workstream (M7)" section PLANNED as `SKEL_SiegeBiped`; the SHIPPED name is `SK_Footman_Skeleton` (auto-created from the Footman rig, confirmed at TASK-297/305), and it is the authority for the shared humanoid rig. Rigging a rebuilt SK onto a NEW skeleton would ORPHAN its anims — DO NOT. **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-07-26 (this clause previously read "Non-humanoid Ogre keeps its EXISTING bespoke `SK_Ogre_Skeleton`"): `SK_Ogre_Skeleton` DOES NOT EXIST AND NEVER DID. Ogre binds the SHARED `SK_Footman_Skeleton` like every other fleet unit — there is exactly ONE skeleton asset in `/Game/Characters`.** Confirmed twice from independent evidence: (a) art-director's chain at TASK-316 — `rig_manifest.json` `assets.Ogre.skeleton = "SiegeBiped"` carrying the explicit TASK-242 manager lane ruling that REJECTS the bespoke option by name; `Tools/ArtPipeline/rig_character.py:105` `SHARED_SKELETON_ROOT = "Footman_Rig"` as a module constant; the 2026-07-22 pre-remaster Ogre rig report (`skeleton: SiegeBiped`, armature `Footman_Rig`, 21 bones); the TASK-244 editor readback showing `SK_Ogre` bound to `SK_Footman_Skeleton` resolving `ABP_Footman_C`; and (b) build-master's in-engine verify at TASK-316-verify (commit `42d2ab2`) — `SK_Footman_Skeleton` is the SOLE skeleton in `/Game/Characters`, no stray created, all four `A_Ogre_*` intact. **ROOT CAUSE OF THE MYTH, recorded so it cannot recur: `rig_character.py:406` writes `report["armature"]["skeleton"] = card_id`, so the Ogre rig report carries a cosmetic NESTED `"skeleton": "Ogre"` — that is the CARD ID, not an asset name. The AUTHORITATIVE field is the report's TOP-LEVEL `"skeleton"` (`"SiegeBiped"`). NEVER read `armature.skeleton` from a rig report as a UE asset name.** The art-director still records the actual bound skeleton per unit in the handoff; a bespoke per-unit skeleton is a NON-DEFAULT that needs an explicit manager ruling before it may be created.
- **Anims + ABP are PRESERVED, NOT regenerated:** the remaster changes ONLY the mesh + textures + material instance. The existing `A_<Unit>_{Idle,Walk,Attack,Death}` sequences and the ABP are UNTOUCHED (do not reimport, retarget, or overwrite them). This is the key DIFFERENCE from the Wizard batch (which authored fresh anims) — here the anims already exist, are good, and must keep binding after the SK is overwritten in place.
- **LOD chain reapplied post-import:** a same-path SK reimport drops the mesh to LOD0-only, so the M7.6 SK-unit LOD law is REGENERATED after import — LOD1 50% @ 0.4 / LOD2 20% @ 0.15 + `VisibilityBasedAnimTickOption = OnlyTickPoseWhenRendered` + `bEnableUpdateRateOptimizations = true` (exactly the TASK-297 `regenerate_lod` recipe; readback `lod_count == 3`).
  - **✅ THE CURRENT FLEET TRUTH (measured live TWICE on 2026-08-02, before AND after the Sorcerer regen — TASK-372 §14 and `handoffs/TASK-376-buildmaster.md`): ALL 13 UNITS ARE `lod_count == 3`.** **⚠️ TASK-289's "SK fleet is LOD0-only" claim is STALE and must not be quoted again** — TASK-297 applied the chains on 2026-07-25 and every board/handoff line that still repeats the audit baseline (e.g. the TASK-297 spec text) is HISTORY, not state. A unit reading `lod_count == 1` today is a REAL GAP, never a fleet norm.
  - **⚠️ THIS REGENERATION WILL BE NEEDED AGAIN, EVERY TIME — it is not a one-off.** Because *every* same-path SK reimport drops the mesh to LOD0-only, the regen is a permanent recurring step of the remaster/rebuild lane. **It has NO durable tool today:** the working commandlet recipe was deliberately left in a session scratchpad because **`Tools/**/*.py` is CODE and needs the tooling QA gate** — parking an unreviewed script in the repo is exactly the file that gets swept into a commit. **Landing it durably is `Tools/regen_sk_lods.py` (name reserved here BEFORE the task is issued, per the house rule), boarded as TASK-392, QA-gated.** Until it exists, the recipe is re-driven by hand from `Content/RawAssets/Characters/<CardID>.lod.json`.
  - **🔬 SANCTIONED LANE + TWO COMMANDLET GOTCHAS (proven 2026-08-02):** the headless **`-run=pythonscript` commandlet** (editor closed) is a first-class alternative to the PythonScriptPlugin remote-exec lane — `Tools/reimport_meshes.py` is the shipped precedent — and it needed neither the denied `bRemoteExecution` flip nor any manual editor step. **Exit-code law applies: `UnrealEditor-Cmd.exe` returns raw 0 regardless**, so the script must print ONE authoritative verdict line and a MISSING verdict is a FAILURE. **(a) `EditorSkeletalMeshLibrary.get_vertex_count` returns `None` inside a commandlet** (it works fine over MCP in a live editor), so per-LOD vert verification must happen post-restart. **(b) `find_assets("/Game","")` returns ~58 `__ExternalObjects__` OFPA registry entries that are NOT loadable assets and RAISE on `is_dirty`** — every project-wide sweep must filter `__External` or it aborts.
- **⚠️ SAME-PATH STATIC REIMPORT — KNOWN TRAP (added 2026-07-26, TASK-320/`f14f325`):** a same-path reimport over an EXISTING `SM_<Unit>` can silently return an UNWELDED LOD0 (`tris × 3` verts, zero vertex sharing) plus `Bad MeshDescription at lod index 1/2/3`, after which the reducer emits **0 triangles at LOD1–3** — while a fresh import of the SAME source FBX to a SCRATCH path builds a correct chain. Observed on `SM_MilitiaMob` only; reproduced with and without `replace_existing_settings` and after `remove_lods` + explicit `set_lods`. **Every same-path STATIC reimport MUST read back the full LOD chain (LOD0 welded vert count + per-LOD triangle counts, expected `15000/7500/3750/1874`) BEFORE the commit — a `lod_count` readback alone does NOT catch it.** Root-cause + durable fix is TASK-322/323; until that lands, the sanctioned workaround is to restore the last-known-good static mesh (`6bd1cee`) and record the residue. NEVER "fix" it by delete+recreate — that breaks refs and violates the same-path law.
- **⚠️ THE TEXTURE-SKIP TRAP — `reimport_meshes.py` SILENTLY SHIPS THE OLD TEXTURES ON A REMASTER (added 2026-07-27, found by the art-director at TASK-329 while writing the TASK-330 recipe; applies to EVERY same-path remaster, not just the Castle).** `Tools/reimport_meshes.py::_ensure_textures_and_mi()` **early-returns when the material instance already exists**: `if unreal.EditorAssetLibrary.does_asset_exist(mi_pkg): … return mi_pkg`. On a REMASTER the MI **always** already exists — so a plain run reimports geometry + LODs + collision and **skips the textures entirely**, pairing the NEW UV layout with the OLD textures. **The run exits 0 and looks like a success while having silently undone the entire point of the rebuild** (and, on a UV-layout change, actively scrambles the mapping). **LAW: every same-path remaster imports the `T_<CardID>_{D,N,ORM}` set EXPLICITLY as its own step (`replace_existing`, same paths) and READS BACK that the new textures landed — file size / dimensions / import timestamp, not just "the asset exists".** **Do NOT delete the MI to force the tool's branch** — the MI already references the texture OBJECTS by path, so overwriting the texture assets in place propagates for free and touches no references, whereas deleting the MI breaks them. This is tooling debt: the early-return is correct for a first import and wrong for a remaster. **🔴 NOW ARMED FOR THE SORCERER TOO (recorded 2026-08-02, TASK-371 §7a): `MI_Sorcerer_PBR` EXISTS, so any future `reimport_meshes.py` run on the Sorcerer will SILENTLY SKIP re-importing `T_Sorcerer_{D,N,ORM}` and reuse stale bakes.** It did not bite on 2026-08-02 only because the facing-fix pass bypassed the tool entirely and imported the three textures **explicitly and unconditionally** in its own commandlet. **Any Sorcerer re-bake does the same — never a plain `reimport_meshes.py` run, and never "delete the MI to force the branch".**
- **⚠️ THE SAMPLER-TYPE TRAP — a same-path texture reimport can silently KILL any MASTER material that bakes the texture in as a NODE DEFAULT (added 2026-07-27, found by the art-director at TASK-337; live defect on main since `fcb1ec0`: every castle crumble stage rendered the engine Default Material — the flat 28.0/28.9/29.2 "stage" table at TASK-331 measured it).** Mechanism: UE validates the NODE-DEFAULT texture at material translation — a `TC_Masks` texture under a `SAMPLERTYPE_LinearColor` sampler is a HARD SM6 error (`Sampler Type is Linear Color, should be Masks`) that drops the master AND every MI on it to the **engine Default Material in game** — while **MI-level texture OVERRIDES are never re-validated**. That asymmetry is exactly why `M_AssetPBR` (node default = the `TC_Default` NeutralORM) sails through with the very same fleet `T_*_ORM` masks textures, and `M_CastleCrumble` (node default = `T_Castle_ORM`, authored at TASK-157 before the TC_Masks import law) died the moment TASK-330's reimport flipped that texture to `TC_Masks`. **The failure is SILENT in the standard sweep:** the fallback logs only a `LogMaterial: Warning` (`Failed to compile Material for platform PCD3D_SM6, Default Material will be used in game`) — the ensure/AccessedNone/Fatal/`LogOutputDevice: Error` greps ALL MISS IT — and on screen the Default Material's grey mottle can pass a casual eye as "char/speckle" (it did, at TASK-331). **LAW, two duties:** (1) every same-path texture reimport that changes (or first sets) a texture's compression/sampler class enumerates **ALL material referencers of that texture — MASTERS included, not just MIs** — and verifies each still COMPILES (Stats panel / `RecompileShaders` / zero `Failed to compile Material` lines on load); (2) **every integration Message-Log sweep adds `Failed to compile Material` to its grep set** — a Default-Material fallback anywhere is an automatic verification FAIL. Fix pattern when hit: the sampler ENUM on the master (`SamplerType → Masks`, the TASK-339 recipe) — never re-flip the texture off `TC_Masks` (the import law wins) and never delete/re-point the MI chain.
- **🪤 THE `albedo_delight` DEFAULT TRAP — "the fleet-wide DEFAULT" IS NOT THE SCRIPT'S DEFAULT. EVERY NEW ASSET MUST PIN THE LOCKED VALUES EXPLICITLY (added 2026-08-01, MEASURED at TASK-370; this is the highest-value tooling entry in this section — read it before authoring any `pipeline_manifest.json` entry).**
  - **The trap, stated plainly:** the bullet below calls `{ao_divide_strength 1.0, ao_floor 0.25, gamma 0.55, gain 1.2}` "the fleet-wide DEFAULT", but **`refine_trellis_glb.py` does not implement it as the default.** Its in-script `ALBEDO_DELIGHT_DEFAULTS` is the **older conservative profile `{0.6, 0.35, 0.85, 1.0}`**, so **OMITTING the `albedo_delight` block does NOT give you the locked profile — it gives you the old one.** (This is why the Wizard's shipped report records exactly those four conservative values and baked to 0.0968.) Any instruction of the form *"start from the locked fleet profile — just omit the block"* — which is what the approved plan and the TASK-370 dispatch both said — **is WRONG and would fail the albedo gate.**
  - **MEASURED, not reasoned — TASK-370 ran the counterfactual in the `--smoke` sandbox on the Sorcerer:**

    | start point | profile | raw | **UV-norm** | floor 0.2536 |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | **omit the block** (as literally written) | 0.6 / 0.35 / 0.85 / 1.0 | 0.1291 | **0.1801** | ❌ **FAIL — 29% under the floor** |
    | **locked fleet profile, PINNED** (what shipped) | 1.0 / 0.25 / 0.55 / 1.2 | 0.3139 | **0.4377** | ✅ **PASS — 73% headroom** |

  - **⇒ LAW: every new `pipeline_manifest.json` asset entry PINS `albedo_delight {ao_divide_strength 1.0, ao_floor 0.25, gamma 0.55, gain 1.2}` EXPLICITLY.** Pinning the locked numbers verbatim is **NOT a per-asset tune** and does **not** invoke the per-asset override law below (no manager flag, no escalation) — it is simply how you select the documented default. A *per-asset* override is only the case where the numbers **differ** from the locked four, and that still carries the full override discipline (manifest + handoff record, anti-bleach guard, concept gate).
  - **The doc and the script disagree — closing that gap is a boarded follow-up task** (align `ALBEDO_DELIGHT_DEFAULTS` to the locked profile so the trap disappears). Until it lands, **the pin is mandatory**; after it lands, the pin remains harmless and is still the recommended explicit form.
- **⚖️ PER-ASSET `albedo_delight` OVERRIDE — the locked fleet profile is the DEFAULT, not a ceiling (added 2026-07-27, Ogre attempt #3 → TASK-340/341).** The locked `{ao_divide_strength 1.0, ao_floor 0.25, gamma 0.55, gain 1.2}` profile (Footman-validated, `d7254da`) remains the fleet-wide DEFAULT — but it is PROVEN insufficient for at least one palette: the Ogre went through it at TASK-316 (`42d2ab2`) and still shipped at raw 0.1416 / UV-normalised ≈0.21 (below the 0.2536 floor), and Jonathan ruled it *"still way too dark"* in play. **LAW:** when a specific asset misses the UV-normalised albedo floor (≥ **0.2536** — the amended gate, binding on units and buildings alike) under the locked profile, the art-director is AUTHORIZED to tune that asset's OWN `albedo_delight` block in `pipeline_manifest.json` — **per-asset, recorded in the manifest AND the handoff, never a silent global change** — until the floor passes, WITHIN the anti-bleach guard (luma retention vs the asset's approved concept **0.85–1.25×**, target ≈1.0; >1.25× retention AND >0.60 UV-norm = BLEACH FLAG — temper, don't ship on numbers). **If the floor and the concept gate genuinely conflict** (an intrinsically dark approved palette), **the CONCEPT gate governs and the manager is flagged with the numbers** — ship neither extreme. Every rebuild handoff quotes BOTH albedo metrics + both retentions, so the third-attempt trap — re-running the same profile and expecting a different result — cannot recur.
- **⚠️ THE HANDEDNESS/MIRROR TRAP (added 2026-07-28 — root cause of the TASK-350 castle Y-mirror integration-block; the mechanism PREDATES the fleet remaster and is structural to the whole pipeline):** Blender is right-handed, UE is left-handed — **UE negates Y on FBX import, so every Stage-2 export renders Y-MIRRORED in-engine vs its Blender/manifest authoring space.** This was invisible for the entire fleet by construction: UNIT collision is generated in-engine from the imported mesh (mirrors WITH the visual — self-consistent), and every shipped BUILDING hull set was a Y-symmetric centered footprint box (mirror-invariant). It detonated on the FIRST Y-ASYMMETRIC authored-collision asset (the hollow castle's off-center gate: visual arch +Y, manifest-stamped UCX gap −Y). **LAWS:** (1) manifest `ucx.boxes` (and any authored-in-Blender-space geometry driving engine-side placement) must account for the Y-negation — the castle mirror-fix chain is the reference implementation; (2) **any Y-ASYMMETRIC feature (gate, portal, socket, off-center hull) makes the in-engine chirality check MANDATORY at integration** (visual feature and collision feature on the SAME side, probed in-engine, not assumed); (3) **FORWARD TRAP — the next Stage-2 re-export of ANY shipped building lands UN-mirrored vs its current in-engine look** (the fleet's shipped looks are the mirrored renders Jonathan approved); **`pre_rotate_z_deg: 180` is the manifest lever when a shipped facing must be kept** — decide DELIBERATELY per re-export, never discover it in Simulate. Fleet audit: ruled CLOSED 2026-07-28 on the structural argument (board: CASTLE-3X rulings block, R1 resolution) — no live functional defect shipped; cosmetic left-handedness closed under Jonathan's blanket approval; does not reopen without new in-engine evidence.
- **🎯 THE UNIT LANE HAS NOW MET THE PRE-COMP — EVERY NEW UNIT EXPORTED THROUGH THE CURRENT PIPELINE PINS `pre_rotate_z_deg = 180.0` (added 2026-08-02; MEASURED at TASK-375-FACING-FIX, `handoffs/TASK-375-facing-fix.md` §1. This is the highest-blast-radius entry in this file for any FUTURE unit — read it before exporting one.)**
  - **The mechanism:** `refine_trellis_glb.py::_ue_handedness_precomp` (the TASK-348 MIRROR-FIX, added **2026-07-28**) bakes `mirror_Y` + a winding flip into the exported FBX so UE's RH→LH import negation lands geometry back in conformed space. **Every other unit FBX PREDATES it** — 11 units 2026-07-26, Ogre 2026-07-27 — and therefore carries NO pre-compensation. **The Sorcerer (2026-08-01) is the FIRST UNIT exported after it.**
  - **⇒ THE SHIPPED FLEET LANDS Y-MIRRORED IN UE** (conformed front `−Y` arrives at UE-local `+Y`) — which is exactly what `ASummonedUnit::ResolveSkeletalVisual`'s comment records and **precisely WHY the C++ `SkeletalVisualYawOffset = −90` is correct for them** — **while a post-2026-07-28 export lands UN-mirrored and reads 180° WRONG at the same −90.** Numeric corroboration, independent of any render: the old `Sorcerer.fbx` measures **Y −39.212 … +39.157** on disk and UE read it back at **Y −39.157 … +39.212** (an exact negation) while X matched identically.
  - **⇒ `−90` IS NOW CORRECT FOR ALL 13 UNITS, BUT FOR TWO DIFFERENT REASONS** — 12 of them by the import mirror, the Sorcerer by a `Rz(180)` source pre-rotation. **Never read the fleet's agreement on `−90` as evidence that the export contract is uniform. It is not.**
  - **LAW (binding until the reconciliation task lands): every NEW unit exported through the current pipeline PINS `pre_rotate_z_deg = 180.0` in its `pipeline_manifest.json` entry** with the `_pre_rotate_source` note. Its Stage-2 previews are then EXPECTED to read inverted (`preview_back.png` shows the character's FRONT). **That inversion IS the compensation — never "fix" it back to 0.0.** The sign cannot be got wrong: `Rz(180) ≡ Rz(−180)` and it COMMUTES with the export mirror, so it flips final UE-local facing by exactly 180° regardless of how many Y-mirrors sit in the chain; the net transform is a pure rotation, so chirality is preserved.
  - **⚠️ THE ALTERNATIVE — RE-EXPORTING THE FLEET — IS NOT A DRIVE-BY: a 25-ASSET BLAST RADIUS that would disturb the castle collision/gate alignment TASK-348/350 exist to fix**, and the fleet's shipped mirrored look is what Jonathan approved. It is a boarded decision (**TASK-391**), never an assignee's initiative.
  - **🔬 THE ONE-STEP DIAGNOSTIC — USE IT, DO NOT REASON ABOUT IT:** render the exported FBX from a KNOWN `−Y` camera in headless Blender **against a `Content/RawAssets/Footman.fbx` CONTROL in the same camera**. Control FRONT + subject FRONT ⇒ conformant. Control FRONT + subject BACK ⇒ it carries the pre-comp and needs the 180. Judge by **POSITIVE identity cues on BOTH sides** (mask / sash / planted prop), never by "no face visible" — that is non-decisive for a faceless-mask unit. A conformed-space preview can NEVER catch this: the divergence is introduced at EXPORT, which is what `export_fbx`'s docstring warns offline probes about.
  - **Cross-check duty:** any change to export handedness must ALSO be checked against the rigger — see "Skeletal rig & animation workstream (M7)" → "THE EXPORTED FRONT AND `rig_character.py`'s ASSUMED FRONT MUST AGREE".
- **⚖️ CHROMA-FIDELITY GATE + ALPHA-MASK METHOD LAW (added 2026-07-27, Archer/Pikeman "chalky" rework → TASK-342/343 — the first fleet CHROMA law).** Two units PASSED the albedo floor (Archer UV-norm 0.3980 / Pikeman 0.3313 vs 0.2536) yet read WASHED-OUT in play — the failure mode is **chroma/hue collapse in the Stage-2 bake, not luminance**; Jonathan confirmed the target is concept-COLOUR fidelity, NOT brightening. Two laws:
  - **METHOD LAW (binding on EVERY retention measurement from now on):** the concept PNG must be **ALPHA-MASKED before any retention metric is computed** — Archer's concept carries a grey studio backdrop that contaminated its corner-sampled luma retention (~0.75 was a false reading); a backdrop pixel in the denominator invalidates the number. Validate the measurement script against recorded fleet values first (the standing 4-dp method law), and name the chroma metric used (mean chroma on covered texels, CIELAB C*ab preferred) — ONE method, used consistently for both sides of the ratio.
  - **THE GATE (v1 — binds CONCEPT-FIDELITY REWORK tasks, where desaturation IS the defect; ordinary rebuilds keep chroma as reported/secondary with the Cleric 0.28× reference):** vs the ALPHA-MASKED concept, on covered texels — **(a) chroma retention ≥ 0.60×** (target ≈1.0×); **(b) oversaturation guard: retention > 1.35× = FLAG** — garish is the opposite failure; temper, don't ship on numbers (the bleach-guard mirror); **(c) hue fidelity: mean hue shift of the top-2 dominant concept colour clusters ≤ 20°** (catches forest-green→grey-teal drift that chroma magnitude alone can miss); **(d) luminance stays IN-BAND — luma retention 0.85–1.25×** and the UV-norm albedo floor (≥ 0.2536) is MAINTAINED; a "fix" that trades saturation for brightness has missed the point. **ESCAPE VALVE (v1 honesty — these are the first-ever chroma numbers for these units):** if the SHIPPED asset already measures ≥ 0.60× yet still reads chalky, or the gate proves unreachable without breaching (b)/(d), STOP and route the numbers to the manager — the gate is re-adjudicated on data, never silently bent.
- **Systemic float-fix dependency:** rebuilt SKs must FEET-GROUND correctly. The prior per-asset Z-offset fix (TASK-259 — `ResolveSkeletalVisual` `SetRelativeLocation(VisualMeshBaseRelativeLocation)`) RECURRED on the Wizard, proving that a fixed member offset is not durable. The offset must be made SYSTEMIC (capsule-half-height-relative — correct for ANY feet-centre mesh) so NO rebuilt or future unit hovers. The batch's ONE code task (TASK-307, from the in-flight TASK-306 diagnosis) delivers this; every per-unit verify gates on it being compiled + live in the editor.
- **Building/tower audit + CONDITIONAL rebuild:** buildings/towers are audited for the same wash-out (TASK-309, art-director eyeball vs `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<Building>.png`) and ONLY the flagged-bad ones are rebuilt — via the BUILDING variant of this pipeline (ground-centre origin, 2048² bakes, authored `UCX_SM_<Building>` wall-footprint-exact hulls, two-slot `[TeamRegion, <Building>PBR]`; `GoldNode` = the single-slot emissive exception per the M7 GoldNode-variant clause). Those per-building rebuild tasks are decomposed by the manager AFTER the audit reports — scope is audit-driven, not pre-created. **✅ AUDIT RETURNED 2026-07-26 (TASK-309) and Jonathan ruled 2026-07-27: EXACTLY ONE building rebuilds — the CASTLE (TASK-329..331, law in "Castle remaster — the BUILDING same-path variant" below). The other eight are OK as shipped and are CLOSED — do not re-open them without new evidence.** Two audit findings are now standing law and live in the sections below: the bleached ArrowTower/Barracks roofs are the `TeamRegion` slot, NOT wash-out (never rebuild to "restore the red roof"), and GoldNode/CrystalTower never use their baked textures (a texture re-bake for either changes nothing). GoldNode's over-bright emissive is a one-lever material tune, not a rebuild — TASK-332/333.
- **Commit / revert model:** build-master commits EACH verified unit as its OWN commit locally on **main, NO push** — so Jonathan reviews the before/after gallery and can revert ANY single unit independently.
- **SCOPE fence:** the Wizard (already Meshy — TASK-301/302) and all non-unit spell/upgrade cards are OUT of scope. This batch SUBSUMES M7.5 Track A's fleet-retexture goal for these 11 units (a full image-to-3D rebuild ⊃ a retexture); M7.5's other tracks (FAB packs, buildings, free pipeline upgrades) are UNTOUCHED.

## Castle remaster — the BUILDING same-path variant + the CRUMBLE-DERIVATION law (2026-07-27)
Added 2026-07-27 (TASK-329..331; Jonathan ruling on the TASK-309 building audit: *"make sure to check the castle, it definitely needs a meshy rebuild"*). The Castle is the BUILDING variant of the "Fleet Meshy remaster" law — **no rig, no skeleton, no `A_*` clips** — and it is the ONLY building the audit flagged. **NO new asset names**: every deliverable overwrites an existing path.
- **Same-path set (LAW — never delete+recreate):** `/Game/Meshes/SM_Castle` · `/Game/Textures/T_Castle_{D,N,ORM}` · `/Game/Materials/Instances/MI_Castle_PBR` · raw `Content/RawAssets/Castle.fbx` · raw textures `Content/RawAssets/Textures/Castle/T_Castle_{D,N,ORM}.png` · concept `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/Castle.png`. Preserves every reference: `ACastle` (`ApplyTeamVisuals` / `ResetCastle` restore the pristine `SM_Castle`), the two `L_Arena` castle instances (win-condition actors), `CastleAnchor_Blue/Red`, the `CastlePlinthClearance` placement dead-zone, and the crumble chain below. Two-slot contract UNCHANGED and ORDER-CRITICAL: **`[0 TeamRegion → MI_TeamColor_<Team>, 1 CastlePBR → MI_Castle_PBR]`** — `ACastle` hardcodes the team recolor onto slot 0, so losing/reordering `TeamRegion` breaks team identification on both castles.
- **⚠️ CRUMBLE-DERIVATION LAW (LOAD-BEARING — the trap this section exists for):** `SM_Castle_Crumble01/02/03` (`/Game/Meshes/`) are **byte-copy DUPLICATES of `SM_Castle`** (TASK-157) and `M_CastleCrumble` **samples the very same `T_Castle_{D,N,ORM}`**. A same-path Castle rebuild therefore hands the OLD crumble geometry a NEW UV layout ⇒ **the 75/50/25 % damage states render scrambled textures** unless they are re-derived. **Whenever `SM_Castle` geometry/UVs OR `T_Castle_*` change, `SM_Castle_Crumble01/02/03` MUST be re-derived by duplicating the REBUILT `SM_Castle` over them (same paths), and `MI_Castle_Crumble0N` re-assigned to BOTH slots of its stage mesh** (the TASK-157 recipe verbatim). The crumble MIs and `M_CastleCrumble` themselves are NOT re-authored — they pick the new albedo up for free through the same-path textures. Crumble MIs live at `/Game/Materials/` (NOT `Instances/`) — the `ACastle` code path is the contract and wins over the prefix-table folder row.
  - **WHY "BOTH SLOTS" IS LOAD-BEARING — verified in C++ at TASK-329, and it is the easy thing to get wrong.** `ACastle::ApplyCrumbleStage()` (`Castle.cpp:326`) does `CastleMesh->SetStaticMesh(CrumbleMesh); CastleMesh->SetMaterial(0, CrumbleMaterial);` — **slot 0 ONLY. Slot 1 is NEVER touched at runtime**, so it comes from the crumble asset's own saved material. A fresh duplicate of the rebuilt `SM_Castle` inherits slot 1 = `MI_Castle_PBR`, so unless slot 1 is **explicitly re-assigned at design time** the damaged castle renders **crumble material on the roofs and pristine castle material on the walls — a half-crumbled look, with NO error logged.** Slot 1 must be baked into the saved asset; there is no runtime path that will fix it.
  - **Evidence the crumbles really are stale byte-copies (read-only `.uasset` string inspection, TASK-329):** `SM_Castle_Crumble01` still carries **`Factory_RootNode.SM_Castle`** internally — proof it is a byte-copy of the OLD `SM_Castle` — and its slots are `[TeamRegion, CastlePBR]` with BOTH pre-assigned to `MI_Castle_Crumble01`. `M_CastleCrumble` samples `/Game/Textures/T_Castle_{D,N,ORM}` — the exact three textures a remaster overwrites. There is **no partial-compatibility path**: the rebuilt mesh has a completely different layout (19,995 tris / 9,792 verts / 19.2 % coverage vs the old 40k), so the crumbles must be **fully re-derived**, never patched.
- **⚖️ CRUMBLE STAGE-LEGIBILITY law (added 2026-07-27 — manager adjudication of the TASK-331 flag; HISTORY CORRECTED same day at the TASK-337 re-adjudication):** the crumble stage params are MULTIPLICATIVE against `T_Castle_D`, so **stage tuning is BASE-RELATIVE: ANY brightness change to the castle base albedo automatically RE-OPENS the stage-spread check.** *Corrected history:* the flat **28.0 / 28.9 / 29.2** table that triggered this law turned out to be three measurements of the **engine Default Material** (the SAMPLER-TYPE TRAP in "Fleet Meshy remaster" — `M_CastleCrumble` had failed compilation since TASK-330's reimport), NOT of the TASK-157 stage params; the earlier "`ScorchAmount` dominates" analysis is WITHDRAWN. **The band below is RETAINED as the standing gate regardless** — the base-relative principle is real, and whether the TASK-157 spread (01 `Darken 0.80/ScorchAmount 0.12`, 02 `0.50/0.45`, 03 `0.30/0.80`, authored against the old 0.0078 albedo) passes against the ~21×-brighter rebuilt base is decided by MEASUREMENT-FIRST at the TASK-337 re-run, against the first real render (fix chain TASK-339 → 337 → 338; retune only on a real-render band FAIL — compiled-material headroom measured 0.957×P before Darken/Scorch). **The GATE, ratio-based so it survives future re-bases** — P = pristine wall luma measured in the SAME session under the TASK-331 protocol (same lit wall region, identical camera pose, exposure-consistent scene, grass control ~11.5): **S1 ∈ 0.55–0.80×P** ("battle-worn but standing") · **S2 ∈ 0.35–0.55×P** ("blackened, clearly worse") · **S3 ≤ 0.40×P** (near-dead charred) · **S1−S2 ≥ 0.10×P** · **S2−S3 ≥ 0.05×P**. Param names are LAW (`Darken`, `ScorchAmount`, `CharColor`, `RoughBoost`, dormant `EmberColor`/`EmberAmount`) — a retune overrides VALUES on `MI_Castle_Crumble0N` only, never renames a param and never edits the `M_CastleCrumble` graph for a spread change. **Jonathan's eye at playtest remains the final acceptance authority over the band** (the albedo-floor doctrine). A stage retune NEVER touches the meshes — the CRUMBLE-DERIVATION law above is orthogonal and stays closed.
- **Castle LOD law — the LANDMARK EXCEPTION, not `LargeProp` (ruling):** the M7.6 "Classic LOD law" exempts castles from the SM fleet's `lod_group='LargeProp'` auto-4-chain. The Castle takes the EXPLICIT reduction chain **LOD1 50 % @ screen 0.4 / LOD2 25 % @ 0.15** ⇒ target readback **`lod_count == 3`**. The audit's "every other building is 4" describes the `LargeProp` group and is NOT the Castle's target — do not "fix" the Castle to 4 by applying `LargeProp`.
- **Brightness profile:** the LOCKED fleet `albedo_delight {ao_divide_strength 1.0, ao_floor 0.25, gamma 0.55, gain 1.2}` (Footman-validated, `d7254da`) is pinned in the manifest for the Castle too.
- **⚖️ ALBEDO ACCEPTANCE FLOOR — AMENDED 2026-07-27 (manager ruling on TASK-329). THE RAW ARM IS RETIRED AS A GATE; the UV-normalised arm is the sole albedo gate, for BUILDINGS AND UNITS ALIKE.** The clause previously read "≥ **0.164** raw **AND** ≥ **0.2536** UV-normalised". That dual form is withdrawn. **THE OPERATIVE FLOOR IS: mean linear albedo ≥ 0.2536 on the UV-NORMALISED (covered-texel) metric.** Raw all-pixel albedo is still MEASURED AND REPORTED on every asset for continuity with the audit record — it is **never a fail reason**. Four reasons, and they are settled; **do not re-litigate this on the next building rebuild**:
  1. **The raw arm carries no independent information — it is the UV-normalised metric multiplied by an atlas-packing variable.** `raw ≈ UV-normalised × UV-coverage`, confirmed to 4 dp across the measured set (Castle rebuilt `0.5847 × 0.192 = 0.1123`; Wall `0.2451 × 0.331 = 0.0811`; Footman `0.2536 × 0.646 = 0.1638`). Coverage is a function of **how the atlas was packed**, not of how the asset looks on screen: re-pack the same textures tighter and "raw" rises with zero visual change; add texel margin and it falls. Gating on raw gates on UV packing.
  2. **It is arithmetically unreachable for a building.** At the buildings' 12–33 % coverage, `raw ≥ 0.164` demands a covered-texel mean of ~0.50–1.37 (the Castle at 19.2 % coverage would need **0.854** — a near-white texture). **NO BUILDING HAS EVER REACHED IT**; the previous best was Wall at 0.0810, and the rebuilt Castle at 0.1123 is now the brightest building in the project by 39 %. Chasing the raw number means BLEACHING the asset — the exact defect this whole remaster exists to remove. TASK-329 correctly refused to chase it.
  3. **The reference asset itself fails it.** The Footman baseline measures **0.1639** against a 0.164 floor. A floor the baseline does not clear is a broken floor, not a high bar.
  4. **Assets Jonathan has already signed off fail it.** Ogre, Knight and Wall fail one or both arms as written.
  - **NOT RETROACTIVE.** This floor gates NEW and REBUILT assets. It does **not** re-open anything already shipped and signed off — in particular the eight audit-closed buildings stay closed (see the Fleet-Meshy-remaster building clause). Jonathan's eye remains the final acceptance authority over any measured number.
  - **THE CEILING IS THE CONCEPT, NOT AN ABSOLUTE (anti-bleach guard).** The floor stops darkness; the concept stops the overcorrection. Every rebuild reports **luma retention vs `Content/RawAssets/Concepts/<CardID>.png`** (target ≈ 1.0×). **A result that is simultaneously > 1.25× luma retention AND > 0.60 UV-normalised is a BLEACH FLAG** — do not ship it on the measurement alone; apply the zero-cost temper lever (`gamma 0.65 / gain 1.0`, Stage-2-only re-run from the cached donor GLB, ~23 s, **no Meshy credits**) or take a manager ruling. *Rebuilt Castle: 0.5847 UV-norm at **1.0133×** luma retention — highest covered albedo in the fleet, yet within **1.3 %** of its own concept's luminance. Inside the guard. PASS.*
  - **Chroma retention vs the concept stays SECONDARY and NON-GATING** (reference floor: Cleric 0.28×). **CORRECTION, binding — the Castle's SHIPPED chroma retention is 0.31×, NOT the 0.20× recorded in the TASK-309 audit** (a flat-lit-preview vs beauty-render/mask difference; the beauty figure is 0.60×). **Do not quote 0.20× as confirmed anywhere again.** Consequence, recorded honestly: at 0.31× chroma alone would *not* have flagged the Castle. **The rebuild's justification never rested on chroma** — it rests on the two stop-gating metrics, which TASK-329 reproduced **exactly** (raw 0.0078, UV-normalised 0.0653) plus the confirmed-absent `albedo_delight` block, and on the clincher that the **p99 of the entire shipped albedo texture was 0.117 — the brightest 1 % of the Castle was darker than the Footman's mean.**
  - **Method law (carry forward on EVERY audit):** buildings carry 12–33 % UV coverage vs units' 59–73 %, so raw all-pixel albedo is NOT comparable across the two groups. Any measurement script must first be validated against the pipeline's own recorded `albedo_delight.mean_linear_after` to 4 dp on at least three shipped assets before its numbers are trusted (TASK-329 validated on Footman 0.1639 / Knight 0.1639 / Cleric 0.3614).
- **Geometry / collision (BUILDING path):** ground-centre origin (bottom of mesh at the actor origin plane; current min-Z ≈ 0.44 uu is the reference), bounds preserved within **±10 %** of ~**814 × 819 × 898 uu** (the plinth dead-zone and `CastlePlinthClearance` 420 are computed off this), authored `UCX_SM_Castle` wall-footprint-exact hulls (M1 ~410-uu plinth dead-zone lesson), Nanite OFF, UV layer `UVMap`, 2048² bakes. Tri budget: **target ≤20k** — **the 40k debt is CLEARED as of TASK-329: the manifest `tri_budget` is now 20000 and the rebuilt mesh ships 19,995 tris / 9,792 welded verts at bounds 814.5 × 820.6 × 894.9 uu. The silhouette got BETTER at half the triangles** (clean rectangular curtain-wall plan, round corner towers, courtyard, central keep, protruding gatehouse porch — the old 40k spent its budget on a melted blob). There is no case for exceeding 20k on this asset.
- **UCX-DRIFT LAW (added 2026-07-27, manager ruling on TASK-329's finding — applies to EVERY building rebuild, not just the Castle):** authored `UCX_` hulls are tuned to a SPECIFIC mesh's wall planes; a same-path rebuild changes the internal massing and **silently invalidates them**. Measured on the rebuilt Castle (area-weighted face centroids, wall band z 40–280 uu): front `-270` vs wall `≈-269` ✅ and east `+386` vs `≈+382` ✅, but **west `-286.5` vs wall `≈-372` (hull ~86 uu INSIDE)** and **back `+306` vs `≈+395` (~89 uu INSIDE)**; gatehouse porch `-329` vs `≈-385` (~56 uu — the deliberate TASK-087 "rock ramp uncollided" trade). Consequence: units visually penetrate the west/back curtain wall by ~85–90 uu before colliding.
  - **RULING: the drift is ACCEPTED as shipped, and hull re-tuning is NOT an integration-task side-effect.** It is cosmetic (~10 % of the castle's 814-uu span, on the two sides the arena camera favours least) and it is a **GAMEPLAY trade, not an art fix**: widening the hulls to meet the walls ENLARGES the M1 placement dead-zone, and `CastlePlinthClearance` (420) was derived from this footprint. The M1 lesson and the TASK-087 note ("the M1 dead-zone SHRINKS, gold-node/miner clearance improves") show the shrinkage was deliberate and gameplay-motivated. **Jonathan's playtest verdict decides it** — parked as TASK-336.
  - **DUTIES this creates.** (a) Every building rebuild MEASURES hull-outer-face vs visual-wall delta per side and REPORTS it in the handoff — the art-director did this unprompted on TASK-329 and it is now required. (b) The integration task OBSERVES the drift in Simulate and captures it; it does **not** fix it. (c) If a re-tune is ever ruled, the ONLY sanctioned lane is a `pipeline_manifest.json` `ucx.boxes` edit + a free Stage-2 re-run by the art-director (~23 s, **no Meshy credits**). **Hand-editing collision primitives in the editor is BANNED** — it is silently lost on the next same-path reimport and does not propagate to the crumble duplicates.
- **Audit-method law (carry forward on EVERY building audit):** a **bleached roof is the `TeamRegion` slot painted `MI_TeamColor_Blue`, NOT wash-out** — ArrowTower and Barracks read near-white in flat-lit previews for exactly this reason. **NEVER commission a rebuild to "restore the red roof"**; the concepts' terracotta was deliberately traded for the team-colour identifier.

## Castle 3× HOLLOW (2026-07-28) — supersedes the Castle bounds/plinth laws above; adds the team-gated interior
Added 2026-07-28 (Jonathan directive: castle **27× volume (3× per dimension)**, HOLLOW walkable interior, own units enter AND spawn inside, enemy units cannot enter; NEW concept art; built through the Meshy pipeline — TASK-347..351). Everything in "Castle remaster" above stays law EXCEPT where this section supersedes it.
- **NEW BOUNDS LAW (supersedes the ~814×819×898 target):** bounds within **±10% of 2442×2460×2694 uu** (3× the shipped 814.5×820.6×894.9). Ground-centre origin, Nanite OFF, LOD law UNCHANGED (explicit 3-chain LOD1 50%@0.4 / LOD2 25%@0.15 — NOT `LargeProp`). **Tri budget raised for the hollow interior: target ≤30k, hard cap 40k** (the solid castle's ≤20k is not achievable with an authored interior; say so in the handoff if >30k).
- **⚠️ THE SHELL LAW (why Stage-2 authors the interior):** Meshy image3d produces closed SHELLS — the hollow interior, gate arch, and flat walkable interior floor are **AUTHORED in the Stage-2 Blender step** (Boolean hollow + gate cut + floor), never expected from generation. **The concept must DEPICT an open front gate** so the generated exterior matches the authored opening.
